RULE 5: The store performed by C.MOP.1 is identical to existing store instructions except that the store width is implicitly XLEN.
RULE 6: When xSSE equals 1, C.MOP.1 stores the source register value to memory at address ssp-(XLEN/8).
RULE 7: When xSSE equals 1, C.MOP.1 decrements the ssp register by XLEN/8 after the store completes successfully.
RULE 8: The ssp register is decremented only if the store performed by C.MOP.1 completes successfully.
RULE 9: For the SSPUSH instruction, only registers x1 and x5 are permitted as the source register (rs2).
RULE 10: C.MOP.1 is defined only for the x1 variant of SSPUSH, i.e., C.SSPUSH x1.
RULE 11: If xSSE is not equal to 1, C.MOP.1 performs no store and does not modify ssp.

INSTRUCTION: C.MOP.11
FORMAT: c.mop.11
RULE 1: C.MOP.11 MUST be encoded as the 16-bit pattern 0110010110000001.
RULE 2: C.MOP.11 MUST NOT write any architectural register.

INSTRUCTION: C.MOP.13
FORMAT: c.mop.13
RULE 1: C.MOP.13 MUST NOT modify any architectural register file entry when it is executed.
RULE 2: C.MOP.13 does NOT read any architectural register under the current specification.

INSTRUCTION: C.MOP.15
FORMAT: c.mop.15
RULE 1: C.MOP.15 MUST NOT write any architectural register.

INSTRUCTION: C.MOP.3
FORMAT: c.mop.3
RULE 1: C.MOP.3 MUST NOT write any architectural register.
RULE 2: C.MOP.3 MUST NOT read any architectural register in its current definition.

INSTRUCTION: C.MOP.5
FORMAT: c.mop.5
RULE 1: C.MOP.5 must be a 16-bit compressed instruction whose binary encoding is 0110001010000001.
RULE 2: C.MOP.5 must not write any integer register.
RULE 3: C.MOP.5 loads a value of size XLEN bits from the memory address contained in the shadow-stack pointer register ssp.
RULE 4: C.MOP.5 performs its load, comparison, possible exception, and ssp increment only when the architectural flag xSSE is set to 1; if xSSE is 0 the instruction has no architectural effect.
RULE 5: C.MOP.5 may be used only with rs1 equal to x5; encodings that specify any other source register are reserved.
RULE 6: C.MOP.5 compares the loaded value to the value held in rs1 (x5) and raises a software-check exception with cause 18 and xtval set to "shadow stack fault (code 3)" if the values are not bitwise equal.
RULE 7: C.MOP.5 does not raise the software-check exception when the loaded value equals the value in rs1.
RULE 8: C.MOP.5 increments the shadow-stack pointer ssp by XLEN/8 bytes only if the load completes without an access-fault exception and without raising the software-check exception.
RULE 9: C.MOP.5 raises a software-check exception that has lower priority than any load/store/AMO access-fault exception that may be generated by the same memory access.

INSTRUCTION: C.MOP.7
FORMAT: c.mop.7
RULE 1: C.MOP.7 MUST NOT write any architectural register.

INSTRUCTION: C.MOP.9
FORMAT: c.mop.9
RULE 1: Execution of C.MOP.9 MUST NOT modify any integer register.
RULE 2: C.MOP.9 does NOT read any integer register.

INSTRUCTION: C.MUL
FORMAT: c.mul rd, rs2
RULE 1: C.MUL MUST be implemented only when the M extension or the Zmmul extension is implemented.
RULE 2: C.MUL is defined for both the RV32 and the RV64 base ISA variants.
RULE 3: C.MUL computes the product of the values in rsd' and rs2' and writes the lower XLEN bits of the product to rd' (the same register as rsd').
RULE 4: C.MUL multiplies the signed XLEN-bit values held in registers rsd' and rs2' and produces a signed 2*XLEN-bit intermediate product.
RULE 5: C.MUL writes the lowest XLEN bits of the signed 2*XLEN-bit product back into register rsd'.
RULE 6: In C.MUL the destination register rsd' and the source register rs2' must be selected from the compressed register set x8-x15.
RULE 7: C.MUL treats the register field rsd' as both the destination (rd') and the first source operand (rs1').
RULE 8: C.MUL does not modify any architectural state other than the destination register rsd'.
RULE 9: C.MUL performs a multiply operation.

INSTRUCTION: C.MV
FORMAT: c.mv rd, rs2
RULE 1: C.MV copies the architectural value held in register rs2 into register rd.
RULE 2: C.MV produces the same architectural result as an add instruction with rd as the destination, x0 as the first source, and rs2 as the second source.
RULE 3: C.MV is defined only for encodings in which rs2 is not x0; encodings with rs2 equal to x0 are defined as the C.JR instruction instead of C.MV.
RULE 4: For encodings where rs2 is not x0 and rd is x0, the encoding is defined as a HINT rather than as a C.MV operation.
RULE 5: C.MV MUST be defined only when the destination register rd is x0 and the source register rs2 is not x0; encodings that do not satisfy these constraints are reserved.
RULE 6: Execution of C.MV MUST advance the program counter to the address of the following instruction.
RULE 7: Execution of C.MV MUST NOT modify any architectural integer register state, including x0.
RULE 8: Execution of C.MV MUST NOT modify any architectural memory state.
RULE 9: Implementations that ignore hint instructions MUST treat C.MV as a no-op, causing only the program-counter advancement and any optional performance-counter update.
RULE 10: C.MV MUST be decoded only when opcode bits inst[15:13] equal 100, inst[12] equals 0, inst[1:0] equal 10, and both the destination register field rd and the source register field rs2 are non-zero.
RULE 11: When C.MV is decoded with rd equal to zero, it MUST be treated as a hint that produces no architectural effect.
RULE 12: Encodings of C.MV with rs2 equal to zero are reserved.
RULE 13: C.MV MUST be defined for RV32 implementations.
RULE 14: C.MV MUST be defined for RV64 implementations.

INSTRUCTION: C.NOP
FORMAT: c.nop
RULE 1: The only instruction encoding that decodes to C.NOP is the code point in which the destination register field rd equals x0 and the immediate field imm equals zero.
RULE 2: Any instruction encoding in which rd equals x0 and imm is not zero does not decode to C.NOP; such encodings are defined as HINT instructions.
RULE 3: C.NOP must not modify any architecturally visible state other than advancing the program counter and incrementing any applicable performance counters.
RULE 4: C.NOP has the same architectural effect as the 32-bit NOP instruction.
RULE 5: If the immediate field of a C.NOP encoding is not zero, the instruction is interpreted as a HINT rather than as a NOP.
RULE 6: C.NOP must advance the program counter to the address of the following instruction.
RULE 7: C.NOP must not modify any architectural state other than the program counter and any applicable performance counters.
RULE 8: C.NOP has no defined architectural side-effects, permissions, or exception conditions.
RULE 9: C.NOP must not alter any architectural register, memory, or program-counter state.
RULE 10: C.NOP MUST be recognized as a valid compressed instruction on RV32 implementations.
RULE 11: C.NOP MUST be recognized as a valid compressed instruction on RV64 implementations.

INSTRUCTION: C.NOT
FORMAT: c.not rd
RULE 1: C.NOT performs a bitwise NOT on the value in the source register and writes the result to the destination register.
RULE 2: C.NOT rd'/rs1' MUST compute the one's complement of the value currently held in rd'/rs1' and write the resulting value back to the same register.
RULE 3: C.NOT MUST have no architectural prerequisites and may be executed in any privilege mode.
RULE 4: C.NOT rd'/rs1' MUST specify a register that is one of the standard eight registers x8-x15.
RULE 5: C.NOT MUST produce an architectural result identical to that of the 32-bit instruction xori rd'/rs1', rd'/rs1', -1.
RULE 6: C.NOT MUST update the destination register by XORing its previous content with the constant 1, thereby yielding the bitwise NOT of the original value.

INSTRUCTION: C.NTL.ALL
FORMAT: c.ntl.all
RULE 1: Execution of C.NTL.ALL must not modify any architectural state nor alter the architecturally visible effects of its following target instruction.
RULE 2: If a trap is taken on the target instruction, implementations should ignore C.NTL.ALL rather than applying its effect to the first instruction of the trap handler.
RULE 3: If an interrupt occurs between the execution of C.NTL.ALL and its target instruction, execution resumes at the target instruction without reexecuting C.NTL.ALL, and program semantics are unchanged.
RULE 4: If software follows C.NTL.ALL with an instruction that does not explicitly access memory, there is no architecturally visible effect, although performance may be reduced.
RULE 5: C.NTL.ALL may be placed inside an LR/SC sequence without voiding the forward-progress guarantee of the LR/SC construct.
RULE 6: C.NTL.ALL must not modify any architectural state other than advancing the program counter and updating any applicable performance counters.
RULE 7: Executing C.NTL.ALL must advance the program counter to the address of the following instruction.
RULE 8: Executing C.NTL.ALL may update any applicable performance counters.
RULE 9: Implementations that ignore HINT instructions must execute C.NTL.ALL as a no-op.

INSTRUCTION: C.NTL.P1
FORMAT: c.ntl.p1
RULE 1: C.NTL.P1 MUST NOT change architectural state nor alter the architecturally visible effects of its target instruction.
RULE 2: If a trap is taken on the target instruction, implementations SHOULD ignore C.NTL.P1 rather than applying it to the first instruction in the trap handler.
RULE 3: If an interrupt occurs between the execution of C.NTL.P1 and its target instruction, execution shall resume at the target instruction without reexecuting C.NTL.P1, and this does not change the program's architectural semantics.
RULE 4: Execution of C.NTL.P1 must not modify any architectural state other than advancing the program counter.
RULE 5: Execution of C.NTL.P1 must not modify any architectural state other than updating any applicable performance counters.
RULE 6: An implementation that chooses to ignore HINT instructions must execute C.NTL.P1 as a no-op, performing only the required program-counter advancement.

INSTRUCTION: C.NTL.PALL
FORMAT: c.ntl.pall
RULE 1: C.NTL.PALL must not change any architectural state and must not alter the architecturally visible effects of its target instruction.
RULE 2: C.NTL.PALL affects all memory-access instructions except cache-management instructions that belong to the Zicbom extension.
RULE 3: If an interrupt occurs between execution of C.NTL.PALL and its target instruction, execution resumes at the target instruction without reexecuting C.NTL.PALL, and program semantics remain unchanged.
RULE 4: Execution of C.NTL.PALL does not modify any architectural state except for advancing the program counter and updating any applicable performance counters.
RULE 5: Implementations may ignore C.NTL.PALL, and when ignored the instruction is executed as a no-op.

INSTRUCTION: C.NTL.S1
FORMAT: c.ntl.s1
RULE 1: C.NTL.S1 must not change any architectural state and must not alter the architecturally visible effects of the immediately subsequent instruction.
RULE 2: Following a C.NTL.S1 with a non-memory-access instruction may reduce performance but must not produce any architecturally visible effect.
RULE 3: When a trap is taken on the target instruction of a C.NTL.S1, implementations should ignore the C.NTL.S1 hint for the first instruction in the trap handler.
RULE 4: If an interrupt occurs between the execution of a C.NTL.S1 and its target instruction, execution must resume at the target instruction and program semantics remain unchanged.
RULE 5: A C.NTL.S1 may be placed inside an LR/SC loop without invalidating the forward-progress guarantee of the LR/SC sequence.
RULE 6: C.NTL.S1 MUST advance the program counter to the address of the next instruction.
RULE 7: C.NTL.S1 MAY update any applicable performance counters as defined for RVC HINT instructions.
RULE 8: C.NTL.S1 MUST NOT modify any architectural state other than the program counter and performance counters.
RULE 9: An implementation MAY treat C.NTL.S1 as a no-operation.
RULE 10: If an implementation does not treat C.NTL.S1 as a no-operation, it executes the instruction as a regular computational instruction that does not mutate architectural state.

INSTRUCTION: C.SB
FORMAT: c.sb rs2, c_uimm2(rs1)
RULE 1: C.SB stores the least-significant byte of the source register to the memory address computed from the base register and the unsigned immediate, having the same architectural effect as the 32-bit store-byte instruction.
RULE 2: C.SB forms a 32-bit unsigned immediate uimm where bits 31 down to 2 are zero, bit 1 is taken from instruction bit 5, and bit 0 is taken from instruction bit 6.
RULE 3: C.SB zero-extends the immediate uimm to the XLEN width before adding it to the base address.
RULE 4: C.SB computes the effective memory address by adding the zero-extended uimm to the value held in the base register rs1'.
RULE 5: C.SB uses the compressed source register rs2' (which maps to one of the architectural registers x8-x15) as the data source for the store.
RULE 6: C.SB stores the least significant byte of the value in rs2' to the memory location whose address is the sum of the value in rs1' and the zero-extended uimm.
RULE 7: C.SB writes only bits 7 down to 0 of the addressed byte, performing a byte-store to memory.
RULE 8: C.SB selects its rs1' and rs2' operands exclusively from the standard compressed register set x8-x15.
RULE 9: C.SB imposes no architectural prerequisites and may be executed in any processor state.

INSTRUCTION: C.SD
FORMAT: c.sd rs2, c_uimm8(rs1)
RULE 1: C.SD is an RV64C-only instruction, meaning it is defined only when the RV64C extension is present.
RULE 2: C.SD stores the 64-bit value held in register rs2′ to memory.
RULE 3: C.SD computes its effective address by adding to the base address in register rs1′ the zero-extended offset field multiplied by 8.
RULE 4: C.SD has the same architectural effect as the 64-bit store instruction sd with operands rs2′ and offset(rs1′).
RULE 5: C.SD is decoded from a 16-bit instruction whose two low bits are 00, bits [15:13] are 111, and whose fields are uimm[5:3], rs1′, uimm[7:6], and rs2′ as specified in the RVC opcode map.
RULE 6: C.SD is defined only for implementations that support the RV64 base ISA and is not defined for RV32 implementations.
RULE 7: In the architectural effect of C.SD, the rs1′ field supplies the base address register whose value is added to the unsigned immediate to form the effective store address.
RULE 8: In the architectural effect of C.SD, the rs2′ field supplies the double-word value that is written to memory at the effective store address.
RULE 9: In C.SD, the unsigned immediate is formed by concatenating uimm[5:3] as the low part and uimm[7:6] as the high part to produce the offset that is added to the base address.
RULE 10: C.SD must store the 64-bit value from the indicated source register into memory.
RULE 11: C.SD must compute the target memory address by adding an implicit offset of zero to the value in the indicated base register.
RULE 12: C.SD must be available only on RV32 implementations and must not be available on RV64 implementations.
RULE 13: C.SD must be encoded as a 16-bit compressed instruction.
RULE 14: C.SD stores a doubleword from the register pair identified by rs2' to the memory address formed by adding the sign-extended offset to the address in rs1'.
RULE 15: C.SD cannot be used on a processor that implements the Zcf extension because the Zclsd encodings overlap with Zcf encodings.
RULE 16: C.SD MUST use only registers x8-x15 for both its source and base register operands.
RULE 17: When the source register field of a C.SD instruction is x0, the 64-bit value stored to memory by the instruction must be zero.
RULE 18: C.SD stores a 64-bit value to memory, where the value is taken from the register pair consisting of rs2' and the next higher register rs2'+1.
RULE 19: C.SD computes the effective memory address by adding to the contents of base register rs1' the product of eight and the zero-extended offset field encoded in the instruction.
RULE 20: C.SD writes the 64-bit value from rs2' and rs2'+1 to the computed effective address.
RULE 21: In C.SD the offset field is zero-extended to XLEN bits prior to being multiplied by eight.

INSTRUCTION: C.SDSP
FORMAT: c.sdsp rs2, c_uimm9(sp)
RULE 1: C.SDSP stores the 64-bit value contained in register rs2 to the memory location addressed by its effective address.
RULE 2: C.SDSP computes its effective address by adding to the stack pointer register x2 the zero-extended offset field multiplied by 8.
RULE 3: C.SDSP has the same architectural effect as executing the base ISA instruction sd rs2, offset(x2).
RULE 4: C.SDSP derives its high-order immediate field from the instruction bits designated uimm[5:3] and uimm[8:6].
RULE 5: C.SDSP derives its low-order immediate field from the same instruction bits designated uimm[5:3] and uimm[8:6].
RULE 6: C.SDSP uses the rs2′ field to specify the source register rs2.
RULE 7: C.SDSP stores a doubleword to memory at the address obtained by adding a sign-extended offset encoded in the instruction to the current value of the stack pointer (sp).
RULE 8: C.SDSP uses the register identified by rs2 (and the adjacent register forming a register pair) as the source of the doubleword to be stored.
RULE 9: If the source register field of a C.SDSP instruction encodes x0, the instruction MUST store a 64-bit value of zero.
RULE 10: If the source register field of a C.SDSP instruction encodes x0, the implementation MUST not read architectural register x0.
RULE 11: C.SDSP stores a 64-bit value taken from the register pair consisting of rs2' and the next higher register rs2'+1 to a memory address that is computed relative to the stack pointer.
RULE 12: C.SDSP computes the effective memory address by adding to the stack pointer register x2 the product of eight and the zero-extended offset field encoded in the instruction.
RULE 13: C.SDSP has the same architectural effect as executing the non-compressed instruction sd rs2, offset(x2) with the same rs2 and offset values.
RULE 14: C.SDSP performs an explicit memory store access.
RULE 15: C.SDSP must be subject to the pointer-masking mechanism, so its effective address is transformed according to the current privilege mode's pointer-masking settings unless the MXR setting disables masking.
RULE 16: C.SDSP may access a misaligned address, and the access is carried out as if the pointer-masking transformation were applied separately to each aligned byte of the access, with the same limitations that apply when pointer masking is not enabled.

INSTRUCTION: C.SEXT.B
FORMAT: c.sext.b rd
RULE 1: C.SEXT.B has a 16-bit encoding and sign-extends the low-order byte of the source register into the destination register.
RULE 2: C.SEXT.B must be implemented in RV32 implementations.
RULE 3: C.SEXT.B must be implemented in RV64 implementations.
RULE 4: C.SEXT.B is only present when the Zbb extension is implemented.
RULE 5: C.SEXT.B must sign-extend the least-significant byte of the source register to XLEN bits by copying bit 7 of that byte into all more-significant bits of the destination register.
RULE 6: C.SEXT.B must use a single register operand that serves as both the source and the destination of the sign-extension operation.
RULE 7: C.SEXT.B must select its operand from the standard eight registers x8-x15, identified as rd′/rs1′.
RULE 8: C.SEXT.B is only defined when the Zbb extension is implemented in the processor.

INSTRUCTION: C.SEXT.H
FORMAT: c.sext.h rd
RULE 1: An implementation that includes the Zcb extension MUST support the compressed instruction C.SEXT.H.
RULE 2: C.SEXT.H must sign-extend the least-significant halfword of the source operand to XLEN bits by copying bit 15 into all more-significant bits of the destination register.
RULE 3: C.SEXT.H must treat its single operand rd′/rs1′ as both the source and the destination register.
RULE 4: C.SEXT.H must restrict rd′/rs1′ to be one of the registers x8, x9, x10, x11, x12, x13, x14, or x15.
RULE 5: C.SEXT.H is only defined when the Zbb extension is implemented.

INSTRUCTION: C.SH
FORMAT: c.sh rs2, c_uimm1(rs1)
RULE 1: C.SH stores a halfword (16 bits) from the source register rs2′ to memory.
RULE 2: The halfword written by C.SH is the low-order 16 bits of rs2′.
RULE 3: C.SH computes the effective memory address by adding the zero-extended immediate uimm to the base register rs1′.
RULE 4: C.SH is only available when the Zcb extension is implemented.
RULE 5: C.SH MUST be decoded using the CSH instruction format.
RULE 6: In the CSH format, bits 15-10 of the 16-bit encoding of C.SH are interpreted as the funct6 field.
RULE 7: In the CSH format, bit 9 of the 16-bit encoding of C.SH selects the rs1' register field.
RULE 8: In the CSH format, bit 8 of the 16-bit encoding of C.SH is reserved and has no architectural effect for C.SH.
RULE 9: In the CSH format, bit 7 of the 16-bit encoding of C.SH is reserved and has no architectural effect for C.SH.
RULE 10: In the CSH format, bit 6 of the 16-bit encoding of C.SH is interpreted as the funct1 field.
RULE 11: In the CSH format, bit 5 of the 16-bit encoding of C.SH provides the uimm field.
RULE 12: In the CSH format, bit 4 of the 16-bit encoding of C.SH selects the rs2' register field.
RULE 13: In the CSH format, bits 3 and 2 of the 16-bit encoding of C.SH are reserved and have no architectural effect for C.SH.
RULE 14: In the CSH format, bit 1 of the 16-bit encoding of C.SH provides the op field.
RULE 15: In the CSH format, bit 0 of the 16-bit encoding of C.SH is reserved and has no architectural effect for C.SH.
RULE 16: The row in the CSH format table that defines C.SH specifies that the funct6, rs1', funct1, uimm, rs2', and op fields are used while all other fields are unused for C.SH.
RULE 17: C.SH stores the least-significant halfword (bits 15..0) of the source register rs2' into memory.
RULE 18: C.SH computes the effective memory address by adding the contents of the base register rs1' to the zero-extended immediate uimm.
RULE 19: C.SH forms the immediate uimm by setting bits 31:2 to zero, setting bit 1 to the instruction encoding bit 5, and setting bit 0 to zero.
RULE 20: C.SH zero-extends the immediate uimm to XLEN before adding it to the value in rs1'.
RULE 21: C.SH selects both rs1' and rs2' from the standard register set x8 through x15.
RULE 22: C.SH has no architectural prerequisites and may be executed in any privilege mode.
RULE 23: C.SH updates only the 16-bit halfword at the effective address, leaving surrounding memory bytes unchanged.

INSTRUCTION: C.SLLI
FORMAT: c.slli rd, shamt
RULE 1: C.SLLI performs a logical left shift of the value in register rd by the amount specified in the shamt field and writes the shifted result back to rd.
RULE 2: C.SLLI encodings with shamt = 0 are treated as HINTs.
RULE 3: C.SLLI encodings with rd = x0 are treated as HINTs.
RULE 4: C.SLLI does not modify any architectural state except that it advances the program counter and may update applicable performance counters.
RULE 5: If an implementation chooses to ignore HINT instructions, C.SLLI is executed as a no-op.
RULE 6: When rd is x0, C.SLLI does not modify any architectural state.
RULE 7: When imm is zero, C.SLLI does not modify any architectural state because the destination register is overwritten with a copy of its original value.
RULE 8: C.SLLI's rs1/rd field must not be zero (rs1/rd ≠ 0).
RULE 9: For C.SLLI the hint condition is indicated when the destination register field is zero or the immediate value is zero.
RULE 10: C.SLLI MUST be supported in RV32 implementations.
RULE 11: C.SLLI MUST be supported in RV64 implementations.

INSTRUCTION: C.SRAI
FORMAT: c.srai rd, shamt
RULE 1: C.SRAI performs an arithmetic right shift of the value in register rd' and writes the resulting value back to register rd'.
RULE 2: When the shamt field of C.SRAI is zero, the encoding is defined as a HINT.
RULE 3: In RV32C, C.SRAI MUST have shamt[5] equal to zero, and any encoding with shamt[5] equal to one is designated for custom extensions.
RULE 4: C.SRAI MUST have its immediate field equal to zero (imm = 0).
RULE 5: When executed as a HINT, C.SRAI MUST NOT modify any architectural state except for advancing the program counter and updating any applicable performance counters.
RULE 6: On implementations that ignore HINTs, C.SRAI MUST behave as a no-op, affecting no architectural state other than advancing the program counter.
RULE 7: In any implementation that treats C.SRAI as a HINT, the instruction MUST satisfy that its destination register is x0 or that the destination register is the same as its source register, ensuring no architectural state change.
RULE 8: C.SRAI MUST have its immediate field uimm equal to zero; encodings with a non-zero uimm are not defined by the specification.
RULE 9: C.SRAI does NOT cause any architectural state change.
RULE 10: C.SRAI does NOT perform permission checks, raise exceptions, or define any execution semantics beyond being a hint.
RULE 11: C.SRAI MUST be present in any implementation that supports the RVC (Compressed) instruction set for both RV32 and RV64 architectures.
RULE 12: C.SRAI MUST obey the same architectural criteria and semantics as defined for the corresponding shift-right-arithmetic-immediate operation in the base integer ISA (RVI).

INSTRUCTION: C.SRLI
FORMAT: c.srli rd, shamt
RULE 1: C.SRLI logically right shifts the value in register rd' by the amount specified in the shamt field and writes the shifted result back to register rd'.
RULE 2: C.SRLI obtains its shift amount from the shamt field of the instruction encoding.
RULE 3: C.SRLI is architecturally equivalent to the 32-bit instruction srli rd', rd', shamt.
RULE 4: C.SRLI with a shamt field equal to zero is defined as a HINT instruction.
RULE 5: In RV32C implementations, C.SRLI must have bit 5 of the shamt field equal to zero, and encodings where shamt[5] is one are designated for custom extensions.
RULE 6: The immediate field of C.SRLI must be zero.
RULE 7: Execution of C.SRLI does not modify any architectural state except that it advances the program counter to the next instruction and may update any applicable performance counters.
RULE 8: An implementation may choose to ignore hint instructions and therefore may execute C.SRLI as a no-op, producing no architectural effect beyond the normal program-counter advance.
RULE 9: C.SRLI with an unsigned immediate value equal to zero is a hint and has no defined architectural effect.
RULE 10: C.SRLI must be defined for RV32 implementations.
RULE 11: C.SRLI must be defined for RV64 implementations.
RULE 12: C.SRLI is a compressed instruction.

INSTRUCTION: C.SUB
FORMAT: c.sub rd, rs2
RULE 1: C.SUB MUST subtract the integer value in register rs2′ from the integer value in register rd′ and write the resulting integer value back to register rd′.
RULE 2: C.SUB MUST produce the same architectural result as executing the integer register-register subtraction instruction sub rd′, rd′, rs2′.
RULE 3: C.SUB must be a 16-bit compressed instruction that resides in Quadrant 1, identified by bits[1:0] = 01.
RULE 4: C.SUB must have bits[15:13] equal to the binary pattern 100.
RULE 5: C.SUB must have bit 12 equal to 0.
RULE 6: C.SUB must have bits[11:10] equal to the binary pattern 11.
RULE 7: C.SUB must encode rs1′/rd′ in bits[9:7], which serve as both the first source register and the destination register.
RULE 8: C.SUB must have bits[6:5] equal to the binary pattern 00.
RULE 9: C.SUB must encode rs2′ in bits[4:2], which serves as the second source register.
RULE 10: C.SUB encodes its destination register and both source registers using the CA fields, thereby selecting registers x8–x15.

INSTRUCTION: C.SUBW
FORMAT: c.subw rd, rs2
RULE 1: C.SUBW subtracts the value in register rs2′ from the value in register rd′, sign-extends the lower 32 bits of the difference, and writes the result to register rd′.
RULE 2: C.SUBW has the same architectural effect as executing the subw rd′, rd′, rs2′ instruction.
RULE 3: An RV64 implementation must treat the execution of C.SUBW as defined behavior and must not raise an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 4: An RV32 implementation must treat the execution of C.SUBW as reserved and must raise an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 5: C.SUBW is not defined for RV32, and its encoding is reserved on RV32.

INSTRUCTION: C.SW
FORMAT: c.sw rs2, c_uimm7(rs1)
RULE 1: C.SW stores the 32-bit value held in register rs2' to the memory location addressed by its effective address.
RULE 2: C.SW computes its effective address by zero-extending the immediate, multiplying the result by 4, and adding it to the contents of register rs1'.
RULE 3: C.SW has the same architectural effect as executing the 32-bit store instruction sw rs2', offset(rs1') with the same register values and immediate.

INSTRUCTION: C.SWSP
FORMAT: c.swsp rs2, c_uimm8(sp)
RULE 1: C.SWSP MUST store the 32-bit value from register rs2 to the memory location whose address is the effective address computed for the instruction.
RULE 2: C.SWSP MUST compute its effective address by adding to the stack pointer register x2 the zero-extended offset field multiplied by 4.
RULE 3: C.SWSP MUST be treated as the 32-bit store instruction sw rs2, offset(x2).
RULE 4: C.SWSP MUST be supported by both RV32 and RV64 implementations.
RULE 5: C.SWSP uses the uimm[5:2|7:6] field as the unsigned immediate offset for the store address.
RULE 6: C.SWSP reads the word to be stored from the rs2 register.
RULE 7: C.SWSP is a compressed instruction that performs an explicit memory store and is included in the set of compressed instructions subject to pointer masking.
RULE 8: Pointer masking MUST be applied to the address generated by C.SWSP.
RULE 9: Misaligned C.SWSP stores are supported and their behavior is identical to applying the pointer masking transformation to each constituent aligned memory access that composes the misaligned store.
RULE 10: Because C.SWSP is not an SFENCE.*, HFENCE.*, SINVAL.*, or HINVAL.* operation, pointer masking MUST be applied to the address used by C.SWSP.

INSTRUCTION: C.XOR
FORMAT: c.xor rd, rs2
RULE 1: C.XOR must compute the bitwise exclusive-OR of the architectural values in registers rd′ and rs2′ and write the resulting value to register rd′.
RULE 2: C.XOR must have the same architectural effect as executing the integer instruction xor rd′, rd′, rs2′.

INSTRUCTION: C.ZEXT.B
FORMAT: c.zext.b rd
RULE 1: C.ZEXT.B reads the least-significant byte of the source register, zero-extends it to the register width, and writes the result to the destination register.
RULE 2: C.ZEXT.B is available in both RV32 and RV64 implementations.
RULE 3: C.ZEXT.B takes a single register operand that serves as both source and destination.
RULE 4: C.ZEXT.B zero-extends the least-significant byte of the specified register to XLEN bits by setting all bits more significant than bit 7 to zero.
RULE 5: C.ZEXT.B has no architectural prerequisites and may be executed in any privilege mode.
RULE 6: The architectural effect of C.ZEXT.B is equivalent to executing the 32-bit instruction andi rd, rd, 0xff on the same register.
RULE 7: The register specified by C.ZEXT.B must be one of the standard registers x8 through x15; using any other register is not defined by the specification.
RULE 8: The C.ZEXT.B instruction performs a zero-extend of a byte.
RULE 9: Implementations that support the RV32 ISA must include the C.ZEXT.B instruction.
RULE 10: Implementations that support the RV64 ISA must include the C.ZEXT.B instruction.

INSTRUCTION: C.ZEXT.H
FORMAT: c.zext.h rd
RULE 1: C.ZEXT.H is a 16-bit compressed instruction that reads the low 16 bits of the register rsd', zero-extends that halfword to XLEN bits, and writes the result back to rsd'.
RULE 2: C.ZEXT.H causes no architectural side effects other than updating rsd' with the zero-extended value.
RULE 3: C.ZEXT.H does not perform any permission checks and does not raise any exceptions.
RULE 4: C.ZEXT.H MUST be available only when the Zbb extension is implemented.
RULE 5: C.ZEXT.H MUST use a register from the standard set x8-x15 as both its source and destination operand.
RULE 6: C.ZEXT.H reads the XLEN-bit value from the specified register, extracts bits 15 down to 0, and writes back a value whose bits 15-0 equal the extracted bits and whose bits XLEN-1 down to 16 are zeros.
RULE 7: C.ZEXT.H updates the same architectural register that provides the operand, performing the zero-extension in place.
RULE 8: C.ZEXT.H instruction is guaranteed to exist only when both the Zcb and Zbb extensions are implemented.

INSTRUCTION: C.ZEXT.W
FORMAT: c.zext.w rd
RULE 1: C.ZEXT.W MUST produce a 64-bit result in which bits 31:0 equal bits 31:0 of the source register rsd′ and bits 63:32 are cleared to zero.
RULE 2: C.ZEXT.W performs the same architectural operation as the 32-bit zero-extend-word instruction from the application-class profile.
RULE 3: C.ZEXT.W MUST read its operand from the architectural register rd'/rs1' which is required to be one of the registers x8-x15.
RULE 4: C.ZEXT.W MUST write its result back to the same architectural register rd'/rs1'.
RULE 5: C.ZEXT.W MUST set bits 0-31 of the destination register to the corresponding bits 0-31 of the source register value.
RULE 6: C.ZEXT.W MUST set bits 32 through XLEN-1 of the destination register to zero.
RULE 7: C.ZEXT.W is only defined on implementations that support the Zba extension; on implementations without Zba the encoding is reserved.
RULE 8: C.ZEXT.W MUST update the architectural register file entry corresponding to rd'/rs1' with the zero-extended 32-bit value of its prior contents.
RULE 9: C.ZEXT.W has an architectural effect equivalent to executing add.uw rd'/rs1', rd'/rs1', zero on a 32-bit implementation.

INSTRUCTION: CBO.CLEAN
FORMAT: cbo.clean (rs1)
RULE 1: CBO.CLEAN MUST raise an illegal-instruction exception if the current privilege mode is not Machine and the menvcfg.CBCFE bit is clear.
RULE 2: CBO.CLEAN MUST raise an illegal-instruction exception if the current privilege mode is User and the senvcfg.CBCFE bit is clear.
RULE 3: CBO.CLEAN MUST raise a virtual-instruction exception if the current privilege mode is VS and the henvcfg.CBCFE bit is clear.
RULE 4: CBO.CLEAN MUST raise a virtual-instruction exception if the current privilege mode is VU and either the henvcfg.CBCFE bit is clear or the senvcfg.CBCFE bit is clear.
RULE 5: CBO.CLEAN MUST execute (perform its cache-block clean operation) when neither its illegal-instruction exception condition nor its virtual-instruction exception condition is true.
RULE 6: CBO.CLEAN's illegal-instruction and virtual-instruction exception conditions are identical to the corresponding exception conditions for CBO.FLUSH.
RULE 7: CBO.CLEAN MUST perform a clean operation that makes data from store operations performed by coherent agents visible to non-coherent agents at a point common to both sets.
RULE 8: CBO.CLEAN MUST ensure that store operations from coherent agents become visible to all non-coherent agents.
RULE 9: CBO.CLEAN MUST operate on the cache block whose effective address is specified in rs1.
RULE 10: CBO.CLEAN MUST ignore the PMA cacheable attribute when determining the cache block to operate on.
RULE 11: CBO.CLEAN MUST ignore any Page-Based Memory Type downgrade from cacheable to non-cacheable when determining the cache block to operate on.
RULE 12: CBO.CLEAN must clean the cache block whose effective address is the base address specified in rs1.
RULE 13: If CBO.CLEAN includes an offset operand, the expression that computes the offset shall evaluate to zero.
RULE 14: CBO.CLEAN execution in any privilege mode less privileged than M MUST NOT raise an illegal-instruction exception when the CBCFE field in the menvcfg CSR is set to 1.
RULE 15: CBO.CLEAN execution in any privilege mode less privileged than M MUST raise an illegal-instruction exception when the CBCFE field in the menvcfg CSR is 0.
RULE 16: When the Zicbom extension is not implemented, the CBCFE field is read-only zero, and CBO.CLEAN execution in any privilege mode less privileged than M MUST raise an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 17: CBO.CLEAN execution in U-mode MUST be permitted only if the instruction is enabled for use in S-mode and the CBCFE field in senvcfg is set to 1.
RULE 18: If CBO.CLEAN is executed in U-mode when either the instruction is not enabled for S-mode use or the CBCFE field is not 1, an illegal-instruction exception MUST be raised.
RULE 19: When the Zicbom extension is not implemented, the CBCFE field is read-only zero, causing any execution of CBO.CLEAN in U-mode to raise an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 20: When V=1 and a CBO.CLEAN instruction is not HS-qualified, execution of the instruction MUST raise an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 21: When V=1, a CBO.CLEAN instruction is HS-qualified, and the CBCFE field in henvcfg is 1, execution of the instruction MUST be enabled.
RULE 22: When V=1, a CBO.CLEAN instruction is HS-qualified, and the CBCFE field in henvcfg is 0, execution of the instruction MUST raise a virtual-instruction exception.
RULE 23: If the Zicbom extension is not implemented, the CBCFE field in henvcfg reads as zero, and execution of a HS-qualified CBO.CLEAN instruction MUST raise a virtual-instruction exception.

INSTRUCTION: CBO.FLUSH
FORMAT: cbo.flush (rs1)
RULE 1: CBO.FLUSH MUST raise an illegal-instruction exception if ((priv_mode != M) && !menvcfg.CBCFE) or ((priv_mode == U) && !senvcfg.CBCFE).
RULE 2: CBO.FLUSH MUST raise a virtual-instruction exception if ((priv_mode == VS) && !henvcfg.CBCFE) or ((priv_mode == VU) && !(henvcfg.CBCFE && senvcfg.CBCFE)).
RULE 3: CBO.FLUSH MUST execute when neither the illegal-instruction nor the virtual-instruction exception conditions are true.
RULE 4: CBO.FLUSH MUST flush the cache block that contains the address held in rs1.
RULE 5: CBO.FLUSH does NOT require rs1 to be aligned to the size of a cache block.
RULE 6: If a fault occurs during execution of CBO.FLUSH, the faulting virtual address is defined to be the value in rs1.
RULE 7: If the Zicbom extension is implemented and the CBCFE field in menvcfg is 1, then execution of CBO.FLUSH in any privilege mode less privileged than M MUST NOT raise an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 8: If the Zicbom extension is implemented and the CBCFE field in menvcfg is 0, then execution of CBO.FLUSH in any privilege mode less privileged than M MUST raise an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 9: If the Zicbom extension is not implemented, then the CBCFE field in menvcfg is read-only zero and execution of CBO.FLUSH in any privilege mode less privileged than M MUST raise an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 10: CBO.FLUSH execution in U-mode MUST be enabled only if the instruction is enabled for S-mode and CBCFE is set to 1; otherwise it MUST raise an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 11: When the Zicbom extension is not implemented, the CBCFE field that controls CBO.FLUSH in U-mode is read-only zero.
RULE 12: When a CBO.FLUSH instruction is executed to the address of a location that is placed between two fence iorw, iorw instructions, it must prevent loss of coherence for accesses that use different cacheability attributes.
RULE 13: When a CBO.FLUSH instruction is executed to the address of a location that is placed between two fence iorw, iorw instructions, it must prevent loss of memory ordering for accesses that use different cacheability attributes.
RULE 14: When the Zicbom extension is implemented, the henvcfg.CBCFE field is 1, and the CBO.FLUSH instruction is HS-qualified, the CBO.FLUSH instruction MUST be enabled for execution.
RULE 15: When the Zicbom extension is implemented, the henvcfg.CBCFE field is 0, and the CBO.FLUSH instruction is HS-qualified, the CBO.FLUSH instruction MUST raise a virtual-instruction exception.
RULE 16: When the V bit in henvcfg is 1 and the CBO.FLUSH instruction is not HS-qualified, the CBO.FLUSH instruction MUST raise an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 17: When the Zicbom extension is not implemented, the henvcfg.CBCFE field reads as zero, and the CBO.FLUSH instruction is HS-qualified, the CBO.FLUSH instruction MUST raise a virtual-instruction exception.

INSTRUCTION: CBO.INVAL
FORMAT: cbo.inval (rs1)
RULE 1: CBO.INVAL MUST raise an illegal-instruction exception if the current privilege mode is not M and menvcfg.CBIE = 00, or if the current privilege mode is U and senvcfg.CBIE = 00.
RULE 2: CBO.INVAL MUST raise a virtual-instruction exception if the current privilege mode is VS and henvcfg.CBIE = 00, or if the current privilege mode is VU and (henvcfg.CBIE = 00 or senvcfg.CBIE = 00).
RULE 3: If the current privilege mode is not M and menvcfg.CBIE = 01, or if the current privilege mode is U and senvcfg.CBIE = 01, or if the current privilege mode is VS and henvcfg.CBIE = 01, or if the current privilege mode is VU and (henvcfg.CBIE = 01 or senvcfg.CBIE = 01), then CBO.INVAL MUST perform a flush operation on the targeted cache block.
RULE 4: If none of the following conditions hold—privilege mode not M and menvcfg.CBIE = 01, privilege mode = U and senvcfg.CBIE = 01, privilege mode = VS and henvcfg.CBIE = 01, privilege mode = VU and (henvcfg.CBIE = 01 or senvcfg.CBIE = 01)—then CBO.INVAL MUST perform an invalidate operation on the targeted cache block.
RULE 5: CBO.INVAL operates on the cache block whose effective address is specified in integer register rs1.
RULE 6: The effective address supplied to CBO.INVAL in rs1 is translated to a physical address by the implementation's address-translation mechanisms before the invalidate operation is applied.
RULE 7: CBO.INVAL ignores cacheability attributes, including the PMA cacheable attribute and any Page-Based Memory Type (PBMT) downgrade from cacheable to non-cacheable, when operating on the target cache block.
RULE 8: CBO.INVAL performs an invalidate operation on the cache block that contains the address held in rs1.
RULE 9: CBO.INVAL does not require the value in rs1 to be aligned to the size of a cache block.
RULE 10: If a fault occurs during execution of CBO.INVAL, the faulting virtual address is defined as the value in rs1.
RULE 11: Depending on CSR configuration, CBO.INVAL may perform a flush operation instead of an invalidate operation.
RULE 12: If CBIE is set to 00b, executing CBO.INVAL in any mode less privileged than M MUST raise an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 13: When the Zicbom extension is not implemented, CBIE is read-only zero, causing CBO.INVAL to raise an illegal-instruction exception in modes less privileged than M.
RULE 14: CBO.INVAL execution in U-mode is enabled only if the instruction is also enabled for use in S-mode and the CBIE field in senvcfg is set to 01b or 11b; otherwise an illegal-instruction exception is raised.
RULE 15: When the Zicbom extension is not implemented, the CBIE field is read-only zero, and CBO.INVAL execution in U-mode raises an illegal-instruction exception because CBIE is not 01b or 11b.
RULE 16: If CBO.INVAL is enabled in S-mode to perform a flush operation, then when the instruction is enabled in U-mode it performs a flush operation even if CBIE is set to 11b.
RULE 17: If CBO.INVAL is enabled in U-mode and CBIE is 01b, the instruction is executed and performs a flush operation.
RULE 18: If CBO.INVAL is enabled in U-mode and CBIE is 11b, the instruction is executed and performs an invalidate operation.
RULE 19: CBO.INVAL execution in VS and VU privilege modes is controlled by the CBIE field in henvcfg.
RULE 20: When the Zicbom extension is not implemented, the CBIE field reads as zero, so CBO.INVAL is not enabled in VS or VU privilege modes.
RULE 21: When V=1 and a CBO.INVAL instruction is not HS-qualified, the instruction raises an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 22: When V=1 and a CBO.INVAL instruction is HS-qualified and the CBIE field is 01b or 11b, the instruction is enabled for execution.
RULE 23: When V=1 and a CBO.INVAL instruction is HS-qualified and the CBIE field is any value other than 01b or 11b, the instruction raises a virtual-instruction exception.
RULE 24: If CBO.INVAL is enabled in HS-mode to perform a flush operation, then any execution of CBO.INVAL that is enabled in VS or VU mode performs a flush operation regardless of the CBIE encoding.
RULE 25: When CBO.INVAL is enabled for execution and the CBIE field is 01b, the instruction executes and performs a flush operation even if VS-mode configuration would otherwise request an invalidate operation.
RULE 26: When CBO.INVAL is enabled for execution and the CBIE field is 11b, the instruction executes and performs an invalidate operation unless VS-mode configuration specifies that the operation shall be a flush, in which case it performs a flush operation.

INSTRUCTION: CBO.ZERO
FORMAT: cbo.zero (rs1)
RULE 1: CBO.ZERO zeros out a range of memory locations.
RULE 2: The access size of CBO.ZERO equals the size of the cache block.
RULE 3: When CBO.ZERO is decomposed into multiple memory operations and the PMP access-control bits are identical for all involved physical addresses, the operation may complete successfully without raising an access fault.
RULE 4: When CBO.ZERO is performed as a single memory operation while the PMP access-control bits differ across the cache block, a store access-fault exception must be raised.
RULE 5: CBO.ZERO is permitted to access a cache block only when a store instruction is permitted to access the corresponding physical addresses and the PMAs indicate that cache-block zero instructions are a supported access type.
RULE 6: If CBO.ZERO access is not permitted because address translation does not allow write access, a store page-fault or store guest-page-fault exception is raised.
RULE 7: If CBO.ZERO access is not permitted for reasons other than disallowed write access, a store access-fault exception is raised.
RULE 8: During address translation, CBO.ZERO checks the accessed and dirty bits and may either raise an exception or set those bits as required.
RULE 9: When a page-fault, guest-page-fault, or access-fault exception is taken due to CBO.ZERO, the tval CSR is written with the faulting effective address supplied in rs1.
RULE 10: CBO.ZERO may be permitted or denied based on the states of the MPRV, MPV, and MPP bits in mstatus and the SUM and MXR bits in mstatus, sstatus, and vsstatus, analogous to load and store instructions.
RULE 11: Implementations must process exceptions generated by CBO.ZERO as store or AMO exceptions, regardless of the specific permission checks that caused the exception.
RULE 12: CBO.ZERO MUST raise an illegal-instruction exception when the current privilege mode is not M and the menvcfg.CBZE bit is clear.
RULE 13: CBO.ZERO MUST raise an illegal-instruction exception when the current privilege mode is U and the senvcfg.CBZE bit is clear.
RULE 14: CBO.ZERO MUST raise a virtual-instruction exception when the current privilege mode is VS and the henvcfg.CBZE bit is clear.
RULE 15: CBO.ZERO MUST raise a virtual-instruction exception when the current privilege mode is VU and either the henvcfg.CBZE bit or the senvcfg.CBZE bit is clear.
RULE 16: CBO.ZERO MUST execute normally when none of the illegal-instruction or virtual-instruction exception conditions are true.
RULE 17: CBO.ZERO must store zeros to every byte of the cache block whose effective address is specified in rs1.
RULE 18: CBO.ZERO must store zeros regardless of whether the underlying memory locations are cacheable.
RULE 19: CBO.ZERO operates on the cache block or the memory locations that correspond to the cache block whose effective address is provided in rs1.
RULE 20: CBO.ZERO MUST store zeros to every byte of the cache block that contains the address specified in rs1.
RULE 21: CBO.ZERO does NOT require the address in rs1 to be aligned to the size of a cache block.
RULE 22: If a fault occurs during execution of CBO.ZERO, the faulting virtual address is defined to be the value in rs1 rather than the base address of the cache block.
RULE 23: In any privilege mode less privileged than M, if the CBZE field is set to 1, CBO.ZERO must execute without raising an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 24: In any privilege mode less privileged than M, if the CBZE field is not set to 1, CBO.ZERO must raise an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 25: When the Zicboz extension is not implemented, the CBZE field is read-only zero, which prevents CBO.ZERO from being enabled in any mode less privileged than M.
RULE 26: When the Zicboz extension is not implemented, CBO.ZERO must raise an illegal-instruction exception in any mode less privileged than M.
RULE 27: CBO.ZERO execution in U-mode is permitted only when the instruction is enabled for S-mode use and the CBZE field is set to 1; otherwise an illegal-instruction exception must be raised.
RULE 28: When the Zicboz extension is not implemented, the CBZE field reads as zero, so the condition for enabling CBO.ZERO in U-mode is not satisfied.
RULE 29: If a CBO.ZERO instruction is not HS-qualified, it MUST raise an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 30: If a CBO.ZERO instruction is HS-qualified and the CBZE field is set to 1, the instruction MUST be enabled for execution.
RULE 31: If a CBO.ZERO instruction is HS-qualified and the CBZE field is set to 0, it MUST raise a virtual-instruction exception.
RULE 32: When the Zicboz extension is not implemented, the CBZE field is read-only zero, causing any HS-qualified CBO.ZERO instruction to raise a virtual-instruction exception.
RULE 33: CBO.ZERO must respect and take into account pointer masking when determining its effective address.
RULE 34: CBO.ZERO must apply the pointer-masking transformation to its address before any memory operation is performed.
RULE 35: CBO.ZERO must not perform a store when pointer masking would block the access.

INSTRUCTION: CLMUL
FORMAT: clmul rd, rs1, rs2
RULE 1: The CLMUL instruction computes the carry-less product of the values in rs1 and rs2 and writes the low-order part of the product to rd.
RULE 2: CLMUL computes the lower XLEN bits of the 2 × XLEN carry-less product of the values held in registers rs1 and rs2.
RULE 3: CLMUL writes the lower XLEN bits of the carry-less product into the destination register rd.
RULE 4: CLMUL performs carry-less multiplication, which is defined as multiplication in the polynomial ring over GF(2).
RULE 5: An implementation that supports the Zbkc extension MUST provide the CLMUL instruction for both RV32 and RV64 base ISA variants.
RULE 6: CLMUL computes the carry-less product of the values in source registers rs1 and rs2 and writes the low-order XLEN bits of that product into the destination register rd.
RULE 7: CLMUL MUST read the XLEN-bit architectural register values of the source registers rs1 and rs2.
RULE 8: CLMUL MUST write the final accumulator value to the destination register rd, producing the lower XLEN bits of the carry-less product of the rs1 and rs2 values.
RULE 9: The CLMUL instruction reads the XLEN-bit values from the source registers specified by rs1 and rs2.
RULE 10: The CLMUL instruction computes a carry-less product of the two XLEN-bit operands by iterating over each bit position i from 0 to XLEN-1 and, when the i-th bit of the rs2 operand is 1, XOR-ing the rs1 operand left-shifted by i into an accumulator initialized to zero.
RULE 11: The CLMUL instruction produces as its architectural result the lower XLEN bits of the full 2·XLEN-bit carry-less product.
RULE 12: After the computation, the CLMUL instruction writes the XLEN-bit accumulator value to the destination register rd.
RULE 13: The CLMUL instruction does not generate any carry or overflow flags; the multiplication is performed using XOR operations only.
RULE 14: The CLMUL instruction's behavior is defined only for the encodings specified by the Zbc and Zbkc extensions; other encodings are reserved and have undefined architectural effect.
RULE 15: The CLMUL instruction computes the low XLEN bits of the carry-less product of the two source registers, treating each operand as a binary polynomial over GF(2).
RULE 16: An RV32 implementation must provide the CLMUL instruction.
RULE 17: An RV64 implementation must provide the CLMUL instruction.
RULE 18: An implementation that includes the Zbkb extension must implement the CLMUL instruction.
RULE 19: An implementation that includes the Zbkc extension must implement the CLMUL instruction.
RULE 20: An implementation that includes the Zbkx extension must implement the CLMUL instruction.
RULE 21: The CLMUL instruction must be implemented with execution latency that is independent of the operand data values.
RULE 22: The CLMULH instruction must be implemented with execution latency that is independent of the operand data values.
RULE 23: The SEW (Standard Element Width) for the CLMUL instruction must be 64.
RULE 24: SEW values other than 64 for the CLMUL instruction are reserved.
RULE 25: The CLMUL instruction vclmul is defined only when the selected element width (SEW) equals 64 bits; encodings with any other SEW value are reserved.
RULE 26: For each element index i from the current vector start (vstart) up to the current vector length minus one (vl-1), the CLMUL operation reads the 64-bit multiplicand operand op2 from element i of source vector register vs2.
RULE 27: In the vector-vector form of the CLMUL instruction, the 64-bit multiplier operand op1 is read from element i of source vector register vs1.
RULE 28: In the vector-scalar form of the CLMUL instruction, the 64-bit multiplier operand op1 is obtained by zero-extending or truncating the integer register rs1 to 64 bits.
RULE 29: The CLMUL operation computes a 64-bit product by initializing a 64-bit result to zero and, for each bit position j from 0 to 63 of op2, XOR-ing the result with op1 shifted left by j positions when op2[j] is 1, discarding any bits shifted beyond the 64-bit width.
RULE 30: The 64-bit result of the CLMUL operation is written to element i of the destination vector register vd.
RULE 31: After processing all active elements, the CLMUL instruction completes with a successful retirement (RETIRE_SUCCESS) and does not raise an exception.
RULE 32: The 64-bit value produced by the CLMUL operation represents the low half (least-significant 64 bits) of the full 128-bit carry-less product of the two 64-bit operands.
RULE 33: CLMUL (vclmulh) with the vector-vector form (vv) multiplies each 64-bit element of vs2 by the corresponding 64-bit element of vs1 using carry-less multiplication and writes the most significant 64 bits of the 128-bit product into the matching element of vd.
RULE 34: CLMUL (vclmulh) with the vector-scalar form (vx) multiplies each 64-bit element of vs2 by the 64-bit integer value taken from rs1 (zero-extended or truncated to the current SEW) using carry-less multiplication and writes the most significant 64 bits of the 128-bit product into the matching element of vd.
RULE 35: CLMUL encodings where the selected element width (SEW) is not 64 are reserved.
RULE 36: CLMUL processes element indices i from vstart through vl-1 inclusive, storing the 64-bit result of each element's carry-less high-half multiplication into vd at the same index.
RULE 37: CLMUL retires successfully after completing the element-wise operations without raising an architectural exception.

INSTRUCTION: CLMULH
FORMAT: clmulh rd, rs1, rs2
RULE 1: The CLMULH instruction takes three integer registers rd, rs1, and rs2 and writes a result only to rd.
RULE 2: The CLMULH instruction computes the high XLEN bits of the carry-less product of the XLEN-bit values in rs1 and rs2 and stores that high part into rd.
RULE 3: CLMULH MUST produce the upper XLEN bits of the 2 × XLEN-bit carry-less product of the values in rs1 and rs2 and write that result to rd.
RULE 4: CLMULH is defined and available in RV32 implementations.
RULE 5: CLMULH is defined and available in RV64 implementations.
RULE 6: The CLMULH instruction computes the carry-less multiplication of the values in source registers rs1 and rs2 and writes the high half of the infinite-precision product into destination register rd.
RULE 7: Execution of the CLMULH instruction updates only the destination register rd; the architectural state of rs1, rs2, and all other registers remains unchanged.
RULE 8: CLMULH must read the architectural XLEN-bit value from source register rs1 and treat it as rs1_val.
RULE 9: CLMULH must read the architectural XLEN-bit value from source register rs2 and treat it as rs2_val.
RULE 10: CLMULH must initialize an XLEN-bit variable named output to zero before performing any further computation.
RULE 11: CLMULH must, for each integer i from 1 through XLEN inclusive, test bit i of rs2_val and, if that bit is 1, replace output with the bitwise XOR of the current output and rs1_val shifted right by (XLEN − i) bits; otherwise output remains unchanged.
RULE 12: CLMULH must write the final value of output to the architectural XLEN-bit destination register rd after the loop completes.
RULE 13: CLMULH must produce as its result the upper XLEN bits of the 2·XLEN-bit carry-less product of the original rs1 and rs2 values.
RULE 14: CLMULH reads the XLEN-bit values from the architectural registers specified by rs1 and rs2 at the start of its execution.
RULE 15: CLMULH initializes a XLEN-bit accumulator named output to zero before any processing of the source operands.
RULE 16: CLMULH iterates i from 1 to XLEN inclusive, and for each i it tests the i-th least-significant bit of the rs2 value by evaluating ((rs2 >> i) & 1).
RULE 17: If the tested bit of rs2 is one, CLMULH updates the accumulator by XOR-ing it with the rs1 value right-shifted by (XLEN − i) bits.
RULE 18: If the tested bit of rs2 is zero, CLMULH leaves the accumulator unchanged for that iteration.
RULE 19: After completing all XLEN iterations, CLMULH writes the final accumulator value to the destination register rd.
RULE 20: The value written by CLMULH to rd is the upper XLEN bits of the 2·XLEN-bit carry-less product of the original rs1 and rs2 values.
RULE 21: If an implementation does not support the Zbc (Carry-less multiplication) extension, execution of CLMULH causes an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 22: CLMULH must satisfy the additional implementation requirements concerning data-independent execution latency described in Section 32.5.
RULE 23: The additional implementation requirements for data-independent execution latency that apply to CLMULH are the same as those that apply to the other carry-less multiply instructions.

INSTRUCTION: CLMULR
FORMAT: clmulr rd, rs1, rs2
RULE 1: Implementations that support the Zbc extension MUST provide the CLMULR instruction for RV32 programs.
RULE 2: Implementations that support the Zbc extension MUST provide the CLMULR instruction for RV64 programs.
RULE 3: CLMULR rd, rs1, rs2 MUST write to rd the bits numbered (2 × XLEN-2) down to (XLEN-1) of the 2 × XLEN-bit carry-less product computed from rs1 and rs2.
RULE 4: CLMULR produces bits 2·XLEN-2 down to XLEN-1 of the 2·XLEN-bit carry-less product of its source operands.
RULE 5: CLMULR computes an XLEN-bit output by initializing the output to zero and, for each integer i from 0 to XLEN-1, XOR-ing the output with rs1 shifted right by (XLEN-i-1) bits whenever bit i of rs2 is 1.
RULE 6: CLMULR writes the resulting XLEN-bit value to the destination register rd, replacing its previous architectural contents.
RULE 7: CLMULR is architecturally equivalent to bit-reversing both rs1 and rs2, performing a carry-less multiplication (CLMUL), bit-reversing the 2·XLEN-bit product, and then extracting bits 2·XLEN-2:XLEN-1 as the final result.
RULE 8: CLMULR's mnemonic suffix "r" indicates that the instruction operates on reversed-bit inputs and yields a reversed-bit output relative to the standard CLMUL operation.

INSTRUCTION: CLZ
FORMAT: clz rd, rs1
RULE 1: The CLZ instruction counts the number of leading zero bits in the XLEN-bit value held in the source register rs and writes that count to the destination register rd.
RULE 2: The CLZ instruction MUST be defined for both the RV32 and RV64 base integer ISAs.
RULE 3: The CLZ instruction reads the XLEN-bit value from source register rs, counts the number of leading zero bits in that value, and writes the resulting count to destination register rd.
RULE 4: The CLZ instruction counts the number of contiguous zero bits starting from the most-significant bit of the source register and writes that count to the destination register.
RULE 5: If the value read from the source register for a CLZ instruction is zero, the CLZ instruction writes the architectural integer register width XLEN to the destination register.
RULE 6: If the most-significant bit of the source register value for a CLZ instruction is one, the CLZ instruction writes zero to the destination register.
RULE 7: For any non-zero source register value where the most-significant one-bit is at bit position i (0 <= i <= XLEN-1), the CLZ instruction writes (XLEN-1-i) to the destination register.
RULE 8: The CLZ instruction obtains the index i of the highest set bit in the source register, computes (XLEN-1-i), and stores that result in the destination register.

INSTRUCTION: CLZW
FORMAT: clzw rd, rs1
RULE 1: A core that implements the Zbb extension must provide a CLZW instruction for both RV32 and RV64 XLEN configurations.
RULE 2: When executing CLZW rd, rs, the processor ignores the upper 32 bits of the source register rs and uses only the least-significant 32 bits as the input value.
RULE 3: CLZW computes the number of leading zero bits in the 32-bit signed representation of the input value and produces a 32-bit signed count result.
RULE 4: CLZW sign-extends the 32-bit count result to the processor's XLEN before writing it to the destination register rd.
RULE 5: CLZW updates only the architectural state of the destination register rd; no other architectural state is modified by the execution of CLZW.
RULE 6: CLZW MUST be defined only for implementations that support the RV64 ISA and is not defined for RV32 implementations.
RULE 7: CLZW counts the number of consecutive zero bits starting from the most-significant bit of the low-order 32-bit word taken from the source register rs.
RULE 8: CLZW writes the resulting count, a value between 0 and 32 inclusive, into the destination register rd and zero-extends the result to the full XLEN width.
RULE 9: CLZW rd, rs sets rd to the number of leading zero bits in the 32-bit value contained in rs, counting from bit 31 down to bit 0.
RULE 10: CLZW rd, rs computes the result as 31 minus the index of the most-significant bit that is set to 1 in rs.
RULE 11: CLZW rd, rs writes a result in the range 0 to 31 inclusive to rd when rs contains at least one bit set to 1.
RULE 12: CLZW rd, rs writes the value 32 to rd when the value in rs is zero.
RULE 13: CLZW rd, rs writes the value 0 to rd when bit 31 of rs is 1.
RULE 14: CLZW rd, rs does not modify any architectural state other than the destination register rd.

INSTRUCTION: CM.JALT
FORMAT: cm.jalt index
RULE 1: An implementation that advertises support for the Zcmt extension MUST implement the CM.JALT instruction.
RULE 2: Execution of CM.JALT causes a table-based jump to the target address obtained from the jvt CSR and writes the address of the following instruction to the link register (x1) as the return address.
RULE 3: The table accessed by CM.JALT must be aligned on a boundary of at least 64 bytes.
RULE 4: The entries in the table accessed by CM.JALT follow the current data endianness rather than the little-endian format used for normal instruction fetch.
RULE 5: The encoding of CM.JALT provides an index into the 256-entry table, and the indexed entry yields a target address from the full XLEN-wide address space.
RULE 6: If a return-address stack is implemented, CM.JALT pushes the return address onto that stack because it is architecturally equivalent to JAL ra.
RULE 7: Execution of a CM.JALT instruction performs a table jump.
RULE 8: Execution of a CM.JALT instruction causes an implicit read fetch of the CM.JALT instruction itself.
RULE 9: Execution of a CM.JALT instruction causes an implicit read fetch from the jump-vector table (JVT).
RULE 10: Both implicit reads performed by a CM.JALT instruction require execute permission and do not consider read permission.
RULE 11: If an exception occurs on either instruction fetch of a CM.JALT instruction, xEPC is set to the PC of that CM.JALT instruction.
RULE 12: If an exception occurs on either instruction fetch of a CM.JALT instruction, xCAUSE is set to the cause appropriate for the fault type.
RULE 13: If an exception occurs on either instruction fetch of a CM.JALT instruction and xTVAL is not zero, xTVAL contains the address of the fetch that caused the fault.
RULE 14: If the index field of the encoding is less than 32, the instruction must decode as CM.JT and therefore must not decode as CM.JALT.
RULE 15: If the index field of the encoding is 32 or greater, the instruction must decode as CM.JALT.
RULE 16: CM.JALT must be decoded only when the index field of the instruction is greater than or equal to 32; if the index field is less than 32 the instruction must decode as CM.JT.
RULE 17: When the jvt.mode field holds a reserved encoding value, CM.JALT is reserved and its architectural effect is undefined.
RULE 18: When XLEN is 32, CM.JALT must compute the table address as jvt.base plus the index field shifted left by 2 bits.
RULE 19: When XLEN is 64, CM.JALT must compute the table address as jvt.base plus the index field shifted left by 3 bits.
RULE 20: CM.JALT must read an XLEN-bit entry from memory at the computed table address.
RULE 21: CM.JALT must write pc+2 into register ra, thereby linking to the return address.
RULE 22: CM.JALT must set the program counter to the fetched XLEN-bit entry with its least-significant bit cleared, causing a jump to that aligned address.
RULE 23: When all transfer-type filter bits in the CTR control register are unimplemented or cleared, the default CTR configuration records a CM.JALT instruction as a control transfer when it executes in an enabled privileged mode.

INSTRUCTION: CM.JT
FORMAT: cm.jt index
RULE 1: CM.JT obtains a jump target address by reading an entry from a 256-entry table that resides in instruction memory, where each entry is XLEN bits wide.
RULE 2: The table used by CM.JT must be aligned to a minimum address boundary of 64 bytes.
RULE 3: When CM.JT reads a table entry, the entry is interpreted according to the processor's current data endianness rather than the little-endian format used for normal instruction fetch.
RULE 4: The index encoded in a CM.JT instruction selects one of the 256 table entries, and the resulting jump target may be any address representable in the XLEN-wide address space.
RULE 5: When an exception occurs on either instruction fetch of a CM.JT instruction, xEPC is set to the address of the CM.JT instruction.
RULE 6: When an exception occurs on either instruction fetch of a CM.JT instruction, xCAUSE is set to the code corresponding to the fault type.
RULE 7: When an exception occurs on either instruction fetch of a CM.JT instruction and xTVAL is non-zero, xTVAL contains the faulting fetch address.
RULE 8: CM.JT decodes only when the instruction's index field is less than 32; if the index field is 32 or greater the instruction decodes as CM.JALT.
RULE 9: When the jvt.mode field equals 0, CM.JT reads an entry from the jump vector table and jumps to the address obtained from that entry.
RULE 10: CM.JT has no architectural prerequisites and may be executed in any privilege mode.
RULE 11: For XLEN equal to 32, CM.JT computes the table address as jvt.base plus the index field shifted left by 2 bits (index multiplied by 4).
RULE 12: For XLEN equal to 64, CM.JT computes the table address as jvt.base plus the index field shifted left by 3 bits (index multiplied by 8).
RULE 13: CM.JT fetches XLEN bits from memory at the computed table address, clears the least-significant bit of the fetched value, and loads the resulting address into the program counter.

INSTRUCTION: CM.MVA01S
FORMAT: cm.mva01s r1s, r2s
RULE 1: The CM.MVA01S instruction is defined in the Zcmp extension and moves the contents of the two source registers s0-s7 specified by rs1' and rs2' into the architectural registers a0 and a1 respectively.
RULE 2: The CM.MVA01S instruction MUST NOT be available on implementations that include the Zcd extension.
RULE 3: The CM.MVA01S instruction is available on RV32 implementations.
RULE 4: The CM.MVA01S instruction is available on RV64 implementations.
RULE 5: CM.MVA01S updates the architectural register a0 (x10) with the value contained in the source s-register identified as r1s'.
RULE 6: CM.MVA01S updates the architectural register a1 (x11) with the value contained in the source s-register identified as r2s'.
RULE 7: CM.MVA01S performs the updates to a0 and a1 atomically, therefore a processor state where only one of a0 or a1 has been changed must never be observable.
RULE 8: If the implementation conforms to the RV32E ISA and either source s-register number is greater than 1, the behavior of CM.MVA01S is reserved.

INSTRUCTION: CM.MVSA01
FORMAT: cm.mvsa01 r1s, r2s
RULE 1: Implementations of the Zcmp extension for RV32 MUST provide the CM.MVSA01 instruction.
RULE 2: Implementations of the Zcmp extension for RV64 MUST provide the CM.MVSA01 instruction.
RULE 3: The two destination registers encoded by CM.MVSA01 must be distinct registers selected from the set s0-s7.
RULE 4: Execution of CM.MVSA01 copies the architectural value of register a0 into the first destination s register and copies the architectural value of register a1 into the second destination s register, leaving all other architectural state unchanged.
RULE 5: CM.MVSA01 moves the architectural register a0 into the destination register identified by the first s-register operand and moves the architectural register a1 into the destination register identified by the second s-register operand.
RULE 6: For a CM.MVSA01 encoding to be legal, the two s-register specifiers MUST be different; r1s' and r2s' MUST not be equal.
RULE 7: If the implementation supports RV32E and either s-register specifier value exceeds 1, the CM.MVSA01 encoding is reserved and its architectural effect is undefined.
RULE 8: For a CM.MVSA01 instruction on implementations where the encoding is not reserved, the first s-register operand selects an architectural x-register number xreg1 according to the mapping defined in the specification, and the second s-register operand selects xreg2 analogously.
RULE 9: The mapping defined for CM.MVSA01 computes xreg1 as the three-bit concatenation of (r1sc[2:1] > 0), (r1sc[2:1] == 0), and (r1sc[2:0]), and computes xreg2 in the same manner from r2sc.

INSTRUCTION: CM.POP
FORMAT: cm.pop {reg_list}, stack_adj
RULE 1: CM.POP MUST be available in RV32 implementations of the Zcmp extension.
RULE 2: CM.POP MUST be available in RV64 implementations of the Zcmp extension.
RULE 3: The reg_list operand of CM.POP MUST contain between one and thirteen registers selected from ra and the s0-s12 registers.
RULE 4: Valid reg_list values for CM.POP are exactly the sets {ra}, {ra, s0}, {ra, s0-s1}, {ra, s0-s2}, {ra, s0-s3}, {ra, s0-s4}, {ra, s0-s5}, {ra, s0-s6}, {ra, s0-s7}, {ra, s0-s8}, {ra, s0-s9}, and {ra, s0-s11}.
RULE 5: The reg_list value {ra, s0-s10} is NOT valid for CM.POP.
RULE 6: CM.POP is only defined when the Zca extension is also implemented.
RULE 7: CM.POP is incompatible with the Zcd extension, which is present when both C and D extensions are implemented.
RULE 8: When a CM.POP instruction executes, it must read a 32-bit word from the memory address formed by adding 12 to the current value of the stack pointer register sp and write that word into the destination register named in the instruction.
RULE 9: After the load performed by a CM.POP instruction, the stack pointer register sp must be incremented by the immediate value 16.
RULE 10: CM.POP {ra, s0-s3},48 MUST load the 32-bit value from the memory address formed by adding 44 to the current stack pointer into register s3.
RULE 11: CM.POP {ra, s0-s3},48 MUST load the 32-bit value from the memory address formed by adding 40 to the current stack pointer into register s2.
RULE 12: CM.POP {ra, s0-s3},48 MUST load the 32-bit value from the memory address formed by adding 36 to the current stack pointer into register s1.
RULE 13: CM.POP {ra, s0-s3},48 MUST load the 32-bit value from the memory address formed by adding 32 to the current stack pointer into register s0.
RULE 14: CM.POP {ra, s0-s3},48 MUST load the 32-bit value from the memory address formed by adding 28 to the current stack pointer into register ra.
RULE 15: CM.POP {ra, s0-s3},48 MUST increment the stack pointer by 48 after all loads have been performed.
RULE 16: CM.POP {ra, s0-s3},48 MUST perform the loads in the order s3, s2, s1, s0, then ra before updating the stack pointer.
RULE 17: CM.POP {ra, s0-s4}, 64 must have the encoding fields rlist equal to 9 and spimm equal to 2.
RULE 18: CM.POP {ra, s0-s4}, 64 must load the 32-bit value from memory address sp+60 into register s4.
RULE 19: CM.POP {ra, s0-s4}, 64 must load the 32-bit value from memory address sp+56 into register s3.
RULE 20: CM.POP {ra, s0-s4}, 64 must load the 32-bit value from memory address sp+52 into register s2.
RULE 21: CM.POP {ra, s0-s4}, 64 must load the 32-bit value from memory address sp+48 into register s1.
RULE 22: CM.POP {ra, s0-s4}, 64 must load the 32-bit value from memory address sp+44 into register s0.
RULE 23: CM.POP {ra, s0-s4}, 64 must load the 32-bit value from memory address sp+40 into register ra.
RULE 24: CM.POP {ra, s0-s4}, 64 must add the immediate 64 to register sp, updating sp to sp+64.
RULE 25: CM.POP {ra, s0-s4}, 64 must not modify any architectural state other than registers s4, s3, s2, s1, s0, ra and sp.
RULE 26: CM.POP loads each register specified in the xreg_list from memory locations starting at address sp + stack_adj − bytes, where bytes is 4 when XLEN = 32 and 8 when XLEN = 64, and after loading a register the address is decremented by bytes.
RULE 27: CM.POP performs the loads for the registers in descending order of register number: 27, 26, 25, 24, 23, 22, 21, 20, 19, 18, 9, 8, and 1, loading a register only if it is present in the xreg_list.
RULE 28: CM.POP updates the stack pointer by adding stack_adj to sp atomically only if the load phase completes without any exception or interrupt.
RULE 29: For RV32E, CM.POP requires stack_adj to be one of 16, 32, 48, or 64 bytes, and the stack_adj_base is defined as 16 bytes.
RULE 30: For RV32I, CM.POP determines stack_adj_base as 16 bytes when rlist ∈ [4, 7], 32 bytes when rlist ∈ [8, 11], 48 bytes when rlist ∈ [12, 14], and 64 bytes when rlist = 15.
RULE 31: For RV32I, the allowed stack_adj values for CM.POP are 16, 32, 48, or 64 bytes when rlist ∈ [4, 7]; 32, 48, 64, or 80 bytes when rlist ∈ [8, 11]; 48, 64, 80, or 96 bytes when rlist ∈ [12, 14]; and 64, 80, 96, or 112 bytes when rlist = 15.
RULE 32: For RV64, CM.POP determines stack_adj_base as 16 bytes when rlist ∈ [4, 5], 32 bytes when rlist ∈ [6, 7], 48 bytes when rlist ∈ [8, 9], 64 bytes when rlist ∈ [10, 11], 80 bytes when rlist ∈ [12, 13], 96 bytes when rlist = 14, and 112 bytes when rlist = 15.
RULE 33: For RV64, the allowed stack_adj values for CM.POP are 16, 32, 48, or 64 bytes when rlist ∈ [4, 5]; 32, 48, 64, or 80 bytes when rlist ∈ [6, 7]; 48, 64, 80, or 96 bytes when rlist ∈ [8, 9]; 64, 80, 96, or 112 bytes when rlist ∈ [10, 11]; 80, 96, 112, or 128 bytes when rlist ∈ [12, 13]; 96, 112, 128, or 144 bytes when rlist = 14; and 112, 128, 144, or 160 bytes when rlist = 15.
RULE 34: For RV32E, a rlist value of 4 encodes the register list containing x1 (ra); a rlist value of 5 encodes the register list containing x1 and x8 (ra, s0); and a rlist value of 6 encodes the register list containing x1, x8, and x9 (ra, s0-s1).
RULE 35: For RV32I and RV64, a rlist value of 4 encodes the register list containing x1 (ra); 5 encodes x1 and x8 (ra, s0); 6 encodes x1, x8, x9 (ra, s0-s1); 7 encodes x1, x8-x9, x18 (ra, s0-s2); 8 encodes x1, x8-x9, x18-x19 (ra, s0-s3); 9 encodes x1, x8-x9, x18-x20 (ra, s0-s4); 10 encodes x1, x8-x9, x18-x21 (ra, s0-s5); 11 encodes x1, x8-x9, x18-x22 (ra, s0-s6); 12 encodes x1, x8-x9, x18-x23 (ra, s0-s7); 13 encodes x1, x8-x9, x18-x24 (ra, s0-s8); 14 encodes x1, x8-x9, x18-x25 (ra, s0-s9); and 15 encodes x1, x8-x9, x18-x27 (ra, s0-s11).
RULE 36: The stack_adj operand of CM.POP is calculated as stack_adj_base plus spimm multiplied by 16, where spimm is the number of additional 16-byte increments allocated for the stack frame.
RULE 37: The value of stack_adj used by CM.POP must be a multiple of 16 bytes, because it is derived from stack_adj_base and spimm × 16.

INSTRUCTION: CM.POPRET
FORMAT: cm.popret {reg_list}, stack_adj
RULE 1: Implementations that include the Zcmp extension must provide the CM.POPRET instruction for both RV32 and RV64 base ISA configurations.
RULE 2: The reg_list operand of CM.POPRET must contain the register ra and may include zero or more consecutive registers from s0 up to s12, with the total number of registers ranging from one to thirteen.
RULE 3: The specific register list {ra, s0-s10} is not a valid reg_list operand for CM.POPRET.
RULE 4: Executing CM.POPRET must load ra from the memory address sp+92, must load s0 from sp+88, must load s1 from sp+84, must load s2 from sp+80, must load s3 from sp+76, must load s4 from sp+72, must load s5 from sp+68, must load s6 from sp+64, must load s7 from sp+60, must load s8 from sp+56, must load s9 from sp+52, must load s10 from sp+48, must load s11 from sp+44, must then add the immediate 96 to the sp register, and must finally transfer control to the address contained in ra (return).
RULE 5: The architectural effect of the 16-instruction sequence that loads ra and s0-s11 from the stack, increments sp by 96, and executes a ret instruction is defined to be identical to a single execution of CM.POPRET.
RULE 6: CM.POPRET includes a stack-pointer adjustment that forms part of its architectural effect.
RULE 7: If a trap occurs during the execution of CM.POPRET, any load operations that completed before the trap may have already updated architectural state.
RULE 8: After a trap handler completes, the load operations of CM.POPRET must be re-executed so that their results overwrite any earlier updates.
RULE 9: An implementation of CM.POPRET is permitted to update some destination registers before a trap is taken.
RULE 10: All load operations of CM.POPRET may execute multiple times, and any intermediate register updates may be visible to an interrupt or exception handler.
RULE 11: The architectural effect of CM.POPRET is defined by expanding it to the same sequence of instructions that CM.POP expands to.
RULE 12: When the rlist field of a CM.POPRET encoding has a value of 0, 1, 2, or 3, the encoding is reserved and the architectural effect is undefined.
RULE 13: In RV32E implementations, a CM.POPRET encoding with rlist = 4 selects the register list containing only ra (x1); with rlist = 5 selects ra and s0 (x1, x8); with rlist = 6 selects ra and s0-s1 (x1, x8-x9); any other rlist value is reserved.
RULE 14: In RV32I and RV64 implementations, a CM.POPRET encoding with rlist = 4 selects ra; rlist = 5 selects ra and s0; rlist = 6 selects ra and s0-s1; rlist = 7 selects ra and s0-s2; rlist = 8 selects ra and s0-s3; rlist = 9 selects ra and s0-s4; rlist = 10 selects ra and s0-s5; rlist = 11 selects ra and s0-s6; rlist = 12 selects ra and s0-s7; rlist = 13 selects ra and s0-s8; rlist = 14 selects ra and s0-s9; rlist = 15 selects ra and s0-s11; any other rlist value is reserved.
