RULE 15: For RV32E, the stack-adjustment base value used by CM.POPRET is 16 bytes, and the only legal stack_adj values are 16, 32, 48, or 64.
RULE 16: For RV32I, CM.POPRET uses stack_adj_base = 16 bytes for rlist = 4-7, 32 bytes for rlist = 8-11, 48 bytes for rlist = 12-14, and 64 bytes for rlist = 15; the legal stack_adj values are the four values obtained by adding a multiple of 16 bytes (0, 16, 32, 48) to the respective base.
RULE 17: For RV64, CM.POPRET uses stack_adj_base = 16 bytes for rlist = 4-5, 32 bytes for rlist = 6-7, 48 bytes for rlist = 8-9, 64 bytes for rlist = 10-11, 80 bytes for rlist = 12-13, 96 bytes for rlist = 14, and 112 bytes for rlist = 15; the legal stack_adj values are the four values obtained by adding a multiple of 16 bytes (0, 16, 32, 48) to the respective base.
RULE 18: The effective stack adjustment used by CM.POPRET is computed as stack_adj = stack_adj_base + spimm × 16, where spimm is an unsigned field encoded in the instruction.
RULE 19: CM.POPRET determines the data size of each load as 4 bytes when XLEN = 32 and as 8 bytes when XLEN = 64.
RULE 20: CM.POPRET computes the initial memory address for the first load as addr = sp + stack_adj − bytes, where bytes is the load size defined by XLEN.
RULE 21: CM.POPRET iterates over the registers with indices 27, 26, 25, 24, 23, 22, 21, 20, 19, 18, 9, 8, and 1 in that order, and for each register that appears in the xreg_list selected by the rlist field, it loads the value from address 0(addr) into the register (using lw for 4-byte loads or ld for 8-byte loads) and then decrements addr by bytes.
RULE 22: Upon successful completion of all loads, CM.POPRET atomically adds stack_adj to the stack pointer (sp += stack_adj).
RULE 23: After updating sp, CM.POPRET executes a ret operation, which jumps to the address contained in ra.
RULE 24: The final return section of CM.POPRET (the sp update and ret) executes atomically and only if the preceding load section terminated without any exceptions or interrupts.
RULE 25: When CM.POPRET(Z) is executed in an enabled privileged mode and the default CTR configuration has all transfer-type-filter bits cleared, CM.POPRET(Z) must be recorded as a function-return transfer.
RULE 26: If a transfer-type-filter bit that controls Function return recording is set, the recording of CM.POPRET(Z) may be omitted according to the filter-bit semantics.

INSTRUCTION: CM.POPRETZ
FORMAT: cm.popretz {reg_list}, stack_adj
RULE 1: CM.POPRETZ reg_list operand must contain between one and thirteen registers selected from ra and s0-s12 inclusive.
RULE 2: CM.POPRETZ reg_list operand is only valid when it matches one of the following sets: {ra}, {ra, s0}, {ra, s0-s1}, {ra, s0-s2}, {ra, s0-s3}, {ra, s0-s4}, {ra, s0-s5}, {ra, s0-s6}, {ra, s0-s7}, {ra, s0-s8}, {ra, s0-s9}, or {ra, s0-s11}.
RULE 3: CM.POPRETZ reg_list operand value {ra, s0-s10} is not a valid encoding.
RULE 4: CM.POPRETZ MUST load each register specified in its register list from the current stack frame.
RULE 5: CM.POPRETZ MUST write the value zero into register a0 as the function return value.
RULE 6: CM.POPRETZ MUST adjust the stack pointer to destroy the current stack frame.
RULE 7: CM.POPRETZ MUST execute a ret operation to return from the function.
RULE 8: When CM.POPRETZ is executed with a register list that contains ra and an immediate value I, it loads ra from the memory address computed as sp + (I-4).
RULE 9: When CM.POPRETZ is executed with a register list that contains s0-s11 and an immediate value I, it loads s0 from sp + (I-8), s1 from sp + (I-12), …, and s11 from sp + (I-48).
RULE 10: CM.POPRETZ writes the constant 0 into register a0.
RULE 11: CM.POPRETZ increments the stack pointer by the immediate value I, thereby discarding the stack frame that held the saved registers.
RULE 12: After performing the loads and updating sp, CM.POPRETZ transfers control to the address now contained in ra (i.e., it executes a return).
RULE 13: The architectural actions of CM.POPRETZ occur in the order: load the registers in the list, write 0 to a0, adjust sp, then return.
RULE 14: After completing the loads, CM.POPRETZ MUST adjust the stack pointer.
RULE 15: If a trap occurs during the execution of CM.POPRETZ, any loads that completed before the trap MAY update architectural state, and those loads will be re-executed after the trap handler completes, overwriting the earlier results.
RULE 16: CM.POPRETZ MUST commit the optional `li a0, 0`, the stack pointer adjustment, and the optional `ret` only when it is certain that the entire CM.POPRETZ instruction will commit.
RULE 17: For CM.POPRETZ, once the stack pointer adjustment has been committed, any included `ret` instruction MUST execute.
RULE 18: CM.POPRETZ with rlist = 4 on RV32E loads register ra (x1) from the stack.
RULE 19: CM.POPRETZ with rlist = 5 on RV32E loads registers ra (x1) and s0 (x8) from the stack.
RULE 20: CM.POPRETZ with rlist = 6 on RV32E loads registers ra (x1), s0 (x8), and s1 (x9) from the stack.
RULE 21: CM.POPRETZ with rlist = 4 on RV32I or RV64 loads register ra (x1) from the stack.
RULE 22: CM.POPRETZ with rlist = 5 on RV32I or RV64 loads registers ra (x1) and s0 (x8) from the stack.
RULE 23: CM.POPRETZ with rlist = 6 on RV32I or RV64 loads registers ra (x1), s0 (x8), and s1 (x9) from the stack.
RULE 24: CM.POPRETZ with rlist = 7 on RV32I or RV64 loads registers ra (x1), s0 (x8), s1 (x9), and s2 (x18) from the stack.
RULE 25: CM.POPRETZ with rlist = 8 on RV32I or RV64 loads registers ra (x1), s0-s3 (x8-x9, x18-x19) from the stack.
RULE 26: CM.POPRETZ with rlist = 9 on RV32I or RV64 loads registers ra (x1), s0-s4 (x8-x9, x18-x20) from the stack.
RULE 27: CM.POPRETZ with rlist = 10 on RV32I or RV64 loads registers ra (x1), s0-s5 (x8-x9, x18-x21) from the stack.
RULE 28: CM.POPRETZ with rlist = 11 on RV32I or RV64 loads registers ra (x1), s0-s6 (x8-x9, x18-x22) from the stack.
RULE 29: CM.POPRETZ with rlist = 12 on RV32I or RV64 loads registers ra (x1), s0-s7 (x8-x9, x18-x23) from the stack.
RULE 30: CM.POPRETZ with rlist = 13 on RV32I or RV64 loads registers ra (x1), s0-s8 (x8-x9, x18-x24) from the stack.
RULE 31: CM.POPRETZ with rlist = 14 on RV32I or RV64 loads registers ra (x1), s0-s9 (x8-x9, x18-x25) from the stack.
RULE 32: CM.POPRETZ with rlist = 15 on RV32I or RV64 loads registers ra (x1), s0-s11 (x8-x9, x18-x27) from the stack.
RULE 33: CM.POPRETZ on RV32E uses a stack_adj_base of 16 bytes and the total stack adjustment (stack_adj) must be one of 16, 32, 48, or 64 bytes.
RULE 34: CM.POPRETZ on RV32I with rlist = 4‥7 uses a stack_adj_base of 16 bytes; with rlist = 8‥11 uses 32 bytes; with rlist = 12‥14 uses 48 bytes; and with rlist = 15 uses 64 bytes.
RULE 35: CM.POPRETZ on RV32I with rlist = 4‥7 permits a total stack_adj of 16, 32, 48, or 64 bytes; with rlist = 8‥11 permits 32, 48, 64, or 80 bytes; with rlist = 12‥14 permits 48, 64, 80, or 96 bytes; and with rlist = 15 permits 64, 80, 96, or 112 bytes.
RULE 36: CM.POPRETZ on RV64 with rlist = 4‥5 uses a stack_adj_base of 16 bytes; with rlist = 6‥7 uses 32 bytes; with rlist = 8‥9 uses 48 bytes; with rlist = 10‥11 uses 64 bytes; with rlist = 12‥13 uses 80 bytes; with rlist = 14 uses 96 bytes; and with rlist = 15 uses 112 bytes.
RULE 37: CM.POPRETZ on RV64 permits a total stack_adj of 16, 32, 48, or 64 bytes when rlist = 4‥5; 32, 48, 64, or 80 bytes when rlist = 6‥7; 48, 64, 80, or 96 bytes when rlist = 8‥9; 64, 80, 96, or 112 bytes when rlist = 10‥11; 80, 96, 112, or 128 bytes when rlist = 12‥13; 96, 112, 128, or 144 bytes when rlist = 14; and 112, 128, 144, or 160 bytes when rlist = 15.
RULE 38: CM.POPRETZ computes the total stack adjustment as stack_adj = stack_adj_base + spimm × 16, where spimm is the number of additional 16-byte increments allocated for the stack frame.
RULE 39: CM.POPRETZ determines the byte size of each register load as 4 bytes when XLEN = 32 and 8 bytes when XLEN = 64.
RULE 40: CM.POPRETZ calculates the initial load address as sp + stack_adj − bytes and then loads each register in the xreg_list in descending register order, decrementing the address by bytes after each successful load.
RULE 41: CM.POPRETZ sets integer register a0 to zero after all specified registers have been successfully loaded from the stack.
RULE 42: CM.POPRETZ increments the stack pointer sp by the total stack_adj after all specified registers have been successfully loaded.
RULE 43: CM.POPRETZ performs a return to the address contained in ra after loading the registers and updating sp and a0.
RULE 44: CM.POPRETZ executes the final phase that writes a0, updates sp, and returns atomically and only if the register-load phase completes without any exception or interrupt.

INSTRUCTION: CM.PUSH
FORMAT: cm.push {reg_list}, -stack_adj
RULE 1: CM.PUSH implements a push operation that stores data onto the stack.
RULE 2: CM.PUSH implements a pop operation that loads data from the stack.
RULE 3: CM.PUSH implements a double-move operation that moves two registers simultaneously.
RULE 4: The register-list operand of CM.PUSH must contain between one and thirteen registers selected from ra and s0-s12.
RULE 5: Valid register-list values for CM.PUSH are exactly the sets `{ra}`, `{ra, s0}`, `{ra, s0-s1}`, `{ra, s0-s2}`, `{ra, s0-s3}`, `{ra, s0-s4}`, `{ra, s0-s5}`, `{ra, s0-s6}`, `{ra, s0-s7}`, `{ra, s0-s8}`, `{ra, s0-s9}`, and `{ra, s0-s11}`.
RULE 6: The register-list `{ra, s0-s10}` is not a valid operand for CM.PUSH.
RULE 7: The stack_adj operand of CM.PUSH specifies the total size of the stack frame and must be a value that is valid for the given register-list length and the specific encoding as defined in the instruction documentation.
RULE 8: CM.PUSH depends on the presence of the Zca extension.
RULE 9: CM.PUSH performs a sequence of store operations that write the required bytes to memory locations from sp-1 down to sp-28.
RULE 10: CM.PUSH MUST perform its stack-pointer adjustment only after it is certain that the entire CM.PUSH instruction will commit.
RULE 11: The final stack-pointer update performed by CM.PUSH executes only once and only after all stores have completed without any precise faults, and therefore becomes visible to interrupt or exception handlers only after CM.PUSH has completed.
RULE 12: CM.PUSH {ra, s0-s2}, -64 must store the value of register s2 to the memory address obtained by adding –4 to the current value of sp.
RULE 13: CM.PUSH {ra, s0-s2}, -64 must store the value of register s1 to the memory address obtained by adding –8 to the current value of sp.
RULE 14: CM.PUSH {ra, s0-s2}, -64 must store the value of register s0 to the memory address obtained by adding –12 to the current value of sp.
RULE 15: CM.PUSH {ra, s0-s2}, -64 must store the value of register ra to the memory address obtained by adding –16 to the current value of sp.
RULE 16: CM.PUSH {ra, s0-s2}, -64 must update sp by adding –64 to its original value, resulting in sp = sp – 64 after the stores.
RULE 17: CM.PUSH MUST store each register in its register list to memory using a 32-bit store-word at an address equal to the original value of sp minus 4 bytes for the first register, minus 8 bytes for the second register, and so on, decreasing by 4 bytes for each subsequent register.
RULE 18: CM.PUSH MUST perform the stores in order of decreasing register number within the list, with the register having the highest number stored at sp-4 and the register with the lowest number stored at the most negative offset.
RULE 19: CM.PUSH MUST update the stack pointer register (sp) by adding the signed immediate operand (spimm) to its original value after all stores have been completed.
RULE 20: CM.PUSH with the operands {ra, s0-s11} and an immediate of -112 MUST be architecturally equivalent to the sequence of store-word instructions that store s11 at sp-4, s10 at sp-8, s9 at sp-12, s8 at sp-16, s7 at sp-20, s6 at sp-24, s5 at sp-28, s4 at sp-32, s3 at sp-36, s2 at sp-40, s1 at sp-44, s0 at sp-48, ra at sp-52, followed by an add-immediate that sets sp = sp + (-112).
RULE 21: CM.PUSH stores the registers specified by the reg_list field to consecutive memory locations below the current stack pointer, using a word store when XLEN=32 and a doubleword store when XLEN=64.
RULE 22: CM.PUSH decrements the stack pointer by the total stack adjustment value stack_adj only after all specified registers have been successfully stored without any exception or interrupt.
RULE 23: The total stack adjustment value used by CM.PUSH is computed as stack_adj = stack_adj_base + spimm * 16, where spimm is the immediate field specifying additional 16-byte increments.
RULE 24: For CM.PUSH, stack_adj_base is the smallest multiple of 16 bytes that can contain the storage required for all registers in the reg_list.
RULE 25: In RV32E implementations of CM.PUSH, rlist value 4 selects reg_list = {ra} (xreg_list = {x1}), rlist value 5 selects reg_list = {ra, s0} (xreg_list = {x1, x8}), rlist value 6 selects reg_list = {ra, s0-s1} (xreg_list = {x1, x8-x9}), and any other rlist value is reserved.
RULE 26: In RV32I implementations of CM.PUSH, rlist values 4 through 15 select the reg_list and xreg_list mappings defined in the specification, and any rlist value outside this range is reserved.
RULE 27: In RV32I, when rlist is in the range 4 to 7, CM.PUSH permits stack_adj values of 16, 32, 48, or 64 bytes.
RULE 28: In RV32I, when rlist is in the range 8 to 11, CM.PUSH permits stack_adj values of 32, 48, 64, or 80 bytes.
RULE 29: In RV32I, when rlist is in the range 12 to 14, CM.PUSH permits stack_adj values of 48, 64, 80, or 96 bytes.
RULE 30: In RV32I, when rlist is 15, CM.PUSH permits stack_adj values of 64, 80, 96, or 112 bytes.
RULE 31: In RV32I, stack_adj_base is 16 bytes for rlist 4-7, 32 bytes for rlist 8-11, 48 bytes for rlist 12-14, and 64 bytes for rlist 15.
RULE 32: In RV64 implementations of CM.PUSH, stack_adj_base values are assigned as follows: 16 bytes for rlist 4-5, 32 bytes for rlist 6-7, 48 bytes for rlist 8-9, 64 bytes for rlist 10-11, 80 bytes for rlist 12-13, 96 bytes for rlist 14, and 112 bytes for rlist 15.
RULE 33: In RV64, when rlist is 4 or 5, CM.PUSH permits stack_adj values of 16, 32, 48, or 64 bytes.
RULE 34: In RV64, when rlist is 6 or 7, CM.PUSH permits stack_adj values of 32, 48, 64, or 80 bytes.
RULE 35: In RV64, when rlist is 8 or 9, CM.PUSH permits stack_adj values of 48, 64, 80, or 96 bytes.
RULE 36: In RV64, when rlist is 10 or 11, CM.PUSH permits stack_adj values of 64, 80, 96, or 112 bytes.
RULE 37: In RV64, when rlist is 12 or 13, CM.PUSH permits stack_adj values of 80, 96, 112, or 128 bytes.
RULE 38: In RV64, when rlist is 14, CM.PUSH permits stack_adj values of 96, 112, 128, or 144 bytes.
RULE 39: In RV64, when rlist is 15, CM.PUSH permits stack_adj values of 112, 128, 144, or 160 bytes.
RULE 40: The pseudocode for CM.PUSH determines the byte size per store as 4 bytes if XLEN equals 32 and 8 bytes if XLEN equals 64, and uses this size for each store operation.
RULE 41: For each register present in the xreg_list, CM.PUSH stores the register value at the current address and then decrements the address by the byte size before processing the next register.

INSTRUCTION: CPOP
FORMAT: cpop rd, rs1
RULE 1: The CPOP instruction counts the number of bits set to 1 in the source register rs and writes the resulting count to the destination register rd.
RULE 2: The CPOP instruction is defined for RV32 implementations, so the instruction is present when XLEN = 32.
RULE 3: The CPOP instruction is defined for RV64 implementations, so the instruction is present when XLEN = 64.
RULE 4: CPOP rd, rs counts the number of set bits in the full architectural value of source register rs and writes the resulting count to destination register rd.
RULE 5: In RV32 implementations, the CPOP instruction is defined and must perform the population-count operation as described in RULE 1.
RULE 6: In RV64 implementations, the CPOP instruction is defined and must perform the population-count operation as described in RULE 1.
RULE 7: In RV64 implementations, CPOPW rd, rs counts the number of set bits in the lower 32-bit word of source register rs and writes that count to destination register rd.
RULE 8: The result written by CPOP to rd is guaranteed to be an integer between 0 and XLEN inclusive.
RULE 9: The result written by CPOPW to rd is guaranteed to be an integer between 0 and 32 inclusive.
RULE 10: CPOP rd, rs counts the number of bits set to 1 in the XLEN-bit value held in integer register rs and writes that count to integer register rd.
RULE 11: CPOP examines each bit position i from 0 through XLEN-1 of the source register rs, adding one to an internal counter for every bit that equals 1.
RULE 12: After CPOP executes, the architectural register rd contains a non-negative integer equal to the population count of rs, with a maximum possible value of XLEN.
RULE 13: CPOP does not modify any architectural registers other than the destination rd; the source register rs retains its original value.

INSTRUCTION: CPOPW
FORMAT: cpopw rd, rs1
RULE 1: CPOPW must ignore the upper 32 bits of the source register and operate only on bits 31-0 of that register.
RULE 2: CPOPW must produce a 32-bit signed count of the set bits and sign-extend that 32-bit result to the processor's XLEN before writing it to the destination register.
RULE 3: CPOPW rd, rs MUST count the number of set bits (1-bits) in the low 32 bits of source register rs.
RULE 4: CPOPW rd, rs MUST write the resulting count into the low 32 bits of destination register rd and MUST set the high 32 bits of rd to zero.
RULE 5: CPOPW rd, rs MUST not alter any architectural state other than the destination register rd.
RULE 6: The CPOPW instruction computes the population count of the lower 32 bits of its source register and writes the zero-extended 32-bit result into the destination register.
RULE 7: On RV32 implementations, the CPOPW instruction encoding is reserved and the instruction is not defined.
RULE 8: CPOPW counts the number of bits that are 1 in the least-significant 32-bit word of the source integer register rs.
RULE 9: CPOPW produces a result that is the integer count of those set bits, which is always in the range 0 to 32 inclusive.
RULE 10: CPOPW writes the count value to the destination integer register rd, placing the count in the low-order bits of rd and zero-extending it to the full XLEN width.
RULE 11: CPOPW does not alter any architectural state other than the destination register rd.
RULE 12: CPOPW executes without raising an exception for any valid source register value.

INSTRUCTION: CSRRC
FORMAT: csrrc rd, csr, rs1
RULE 1: CSRRC MUST read the current value of the addressed CSR, zero-extend it to XLEN bits, and write the resulting value to integer register rd.
RULE 2: CSRRC MUST treat the value in integer register rs1 as a bit mask and clear each CSR bit that corresponds to a high bit in rs1, provided that the CSR bit is writable.
RULE 3: CSRRC MUST write the CSR with the value obtained after applying the mask-clearing operation, thereby committing any changes to underlying bits that were read.
RULE 4: If rs1 equals x0, CSRRC MUST NOT write to the CSR, MUST NOT cause any side effects that would occur on a CSR write, and MUST NOT raise an illegal-instruction exception when the CSR is read-only.
RULE 5: CSRRC MUST always read the addressed CSR and MUST cause any read side effects regardless of the values of rs1 and rd.
RULE 6: If rs1 is not x0 and the value held in rs1 is zero, CSRRC MUST NOT trigger any per-field side effects but MUST still trigger side effects that result from writing the entire CSR.
RULE 7: CSRRC MUST write to the CSR if rs1 is not x0, and MUST NOT write to the CSR if rs1 is x0.
RULE 8: CSRRC executed with the source register x0 reads the addressed CSR, writes the original CSR value to the destination register, and leaves the CSR unchanged, providing a legal read-only way to read counter CSRs.
RULE 9: When XLEN ≥ 64, CSRRC may access and modify the full 64-bit width of a CSR in a single instruction.
RULE 10: CSRRC reads the CSR identified by its csr field as a source operand, independent of the destination register rd.
RULE 11: CSRRC writes a new value to the CSR identified by its csr field, making the csr field a destination register, unless the rs1 register is x0.
RULE 12: If the rs1 register of a CSRRC instruction is x0, the CSR identified by the csr field is not written.
RULE 13: An execution of CSRRC that targets the seed CSR with rs1 = x0 must raise an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 14: When CSRRC accesses the seed CSR, the bits supplied in rs1 (or uimm) are ignored and must not modify the stored value of the seed CSR.
RULE 15: Executing CSRRC on the seed CSR with rs1 ≠ x0 is permitted and must not raise an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 16: If CSRRC attempts to read or write the seed CSR while the current privilege mode is not M and the implementation has not explicitly granted access, an illegal-instruction exception must be raised.
RULE 17: In a CSRRC instruction that accesses the mip CSR, only the software-writable SEIP bit participates in the read-modify-write sequence.
RULE 18: When a CSRRC instruction reads the mip CSR, the value placed in the destination register bit 9 is the logical OR of the software-writable SEIP bit (B) and the external interrupt controller signal (E).
RULE 19: During a CSRRC instruction on mip, the external interrupt controller signal (E) must not be used to compute the value written back to the software-writable SEIP bit; only the original software-writable value (B) is used.
RULE 20: After the read phase of a CSRRC instruction on mip, the software-writable SEIP bit is updated to B AND NOT rs1[9], where rs1[9] is bit 9 of the source register.
RULE 21: A CSRRC instruction on mip must not modify the read-only hardware-driven portion of the SEIP field; that portion remains unchanged.

INSTRUCTION: CSRRCI
FORMAT: csrrci rd, csr, zimm5
RULE 1: CSRRCI updates a CSR by applying an XLEN-bit value obtained by zero-extending the 5-bit unsigned immediate field (uimm[4:0]) encoded in the rs1 position, instead of using a value from an integer register.
RULE 2: CSRRCI always reads the targeted CSR and triggers any read-side effects regardless of the values in the rd and rs1 fields.
RULE 3: If the uimm[4:0] field of a CSRRCI instruction is zero, the instruction must not write to the CSR, must not cause any write-side effects, and must not raise an illegal-instruction exception when the CSR is read-only.
RULE 4: If the uimm[4:0] field of a CSRRCI instruction is non-zero, the instruction must write the CSR and therefore may cause the write-side effects associated with CSR writes.
RULE 5: CSRRCI only triggers side effects for those CSR fields whose corresponding bit positions are set to one in the zero-extended immediate value.
RULE 6: Executing CSRRCI provides a legal method to read a CSR value.
RULE 7: CSRRCI MUST be recognized as a CSR instruction.
RULE 8: CSRRCI MUST treat its CSR operand as a source register.
RULE 9: CSRRCI MUST treat its CSR operand as a destination register when the immediate field uimm[4:0] is not zero.
RULE 10: CSRRCI MUST NOT treat its CSR operand as a destination register when the immediate field uimm[4:0] is zero.
RULE 11: If CSRRCI is executed with uimm = 0 and the target CSR is the seed CSR, then an illegal-instruction exception must be raised.
RULE 12: When CSRRCI is executed, the implementation must ignore the write value encoded in its uimm field.

INSTRUCTION: CSRRS
FORMAT: csrrs rd, csr, rs1
RULE 1: CSRRS atomically reads the addressed CSR, zero-extends the read value to XLEN bits, and writes the zero-extended value to integer register rd.
RULE 2: In a CSRRS execution, each bit that is high in the value held in integer register rs1 acts as a mask that sets the corresponding writable bit in the CSR.
RULE 3: During CSRRS, if a CSR bit is readable as a value that differs from its underlying hardware value, the write-back of the read value may change that underlying value even when the corresponding bit in rs1 is not set.
RULE 4: When rs1 is x0, CSRRS does not write to the CSR, does not cause any side effects that would result from a CSR write, and does not raise an illegal-instruction exception on a read-only CSR.
RULE 5: CSRRS always reads the addressed CSR and always triggers any read side effects of that CSR, regardless of the values in rs1 or rd.
RULE 6: If rs1 specifies a register other than x0 and the register contains zero, CSRRS does not trigger per-field side effects but still triggers any side effects that result from writing the whole CSR.
RULE 7: CSRRS only triggers side effects for CSR fields for which the rs1 mask has at least one bit set that corresponds to that field.
RULE 8: If rs1 is not x0, CSRRS reads the CSR and also writes the CSR after applying the mask.
RULE 9: The assembler pseudoinstruction CSRR rd, csr is implemented by encoding CSRRS rd, csr, x0, and therefore exhibits the same read-only, no-write behavior as CSRRS with rs1 = x0.
RULE 10: Counter pseudoinstructions shall be implemented as the read-only CSRRS instruction with the destination register rd, the counter CSR as the CSR address, and rs1 set to x0.
RULE 11: A CSRRS instruction with rs1 equal to x0 is a legal read-only access to the specified CSR, and the other read-only CSR instruction forms based on CSRRC, CSRRSI, and CSRRCI are also legal ways to read these CSRs.
RULE 12: CSRRS treats the CSR identified by the csr field as a source register for the instruction, except when the CSR address is x0.
RULE 13: The exception that makes the CSR a source register for CSRRW or CSRRWI when rd is x0 does not apply to CSRRS.
RULE 14: CSRRS treats the CSR identified by the csr field as a destination register for the instruction, unless the source register rs1 is set to x0.
RULE 15: When a CSRRS instruction attempts to access a seed CSR and the source register rs1 is x0, the instruction must raise an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 16: A CSRRS instruction must ignore the value supplied in rs1, so that no bits are set in the destination CSR regardless of the contents of rs1.
RULE 17: Only the software-writable SEIP bit must participate in the read-modify-write sequence of a CSRRS instruction.
RULE 18: When a CSRRS instruction reads the mip CSR, the value placed in the destination register's SEIP field must be the logical OR of the current software-writable SEIP bit and the external interrupt signal.
RULE 19: When a CSRRS instruction writes to the mip CSR, the software-writable SEIP bit must be updated to the logical OR of its previous value and the corresponding source register bit.
RULE 20: The external interrupt signal must not affect the value written back to the software-writable SEIP bit during a CSRRS instruction.
RULE 21: In a CSRRS rd, mip, rs1 instruction, the destination register bit rd[9] must receive B OR E, where B is the current software-writable SEIP bit and E is the external interrupt signal.
RULE 22: In a CSRRS rd, mip, rs1 instruction, the SEIP bit B must be updated to B OR rs1[9], where rs1[9] is the source register bit corresponding to SEIP.

INSTRUCTION: CSRRSI
FORMAT: csrrsi rd, csr, zimm5
RULE 1: CSRRSI uses a 5-bit unsigned immediate field (uimm[4:0]) taken from the rs1 field, which is zero-extended to XLEN bits and used as a bit-mask.
RULE 2: CSRRSI atomically reads the addressed CSR, zero-extends the read value to XLEN bits, and writes that value to integer register rd.
RULE 3: After reading the CSR, CSRRSI updates the CSR by setting to 1 each bit position where the zero-extended immediate mask has a 1, provided the corresponding CSR bit is writable.
RULE 4: CSRRSI MUST always read the CSR and MUST cause any read side effects, regardless of the values of rd or the immediate.
RULE 5: If the immediate value is zero, CSRRSI MUST NOT write to the CSR.
RULE 6: If the immediate value is zero, CSRRSI MUST NOT cause any side effects that would occur on a CSR write.
RULE 7: If the immediate value is zero, CSRRSI MUST NOT raise an illegal-instruction exception when the addressed CSR is read-only.
RULE 8: If the immediate value is non-zero, CSRRSI MUST write to the CSR.
RULE 9: If the immediate value is non-zero, CSRRSI MUST cause write side effects only for CSR fields for which the immediate mask has at least one bit set corresponding to that field.
RULE 10: If the immediate value is non-zero and the addressed CSR is read-only, CSRRSI MUST raise an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 11: CSRRSI is a legal instruction that can be used to read the value of a control-and-status register (CSR).
RULE 12: For CSRRSI, the CSR operand (csr) shall be a destination register unless the immediate field uimm[4:0] is zero.
RULE 13: CSRRSI must be treated as a read-only CSR-access instruction, meaning it does not modify the addressed CSR.
RULE 14: If CSRRSI targets the seed CSR and the immediate field uimm is zero, the instruction must raise an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 15: CSRRSI must ignore the write value supplied in its immediate field; the uimm does not affect any architectural state.

INSTRUCTION: CSRRW
FORMAT: csrrw rd, csr, rs1
RULE 1: CSRRW shall atomically read-modify-write a single CSR identified by the 12-bit csr field in bits 31-20 of the instruction encoding.
RULE 2: When the destination register rd of a CSRRW is not x0, CSRRW shall read the old value of the specified CSR, zero-extend it to XLEN bits, and write the resulting value to integer register rd.
RULE 3: When the destination register rd of a CSRRW is not x0, CSRRW shall write the initial value held in integer register rs1 to the specified CSR.
RULE 4: When the destination register rd of a CSRRW is x0, CSRRW shall not read the specified CSR and shall not cause any side effects that might occur on a CSR read.
RULE 5: When the destination register rd of a CSRRW is x0, CSRRW shall still write the initial value held in integer register rs1 to the specified CSR.
RULE 6: When the source register rs1 of a CSRRW is x0, CSRRW shall attempt to write the value zero to the specified CSR.
RULE 7: CSRRW shall cause the defined side effects for all fields within the CSR that is written.
RULE 8: CSRRW shall always perform a write to the specified CSR regardless of the values of rd or rs1.
RULE 9: In a CSRRW instruction, when the destination register rd is x0, the CSR operand is not treated as a source register.
RULE 10: In a CSRRW instruction, when the destination register rd is not x0, the CSR operand, which must be a register other than x0, is treated as a source register.
RULE 11: In a CSRRW instruction, the CSR operand, provided it is a register other than x0, is treated as a destination register.
RULE 12: In a CSRRW instruction, the architectural register specified by rd, when rd is a register other than x0, is treated as a destination register.
RULE 13: CSRRW targeting the seed CSR must copy the current value of the seed CSR into the destination register rd.
RULE 14: CSRRW targeting the seed CSR must ignore the write value supplied in rs1 (or uimm) and must not modify the seed CSR.
RULE 15: CSRRW is permitted to access the seed CSR and therefore does not raise an illegal-instruction exception solely because it is not a read-only CSR instruction.
RULE 16: CSRRW that attempts to read or write the seed CSR while the processor is in a privilege mode other than M and access has not been explicitly granted must raise an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 17: Execution of CSRRW ra, ssp, ra writes the previous value of the ssp CSR into register ra.
RULE 18: Execution of CSRRW ra, ssp, ra writes the previous value of register ra into the ssp CSR.
RULE 19: When a CSRRW instruction is executed on the mip CSR, the destination register receives the value of the CSR as read, and for the SEIP field this read value equals the logical-OR of the software-writable bit B and the external-interrupt signal E (B || E).
RULE 20: When a CSRRW instruction writes the mip CSR, only the software-writable bit B of the SEIP field is updated, and it is set to the value of source-register bit t1[9].
RULE 21: The write performed by a CSRRW instruction to the SEIP field of the mip CSR does not depend on the external-interrupt signal E.
RULE 22: CSRRW shall read the current value of the specified CSR into the destination integer register and shall write the value contained in the source integer register into that CSR, thereby swapping the two values.
RULE 23: CSRRW is permitted to be performed on the sscratch CSR because sscratch is defined as an SXLEN-bit read/write register.

INSTRUCTION: CSRRWI
FORMAT: csrrwi rd, csr, zimm5
RULE 1: CSRRWI obtains the XLEN-bit value to write to the CSR by zero-extending the 5-bit unsigned immediate field (uimm[4:0]) encoded in the rs1 field.
RULE 2: If the destination register rd of a CSRRWI instruction is x0, the instruction shall not read the CSR and shall not cause any side effects that could occur on a CSR read.
RULE 3: When rd of a CSRRWI instruction is x0, the instruction shall write the CSR with the zero-extended immediate value.
RULE 4: When rd of a CSRRWI instruction is not x0, the instruction shall read the current value of the CSR, write the CSR with the zero-extended immediate value, and place the original CSR value into rd.

INSTRUCTION: CTZ
FORMAT: ctz rd, rs1
RULE 1: The CTZ instruction MUST count the number of trailing zero bits in the value held in source register rs and write that count to destination register rd.
RULE 2: An implementation that includes the Zbb extension MUST provide the CTZ instruction for RV32.
RULE 3: An implementation that includes the Zbb extension MUST provide the CTZ instruction for RV64.
RULE 4: The CTZW instruction MUST count the number of trailing zero bits in the low-order 32-bit word of the value held in source register rs and write that count to destination register rd.
RULE 5: An implementation that includes the Zbb extension MUST provide the CTZW instruction for RV32.
RULE 6: The CTZW instruction is not listed as defined for RV64 implementations.
RULE 7: The CTZ instruction MUST be implemented in RV32 implementations and in RV64 implementations.
RULE 8: The CTZ instruction computes the number of consecutive zero bits starting from the least-significant bit of the value in rs and writes that count to rd.
RULE 9: The CTZ instruction reads the value of the architectural register identified by rs.
RULE 10: The CTZ instruction counts the number of consecutive zero bits starting at bit position 0 (the least-significant bit) of the value read from rs.
RULE 11: The CTZ instruction writes to the architectural register identified by rd the count produced by its trailing-zero calculation.
RULE 12: If the value read from rs is zero, the CTZ instruction produces the count XLEN and writes XLEN to rd.
RULE 13: If the least-significant bit of the value read from rs is one, the CTZ instruction produces the count 0 and writes 0 to rd.
RULE 14: For any non-zero value read from rs whose least-significant set bit is at position i (where 0 ≤ i < XLEN), the CTZ instruction writes the integer i to rd.
RULE 15: CTZ must read the value in source register rs and write to destination register rd the number of consecutive zero bits starting at bit position 0 and ending before the first bit that is 1 within bits 0-31 of rs.
RULE 16: CTZ must produce a result of 0 in rd when bit 0 of the least-significant word of rs is 1.
RULE 17: CTZ must produce a result of 32 in rd when all bits 0-31 of the least-significant word of rs are 0.
RULE 18: CTZ must ignore bits 32 and above of rs, guaranteeing that the value written to rd is never greater than 32.
RULE 19: An implementation that claims support for the Zvkb extension MUST provide the vector count-trailing-zeros instruction vctz.v.
RULE 20: The vctz.v instruction performs a count-trailing-zeros (CTZ) operation on each element of the source vector register vs2 and writes the resulting count to the corresponding element of the destination vector register vd.
RULE 21: For every element index i from vstart up to and including vl-1, the CTZ operation reads the SEW-wide element vs2[i], examines bits starting at the least-significant position, and determines the smallest j such that bit j of the element is 1, or j = SEW if no such bit exists.
RULE 22: The CTZ instruction stores the value j obtained for element i into the SEW-wide element vd[i].
RULE 23: The CTZ instruction completes execution with a RETIRE_SUCCESS architectural result after processing all elements in the specified index range.
RULE 24: The CTZ instruction does not modify any architectural state other than the destination vector register vd.
RULE 25: The CTZ instruction is defined as part of the Zvbb vector extension.

INSTRUCTION: CTZW
FORMAT: ctzw rd, rs1
RULE 1: The CTZW instruction MUST be defined for both RV32 and RV64 implementations.
RULE 2: When executing CTZW, the upper 32 bits of the source register are ignored and only the least-significant 32 bits are used.
RULE 3: CTZW counts the number of trailing zero bits in the least-significant 32-bit word of the source register.
RULE 4: CTZW writes the count as a 32-bit signed integer into the destination register.
RULE 5: CTZW sign-extends the 32-bit signed result to the processor's XLEN before writing it to the destination register.
RULE 6: CTZW MUST be defined only for implementations that support the RV64 base ISA and is not defined for the RV32 base ISA.
RULE 7: CTZW reads the value in source register rs and writes the computed result to destination register rd.
RULE 8: CTZW computes the number of consecutive zero bits starting at the least-significant bit of the low-order 32-bit word of rs and writes that count (a value between 0 and 32 inclusive) to rd.
RULE 9: CTZW counts the number of consecutive zero bits starting at the least-significant bit and stopping before the first one-bit within the least-significant 32-bit word of the source register.
RULE 10: CTZW produces the result value 32 when every bit of the least-significant 32-bit word of the source register is zero.
RULE 11: CTZW produces the result value 0 when the least-significant bit of the source register is one.
RULE 12: CTZW writes to the destination register the integer equal to the index of the first one-bit found in the least-significant 32-bit word of the source register, where index 0 corresponds to bit 0.
RULE 13: CTZW ignores all bits of the source register above bit 31; only the least-significant 32-bit word influences the computed result.
RULE 14: CTZW stores the computed result in the destination register with zero-extension to the full XLEN width.

INSTRUCTION: CZERO.EQZ
FORMAT: czero.eqz rd, rs1, rs2
RULE 1: CZERO.EQZ must be available in implementations that support the Zicond extension for both RV32 and RV64 base ISAs.
RULE 2: When CZERO.EQZ is executed and the evaluated condition is equal to zero, CZERO.EQZ writes the integer value zero to the destination register rd.
RULE 3: When CZERO.EQZ is executed and the evaluated condition is not equal to zero, CZERO.EQZ leaves the destination register rd unchanged.
RULE 4: CZERO.EQZ does not modify any architectural state other than the destination register rd.
RULE 5: CZERO.EQZ does not cause any memory accesses, CSR updates, or changes to the program counter other than the normal sequential advance.
RULE 6: CZERO.EQZ MUST write the zero value to rd when the value read from rs2 is zero.
RULE 7: CZERO.EQZ MUST copy the value read from rs1 to rd when the value read from rs2 is not zero.
RULE 8: CZERO.EQZ MUST have syntactic dependencies from both rs1 and rs2 to rd.
RULE 9: CZERO.EQZ establishes a predicate that causes the integer instruction that immediately follows CZERO.EQZ to be architecturally executed only when the third operand register of CZERO.EQZ contains a non-zero value.
RULE 10: When the third operand register of CZERO.EQZ contains zero, the integer instruction that immediately follows CZERO.EQZ is not architecturally executed and the destination register of that instruction retains its original architectural value.
RULE 11: CZERO.EQZ does not modify any architectural integer register; its only architectural effect is the creation of the predicate for the following instruction.
RULE 12: The predicate created by CZERO.EQZ applies exclusively to the single instruction that directly follows CZERO.EQZ and has no effect on any later instructions.
RULE 13: CZERO.EQZ can be paired with an integer add, subtract, bitwise-or, or bitwise-xor instruction to form a two-instruction conditional operation where the arithmetic result is written to the destination register only if the condition register operand of CZERO.EQZ is non-zero.
RULE 14: In a three-instruction sequence implementing a conditional bitwise-and, CZERO.EQZ controls execution of the final move of the temporary register to the destination register, and this final move occurs only if the condition register operand of CZERO.EQZ is non-zero.
RULE 15: In a three-instruction sequence implementing a conditional select, CZERO.EQZ controls execution of the final addition that combines the temporary register holding rs2 with the destination register, and this addition occurs only if the condition register operand of CZERO.EQZ is non-zero.

INSTRUCTION: CZERO.NEZ
FORMAT: czero.nez rd, rs1, rs2
RULE 1: The CZERO.NEZ instruction must be implemented in RV32 implementations.
RULE 2: The CZERO.NEZ instruction must be implemented in RV64 implementations.
RULE 3: When a CZERO.NEZ instruction executes and the condition operand rs2 is nonzero, the architectural result written to the destination register rd is zero.
RULE 4: CZERO.NEZ MUST write the architectural zero value to destination register rd when the architectural value read from source register rs2 is not zero.
RULE 5: CZERO.NEZ MUST copy the architectural value read from source register rs1 to destination register rd when the architectural value read from source register rs2 is zero.
RULE 6: The CZERO.NEZ add operation produces rd = rs1 + rs2 when rc equals zero; otherwise rd is left unchanged.
RULE 7: The execution of CZERO.NEZ add requires two compressed instructions: a CZERO.NEZ prefix followed by an ADD instruction.
RULE 8: The CZERO.NEZ sub operation produces rd = rs1 - rs2 when rc equals zero; otherwise rd is left unchanged.
RULE 9: The execution of CZERO.NEZ sub requires two compressed instructions: a CZERO.NEZ prefix followed by a SUB instruction.
RULE 10: The CZERO.NEZ or operation produces rd = rs1 | rs2 when rc equals zero; otherwise rd is left unchanged.
RULE 11: The execution of CZERO.NEZ or requires two compressed instructions: a CZERO.NEZ prefix followed by an OR instruction.
RULE 12: The CZERO.NEZ xor operation produces rd = rs1 ^ rs2 when rc equals zero; otherwise rd is left unchanged.
RULE 13: The execution of CZERO.NEZ xor requires two compressed instructions: a CZERO.NEZ prefix followed by an XOR instruction.
RULE 14: In the three-instruction sequence AND CZERO.NEZ OR, the CZERO.NEZ component causes the destination register to be written with rs1 & rs2 if rc is non-zero, otherwise the destination register is left unchanged.
RULE 15: The sequence AND CZERO.NEZ OR occupies three compressed instructions.
RULE 16: In the three-instruction sequence CZERO.NEZ CZERO.EQZ ADD, the combined effect is that the destination register receives rs1 if rc equals zero, otherwise it receives rs2.
RULE 17: The sequence CZERO.NEZ CZERO.EQZ ADD occupies three compressed instructions.

INSTRUCTION: DIV
FORMAT: div rd, rs1, rs2
RULE 1: DIV must compute the signed integer division of the XLEN-bit dividend in rs1 by the XLEN-bit divisor in rs2, producing a quotient that is rounded toward zero.
RULE 2: DIV must ensure that, when the divisor is non-zero and the operation does not overflow, the computed quotient and remainder satisfy dividend = divisor × quotient + remainder.
RULE 3: DIV must produce a quotient whose bits are all set (i.e., -1) when the divisor supplied to the instruction is zero.
RULE 4: DIV experiences signed-division overflow only when the dividend equals the most-negative signed XLEN-bit integer (-2^{XLEN-1}) and the divisor equals -1.
RULE 5: When signed-division overflow occurs for DIV (i.e., dividend = -2^{XLEN-1} and divisor = -1), DIV must return a quotient equal to the dividend (-2^{XLEN-1}) and a remainder of zero.

INSTRUCTION: DIVU
FORMAT: divu rd, rs1, rs2
RULE 1: DIVU must compute an XLEN-bit unsigned integer quotient by dividing the value in rs1 (the dividend) by the value in rs2 (the divisor) and must round the result toward zero.
RULE 2: For any DIVU execution where the divisor in rs2 is non-zero, the produced quotient Q must satisfy the identity dividend = divisor × Q + remainder, where the remainder is the unsigned remainder defined by the corresponding REMU operation.
RULE 3: DIVU must never raise an overflow condition because unsigned division overflow cannot occur.
RULE 4: When the divisor in rs2 is zero, DIVU must return a quotient whose all XLEN bits are set (value 2^XLEN-1).

INSTRUCTION: DIVUW
FORMAT: divuw rd, rs1, rs2
RULE 1: DIVUW divides the lower 32 bits of rs1 by the lower 32 bits of rs2 as unsigned integers and writes the 32-bit quotient into rd with sign-extension to 64 bits.
RULE 2: DIVUW rounds the computed quotient toward zero.
RULE 3: If the lower 32 bits of rs2 are zero, DIVUW produces the 32-bit value 2^32-1 and sign-extends it to 64 bits as the result.
RULE 4: DIVUW never generates an overflow because unsigned division overflow cannot occur.
RULE 5: DIVUW does not cause an architectural trap or exception when a divide-by-zero occurs; the all-1s result is returned instead.

INSTRUCTION: DIVW
FORMAT: divw rd, rs1, rs2
RULE 1: DIVW computes the signed division of the lower 32 bits of rs1 by the lower 32 bits of rs2, places the 32-bit quotient into rd, and sign-extends the result to 64 bits.
RULE 2: DIVW produces a result of -1 (all 32 low bits set) sign-extended to 64 bits when the lower 32 bits of rs2 (the divisor) are zero.
RULE 3: DIVW experiences signed-division overflow only when the lower 32 bits of rs1 equal -2^31 and the lower 32 bits of rs2 equal -1, and in that case the result written to rd is the sign-extension of -2^31.
RULE 4: DIVW defines that, in the signed-division overflow case, the architectural quotient equals the dividend and the corresponding remainder would be zero.

INSTRUCTION: FADD.Q
FORMAT: fadd.q fd, fs1, fs2, rm
RULE 1: FADD.Q must compute the sum of the quad-precision floating-point value in source register rs1 and the quad-precision floating-point value in source register rs2 and produce a quad-precision floating-point result.
RULE 2: FADD.Q must write the quad-precision result of the addition to destination register rd.
RULE 3: FADD.Q must apply the rounding mode specified by the rm field of the instruction to the result.
RULE 4: FADD.Q must take the first source operand from the register indicated by the rs1 field.
RULE 5: FADD.Q must take the second source operand from the register indicated by the rs2 field.
RULE 6: FADD.Q must designate the destination register for the result using the rd field.

INSTRUCTION: FADD.S
FORMAT: fadd.s fd, fs1, fs2, rm
RULE 1: FADD.S performs single-precision floating-point addition of the values held in source registers rs1 and rs2.
RULE 2: FADD.S writes the result of the floating-point addition to the destination register rd.
RULE 3: FADD.S MAY use the rm field to select the rounding mode applied to the addition operation.

INSTRUCTION: FCLASS.D
FORMAT: fclass.d rd, fs1
RULE 1: FCLASS.D MUST classify a double-precision floating-point operand using the same classification categories defined for the single-precision FCLASS.S instruction.
RULE 2: The operand supplied to FCLASS.D MUST be a double-precision floating-point value.

INSTRUCTION: FCLASS.H
FORMAT: fclass.h rd, fs1
RULE 1: FCLASS.H classifies a half-precision floating-point operand and produces a classification result.
RULE 2: The classification performed by FCLASS.H follows the same definition as the single-precision floating-point classify instruction, applied to half-precision operands.

INSTRUCTION: FCLASS.S
FORMAT: fclass.s rd, fs1
RULE 1: FCLASS.S MUST read the floating-point value contained in source register rs1.
RULE 2: FCLASS.S MUST write to destination integer register rd a 10-bit mask in which each bit position 0 through 9 corresponds to a distinct class of the rs1 value as defined in the specification.
RULE 3: For each class, FCLASS.S MUST set the corresponding rd bit if the rs1 value belongs to that class and MUST clear that bit if the rs1 value does not belong to that class.
RULE 4: FCLASS.S MUST clear all bits of rd that are not among bits 0 through 9 of the class mask.
RULE 5: FCLASS.S MUST ensure that exactly one of bits 0 through 9 in rd is set after execution.
RULE 6: FCLASS.S MUST not modify any floating-point exception flags.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.BF16.S
FORMAT: fcvt.bf16.s fd, fs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.BF16.S MUST convert a 32-bit single-precision floating-point (FP32) value to a BF16 value.
RULE 2: FCVT.BF16.S MUST only support conversion where the source operand is FP32 and the destination operand is BF16; conversions involving any other floating-point formats are not supported.
RULE 3: FCVT.BF16.S is only defined when the single-precision floating-point extension F is implemented; if extension F is absent, the FCVT.BF16.S instruction is not defined.
RULE 4: FCVT.BF16.S rd, rs1 converts the 32-bit floating-point value in floating-point register rs1 to a bfloat16 value and writes the result to floating-point register rd.
RULE 5: FCVT.BF16.S must round the conversion result according to the current rounding-mode (RM) field.
RULE 6: FCVT.BF16.S may raise the overflow exception if the converted BF16 result is too large to be represented.
RULE 7: FCVT.BF16.S may raise the underflow exception if the converted BF16 result is too small to be represented as a normalized BF16 value.
RULE 8: FCVT.BF16.S may raise the inexact exception when any non-zero bits of the FP32 mantissa are discarded during conversion.
RULE 9: FCVT.BF16.S may raise the invalid exception when the source FP32 value is a NaN or otherwise not representable as a BF16 value.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.D.H
FORMAT: fcvt.d.h fd, fs1
RULE 1: FCVT.D.H reads a half-precision floating-point value from the source floating-point register rs1 and writes the resulting double-precision floating-point value to the destination floating-point register rd.
RULE 2: FCVT.D.H performs the conversion using the rounding mode indicated by the rm field, applying the rounding rules defined for floating-point-to-floating-point conversions.
RULE 3: The FCVT.D.H instruction MUST be defined only when the Zdinx extension is implemented; if Zdinx is not implemented, the FCVT.D.H instruction is not defined by the architecture.
RULE 4: When the Zdinx extension is implemented, the Zhinxmin extension MUST include the FCVT.D.H instruction (along with the FCVT.H.D instruction).

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.D.L
FORMAT: fcvt.d.l fd, rs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.D.L MUST convert the 64-bit signed integer value held in integer register rs1 to a double-precision floating-point value written to floating-point register rd.
RULE 2: For FCVT.D.L the range of integer values that are considered valid inputs and the architectural behavior for inputs outside that range are identical to those specified for the FCVT.int.S instruction.
RULE 3: FCVT.D.L MUST round the conversion result according to the rounding mode indicated by the rm field.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.D.LU
FORMAT: fcvt.d.lu fd, rs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.D.LU reads the unsigned integer value from integer register rs1 and writes the resulting double-precision floating-point value to floating-point register rd.
RULE 2: FCVT.D.LU operates on a 64-bit unsigned integer source and therefore is defined only for RV64 implementations.
RULE 3: FCVT.D.LU rounds the conversion result according to the rounding mode specified in the rm field of the instruction.
RULE 4: FCVT.D.LU must apply the same valid-input range limits and raise the same floating-point exceptions for out-of-range inputs as the corresponding single-precision conversion instructions FCVT.int.S.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.D.Q
FORMAT: fcvt.d.q fd, fs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.D.Q converts a quad-precision floating-point number to a double-precision floating-point number.
RULE 2: FCVT.D.Q applies the rounding mode indicated by the rm field in the same manner as other double-precision floating-point-to-floating-point conversion instructions.
RULE 3: The rs2 field of the FCVT.D.Q instruction encoding selects the quad-precision source floating-point register.
RULE 4: The rs1 field of the FCVT.D.Q instruction encoding selects the source register containing the value to be converted.
RULE 5: The rm field of the FCVT.D.Q instruction encoding selects the rounding mode used for the conversion.
RULE 6: The rd field of the FCVT.D.Q instruction encoding selects the destination register that receives the resulting double-precision floating-point value.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.D.W
FORMAT: fcvt.d.w fd, rs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.D.W must read a signed 32-bit integer from integer register rs1 and write the corresponding double-precision floating-point value to floating-point register rd.
RULE 2: For any integer input that is not within the valid input set, FCVT.D.W must exhibit the same architectural behavior as defined for FCVT.int.D (and FCVT.int.S) for such inputs.
RULE 3: FCVT.D.W must produce an exact conversion result regardless of the rounding mode specified in the rm field.
RULE 4: The rounding mode field (rm) has no effect on the result of FCVT.D.W.
RULE 5: No additional privilege checks or exception conditions are defined for FCVT.D.W beyond those already specified for its input range handling.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.D.WU
FORMAT: fcvt.d.wu fd, rs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.D.WU reads an unsigned integer value from integer register rs1 (32-bit on RV32, 32- or 64-bit on RV64) and writes the converted double-precision floating-point value to floating-point register rd.
RULE 2: FCVT.D.WU converts the unsigned integer operand to a double-precision floating-point value and the conversion always yields an exact result, ignoring the rounding mode specified by the rm field.
RULE 3: The set of input values for which FCVT.D.WU produces a defined result and the architectural response to inputs outside that set are identical to those defined for the FCVT.int.S instruction.
RULE 4: FCVT.D.WU produces a double-precision floating-point result and no sign-extension of the value is performed.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.H.D
FORMAT: fcvt.h.d fd, fs1, rm
RULE 1: The FCVT.H.D instruction MUST be decoded when opcode=OP-FP, funct5=5, fmt=H, rs2=D, with any values for rs1, rd, and rm.
RULE 2: The FCVT.H.D instruction is only defined when the D extension is implemented; if the D extension is not implemented the encoding is reserved.
RULE 3: The FCVT.H.D instruction MUST read the double-precision floating-point value from the source register indicated by rs1.
RULE 4: The FCVT.H.D instruction MUST convert the read double-precision floating-point value to a half-precision floating-point value using the rounding mode specified by the rm field.
RULE 5: The FCVT.H.D instruction MUST write the resulting half-precision floating-point value to the destination floating-point register indicated by rd.
RULE 6: The FCVT.H.D instruction MUST select its rounding mode according to the standard floating-point rounding-mode field rm.
RULE 7: The FCVT.H.D instruction is only available in an implementation that implements both the Zfhmin extension and the D (double-precision) extension.
RULE 8: An implementation that includes the D extension MUST also include the FCVT.H.D instruction.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.H.L
FORMAT: fcvt.h.l fd, rs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.H.L must be defined only for implementations where XLEN = 64; for implementations with XLEN ≠ 64 the FCVT.H.L encoding is reserved and its behavior is not defined by the architecture.
RULE 2: FCVT.H.L converts the signed 64-bit integer value in the integer source register rs1 to a half-precision floating-point value and writes that result to the floating-point destination register rd.
RULE 3: FCVT.H.L uses the rounding mode specified by the 3-bit rm field to round the exact conversion result to the nearest representable half-precision value.
RULE 4: FCVT.H.L exhibits the same rounding, overflow, underflow, inexact, and invalid-operation exception behavior as the single-precision integer-to-floating-point conversion instructions.
RULE 5: FCVT.H.L is encoded as an OP-FP instruction with funct5 = FCVT.H.int, fmt = H, rs2 = L, rs1 = the integer source register, rm = the rounding-mode field, rd = the floating-point destination register, and opcode = OP-FP; any deviation from this encoding yields a reserved encoding.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.H.LU
FORMAT: fcvt.h.lu fd, rs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.H.LU must be executed only on RV64 implementations; executing it on an RV32 implementation causes an illegal instruction exception.
RULE 2: For FCVT.H.LU the rs2 field must be encoded as L[U] to select conversion from an unsigned 64-bit integer source.
RULE 3: FCVT.H.LU uses the rm field to specify the rounding mode that is applied during the conversion from integer to half-precision floating-point.
RULE 4: FCVT.H.LU reads the unsigned 64-bit integer value from the integer register specified by rs1.
RULE 5: FCVT.H.LU writes the half-precision floating-point result into the floating-point register specified by rd.
RULE 6: The half-precision result written by FCVT.H.LU is the value of the unsigned integer operand from rs1 converted according to the rounding mode indicated by rm.
RULE 7: FCVT.H.LU requires the fmt field to be set to H, indicating that the conversion result is in half-precision format.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.H.Q
FORMAT: fcvt.h.q fd, fs1, rm
RULE 1: If the Q extension is present, execution of FCVT.H.Q must convert the value in the source half-precision floating-point register to a quad-precision floating-point value and write the result to the destination quad-precision floating-point register.
RULE 2: The rm field of the FCVT.H.Q encoding selects the rounding mode used for the conversion.
RULE 3: The conversion performed by FCVT.H.Q must follow the same architectural rules as the double-precision floating-point-to-floating-point conversion instructions, applied between half-precision and quad-precision formats.
RULE 4: If the Q extension is not present, the encoding for FCVT.H.Q is reserved and its behavior is not defined by the specification.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.H.S
FORMAT: fcvt.h.s fd, fs1, rm
RULE 1: If the Zfhmin standard extension is implemented, the FCVT.H.S instruction MUST be implemented.
RULE 2: The FCVT.H.S instruction does NOT depend on the D or Q extensions and is available only when both the Zfhmin extension and the required single-precision floating-point extension F are implemented.
RULE 3: FCVT.H.S is only available when the Zfinx extension is implemented, because the Zhinxmin extension depends upon the Zfinx extension.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.H.W
FORMAT: fcvt.h.w fd, rs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.H.W MUST convert the 32-bit signed integer value read from integer register rs1 into a half-precision floating-point number and write the resulting value to floating-point register rd.
RULE 2: FCVT.H.W MUST use the rm field of the instruction to select the rounding mode applied during the integer-to-floating-point conversion.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.H.WU
FORMAT: fcvt.h.wu fd, rs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.H.WU converts the 32-bit unsigned integer value in the integer source register rs1 to a half-precision floating-point value and writes the result to the floating-point destination register rd.
RULE 2: FCVT.H.WU interprets the source integer register value as an unsigned integer, ignoring any sign extension.
RULE 3: FCVT.H.WU applies the rounding mode specified in the rm field to the conversion according to the IEEE-754 rounding rules for floating-point conversions.
RULE 4: In RV64, the unsigned 64-bit integer conversion is provided by the separate instruction FCVT.H.LU; FCVT.H.WU still converts only a 32-bit unsigned integer.
RULE 5: FCVT.H.WU does not specify any additional permission checks or raise any exceptions beyond those required for standard floating-point conversion instructions.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.L.D
FORMAT: fcvt.l.d rd, fs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.L.D converts the double-precision floating-point value in floating-point register rs1 to a signed 64-bit integer and writes the result to integer register rd.
RULE 2: FCVT.L.D is an RV64-only instruction and must be illegal on implementations that do not support the RV64 integer register width.
RULE 3: FCVT.L.D rounds the conversion result according to the rounding mode specified in the rm field of the instruction.
RULE 4: FCVT.L.D must apply the same range of valid inputs and the same handling of invalid inputs as defined for the single-precision floating-point-to-integer conversion instruction.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.L.H
FORMAT: fcvt.l.h rd, fs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.L.H MUST convert the half-precision floating-point value held in the source register to a signed 64-bit integer and write the resulting integer to the destination register.
RULE 2: FCVT.L.H uses the rounding mode specified in the rm field to round the source half-precision floating-point value before performing the conversion to a signed 64-bit integer.
RULE 3: FCVT.L.H is defined only for RV64 implementations and is not a valid instruction on RV32 systems.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.L.Q
FORMAT: fcvt.l.q rd, fs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.L.Q converts a quad-precision floating-point number in the source floating-point register to a signed 64-bit integer placed in the destination integer register, using the current rounding mode.
RULE 2: FCVT.L.Q follows the same conversion semantics as the double-precision-to-integer conversion instructions, including overflow, NaN, and rounding behavior defined by the current rounding mode.
RULE 3: FCVT.L.Q is defined only for RV64 implementations; its encoding is reserved on RV32 implementations.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.L.S
FORMAT: fcvt.l.s rd, fs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.L.S converts the floating-point number in source register rs1 to a signed 64-bit integer placed in destination integer register rd.
RULE 2: FCVT.L.S is defined only for RV64; executing it on an RV32 implementation must raise an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 3: FCVT.L.S must round the source floating-point value according to the rounding-mode field rm.
RULE 4: FCVT.L.S must set the Invalid exception flag when the rounded result is not representable in the signed 64-bit integer format.
RULE 5: When the Invalid flag is set, FCVT.L.S must clip the result to the nearest representable signed 64-bit integer value.
RULE 6: FCVT.L.S must produce –2^63 as the result for any input whose rounded value is less than –2^63, including out-of-range negative inputs.
RULE 7: FCVT.L.S must produce 2^63-1 as the result for any input whose rounded value exceeds 2^63-1, including out-of-range positive inputs.
RULE 8: FCVT.L.S must produce –2^63 as the result when the input is negative infinity (–∞).
RULE 9: FCVT.L.S must produce 2^63-1 as the result when the input is positive infinity (+∞).
RULE 10: FCVT.L.S must produce 2^63-1 as the result when the input is a NaN.
RULE 11: FCVT.L.S must set the Inexact exception flag if the rounded result differs from the original operand value and the Invalid flag is not set.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.LU.D
FORMAT: fcvt.lu.d rd, fs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.LU.D must convert the double-precision floating-point value held in floating-point register rs1 to an unsigned integer value and write the result to integer register rd.
RULE 2: FCVT.LU.D is defined only on implementations with XLEN = 64 and is not defined for RV32 (RV64-only).
RULE 3: FCVT.LU.D must treat as valid exactly the double-precision floating-point inputs that are valid for the FCVT.int.S instruction.
RULE 4: For any double-precision floating-point input that is invalid for FCVT.int.S, FCVT.LU.D must produce the same architectural result as FCVT.int.S (e.g., raise the same exception).
RULE 5: FCVT.LU.D must round the conversion result according to the rounding mode specified in the rm field of the instruction.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.LU.H
FORMAT: fcvt.lu.h rd, fs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.LU.H MUST convert a half-precision floating-point source operand to an unsigned 64-bit integer result.
RULE 2: FCVT.LU.H is defined only for RV64 implementations and is not defined for RV32.
RULE 3: In the encoding of FCVT.LU.H the rm field supplies the rounding mode used for the conversion.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.LU.Q
FORMAT: fcvt.lu.q rd, fs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.LU.Q converts a quad-precision floating-point number to an unsigned integer value.
RULE 2: FCVT.LU.Q is an RV64-only instruction because the conversion instructions FCVT.L[U].Q and FCVT.Q.L[U] are defined only for RV64.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.LU.S
FORMAT: fcvt.lu.s rd, fs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.LU.S MUST convert the single-precision floating-point value in floating-point register rs1 to an unsigned 64-bit integer written to integer register rd.
RULE 2: FCVT.LU.S MUST be defined only for RV64 implementations and does not exist in RV32.
RULE 3: FCVT.LU.S MUST round the source floating-point value according to the rounding mode specified in the rm field of the instruction.
RULE 4: If the rounded value produced by FCVT.LU.S is not representable in the unsigned 64-bit integer format, FCVT.LU.S MUST clip the result to the nearest representable unsigned 64-bit value and MUST set the invalid exception flag.
RULE 5: When the rounded input to FCVT.LU.S is the minimum valid input (after rounding), FCVT.LU.S MUST produce the result 0.
RULE 6: When the rounded input to FCVT.LU.S is the maximum valid input (after rounding), FCVT.LU.S MUST produce the result 2^64−1.
RULE 7: For any out-of-range negative input to FCVT.LU.S, the instruction MUST produce the result 0.
RULE 8: When the input to FCVT.LU.S is negative infinity, FCVT.LU.S MUST produce the result 0.
RULE 9: For any out-of-range positive input to FCVT.LU.S, the instruction MUST produce the result 2^64−1.
RULE 10: When the input to FCVT.LU.S is positive infinity, FCVT.LU.S MUST produce the result 2^64−1.
RULE 11: When the input to FCVT.LU.S is NaN, FCVT.LU.S MUST produce the result 2^64−1.
RULE 12: FCVT.LU.S MUST set the inexact exception flag if the rounded result differs from the operand value and the invalid exception flag is not set.
RULE 13: FCVT.LU.S does NOT perform sign-extension of a 32-bit result because it directly produces a full 64-bit integer result.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.Q.D
FORMAT: fcvt.q.d fd, fs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.Q.D must convert the double-precision floating-point value contained in the source floating-point register rs1 to a quad-precision floating-point value and write the converted result to the destination floating-point register rd.
RULE 2: FCVT.Q.D must apply the rounding mode specified in the rm field of the instruction when rounding the double-precision value during the conversion to quad-precision.
RULE 3: FCVT.Q.D must produce the same architectural result as other double-precision floating-point-to-floating-point conversion instructions, including the handling of NaNs, infinities, signed zeros, and the raising of floating-point exception flags in accordance with the IEEE 754 standard.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.Q.H
FORMAT: fcvt.q.h fd, fs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.Q.H MUST convert the half-precision floating-point value in the source register specified by rs2 to a quad-precision floating-point value placed in the destination register specified by rd, using the rounding mode indicated by the rm field, when the Q extension is implemented.
RULE 2: FCVT.Q.H ignores the rs1 field; the rs1 field is not used by the instruction.
RULE 3: The rm field of FCVT.Q.H selects the rounding mode applied during the conversion.
RULE 4: The rs2 field of FCVT.Q.H specifies the source floating-point register containing the half-precision value to be converted.
RULE 5: The rd field of FCVT.Q.H specifies the destination floating-point register that receives the quad-precision result.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.Q.L
FORMAT: fcvt.q.l fd, rs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.Q.L must convert the 64-bit signed integer value in integer register rs1 to a quad-precision floating-point value and write the result to floating-point register rd.
RULE 2: FCVT.Q.L must be executed only on an RV64 implementation; on an implementation that does not support the RV64 base ISA, execution of FCVT.Q.L must raise an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 3: FCVT.Q.L must produce an exact conversion result and must ignore the rounding-mode field (rm) so that the rounding mode does not affect the result.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.Q.LU
FORMAT: fcvt.q.lu fd, rs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.Q.LU converts a 64-bit unsigned integer source operand to a quad-precision floating-point result.
RULE 2: FCVT.Q.LU is defined only for RV64 implementations; its encoding is reserved on RV32 or on implementations that do not support RV64.
RULE 3: FCVT.Q.LU always produces an exact conversion result and the current floating-point rounding mode does not affect its operation.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.Q.W
FORMAT: fcvt.q.w fd, rs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.Q.W converts a 32-bit signed integer operand to a quad-precision floating-point result.
RULE 2: FCVT.Q.W performs its conversion using the same architectural rules for rounding, overflow, and special cases as the existing integer-to-double-precision conversion instructions.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.Q.WU
FORMAT: fcvt.q.wu fd, rs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.Q.WU converts a 32-bit unsigned integer to a quad-precision floating-point number, and also converts a quad-precision floating-point number to a 32-bit unsigned integer.
RULE 2: FCVT.Q.WU is not an RV64-only instruction and therefore must be present in any RV32 or RV64 implementation that includes the floating-point extension.
RULE 3: FCVT.Q.WU obeys the rounding mode specified in the rm field of the instruction and may raise the same floating-point exception flags (invalid operation, overflow, underflow, inexact) as the analogous double-precision conversion instructions.
RULE 4: FCVT.Q.WU is not specified to always produce an exact result and therefore its result may be rounded according to the current rounding mode.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.S.BF16
FORMAT: fcvt.s.bf16 fd, fs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.S.BF16 must convert a BF16 source operand to an IEEE-754 single-precision (FP32) result placed in the destination floating-point register.
RULE 2: FCVT.S.BF16 converts a BF16 value to an FP32 value.
RULE 3: FCVT.S.BF16 must perform an exact conversion of the BF16 input to the FP32 result.
RULE 4: When the BF16 input to FCVT.S.BF16 is a normal number or infinity, FCVT.S.BF16 shifts the BF16 encoded bits left by 16 positions and writes zeros into the least-significant 16 bits of the FP32 result.
RULE 5: FCVT.S.BF16 NaN-boxes the FP32 result by setting the most-significant FLEN-32 bits of the destination register to 1.
RULE 6: FCVT.S.BF16 raises the invalid exception for inputs that are NaNs.
RULE 7: FCVT.S.BF16 converts the BF16 value held in the source floating-point register to an FP32 (single-precision) value and writes the converted FP32 result to the destination floating-point register.
RULE 8: FCVT.S.BF16 reads its source operand as a BF16 value from a floating-point register.
RULE 9: FCVT.S.BF16 produces an FP32 value as its result and stores that result in a floating-point register.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.S.D
FORMAT: fcvt.s.d fd, fs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.S.D converts a double-precision floating-point value in a source floating-point register to a single-precision floating-point value that is written to a destination floating-point register.
RULE 2: FCVT.S.D rounds the conversion result according to the rounding mode specified in the RM field.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.S.H
FORMAT: fcvt.s.h fd, fs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.S.H reads the half-precision floating-point value from source register rs1 (src) and writes the resulting single-precision floating-point value to destination register rd (dest).
RULE 2: FCVT.S.H applies the rounding mode indicated by the rm field to the half-precision to single-precision conversion.
RULE 3: FCVT.S.H is a conversion instruction that operates on 16-bit half-precision floating-point values as part of the minimal half-precision support provided by Zfhmin.
RULE 4: FCVT.S.H must be implemented only when the single-precision floating-point extension (F) is present.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.S.LU
FORMAT: fcvt.s.lu fd, rs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.S.LU converts the unsigned integer value held in integer register rs1 to a single-precision floating-point value that is written to floating-point register rd.
RULE 2: FCVT.S.LU rounds its conversion result according to the rounding mode specified in the rm field, using the same rounding rules as all other floating-point to integer and integer to floating-point conversion instructions.
RULE 3: If the rounded conversion result cannot be represented in the single-precision destination format, FCVT.S.LU clips the result to the nearest representable value and sets the invalid-operation exception flag.
RULE 4: FCVT.S.LU sets the inexact-operation exception flag when the rounded conversion result differs from the original unsigned integer operand value and the invalid-operation exception flag is not set.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.S.Q
FORMAT: fcvt.s.q fd, fs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.S.Q converts a quad-precision floating-point number to a single-precision floating-point number.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.S.WU
FORMAT: fcvt.s.wu fd, rs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.S.WU MUST convert the unsigned integer value in integer register rs1 to a single-precision floating-point value written to floating-point register rd.
RULE 2: FCVT.S.WU MUST round the conversion result according to the rounding mode specified in the rm field.
RULE 3: FCVT.S.WU MUST clip the rounded result to the nearest representable single-precision value and set the Invalid exception flag when the rounded result is not representable in the destination format.
RULE 4: FCVT.S.WU MUST set the Inexact exception flag when the rounded result differs from the operand value and the Invalid exception flag is not set.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.W.D
FORMAT: fcvt.w.d rd, fs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.W.D converts the double-precision floating-point value in floating-point register rs1 to a signed 32-bit integer and writes the result to integer register rd.
RULE 2: FCVT.W.D rounds the conversion result according to the rounding mode specified in the rm field of the instruction.
RULE 3: When executed on an RV64 implementation, FCVT.W.D sign-extends the 32-bit integer result to produce a 64-bit value written to integer register rd.
RULE 4: FCVT.W.D must raise the floating-point exception flags that correspond to converting its double-precision source operand to a signed 32-bit integer, and the set of flags raised is identical to those that would be raised for FCVTMOD.W.D with the same operand.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.W.H
FORMAT: fcvt.w.h rd, fs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.W.H must convert the half-precision floating-point value in source floating-point register rs1 to a signed 32-bit integer and write the resulting integer into integer register rd.
RULE 2: FCVT.W.H must apply the rounding mode indicated by the rm field when rounding the source value during the conversion.
RULE 3: FCVT.W.H must be implemented in both RV32 and RV64 processors and is not restricted to RV64 implementations.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.W.Q
FORMAT: fcvt.w.q rd, fs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.W.Q converts a quad-precision floating-point number to a signed 32-bit integer.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.W.S
FORMAT: fcvt.w.s rd, fs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.W.S converts the floating-point value in source register rs1 to a signed 32-bit integer placed in destination integer register rd.
RULE 2: When XLEN is greater than 32, FCVT.W.S sign-extends the 32-bit conversion result to fill the full XLEN bits of rd.
RULE 3: FCVT.W.S rounds the floating-point operand according to the rounding mode specified in the rm field of the instruction.
RULE 4: If the rounded result of FCVT.W.S is not representable in the signed 32-bit destination format, FCVT.W.S clips the result to the nearest representable value and sets the invalid exception flag.
RULE 5: For FCVT.W.S, any input whose rounded value is less than –2³¹ is considered out-of-range negative and the produced integer result is –2³¹.
RULE 6: For FCVT.W.S, an input value of negative infinity –∞ yields the integer result –2³¹.
RULE 7: For FCVT.W.S, any input whose rounded value exceeds 2³¹–1 is considered out-of-range positive and the produced integer result is 2³¹–1.
RULE 8: For FCVT.W.S, an input value of positive infinity +∞ or a NaN produces the integer result 2³¹–1.
RULE 9: When FCVT.W.S produces a result that differs from the original operand after rounding and the invalid exception flag is not set, FCVT.W.S sets the inexact exception flag.
RULE 10: If the rounded result of FCVT.W.S lies within the inclusive range –2³¹ to 2³¹–1, FCVT.W.S writes that exact integer value to rd without clipping.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.WU.D
FORMAT: fcvt.wu.d rd, fs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.WU.D converts the double-precision floating-point value in floating-point register rs1 to an unsigned 32-bit integer and writes the result to integer register rd.
RULE 2: FCVT.WU.D rounds the source floating-point value according to the rounding mode specified in the rm field before performing the conversion.
RULE 3: In RV64 implementations, FCVT.WU.D sign-extends the 32-bit unsigned integer result to 64 bits when writing to integer register rd.
RULE 4: FCVT.WU.D accepts as valid inputs exactly the same range of double-precision floating-point values defined for FCVT.int.S, and any input outside that range is handled identically to FCVT.int.S.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.WU.H
FORMAT: fcvt.wu.h rd, fs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.WU.H converts the half-precision floating-point value held in the source floating-point register (rs1) to an unsigned 32-bit integer and writes the result to the destination integer register (rd).
RULE 2: FCVT.WU.H applies the rounding mode specified by the rounding-mode field (rm) when performing the conversion from half-precision to unsigned integer.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.WU.S
FORMAT: fcvt.wu.s rd, fs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.WU.S converts the floating-point value in floating-point register rs1 to an unsigned 32-bit integer and writes the result to integer register rd.
RULE 2: When XLEN is greater than 32, FCVT.WU.S sign-extends the 32-bit integer result to the full XLEN width of the destination register.
RULE 3: FCVT.WU.S rounds the conversion result according to the rounding mode specified in the rm field.
RULE 4: If the rounded result of FCVT.WU.S is not representable in the unsigned 32-bit destination format, FCVT.WU.S clips the result to the nearest representable value and sets the invalid exception flag.
RULE 5: FCVT.WU.S sets the inexact exception flag when the rounded result differs from the original operand value and the invalid exception flag is not set.
RULE 6: For FCVT.WU.S the minimum valid input value after rounding is 0.
RULE 7: For FCVT.WU.S the maximum valid input value after rounding is 2^32 - 1.
RULE 8: When FCVT.WU.S receives a negative input that is out of range, it produces the output 0.
RULE 9: When FCVT.WU.S receives negative infinity as input, it produces the output 0.
RULE 10: When FCVT.WU.S receives a positive input that exceeds the representable range, it produces the output 2^32 - 1.
RULE 11: When FCVT.WU.S receives positive infinity or a NaN as input, it produces the output 2^32 - 1.

INSTRUCTION: FCVTMOD.W.D
FORMAT: fcvtmod.w.d rd, fs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVTMOD.W.D must round the conversion toward zero regardless of any rounding-mode field value.
