RULE 5: An SC.D must fail if a store from another hart to any byte of the reservation set can be observed to occur between the LR and the SC.D.
RULE 6: An SC.D must fail if any other SC instruction, to any address, appears in program order between the LR and the SC.D.
RULE 7: An SC.D must fail if a write from another device to any byte accessed by the LR can be observed to occur between the LR and the SC.D.
RULE 8: If a write from another device updates the reservation set but does not update any byte accessed by the LR, then an SC.D may either fail or succeed.
RULE 9: The effects of an SC.D can never be observed by another RISC-V hart before the LR instruction that established the reservation is observed.
RULE 10: Executing an SC.D invalidates any reservation held by the executing hart.
RULE 11: When an SC.D succeeds, it writes the doubleword contained in rs2 to the memory address in rs1 and writes zero to rd.
RULE 12: When an SC.D fails, it does not modify memory at the address in rs1 and writes a non-zero value to rd.

INSTRUCTION: SC.W
FORMAT: sc.w rd, rs2, (rs1)
RULE 1: SC.W succeeds only if the reservation is still valid and the reservation set contains the bytes being written.
RULE 2: When SC.W succeeds, it writes the word from rs2 to the memory address in rs1 and writes zero to rd.
RULE 3: When SC.W fails, it does not write to memory and writes a nonzero value to rd.
RULE 4: SC.W must not retire unless it passes memory permission checks.
RULE 5: Executing SC.W invalidates any reservation held by the executing hart, regardless of success or failure.
RULE 6: On RV64, SC.W sign-extends the value placed in rd.
RULE 7: SC.W requires the address in rs1 to be naturally aligned to a four-byte boundary; otherwise an address-misaligned exception or an access-fault exception is generated.
RULE 8: SC.W fails if its address is not within the reservation set of the most recent LR in program order.
RULE 9: SC.W fails if a store to the reservation set from another hart can be observed to occur between the LR and the SC.W.
RULE 10: SC.W fails if a write from another device to the bytes accessed by the LR can be observed to occur between the LR and the SC.W.
RULE 11: SC.W fails if another SC (to any address) occurs between the LR and the SC.W in program order.
RULE 12: SC.W may succeed only if no store from another hart to the reservation set is observed between the LR and the SC.W and no other SC occurs between them in program order.
RULE 13: SC.W may succeed only if no write from a non-hart device to the bytes accessed by the LR is observed between the LR and the SC.W.

INSTRUCTION: SCTRCLR
FORMAT: sctrclr
RULE 1: Execution of the SCTRCLR instruction must zero all CTR Entry Registers for every DEPTH value.
RULE 2: Execution of the SCTRCLR instruction must set the CTR cycle counter to zero.
RULE 3: Execution of the SCTRCLR instruction must set the CTR CCV field to zero.
RULE 4: After an SCTRCLR instruction and before the next qualified control transfer, a read of the ctrsource register must return zero.
RULE 5: After an SCTRCLR instruction and before the next qualified control transfer, a read of the ctrtarget register must return zero.
RULE 6: After an SCTRCLR instruction and before the next qualified control transfer, a read of the ctrdata register must return zero.
RULE 7: The first control transfer recorded after an SCTRCLR instruction must have the ctrdata.CCV field equal to zero.
RULE 8: If the SCTRCLR instruction is executed in U-mode and CTR state enable access restrictions do not apply, the implementation must raise an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 9: If the SCTRCLR instruction is executed in VU-mode and CTR state enable access restrictions do not apply, the implementation must raise a virtual-instruction exception.
RULE 10: If CTR state enable access restrictions apply, execution of the SCTRCLR instruction must not raise the illegal-instruction or virtual-instruction exception specified for U-mode or VU-mode.
RULE 11: If Smstateen is implemented and mstateen0.CTR = 1, then SCTRCLR must behave as described in Section 11.3.1.
RULE 12: If Smstateen is implemented, mstateen0.CTR = 0, and the current privilege mode is less privileged than M-mode, then execution of SCTRCLR must raise an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 13: If Smstateen is implemented, mstateen0.CTR = 1, and hstateen0.CTR = 1, then SCTRCLR must behave as described in Section 11.3.1.
RULE 14: If Smstateen is implemented, mstateen0.CTR = 1, hstateen0.CTR = 0, and the processor is in VSmode, then execution of SCTRCLR must raise a virtual-instruction exception.
RULE 15: Execution of SCTRCLR MUST reset the CtrCycleCounter.
RULE 16: After execution of SCTRCLR, the Cycle Count Valid (CCV) field of the next record MUST be 0.
RULE 17: Execution of SCTRCLR MUST have the same architectural effect as a write to the xctrctl CSR that resets the CtrCycleCounter and clears CCV.
RULE 18: The 32-bit encoding of SCTRCLR must have funct7 = 0b0001000, rs2 = 0b00100, rs1 = 0b00000, funct3 = 0b000, rd = 0b00000, and opcode = 0b1110011.

INSTRUCTION: SD
FORMAT: sd rs2, imm12(rs1)
RULE 1: SD stores the low 64 bits of register rs2 into the memory location addressed by the instruction.
RULE 2: SD MUST atomically store a doubleword (2 × XLEN/8 bytes) to the memory address computed from the rs1 base register.
RULE 3: SD MUST have a release-RCsc ordering annotation.
RULE 4: SD MAY have an acquire-RCsc ordering annotation when the encoded aq bit is 1.
RULE 5: SD encodings MUST have the rl bit set because the rl bit is mandatory for all defined forms of the instruction.
RULE 6: An SD instruction generated by the RV64C C.SDSP encoding MUST store the 64-bit value from source register rs2 into the memory location whose effective address is the stack pointer x2 plus the zero-extended offset scaled by eight.
RULE 7: The effective address for the SD instruction in the C.SDSP expansion MUST be calculated by zero-extending the compressed offset, multiplying it by eight, and adding the result to the current value of register x2.
RULE 8: The RV64C C.SDSP compressed instruction MUST expand to the uncompressed form SD rs2, offset(x2), producing identical architectural behavior.
RULE 9: In RV64 implementations (XLEN ≠ 32), the SD instruction stores the 8-byte double-word value from the specified integer register to the memory address formed by the base address with a zero offset.
RULE 10: After each execution of an SD instruction, the base address used for the next SD is decremented by 8 bytes, causing each successive SD to write to a lower address in the stack frame.
RULE 11: All SD instructions generated by the cm.push pseudocode are completed before the subsequent atomic adjustment of the stack pointer (sp -= stack_adj) is performed.
RULE 12: The cm.push pseudocode for RV64 defines the number of bytes transferred by each SD as 8, reflecting that SD stores a double-word.
RULE 13: SD stores a 64-bit doubleword composed of the contents of register pair rs2 into memory at the effective address computed as rs1 plus the signed offset.
RULE 14: When the effective address of an SD instruction is a multiple of 8, the implementation must not raise an address-misaligned exception.
RULE 15: When the effective address of an SD instruction is a multiple of 4, each 32-bit word access performed by the store must be performed atomically.
RULE 16: When the effective address of an SD instruction is a multiple of 8, each word access is guaranteed to be atomic.
RULE 17: When the effective address of an SD instruction is a multiple of 8, the implementation must not raise a misaligned-address trap.
RULE 18: When the effective address of an SD instruction is a multiple of 4 but not a multiple of 8, the implementation may raise an address-misaligned exception.
RULE 19: When the effective address of an SD instruction is a multiple of 4 but not a multiple of 8, each word access performed by the store must be performed atomically.
RULE 20: When the effective address of an SD instruction is not a multiple of 4, the implementation may raise an address-misaligned exception.
RULE 21: If the source register specified for an SD (or C.SDSP) instruction is x0, the 64-bit value stored is zero and the source register is not architecturally accessed.
RULE 22: A C.SD instruction must specify a source register in the range x8-x15, and encodings that specify any other register are reserved.
RULE 23: For RVWMO ordering and exception handling, an SD instruction is treated as a misaligned store.
RULE 24: An SD instruction whose effective address is a multiple of 4 generates exactly two 4-byte memory operations.
RULE 25: When an SD instruction with a 4-byte-aligned effective address is decomposed into two 32-bit operations, the architectural specification imposes no ordering constraint between the two operations.
RULE 26: Each of the two 4-byte memory operations produced by a decomposed SD instruction is guaranteed to be atomic.
RULE 27: The two-operation sequence produced by an SD instruction with a 4-byte-aligned effective address may be interrupted before both operations complete.
RULE 28: If an exception is taken after the first of the two 4-byte operations of an SD, the architectural effect of the first operation remains visible.
RULE 29: After an interrupt that occurs during execution of an SD instruction, re-execution of the instruction may repeat one or both of its 4-byte memory accesses, potentially overwriting bytes written by earlier accesses of the same instruction.
RULE 30: SD reads a 64-bit value formed by concatenating the contents of register rs2 (lower half) with the contents of register rs2+1 (upper half) and stores that doubleword to memory.
RULE 31: SD computes its effective address by adding the value in register rs1 to the sign-extended 12-bit offset encoded in the instruction.
RULE 32: SD sign-extends the 12-bit offset to the XLEN width before adding it to the value in rs1 to form the effective address.
RULE 33: SD must store the 64-bit value held in register rs2 to the memory location whose effective address is computed by adding the instruction's offset to the contents of register x2.
RULE 34: The compressed instruction c.sdsp must expand to an SD instruction with operands rs2 and offset(x2).
RULE 35: SD stores a 64-bit doubleword value from a register to memory.
RULE 36: SD obtains the 64-bit value from the architectural register rs2′, and when the instruction is the expansion of the compressed form c.sd the upper 32 bits are taken from the register rs2′+1.
RULE 37: SD computes the effective address by adding to the base address in register rs1′ the zero-extended offset multiplied by 8.
RULE 38: For a non-compressed standard store instruction that is SD, the transformed instruction must be encoded according to the format defined in Figure 122.
RULE 39: In the transformed encoding of an SD instruction, the rs2, funct3, and opcode fields must be identical to those in the original trapping SD instruction.
RULE 40: For a transformed SD instruction, the Addr. Offset field occupying bits 19-15 must contain the positive difference between the faulting virtual address recorded in mtval or stval and the original virtual address, and this value may be non-zero only when the original SD caused a misaligned memory access.
RULE 41: In the transformed encoding of an SD instruction, all immediate offset fields (bits 31-25, 24-20, and 11-7) must be set to zero.
RULE 42: The address used by an SD instruction MUST be transformed by the pointer masking mechanism.
RULE 43: Misaligned SD stores are supported, provided they satisfy the same limitations that apply when pointer masking is not used.
RULE 44: The effect of a misaligned SD store is identical to applying the pointer masking transformation to each aligned memory access that composes the store.
RULE 45: When MPRV or SPVP are set, the pointer masking settings of the effective privilege mode MUST be applied to the address used by an SD instruction.
RULE 46: If MXR is enabled in the effective privilege mode at the time an SD instruction executes, pointer masking MUST NOT be applied to the store address.

INSTRUCTION: SEXT.B
FORMAT: sext.b rd, rs1
RULE 1: SEXT.B reads the register specified by rd'/rs1' from the register set x8-x15 and writes the result to the same register.
RULE 2: SEXT.B extracts bits 7:0 of the source register, treats bit 7 as a sign bit, and creates an XLEN-wide value by replicating bit 7 into all higher-order bits.
RULE 3: SEXT.B stores the XLEN-wide sign-extended value into the destination register, replacing its previous contents.
RULE 4: For the SEXT.B instruction, the .b suffix causes the operation to consider only the least-significant 8 bits of the source register.
RULE 5: The SEXT.B instruction extracts the least-significant 8 bits of the source register rs, sign-extends that 8-bit value to the processor's XLEN, and writes the XLEN-wide result to the destination register rd.
RULE 6: SEXT.B MUST sign-extend the least-significant 8 bits of the source register and write the XLEN-bit result to the destination register.
RULE 7: SEXT.B is architecturally equivalent to executing the sequence "slli rd, rs, (XLEN-8); srai rd, rd, (XLEN-8)".
RULE 8: An implementation that includes the RV32 or RV64 integer base ISA MUST provide the SEXT.B instruction.
RULE 9: The SEXT.B instruction must write to the destination register a value whose low-order 8 bits are identical to the source register's low-order 8 bits and whose remaining bits (bits XLEN-1 down to 8) are all equal to bit 7 of the source register's low-order byte, thereby sign-extending that byte to XLEN.
RULE 10: Implementations that include the Zbb (Basic bit-manipulation) extension at its minimum version 0.93 and later must provide the SEXT.B instruction, which is a ratified instruction of that extension.

INSTRUCTION: SEXT.H
FORMAT: sext.h rd, rs1
RULE 1: SEXT.H rd, rs MUST sign-extend the least-significant 16 bits of the value in register rs and write the resulting XLEN-bit value to register rd.
RULE 2: SEXT.H MUST replicate bit 15 of rs into all bits of the result above bit 15.
RULE 3: SEXT.H MUST read rs, write the sign-extended result to rd, and MUST NOT modify any other architectural state.
RULE 4: When SEXT.H specifies x0 as rd, the sign-extended result MUST be discarded and no architectural state change occurs.
RULE 5: SEXT.H is defined for both RV32 and RV64 implementations and MUST produce an XLEN-bit result appropriate to the current XLEN.
RULE 6: SEXT.H rd, rs MUST read the value in integer register rs, extract bits 15 down to 0, and produce a result in which bits 15 down to 0 equal the extracted halfword and bits XLEN-1 down to 16 are all set to the value of bit 15 of rs.
RULE 7: SEXT.H rd, rs MUST write the sign-extended result into integer register rd, replacing the previous contents of rd.
RULE 8: SEXT.H rd, rs MUST NOT modify any integer register other than rd nor affect any memory location.
RULE 9: SEXT.H rd, rs MUST perform the sign-extension operation for the implementation's XLEN (the width of integer registers), replicating the half-word sign bit into all more-significant bits of the XLEN-bit result.

INSTRUCTION: SFENCE.INVAL.IR
FORMAT: sfence.inval.ir
RULE 1: SFENCE.INVAL.IR MUST guarantee that any SINVAL.VMA instructions previously executed by the same hart are ordered before any later implicit references by that hart to memory-management data structures.
RULE 2: When a single hart executes the sequence SFENCE.W.INVAL, then SINVAL.VMA, then SFENCE.INVAL.IR in that order, the resulting architectural effect is equivalent to a hypothetical SFENCE.VMA that orders all subsequent reads and writes as occurring after that SFENCE.VMA.
RULE 3: Executing SFENCE.INVAL.IR while the hart is in U-mode MUST raise an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 4: Executing SFENCE.INVAL.IR while the hart is in VU-mode MUST raise a virtual-instruction exception.
RULE 5: Execution of SFENCE.INVAL.IR in S-mode or VS-mode is always permitted regardless of the values of the mstatus.TVM and hstatus.VTVM fields.
RULE 6: When mstatus.TVM=1 or hstatus.VTVM=1, execution of SFENCE.INVAL.IR does NOT cause a trap because it has only ordering effects and no visible side effects.

INSTRUCTION: SFENCE.VMA
FORMAT: sfence.vma rs1, rs2
RULE 1: SFENCE.VMA must order store operations.
RULE 2: SFENCE.VMA does not order invalidate, clean, or flush operations.
RULE 3: M-mode software MUST execute an SFENCE.VMA instruction with rs1 = x0 and rs2 = x0 whenever any memory-management data structures remain in use across a change to SBE.
RULE 4: When SBE is changed on a world switch and neither the old nor the new memory-management data structure is reinterpreted in a different endianness, no additional SFENCE.VMA instruction is required beyond the ordinary requirements for a world switch.
RULE 5: The TVM bit that controls the legality of SFENCE.VMA in S-mode is a WARL field.
RULE 6: If the TVM bit is 1, executing an SFENCE.VMA instruction in S-mode must raise an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 7: If the TVM bit is 0, executing an SFENCE.VMA instruction in S-mode is permitted and must not raise an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 8: When S-mode is not supported, the TVM bit is read-only 0, and therefore an SFENCE.VMA instruction that would execute in S-mode does not cause an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 9: After software modifies the menvcfg.PBMTE field, executing an SFENCE.VMA instruction with rs1 set to x0 and rs2 set to x0 synchronizes the address-translation caches with respect to the new interpretation of page-table entries' PBMT fields.
RULE 10: After software modifies the menvcfg.ADUE field, executing an SFENCE.VMA instruction with rs1 set to x0 and rs2 set to x0 synchronizes the address-translation caches with respect to the new interpretation of page-table entries' A/D bits.
RULE 11: An SFENCE.VMA instruction that matches the target address and ASID causes translations that began while satp was active to be required to complete or terminate after satp is no longer active.
RULE 12: SFENCE.VMA synchronizes updates to in-memory memory-management data structures with the execution of the current hart.
RULE 13: Executing SFENCE.VMA guarantees that any previous stores already visible to the current hart are ordered before the implicit references to memory-management data structures performed by subsequent instructions in that hart.
RULE 14: The set of operations ordered by SFENCE.VMA is determined by the values in rs1 and rs2.
RULE 15: SFENCE.VMA also invalidates entries in the address-translation cache associated with the executing hart.
RULE 16: SFENCE.VMA orders only the local hart's implicit references to memory-management data structures.
RULE 17: If rs1=x0 and rs2=x0, SFENCE.VMA orders all reads and writes made to any level of the page tables for all address spaces and invalidates all address-translation cache entries for all address spaces.
RULE 18: If rs1=x0 and rs2≠x0, SFENCE.VMA orders all reads and writes made to any level of the page tables only for the address space identified by rs2, does not order accesses to global mappings, and invalidates all address-translation cache entries that match that address space except those containing global mappings.
RULE 19: If rs1≠x0 and rs2=x0, SFENCE.VMA orders only reads and writes made to leaf page-table entries corresponding to the virtual address in rs1 for all address spaces and invalidates all address-translation cache entries that contain leaf page-table entries for that virtual address for all address spaces.
RULE 20: If rs1≠x0 and rs2≠x0, SFENCE.VMA orders only reads and writes made to leaf page-table entries corresponding to the virtual address in rs1 for the address space identified by rs2, does not order accesses to global mappings, and invalidates all address-translation cache entries that contain leaf page-table entries for that virtual address and that address space except those containing global mappings.
RULE 21: If the value held in rs1 is not a valid virtual address, SFENCE.VMA has no effect and does not raise an exception.
RULE 22: When rs2≠x0, software should zero bits SXLEN-1:ASIDMAX of the rs2 value used by SFENCE.VMA, and current implementations must ignore those bits because they are reserved for future standard use.
RULE 23: If ASIDLEN < ASIDMAX, implementations must ignore bits ASIDMAX-1:ASIDLEN of the rs2 value used by SFENCE.VMA.
RULE 24: An implicit read of the memory-management data structures performed after a SFENCE.VMA may return any translation that was valid at any time since the most recent SFENCE.VMA that subsumes the accessed address.
RULE 25: The ordering imposed by SFENCE.VMA does not place implicit reads and writes to memory-management data structures into the global memory order, so prior explicit accesses are not necessarily ordered before subsequent explicit accesses in the global memory order.
RULE 26: Implicit loads generated by SFENCE.VMA need not obey normal program-order semantics with respect to prior loads or stores to the same address.
RULE 27: After a SFENCE.VMA, an implementation may use any translation for a virtual address that was valid at any time since the most recent subsuming SFENCE.VMA, and the choice of translation is unpredictable but the behavior of the access is otherwise well defined.
RULE 28: The possibility of using any translation since the last subsuming SFENCE.VMA applies even when the virtual-address width of those translations differs from the width currently specified by satp.MODE.
RULE 29: For a given virtual address and ASID, any translation since the last subsuming SFENCE.VMA may be used even if that translation employed a different virtual-address width.
RULE 30: For a given virtual address, any global translation since the last subsuming SFENCE.VMA may be used regardless of ASID or virtual-address width.
RULE 31: When multiple TLB entries match a single address, SFENCE.VMA does not guarantee which entry is used, making the choice unpredictable but the resulting behavior well defined.
RULE 32: Implementations must only perform implicit reads of translation data structures pointed to by the current satp register or by a subsequent valid (V=1) translation-data-structure entry, and must raise exceptions only for implicit accesses that are generated as a result of SFENCE.VMA instruction execution, not for speculative accesses.
RULE 33: When SFENCE.VMA is executed on a hart that employs an address-translation cache, that cache must appear private to that hart, and the meaning of an ASID is local to the hart, allowing the same ASID to refer to different address spaces on different harts.
RULE 34: For implementations where satp.MODE is read-only zero (always Bare), executing SFENCE.VMA may raise an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 35: An SFENCE.VMA instruction must be executed after writes to the memory-management tables in order to flush the relevant cached translations so that subsequent implicit reads observe those writes.
RULE 36: If an SFENCE.VMA instruction is not executed between a write to the memory-management tables and a subsequent implicit read of the same address, the implementation may unpredictably use either the old non-leaf PTE or the new leaf PTE while all other behavior remains well defined.
RULE 37: Speculative executions of the address-translation algorithm must not set the dirty bit for any PTE.
RULE 38: Speculative executions of the address-translation algorithm must not trigger an exception.
RULE 39: A SFENCE.VMA instruction with rs1 = x0 flushes the address-translation caches for all 4 KiB regions in the entire address space.
RULE 40: A SFENCE.VMA instruction with rs1 ≠ x0 flushes the address-translation caches for the single 4 KiB region whose starting virtual address is contained in rs1.
RULE 41: If any page-table inconsistencies are present when SFENCE.VMA is executed, the hardware MAY select any one of the possible translations for the affected virtual addresses, and the selected translation is unpredictable, which is the same effect as using SFENCE.VMA incorrectly.
RULE 42: SFENCE.VMA must invalidate address-translation cache entries as determined by the values in its rs1 and rs2 operands.
RULE 43: The set of address-translation cache entries that SFENCE.VMA would invalidate for given rs1 and rs2 values must be the set that SINVAL.VMA invalidates when executed with the same rs1 and rs2 values.
RULE 44: SFENCE.VMA must order all reads and writes that occur before the SFENCE.VMA to be globally observable before the SFENCE.VMA.
RULE 45: SFENCE.VMA must order all reads and writes that occur after the SFENCE.VMA to be globally observable after the SFENCE.VMA.
RULE 46: SFENCE.VMA has the same overall ordering effect as executing the ordered sequence SFENCE.W.INVAL, SINVAL.VMA, and SFENCE.INVAL.IR with identical rs1 and rs2 operand values.
RULE 47: Executing SFENCE.VMA in user mode must raise an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 48: Executing SFENCE.VMA in supervisor mode or hypervisor-supervisor mode when the mstatus.TVM field is 1 must raise an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 49: SFENCE.VMA requires the same execution permissions and raises the same exceptions as SINVAL.VMA, HINVAL.VVMA, and HINVAL.GVMA.
RULE 50: If VTVM=1 and an attempt is made to execute SFENCE.VMA in VS-mode, a virtual-instruction exception MUST be raised.
RULE 51: When V = 0, SFENCE.VMA treats its virtual-address argument as an HS-level virtual address.
RULE 52: When V = 0, SFENCE.VMA treats its ASID argument as an HS-level ASID.
RULE 53: When V = 0, SFENCE.VMA orders stores only to HS-level address-translation structures and orders them with subsequent HS-level address translations.
RULE 54: When V = 1, SFENCE.VMA treats its virtual-address argument as a guest virtual address within the current virtual machine.
RULE 55: When V = 1, SFENCE.VMA treats its ASID argument as a VS-level ASID belonging to the current virtual machine.
RULE 56: When V = 1, SFENCE.VMA identifies the current virtual machine by the VMID field of CSR hgatp.
RULE 57: When V = 1, the effective ASID for SFENCE.VMA is considered to be the combination of the VMID from hgatp with the VS-level ASID argument.
RULE 58: When V = 1, SFENCE.VMA orders stores only to VS-level address-translation structures and orders them with subsequent VS-stage address translations for the same virtual machine.
RULE 59: The ordering effect of SFENCE.VMA in V = 1 mode applies only when the VMID in hgatp at the time of a translation equals the VMID value captured at the execution of the SFENCE.VMA.
RULE 60: In VS-mode, when hstatus.VTVM=1 and an SFENCE.VMA instruction is attempted, a virtual-instruction exception must be raised.
RULE 61: In VU-mode, when an SFENCE.VMA instruction is attempted, a virtual-instruction exception must be raised.
RULE 62: Changes to xSSE take effect immediately and are not required to be synchronized by executing SFENCE.VMA.
RULE 63: Address-translation caches need not be synchronized with SFENCE.VMA after changes to xSSE.

INSTRUCTION: SFENCE.W.INVAL
FORMAT: sfence.w.inval
RULE 1: SFENCE.W.INVAL guarantees that all prior stores already visible to the executing hart are ordered before any subsequent SINVAL.VMA instructions executed by that hart.
RULE 2: Executing SFENCE.W.INVAL while the hart is in user mode MUST raise an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 3: Executing SFENCE.W.INVAL while the hart is in virtual user mode MUST raise a virtual-instruction exception.
RULE 4: When the hart is in supervisor mode or virtual supervisor mode, SFENCE.W.INVAL is not affected by the mstatus.TVM or hstatus.VTVM fields and therefore is always permitted.

INSTRUCTION: SH
FORMAT: sh rs2, imm12(rs1)
RULE 1: SH stores the low 16 bits of integer register rs2 to the memory location identified by the instruction's effective address.
RULE 2: If the effective address of an SH instruction is naturally aligned (divisible by 2), the SH shall not raise an address-misaligned exception.
RULE 3: An EEI may guarantee that a misaligned SH instruction is fully supported, causing the SH to complete execution without raising any exception.
RULE 4: If an EEI does not guarantee invisible handling of misaligned accesses, a misaligned SH may either complete execution successfully or raise an exception.
RULE 5: When a misaligned SH raises an exception, the exception may be either an address-misaligned exception or an access-fault exception.
RULE 6: When an EEI does not guarantee invisible handling of misaligned accesses, the EEI must define whether an exception caused by a misaligned SH results in a contained trap or a fatal trap.
RULE 7: Even when a misaligned SH completes successfully, its execution may be extremely slow compared to a naturally aligned SH.
RULE 8: Naturally aligned SH instructions are guaranteed to execute atomically, whereas misaligned SH instructions are not guaranteed to be atomic and may require additional synchronization to ensure atomicity.
RULE 9: SH stores the low-order 16 bits of the value in register rs2 to the memory address specified by the instruction.
RULE 10: SH performs an explicit memory access that stores a half-word to the address computed by adding a signed offset to the contents of its base register.
RULE 11: If a FENCE.I instruction follows SH in program order, the half-word store performed by SH is ordered before any instruction fetch that occurs after that FENCE.I.
RULE 12: SH MUST atomically store a 2-byte value taken from the low bits of register rs2 to the memory address calculated from register rs1.
RULE 13: SH MUST include the release-RCsc ordering annotation, which is encoded by the rl bit.
RULE 14: SH MAY include the acquire-RCsc ordering annotation, which is encoded by the aq bit.
RULE 15: SH always has a release-RCsc annotation regardless of any other bits.
RULE 16: If the aq bit of SH is set, SH also has an acquire-RCsc annotation.
RULE 17: SH encodings that do not set the rl bit are defined as RESERVED.
RULE 18: The c.sh instruction MUST store the low 16 bits of the source register rs2' to the memory location whose address is the sum of the base register rs1' and the zero-extended immediate uimm encoded in the instruction.
RULE 19: The c.sh instruction MUST raise a misaligned-store exception if the computed effective address is not a multiple of two bytes.
RULE 20: The c.sh instruction MUST raise the same access-fault, page-fault, and other memory-access exceptions as the 32-bit sh instruction for the same effective address and data.
RULE 21: The c.sh instruction MUST have the same architectural effect on the architectural state (memory, PC, registers) as the 32-bit sh instruction, including not modifying any integer register and advancing the PC by the instruction length unless a trap occurs.
RULE 22: SH MUST store the least-significant halfword of the source register rs2' into memory.
RULE 23: SH MUST form the immediate uimm such that uimm[31:2] = 0, uimm[1] = encoding[5], and uimm[0] = 0.
RULE 24: SH MUST compute the effective address by adding the zero-extended immediate uimm to the value held in the base register rs1'.
RULE 25: SH MUST write the 16-bit value to memory at the effective address, updating bits 15..0 of that memory location.
RULE 26: SH MUST take its rs1' operand from one of the registers x8 through x15.
RULE 27: SH MUST take its rs2' operand from one of the registers x8 through x15.
RULE 28: When a synchronous exception caused by an explicit memory access of an SH instruction is a Store/AMO address-misaligned, Store/AMO access-fault, or Store/AMO page-fault, the trap-instruction CSR (mtinst or htinst) may be written with a transformed encoding of that SH instruction.
RULE 29: In a transformed SH instruction written to mtinst or htinst, the rs2, funct3, and opcode fields are identical to those of the trapping SH instruction.
RULE 30: In a transformed SH instruction, the bits that normally encode rs1 (bits 19:15) are replaced by an Addr. Offset field that contains the positive difference between the faulting virtual address (as written to mtval or stval) and the original virtual address.
RULE 31: The Addr. Offset field of a transformed SH instruction is non-zero only when the original memory access was misaligned; otherwise the field is zero.
RULE 32: In a transformed SH instruction, all immediate offset fields (bits 31:25, 24:20, and 11:7) are set to zero.
RULE 33: Because SH is not a compressed instruction, the two low bits (bits 1:0) of its transformed encoding are binary 11.
RULE 34: During normal execution of an SH instruction without exception, mtinst or htinst is not written.
RULE 35: For exceptions other than Store/AMO address-misaligned, access-fault, or page-fault (e.g., illegal instruction or breakpoint) occurring on an SH instruction, mtinst or htinst may contain zero or any other allowed value but is not required to contain a transformed SH encoding.
RULE 36: SH is an explicit memory access and therefore its effective address is subject to pointer masking.
RULE 37: When SH is executed, the pointer-masking settings of the effective privilege mode, as possibly altered by MPRV or SPVP, are applied to the address used by SH.
RULE 38: If MXR is set for the effective privilege mode during execution of SH, pointer masking is not applied to the address used by SH.
RULE 39: If SH accesses a half-word at a misaligned address, the operation proceeds as if each aligned sub-access were performed separately with pointer masking applied to each sub-access.
RULE 40: SH is not among the instructions (SFENCE.*, HFENCE.*, SINVAL.*, HINVAL.*) for which pointer masking is explicitly exempted, so pointer masking may apply to SH unless other exemptions apply.
RULE 41: Pointer masking is applied only to the address used by SH and does not apply to addresses generated by page-table walks, the IOMMU, devices, or DMA controllers.
RULE 42: Software reads or writes to CSRs are not subject to pointer masking, but the address subsequently used by SH after being obtained from a CSR is subject to pointer masking.
RULE 43: When hardware writes a transformed address to a CSR as part of handling an exception caused by SH, that written address is subject to pointer masking if the masking conditions for SH are met.

INSTRUCTION: SH1ADD
FORMAT: sh1add rd, rs1, rs2
RULE 1: SH1ADD takes three register operands named rd, rs1, and rs2.
RULE 2: SH1ADD computes the value of rs1 shifted left by one bit, adds the value of rs2, and writes the XLEN-wide result to rd.
RULE 3: Because SH1ADD has no w or .uw suffix, the shift-left-by-1 and add operation is performed on the full XLEN-wide values of rs1 and rs2 without truncating the result to 32 bits.
RULE 4: SH1ADD MUST shift the value in rs2 left by 1 bit, add the shifted value to the value in rs1, and write the resulting sum to rd.
RULE 5: SH1ADD rd, rs1, rs2 computes a shift-left-by-one of the value in rs1, inserting a zero into the least-significant bit and discarding the most-significant bit, producing an XLEN-bit intermediate result.
RULE 6: SH1ADD rd, rs1, rs2 adds the intermediate result of the left shift of rs1 to the value in rs2, performing XLEN-bit addition modulo 2^XLEN.
RULE 7: SH1ADD rd, rs1, rs2 writes the XLEN-bit sum to the destination register rd, replacing its previous architectural value.
RULE 8: SH1ADD sets X(rd) to the XLEN-bit sum of X(rs2) and the value obtained by zero-extending the low 32 bits of X(rs1) and shifting that value left by one bit.
RULE 9: SH1ADD treats the least-significant word of X(rs1) as an unsigned value by applying zero-extension before any further operation.
RULE 10: SH1ADD performs the addition defined in its operation modulo 2^XLEN, so any overflow is discarded.
RULE 11: SH1ADD updates only the destination register rd and leaves all other integer registers and architectural state unchanged.

INSTRUCTION: SH1ADD.UW
FORMAT: sh1add.uw rd, rs1, rs2
RULE 1: SH1ADD.UW extracts an unsigned 32-bit value from the least-significant 32 bits of the source register designated as the shifted operand.
RULE 2: SH1ADD.UW left-shifts the extracted unsigned 32-bit value by one bit.
RULE 3: SH1ADD.UW adds the one-bit-left-shifted unsigned 32-bit result to the full-XLEN value contained in the other source register.
RULE 4: SH1ADD.UW writes the full-XLEN sum produced by the addition into the destination register rd.
RULE 5: SH1ADD.UW performs the addition and result write as a full-XLEN operation, despite the shifted operand being derived from a 32-bit unsigned extraction.
RULE 6: An implementation that includes the Zba extension must provide the SH1ADD.UW instruction.
RULE 7: The SH1ADD.UW instruction is defined only for RV64 implementations; on RV32 the encoding is reserved and its architectural effect is undefined.
RULE 8: The SH1ADD.UW instruction reads the low 32 bits of source register rs1 as an unsigned integer.
RULE 9: The SH1ADD.UW instruction reads the low 32 bits of source register rs2 as an unsigned integer.
RULE 10: The SH1ADD.UW instruction left-shifts the unsigned value obtained from rs1 by one bit, inserting a zero into the least-significant bit.
RULE 11: The SH1ADD.UW instruction adds the shifted unsigned value from rs1 to the unsigned value obtained from rs2, producing an XLEN-bit result.
RULE 12: The SH1ADD.UW instruction writes the XLEN-bit result to destination register rd, overwriting its previous contents.
RULE 13: The SH1ADD.UW instruction performs the shift and addition modulo 2^XLEN, discarding any overflow beyond the XLEN-bit result.
RULE 14: SH1ADD.UW forms its second addend by extracting bits 31 down to 0 of the value in rs1, zero-extending that 32-bit word to XLEN bits, and then left-shifting the zero-extended value by one bit.
RULE 15: SH1ADD.UW adds the XLEN-wide value contained in rs2 to the second addend produced as specified for SH1ADD.UW, performing the addition modulo 2^XLEN.
RULE 16: SH1ADD.UW writes the XLEN-wide result of the addition to the destination register rd, leaving all other architectural state unchanged.

INSTRUCTION: SH2ADD
FORMAT: sh2add rd, rs1, rs2
RULE 1: SH2ADD computes the value of rd by adding the result of a logical left shift of rs1 by two bits to the value in rs2.
RULE 2: SH2ADD shifts rs1 left by two bits using a logical shift that inserts zeros into the least-significant bits and discards any bits shifted out of the XLEN width.
RULE 3: SH2ADD adds the shifted rs1 value to rs2 using standard XLEN-wide integer addition that wraps around on overflow.
RULE 4: SH2ADD performs a left shift of the second source register (rs2) by 2 bits, adds the shifted value to the first source register (rs1), and writes the resulting sum to the destination register (rd).
RULE 5: SH2ADD is defined for RV32 implementations.
RULE 6: SH2ADD is defined for RV64 implementations.
RULE 7: The acceleration provided by SH2ADD works by adding a shifted index to a base address.
RULE 8: SH2ADD rd, rs1, rs2 must compute rd as the XLEN-bit result of adding rs2 to the value obtained by shifting rs1 left by two bit positions, discarding any overflow beyond XLEN bits.
RULE 9: SH2ADD rd, rs1, rs2 performs a logical left shift of rs1 by two bits, inserting zeros into the least-significant positions.
RULE 10: SH2ADD rd, rs1, rs2 adds the shifted rs1 value to rs2 using two's-complement addition modulo 2^XLEN and writes the lower XLEN bits to rd.
RULE 11: SH2ADD adds the first addend (the value of rs2) to the second addend (the zero-extended, left-shifted low word of rs1) using XLEN-bit modular arithmetic and writes the result to destination register rd.
RULE 12: SH2ADD writes the computed sum to rd and does not modify any architectural state other than the destination register.

INSTRUCTION: SH2ADD.UW
FORMAT: sh2add.uw rd, rs1, rs2
RULE 1: SH2ADD.UW extracts an unsigned 32-bit value from the least-significant 32 bits of the register specified as rs2 and zero-extends that value to the processor's XLEN.
RULE 2: SH2ADD.UW left-shifts the zero-extended 32-bit value from rs2 by exactly 2 bits, producing an XLEN-wide result.
RULE 3: SH2ADD.UW adds the 2-bit-left-shifted XLEN-wide value to the XLEN-wide value contained in the register specified as rs1, performing the addition modulo 2^XLEN.
RULE 4: SH2ADD.UW writes the XLEN-wide result of the addition to the destination register rd.
RULE 5: SH2ADD.UW is defined only for implementations with XLEN = 64; the encoding is reserved for implementations where XLEN is not 64.
RULE 6: SH2ADD.UW is defined only for RV64 implementations and is not defined for RV32 implementations.
RULE 7: For SH2ADD.UW the value in source register rs2 is interpreted as an unsigned 32-bit word.
RULE 8: For SH2ADD.UW the unsigned word value from rs2 is left-shifted by 2 bits.
RULE 9: For SH2ADD.UW the shifted result is added to the value contained in source register rs1.
RULE 10: For SH2ADD.UW the sum produced by adding the shifted rs2 value to rs1 is written to destination register rd.
RULE 11: SH2ADD.UW does not define any architecturally specified exceptions or permission checks beyond the illegal-instruction exception for an undefined encoding.
RULE 12: SH2ADD.UW MUST read the XLEN-bit value of register rs2 and use it as the first addend (base) for the addition.
RULE 13: SH2ADD.UW MUST extract bits 31 down to 0 of register rs1, zero-extend the extracted word to XLEN bits, and treat the result as the second addend (index) before shifting.
RULE 14: SH2ADD.UW MUST left-shift the zero-extended index by two bit positions.
RULE 15: SH2ADD.UW MUST add the shifted index to the base value using XLEN-wide modular arithmetic and write the resulting XLEN-bit value to destination register rd.
RULE 16: SH2ADD.UW MUST not modify any architectural state other than the destination register rd.

INSTRUCTION: SH3ADD
FORMAT: sh3add rd, rs1, rs2
RULE 1: SH3ADD rd, rs1, rs2 must compute rd = rs1 + (rs2 << 3) using XLEN-wide integer arithmetic appropriate to the current ISA (32-bit for RV32 and 64-bit for RV64).
RULE 2: SH3ADD is defined for RV32 implementations.
RULE 3: SH3ADD is defined for RV64 implementations.
RULE 4: SH3ADD shifts the value in source register rs2 left by three bits as part of its operation.
RULE 5: SH3ADD adds the three-bit-left-shifted value of rs2 to the value in source register rs1 and writes the sum to destination register rd.
RULE 6: SH3ADD rd, rs1, rs2 computes the sum of rs2 and the value of rs1 shifted left by 3 bits, and writes the XLEN-bit result to register rd.
RULE 7: SH3ADD performs a logical left shift of rs1 by 3 positions, inserting zeros into the vacated low-order bits and discarding any bits shifted out beyond XLEN.
RULE 8: SH3ADD adds the shifted rs1 value to rs2 using XLEN-bit modular arithmetic, so any overflow wraps around and only the low XLEN bits are written to rd.
RULE 9: SH3ADD reads the architectural registers specified by rs1 and rs2 and writes only the architectural register specified by rd; no other architectural registers are read or written.
RULE 10: SH3ADD does not perform any memory accesses, does not read or write any CSR, and does not modify the program counter except for the normal sequential advancement.
RULE 11: When the Zba extension is supported, execution of SH3ADD must complete without raising an exception unless a preceding trap or interrupt occurs.
RULE 12: SH3ADD rd, rs1, rs2 performs an XLEN-wide addition of the value in rs2 and the unsigned value obtained by extracting the low word of rs1 and shifting it left three bits.
RULE 13: SH3ADD uses the value in register rs2 as the first XLEN-sized addend.
RULE 14: SH3ADD forms the second addend by zero-extending the least-significant 32-bit word of rs1 and then shifting that value left by three positions.
RULE 15: SH3ADD writes the XLEN-wide sum of the two addends to register rd, with the result reduced modulo 2^XLEN.
RULE 16: SH3ADD does not raise any architectural exception for the addition operation, even if the sum overflows XLEN bits.
RULE 17: SH3ADD permits rs1, rs2, and rd to refer to the same architectural register, and the defined addition semantics are applied without additional restrictions.
RULE 18: An implementation that implements the Zba extension shall provide the SH3ADD instruction with the defined behavior.

INSTRUCTION: SH3ADD.UW
FORMAT: sh3add.uw rd, rs1, rs2
RULE 1: SH3ADD.UW is defined only for RV64 implementations and its encoding is reserved (behavior not defined) on RV32 implementations.
RULE 2: For SH3ADD.UW the operand taken from rs1 is an unsigned 32-bit value extracted from the least-significant 32 bits of rs1.
RULE 3: For SH3ADD.UW the operand taken from rs2 is an unsigned 32-bit value extracted from the least-significant 32 bits of rs2.
RULE 4: SH3ADD.UW performs its arithmetic on the full XLEN width (64 bits on RV64) using the zero-extended 32-bit unsigned values from rs1 and rs2.
RULE 5: SH3ADD.UW computes the value (zero_extend(rs1[31:0]) << 3) + zero_extend(rs2[31:0]) and writes the 64-bit result to rd.
RULE 6: The architecture does not define any additional exception or permission conditions for SH3ADD.UW beyond the general illegal-instruction handling.
RULE 7: SH3ADD.UW is defined only for RV64 processors and its encoding is reserved on RV32 implementations.
RULE 8: SH3ADD.UW reads the value in integer register rs1 as a 64-bit base operand.
RULE 9: SH3ADD.UW reads the lower 32 bits of integer register rs2, zero-extends them to 64 bits, and treats that as an unsigned word operand.
RULE 10: SH3ADD.UW left-shifts the zero-extended unsigned word operand by three bits using an unsigned logical shift, discarding any bits that overflow beyond the XLEN width.
RULE 11: SH3ADD.UW adds the shifted value to the 64-bit base operand from rs1 using 64-bit integer addition modulo 2^64.
RULE 12: SH3ADD.UW writes the 64-bit result of the addition to integer register rd.
RULE 13: SH3ADD.UW effectively computes rd = rs1 + ((rs2[31:0] << 3)), which is equivalent to adding rs1 to eight times the unsigned word index in rs2.
RULE 14: SH3ADD.UW MUST form the second addend by extracting bits 31 down to 0 of the XLEN-wide value in rs1, zero-extending that 32-bit field to XLEN bits, and then shifting the zero-extended result left by three bit positions.
RULE 15: SH3ADD.UW MUST take the first addend as the XLEN-wide architectural value contained in rs2.
RULE 16: SH3ADD.UW MUST add the first addend to the second addend using XLEN-wide modular arithmetic and write the XLEN-bit sum to the destination register rd.
RULE 17: SH3ADD.UW MUST not modify any architectural state other than updating rd with the addition result.

INSTRUCTION: SHA256SIG0
FORMAT: sha256sig0 rd, rs1
RULE 1: Implementations that include the Zknh extension shall provide the SHA256SIG0 instruction for both RV32 and RV64 base ISA variants.
RULE 2: Execution of the SHA256SIG0 instruction shall produce a result equal to the SHA-2-256 Sigma0 transformation applied to the instruction's input word, as specified in the NIST SHA-2 specification (2015).
RULE 3: SHA256SIG0 implements the Sigma0 transformation function defined in the SHA-2-256 hash algorithm specification (NIST, 2015).
RULE 4: SHA256SIG0 is available on implementations that support either the RV32 or the RV64 base ISA.
RULE 5: On an RV32 implementation, SHA256SIG0 operates on the full XLEN-wide source register value.
RULE 6: On an RV64 implementation, SHA256SIG0 operates on the low 32 bits of the source register and sign-extends the 32-bit result to XLEN bits.
RULE 7: The functional result of SHA256SIG0 is defined as the bitwise XOR of a 7-bit rotate-right of the low 32-bit input, an 18-bit rotate-right of the same input, and the input shifted right logically by 3 bits.
RULE 8: The destination register written by SHA256SIG0 receives the 32-bit result sign-extended to the processor's XLEN.
RULE 9: SHA256SIG0 MUST be implemented for RV32 implementations that include the RVK extension.
RULE 10: SHA256SIG0 MUST be implemented for RV64 implementations that include the RVK extension.

INSTRUCTION: SHA256SIG1
FORMAT: sha256sig1 rd, rs1
RULE 1: SHA256SIG1 computes the SHA2-256 Sigma1 function on its input operands.
RULE 2: When SHA256SIG1 is executed on an RV32 implementation, the instruction operates on the full 32-bit value contained in the source register rs1.
RULE 3: When SHA256SIG1 is executed on an RV64 implementation, the instruction extracts the low 32 bits of the source register rs1, applies the Sigma1 transformation to those bits, and then sign-extends the 32-bit result to 64 bits before writing it to the destination register rd.
RULE 4: SHA256SIG1 computes the Sigma1 function by rotating the 32-bit input left-rotated by 17 bits, rotating the same input left-rotated by 19 bits, and logically right-shifting the input by 10 bits, then XOR-ing the three intermediate values to produce a 32-bit result.
RULE 5: The 32-bit result produced by the Sigma1 computation in SHA256SIG1 is sign-extended to the XLEN width of the executing architecture and written to the destination register rd.
RULE 6: Upon successful execution, SHA256SIG1 updates rd with the sign-extended result and then retires normally without raising an exception.

INSTRUCTION: SHA256SUM0
FORMAT: sha256sum0 rd, rs1
RULE 1: In an RV32 implementation, the SHA256SUM0 instruction extracts bits 31 down to 0 of the source register rs1 and uses that 32-bit value as the input to the Sum0 transformation.
RULE 2: In an RV64 implementation, the SHA256SUM0 instruction extracts the low 32 bits of the source register rs1 and uses that 32-bit value as the input to the Sum0 transformation, ignoring the upper 32 bits.
RULE 3: The SHA256SUM0 instruction computes the result as the bitwise XOR of the 32-bit input rotated right by 2 bits, rotated right by 13 bits, and rotated right by 22 bits.
RULE 4: The SHA256SUM0 instruction writes the sign-extended 32-bit result into the destination register rd, filling the upper XLEN-32 bits with copies of the result's most-significant bit.
RULE 5: When executed with a valid destination register, the SHA256SUM0 instruction retires successfully without causing an exception.
RULE 6: An implementation that includes the RVK (Scalar Cryptography) extension must provide the SHA256SUM0 instruction.
RULE 7: The SHA256SUM0 instruction is a K-specific instruction.
RULE 8: The SHA256SUM0 instruction must be defined for the RV32 base ISA width.
RULE 9: The SHA256SUM0 instruction must be defined for the RV64 base ISA width.

INSTRUCTION: SHA256SUM1
FORMAT: sha256sum1 rd, rs1
RULE 1: An implementation that incorporates the Zknh extension MUST provide the SHA256SUM1 instruction.
RULE 2: The SHA256SUM1 instruction is indicated as supported on RV32 implementations of the ISA.
RULE 3: The SHA256SUM1 instruction is indicated as supported on RV64 implementations of the ISA.
RULE 4: When executed on an RV32 implementation, SHA256SUM1 takes the low 32 bits of source register rs1 as its input value.
RULE 5: When executed on an RV64 implementation, SHA256SUM1 takes the low 32 bits of source register rs1 as its input value and sign-extends the 32-bit result to XLEN bits before writing to rd.
RULE 6: SHA256SUM1 computes a 32-bit result equal to the bitwise XOR of ror32(inb, 6), ror32(inb, 11) and ror32(inb, 25), where inb is the 32-bit input taken from rs1.
RULE 7: SHA256SUM1 writes the sign-extended 32-bit result to the destination register rd.
RULE 8: SHA256SUM1 completes with normal retirement (RETIRE_SUCCESS) and does not raise an exception for any defined operand values.
RULE 9: SHA256SUM1 MUST be defined for RV32 implementations.
RULE 10: SHA256SUM1 MUST be defined for RV64 implementations.
RULE 11: SHA256SUM1 is a K-specific instruction and therefore MUST be present when the K extension is implemented.
RULE 12: SHA256SUM1 MUST compute the SHA-256 Sum1 function on its source operands and write the result to the destination register as defined by the SHA-2 specification.

INSTRUCTION: SHA512SIG0
FORMAT: sha512sig0 rd, rs1
RULE 1: When the SHA512SIG0 instruction is executed on an RV64 implementation, it performs the SHA-2-512 Sigma0 transformation on the 64-bit source operand and writes the 64-bit result to the destination register.
RULE 2: The SHA512SIG0 instruction is not defined for RV32 implementations; its encoding is reserved on RV32 and its execution behavior is not specified.
RULE 3: SHA512SIG0 implements the Sigma0 transformation function as specified in the SHA-2-512 hash algorithm defined by NIST in 2015.
RULE 4: SHA512SIG0 is defined for the RV64 base ISA and is not defined for other integer register widths.
RULE 5: SHA512SIG0 writes to the destination register the 64-bit value equal to the bitwise XOR of the source register rotated right by 1 bit, the source register rotated right by 8 bits, and the source register logically shifted right by 7 bits.
RULE 6: SHA512SIG0 causes a successful retirement of the instruction after updating the destination register as defined.
RULE 7: SHA512SIG0 is defined only for RV64 implementations; on RV32 implementations the encoding of SHA512SIG0 is reserved.
RULE 8: SHA512SIG0 performs the SHA-2-512 Sigma0 transformation on a 64-bit operand as specified by the RVK extension.
RULE 9: SHA512SIG0 must be recognized and executed only when the RVK extension is implemented on an RV64 core.

INSTRUCTION: SHA512SIG0H
FORMAT: sha512sig0h rd, rs1, rs2
RULE 1: RV32 implementations must define the SHA512SIG0H instruction.
RULE 2: RV64 implementations must not define the SHA512SIG0H instruction.
RULE 3: SHA512SIG0H performs the high 32-bit portion of the SHA-2-512 Sigma0 operation on RV32 implementations.
RULE 4: SHA512SIG0H is defined only for RV32 implementations and is not defined for implementations with an XLEN other than 32.
RULE 5: SHA512SIG0H computes the high 32-bit half of the SHA-512 Sigma0 transformation using the low-half operand in rs1 and the high-half operand in rs2.
RULE 6: For SHA512SIG0H the 64-bit input value is formed by concatenating the contents of rs2 as the high 32 bits and rs1 as the low 32 bits, and the instruction produces the high-half result in rd.
RULE 7: SHA512SIG0H writes to rd the sign-extended value of ((X(rs1) >> 1) XOR (X(rs1) >> 7) XOR (X(rs1) >> 8) XOR (X(rs2) << 31) XOR (X(rs2) << 24)).
RULE 8: SHA512SIG0H completes execution with RETIRE_SUCCESS and does not raise an exception under normal operation.
RULE 9: SHA512SIG0H computes the SHA-2-512 Sigma0 high function on a 32-bit source operand and writes the 32-bit result to the destination register.

INSTRUCTION: SHA512SIG0L
FORMAT: sha512sig0l rd, rs1, rs2
RULE 1: The SHA512SIG0L instruction is defined only for implementations of the RV32 base ISA that include the Zknh extension.
RULE 2: The SHA512SIG0L instruction is not defined for any RV64 implementation, regardless of extension support.
RULE 3: Execution of the SHA512SIG0L instruction computes the low part of the SHA-512 Σ0 function as specified for the RV32 architecture.
RULE 4: SHA512SIG0L MUST be implemented only on RV32 implementations.
RULE 5: SHA512SIG0L computes the low 32-bit half of the 64-bit SHA-512 σ0 function using the low 32 bits of registers rs1 and rs2 as inputs and writes the result to the low 32 bits of rd.
RULE 6: SHA512SIG0L writes to rd the sign-extended value of the 32-bit expression ((X(rs1) >> 1) ^ (X(rs1) >> 7) ^ (X(rs1) >> 8) ^ (X(rs2) << 31) ^ (X(rs2) << 25) ^ (X(rs2) << 24)).
RULE 7: SHA512SIG0L MUST retire successfully after performing its defined computation.
RULE 8: Implementations that support the RVK (Scalar Cryptography) extension on RV32 must provide the SHA512SIG0L instruction.
RULE 9: The SHA512SIG0L instruction computes the low 32-bit result of the SHA-512 Sigma0 function as defined by the SHA-2-512 algorithm.

INSTRUCTION: SHA512SIG1
FORMAT: sha512sig1 rd, rs1
RULE 1: SHA512SIG1 MUST be defined only for implementations that support the RV64 integer base ISA.
RULE 2: SHA512SIG1 MUST NOT be defined for implementations that support only the RV32 integer base ISA.
RULE 3: SHA512SIG1L takes the 32-bit low word in rs1 and the 32-bit high word in rs2 as the 64-bit input for the Sigma1 function and writes the sign-extended low-word result to rd.
RULE 4: The result written by SHA512SIG1L is the sign-extended value of ((rs1 << 3) XOR (rs1 >> 6) XOR (rs1 >> 19) XOR (rs2 >> 29) XOR (rs2 << 26) XOR (rs2 << 13)).
RULE 5: SHA512SIG1L completes without raising any architectural exception and retires successfully.
RULE 6: The high-order 32-bit word of the 64-bit Sigma1 transform must be obtained by executing the SHA512SIG1H instruction with the same source registers.
RULE 7: Executing SHA512SIG1L followed by SHA512SIG1H with identical source registers computes the full 64-bit Sigma1 transform of the concatenated input (rs2‖rs1).
RULE 8: SHA512SIG1 computes the Sigma1 transformation defined by the SHA-512 algorithm, setting X(rd) to the bitwise XOR of ror64(X(rs1),19), ror64(X(rs1),61), and X(rs1) right-shifted by 6 bits.
RULE 9: SHA512SIG1 always retires successfully and does not raise any architectural exception.

INSTRUCTION: SHA512SIG1H
FORMAT: sha512sig1h rd, rs1, rs2
RULE 1: An RV32 implementation that implements the Zknh extension must provide the SHA512SIG1H instruction.
RULE 2: An RV64 implementation must not provide the SHA512SIG1H instruction, even if the Zknh extension is implemented.
RULE 3: SHA512SIG1H is defined only for RV32 implementations and is not defined for RV64.
RULE 4: SHA512SIG1H must compute the high 32-bit half of the SHA-512 Sigma1 transformation using the specified bitwise operations on the values in rs1 and rs2.
RULE 5: SHA512SIG1H writes the sign-extended 32-bit result of the transformation to the destination register rd.
RULE 6: SHA512SIG1H computes rd = sign-extend((rs1 << 3) XOR (rs1 >> 6) XOR (rs1 >> 19) XOR (rs2 >> 29) XOR (rs2 << 13)).
RULE 7: In SHA512SIG1H the source register rs1 supplies the low 32-bit half and rs2 supplies the high 32-bit half of the 64-bit input value to the Sigma1 transformation.
RULE 8: In SHA512SIG1H the destination register rd receives the high 32-bit half of the 64-bit Sigma1 output.

INSTRUCTION: SHA512SIG1L
FORMAT: sha512sig1l rd, rs1, rs2
RULE 1: The SHA512SIG1L instruction performs the SHA2-512 Sigma1 low operation on RV32 implementations.
RULE 2: An RV32 implementation that includes the Zknh extension MUST provide the SHA512SIG1L instruction.
RULE 3: An RV64 implementation that includes the Zknh extension MUST NOT provide the SHA512SIG1L instruction.
RULE 4: SHA512SIG1L is defined only for RV32 implementations and must not be provided on RV64 or RV128.
RULE 5: SHA512SIG1L requires the Zknh, Zkn, and Zk extensions for RV32, each at minimum version v1.0.0 and ratified.
RULE 6: SHA512SIG1L reads the 64-bit architectural values associated with registers rs1 and rs2, where each 64-bit value is represented by a pair of 32-bit registers, and computes the expression (X(rs1) << 3) XOR (X(rs1) >> 6) XOR (X(rs1) >> 19) XOR (X(rs2) >> 29) XOR (X(rs2) << 26) XOR (X(rs2) << 13).
RULE 7: SHA512SIG1L writes the low 32 bits of the computed expression, sign-extended to 64 bits, into the destination register rd, thereby updating rd with the low half of the Sigma1 transformation result.
RULE 8: SHA512SIG1L must retire successfully without raising any architectural exception when executed with valid operand registers.
RULE 9: An RV32 implementation that includes the K extension MUST implement the SHA512SIG1L instruction.
RULE 10: SHA512SIG1L is not defined for RV64 processors, even if the K extension is present.

INSTRUCTION: SHA512SUM0
FORMAT: sha512sum0 rd, rs1
RULE 1: SHA512SUM0 is defined only for the RV64 base ISA and is not defined for RV32 or other base variants.
RULE 2: SHA512SUM0 computes X(rd) = ror64(X(rs1),28) ^ ror64(X(rs1),34) ^ ror64(X(rs1),39) and writes this 64-bit result to the destination register.
RULE 3: An implementation that includes the RVK extension MUST implement the SHA512SUM0 instruction.

INSTRUCTION: SHA512SUM0R
FORMAT: sha512sum0r rd, rs1, rs2
RULE 1: SHA512SUMR instruction is defined only for RV32 implementations and must not be defined for RV64 implementations.
RULE 2: For RV64 implementations, the encoding of SHA512SUMR is reserved and its architectural behavior is not defined by the specification.
RULE 3: SHA512SUMR performs the SHA-2-512 Σ0 (Sum0) operation on 32-bit data as defined by the NIST 2015 SHA-2 specification.
RULE 4: SHA512SUM0R is defined only for RV32 implementations and its behavior is reserved on implementations with other XLENs.
RULE 5: SHA512SUM0R computes the SHA-2-512 Sum0 transformation on a 64-bit input value formed from the two 32-bit source registers rs1 and rs2.
RULE 6: SHA512SUM0R treats each source register as a zero-extended 64-bit operand when performing the shift and XOR operations specified for the Sum0 function.
RULE 7: SHA512SUM0R produces a 64-bit result, discards the upper 32 bits, and writes the low 32 bits of the result to the destination register rd.
RULE 8: SHA512SUM0R executes the computation rd = EXTS(((X(rs1) << 25) ^ (X(rs1) << 30) ^ (X(rs1) >> 28) ^ (X(rs2) >> 7) ^ (X(rs2) >> 2) ^ (X(rs2) << 4))) where X(reg) denotes the zero-extended 64-bit value of the register.
RULE 9: In an RV32 implementation the SHA512SUM0R instruction is defined and its execution follows the architectural specification.
RULE 10: In an RV64 implementation the SHA512SUM0R instruction is not defined, and its behavior is reserved.

INSTRUCTION: SHA512SUM1
FORMAT: sha512sum1 rd, rs1
RULE 1: SHA512SUM1 performs the SHA-2 512-bit Sum1 operation as specified in the SHA-2 standard.
RULE 2: SHA512SUM1 must compute the SHA-512 Sum1 transformation by writing to the destination register the bitwise XOR of three 64-bit right rotations of the source register by 14, 18, and 41 bits.
RULE 3: SHA512SUM1 must retire successfully without raising any architectural exception when executed with a legal encoding.
RULE 4: SHA512SUM1 is defined only for RV64 implementations and is not defined for RV32 implementations.
RULE 5: SHA512SUM1 implements the SHA-512 Σ1 (Sum1) function as specified by the SHA-2 algorithm.

INSTRUCTION: SHA512SUM1R
FORMAT: sha512sum1r rd, rs1, rs2
RULE 1: An implementation that includes the Zknh extension for the RV32 ISA must provide the SHA512SUM1R instruction.
RULE 2: An RV64 implementation must not define the SHA512SUM1R instruction.
RULE 3: SHA512SUM1R implements the Sum1 transformation as defined in the SHA-2-512 hash function (NIST 2015).
RULE 4: SHA512SUM1R is defined only for RV32 implementations and its encoding is reserved on RV64.
RULE 5: SHA512SUM1R interprets the 64-bit input as the concatenation of the 32-bit contents of rs1 (lower half) and rs2 (upper half).
RULE 6: The architectural effect of SHA512SUM1R is that rd receives the sign-extended value of ((X(rs1) << 23) XOR (X(rs1) >> 14) XOR (X(rs1) >> 18) XOR (X(rs2) >> 9) XOR (X(rs2) << 18) XOR (X(rs2) << 14)).
RULE 7: SHA512SUM1R completes execution and retires successfully without raising an exception when its encoding is defined.
RULE 8: SHA512SUM1R is defined only for RV32 ISA width and has no definition for RV64.
RULE 9: SHA512SUM1R performs the SHA-2 512 Sum1 operation as its architectural effect.

INSTRUCTION: SINVAL.VMA
FORMAT: sinval.vma rs1, rs2
RULE 1: If the TVM bit in mstatus is 1 and the processor is executing in S-mode, an attempt to execute the SINVAL.VMA instruction MUST raise an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 2: If the TVM bit in mstatus is 0, execution of the SINVAL.VMA instruction while in S-mode is permitted and must not raise an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 3: SINVAL.VMA invalidates any address-translation cache entries that an SFENCE.VMA instruction with the same rs1 and rs2 values would invalidate.
RULE 4: SINVAL.VMA instructions are ordered only with respect to SFENCE.VMA, SFENCE.W.INVAL, and SFENCE.INVAL.IR instructions.
RULE 5: A SFENCE.W.INVAL instruction executed by a hart orders all stores that are already visible to that hart before any later SINVAL.VMA instructions executed by the same hart.
RULE 6: A SFENCE.INVAL.IR instruction executed by a hart orders all earlier SINVAL.VMA instructions executed by that hart before any subsequent implicit references by that hart to memory-management data structures.
RULE 7: When a single hart executes, in order, the sequence SFENCE.W.INVAL followed by SINVAL.VMA followed by SFENCE.INVAL.IR, the combined effect is equivalent to a SFENCE.VMA instruction with the same rs1 and rs2 values, where reads and writes prior to the SFENCE.W.INVAL correspond to those prior to the SFENCE.VMA and reads and writes following the SFENCE.INVAL.IR correspond to those after the SFENCE.VMA.
RULE 8: Executing SINVAL.VMA in U-mode raises an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 9: Executing SINVAL.VMA in S-mode or HS-mode while mstatus.TVM=1 raises an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 10: Executing SINVAL.VMA in VS-mode while hstatus.VTVM=1 raises a virtual-instruction exception.
RULE 11: Executing SINVAL.VMA in VU-mode raises a virtual-instruction exception.
RULE 12: Executing SINVAL.VMA in any other case does not raise an exception and the instruction executes with the same permissions as SFENCE.VMA.
RULE 13: Executing SINVAL.VMA synchronizes the hart's address-translation caches when a memory-resident page-translation entry (PTE) is changed from Invalid to Valid.
RULE 14: Executing SINVAL.VMA synchronizes the hart's address-translation caches when a memory-resident PTE is changed from Valid to Invalid.
RULE 15: When the Svvptc extension is implemented, executing SINVAL.VMA after a memory-resident PTE change from Invalid to Valid has no required effect on address-translation cache visibility.
RULE 16: When VTVM = 1, an attempt in VS-mode to execute SINVAL.VMA raises a virtual-instruction exception.
RULE 17: When VTVM = 1, the condition that raises a virtual-instruction exception for SINVAL.VMA also raises a virtual-instruction exception for SFENCE.VMA.
RULE 18: When an SINVAL.VMA instruction is executed in VS-mode while hstatus.VTVM=1, a virtual-instruction exception MUST be raised.
RULE 19: The virtual-instruction exception raised by executing SINVAL.VMA with hstatus.VTVM=1 MUST have cause code 22.
RULE 20: When a virtual-instruction exception is taken for an SINVAL.VMA instruction, the mtval or stval CSR MUST be written with the same value that would be written for an illegal-instruction trap.
RULE 21: SINVAL.VMA is an R-type instruction that reads the integer registers specified by rs1 and rs2 as its first and second operands.
RULE 22: SINVAL.VMA has its destination register field rd fixed to x0, so the instruction never writes an architectural register.
RULE 23: SINVAL.VMA is a privileged instruction and therefore must be executed only in a privileged privilege level.

INSTRUCTION: SLL
FORMAT: sll rd, rs1, rs2
RULE 1: The SLL instruction must read the registers rs1 and rs2 as source operands and must write the resulting value into register rd.
RULE 2: The SLL instruction must shift the value contained in rs1 left logically by the amount specified in the lower five bits of rs2, and must store the shifted result in rd.
RULE 3: The SLL instruction must be selected when the funct7 and funct3 fields of the OP opcode contain the encoding defined for SLL.
RULE 4: SLL MUST perform a logical left shift on the value in register rs1 by the shift amount held in register rs2.
RULE 5: In RV64I, SLL MUST consider only the low 6 bits of rs2 as the shift amount.
RULE 6: SLL is a logical shift left operation that is part of the full set of vector shift instructions.
RULE 7: SLL must take the data to be shifted from the vector register group specified by the vs2 operand.
RULE 8: SLL may take the shift-amount value from a vector register group specified by the vs1 operand.
RULE 9: SLL may take the shift-amount value from a scalar integer register specified by the rs1 operand.
RULE 10: SLL may take the shift-amount value from a zero-extended 5-bit immediate encoded in the instruction.
RULE 11: SLL must use only the low lg2(SEW) bits of the supplied shift-amount value to control the amount each element is shifted.
RULE 12: When the shift-amount source for SLL is an immediate, the immediate is zero-extended before the low lg2(SEW) bits are selected.

INSTRUCTION: SLLI
FORMAT: slli rd, rs1, shamt
RULE 1: For an SLLI instruction, the operand to be shifted is taken from register rs1.
RULE 2: For an SLLI instruction, the shift amount is taken from the lower five bits of the I-type immediate field (shamt[4:0]).
RULE 3: For an SLLI instruction, bits 11 through 5 of the immediate field (imm[11:5]) must be zero.
RULE 4: For an SLLI instruction, bit 30 of the encoding is not used and does not affect the operation.
RULE 5: An SLLI instruction performs a logical left shift of the value in rs1 by the amount specified in shamt[4:0].
RULE 6: An SLLI instruction inserts zeros into the vacated low-order bits of the result.
RULE 7: An SLLI instruction writes the shifted result to the destination register rd.
RULE 8: SLLI must read the value to be shifted from register rs1.
RULE 9: In RV64I, SLLI must use the lower 6 bits of the I-immediate field as the shift amount.
RULE 10: SLLI must perform a logical left shift, inserting zeros into the lower bits of the result.
RULE 11: SLLI performs a logical left shift of the value in the source register by the immediate amount, producing a shifted result.

INSTRUCTION: SLLI.UW
FORMAT: slli.uw rd, rs1, shamt
RULE 1: SLLI.UW extracts the least-significant 32 bits of the rs1 register, treats that field as an unsigned 32-bit value, zero-extends it to the processor's XLEN, shifts the XLEN-wide value left by the immediate amount, and writes the XLEN-wide result to rd.
RULE 2: SLLI.UW is defined only for RV64 implementations; its encoding is not listed for RV32, so execution on RV32 is reserved.
RULE 3: The SLLI.UW instruction shifts the unsigned word in rs1 left by the immediate imm and writes the resulting value to rd.
RULE 4: SLLI.UW computes its result by extracting bits 31 down to 0 of the source register rs1, zero-extending that 32-bit word to the XLEN width, shifting the extended value left by the immediate shamt, and writing the final XLEN-wide value to destination register rd.
RULE 5: SLLI.UW zero-extends the least-significant word of rs1, meaning that after extracting bits 31..0 all higher bits of the intermediate value are set to zero before any shift is performed.
RULE 6: SLLI.UW uses the encoded immediate field as the shift amount shamt for a logical left shift, and the shift amount is applied directly to the zero-extended word.
RULE 7: The architectural effect of SLLI.UW is defined by the equation X(rd) = (EXTZ(X(rs)[31..0]) << shamt).
RULE 8: SLLI.UW produces the same architectural result as an slli instruction applied to the zero-extended 32-bit value of rs1.

INSTRUCTION: SLLIW
FORMAT: slliw rd, rs1, shamt
RULE 1: SLLIW operates on the low 32 bits of the source register, performs a logical left shift by the amount specified in the lower 5 bits of the immediate, and sign-extends the 32-bit result to 64 bits before writing it to the destination register.
RULE 2: When the immediate field bit imm[5] of a SLLIW encoding is zero, the instruction is defined and executes according to its specified operation.
RULE 3: Encodings of SLLIW with imm[5] not equal to zero are no longer defined to raise illegal-instruction exceptions; they are instead reserved.

INSTRUCTION: SLLW
FORMAT: sllw rd, rs1, rs2
RULE 1: SLLW MUST be defined only for implementations that support the RV64I base ISA; in implementations that do not support RV64I the SLLW encoding is reserved and its behavior is not defined.
RULE 2: When an SLLW instruction is executed, the shift amount used by the operation MUST be the value of bits 4-0 of the second source register rs2.
RULE 3: When an SLLW instruction is executed, the low 32 bits of the first source register rs1 MUST be logically left-shifted by the shift amount, discarding any bits that are shifted out.
RULE 4: The 32-bit result of the logical left shift performed by SLLW MUST be sign-extended to 64 bits before being written to the destination register.
RULE 5: The destination register rd of an SLLW instruction MUST be written with the sign-extended 64-bit result, replacing its previous architectural value.

INSTRUCTION: SLT
FORMAT: slt rd, rs1, rs2
RULE 1: The destination register for SLT is rd.
RULE 2: SLT does not cause arithmetic exceptions.
RULE 3: SLT performs a signed comparison of the values in rs1 and rs2 and writes the integer 1 to rd if the signed value in rs1 is less than the signed value in rs2.
RULE 4: SLT writes the integer 0 to rd if the signed value in rs1 is not less than the signed value in rs2.
RULE 5: SLT places the 1-or-0 result in the low XLEN bits of rd and any overflow bits are ignored.

INSTRUCTION: SLTI
FORMAT: slti rd, rs1, imm12
RULE 1: SLTI writes its result to the destination register rd.
RULE 2: SLTI does not cause any arithmetic exception.
RULE 3: SLTI must write the integer 1 to register rd when the signed value in register rs1 is less than the sign-extended immediate.
RULE 4: SLTI must write the integer 0 to register rd when the signed value in register rs1 is not less than the sign-extended immediate.

INSTRUCTION: SLTIU
FORMAT: sltiu rd, rs1, imm12
RULE 1: SLTIU must sign-extend the immediate field to XLEN bits before performing the comparison.
RULE 2: SLTIU must treat the sign-extended immediate as an unsigned integer for the comparison.
RULE 3: SLTIU must treat the value read from register rs1 as an unsigned integer for the comparison.
RULE 4: SLTIU must write the integer 1 to destination register rd if the unsigned value in rs1 is strictly less than the unsigned sign-extended immediate; otherwise SLTIU must write the integer 0 to rd.
RULE 5: When the immediate encoded in SLTIU equals 1, SLTIU must set rd to 1 if and only if the unsigned value in rs1 is zero, and must set rd to 0 otherwise.

INSTRUCTION: SLTU
FORMAT: sltu rd, rs1, rs2
RULE 1: An SLTU instruction must read the architectural registers specified as rs1 and rs2 as its source operands.
RULE 2: An SLTU instruction must write the result of the comparison into the architectural register specified as rd.
RULE 3: An SLTU instruction must treat the values in rs1 and rs2 as unsigned integers and must set rd to the integer 1 if the unsigned value in rs1 is less than the unsigned value in rs2, otherwise it must set rd to the integer 0.
RULE 4: When the rs1 operand of an SLTU instruction is the zero register (x0), the SLTU instruction must set rd to the integer 1 if the unsigned value in rs2 is not equal to zero, otherwise it must set rd to the integer 0.
RULE 5: SLTU MUST operate on XLEN-bit values.
RULE 6: In RV64I, SLTU MUST compare its two source operands as 64-bit unsigned integers.
RULE 7: When 32-bit unsigned values are held sign-extended in 64-bit registers, SLTU performed as a 64-bit unsigned comparison MUST produce the same result as a comparison of the original 32-bit unsigned values.

INSTRUCTION: SM3P0
FORMAT: sm3p0 rd, rs1
RULE 1: SM3P0 is supported for the RV32 and RV64 base architectures.
RULE 2: When SM3P0 is executed, the lower 32 bits of the source register rs1 are used as the value r1.
RULE 3: SM3P0 computes the result as r1 XOR rol32(r1, 9) XOR rol32(r1, 17).
RULE 4: SM3P0 writes the sign-extended 32-bit result into the destination register rd.
RULE 5: SM3P0 completes execution with RETIRE_SUCCESS.
RULE 6: When executed, SM3P0 must perform the SM3 P0 transform on its source operand and produce the transformed value as defined by the SM3 algorithm.

INSTRUCTION: SM3P1
FORMAT: sm3p1 rd, rs1
RULE 1: SM3P1 must be implemented and available in any RV32 implementation that includes the Zksh extension.
RULE 2: SM3P1 must be implemented and available in any RV64 implementation that includes the Zksh extension.
RULE 3: Executing SM3P1 performs the SM3 P1 transform as specified by the SM3 algorithm.
RULE 4: An implementation that includes the Zksh or Zks extension must provide the SM3P1 instruction for both RV32 and RV64 base architectures.
RULE 5: When SM3P1 rd, rs1 is executed, the low 32 bits of the value in rs1 are extracted as a 32-bit operand r1.
RULE 6: The SM3P1 instruction computes a 32-bit result equal to r1 XOR rol32(r1,15) XOR rol32(r1,23).
RULE 7: The SM3P1 instruction writes the sign-extended 32-bit result to the destination register rd.
RULE 8: The SM3P1 instruction completes with a normal successful retirement (RETIRE_SUCCESS) when executed with valid registers.
RULE 9: Execution of the SM3P1 instruction must perform the SM3 P1 transform as defined by the SM3 algorithm.

INSTRUCTION: SM4ED
FORMAT: sm4ed rd, rs1, rs2, bs
RULE 1: The SM4ED instruction performs an SM4 block cipher encryption or decryption on the specified source data and writes the resulting ciphertext or plaintext to the destination register.
RULE 2: SM4ED computes a shift amount shamt equal to the value of the bs field multiplied by 8, implemented as the 5-bit value formed by concatenating bs with three zero bits.
RULE 3: SM4ED extracts an 8-bit value sb_in from bits 31..0 of rs2 by right-shifting those bits by shamt and taking the least-significant byte of the shifted result.
RULE 4: SM4ED applies the SM4 S-Box to sb_in and forms a 32-bit word x whose most-significant 24 bits are zero and whose least-significant byte is the S-Box output.
RULE 5: SM4ED computes a 32-bit intermediate y as the bitwise XOR of x, x shifted left by 8 bits, x shifted left by 2 bits, x shifted left by 18 bits, (x AND 0x0000003F) shifted left by 26 bits, and (x AND 0x000000C0) shifted left by 10 bits.
RULE 6: SM4ED rotates the 32-bit value y left by shamt bits (32-bit rotate) to produce the 32-bit value z.
RULE 7: SM4ED XORs z with bits 31..0 of rs1 to obtain a 32-bit result.
RULE 8: On RV32 implementations, SM4ED writes the 32-bit result to rd zero-extended to XLEN bits.
RULE 9: On RV64 implementations, SM4ED writes the 32-bit result to rd sign-extended to XLEN bits.
RULE 10: SM4ED retires successfully without raising an exception when executed under architecturally defined conditions.
RULE 11: The SM4ED instruction must be implemented in RV32 implementations.
RULE 12: The SM4ED instruction must be implemented in RV64 implementations.

INSTRUCTION: SM4KS
FORMAT: sm4ks rd, rs1, rs2, bs
RULE 1: An RV32 or RV64 implementation MUST provide support for the SM4KS instruction.
RULE 2: SM4KS extracts an 8-bit byte from the low 32 bits of source register rs2 by right-shifting those bits by (bs × 8) positions and selecting the least-significant 8 bits of the shifted value.
RULE 3: SM4KS applies the SM4 S-box to the extracted byte and forms a 32-bit intermediate value x whose low 8 bits are the S-box output and whose upper 24 bits are zero.
RULE 4: SM4KS computes a 32-bit value y by applying the SM4 linear layer to x according to the expression y = x ^ ((x & 0x00000007) << 29) ^ ((x & 0x000000FE) << 7) ^ ((x & 0x00000001) << 23) ^ ((x & 0x000000F8) << 13).
RULE 5: SM4KS rotates the 32-bit value y left by (bs × 8) bits to produce the 32-bit value z.
RULE 6: SM4KS XORs the rotated value z with the low 32 bits of source register rs1 to produce a 32-bit result.
RULE 7: On RV32 implementations SM4KS writes the 32-bit result directly into destination register rd.
RULE 8: On RV64 implementations SM4KS sign-extends the 32-bit result to XLEN bits before writing it into destination register rd.
RULE 9: The SM4KS instruction MUST be available in RV32 implementations of the ISA.
RULE 10: The SM4KS instruction MUST be available in RV64 implementations of the ISA.

INSTRUCTION: SRA
FORMAT: sra rd, rs1, rs2
