RULE 1: SRA produces as its result the arithmetic right-shift of the value in integer register rs1 by a shift amount equal to the low-order five bits of integer register rs2.
RULE 2: The shift amount used by SRA is derived from bits 4 through 0 of integer register rs2, ignoring all higher bits.
RULE 3: SRA must shift the value in register rs1 right by the shift amount contained in register rs2, performing an arithmetic right shift that replicates the sign bit into the vacated high-order bits.
RULE 4: In RV64I, SRA must consider only the low 6 bits of register rs2 when determining the shift amount.
RULE 5: The SRA vector instruction performs a sign-extending arithmetic right shift.
RULE 6: The shift amount for SRA may be supplied from a vector register group vs1 using the vector-vector form vsra.vv.
RULE 7: The shift amount for SRA may be supplied from a scalar integer register rs1 using the vector-scalar form vsra.vx.
RULE 8: The shift amount for SRA may be supplied from a zero-extended 5-bit immediate using the vector-immediate form vsra.vi.
RULE 9: Only the low lg2(SEW) bits of the shift-amount value are used by SRA to control the shift amount, where SEW is the selected element width.
RULE 10: The SRA narrowing right-shift extracts a smaller field from a wider operand and sign-extends the result.
RULE 11: The shift-amount value for an SRA narrowing right-shift may be supplied by a vector register group, a scalar x register, or a zero-extended 5-bit immediate.
RULE 12: The low lg2(2*SEW) bits of the shift-amount value are used to determine the shift count for an SRA narrowing right-shift (for example, the low 6 bits when SEW=64-bit to SEW=32-bit narrowing).

INSTRUCTION: SRAI
FORMAT: srai rd, rs1, shamt
RULE 1: In SRAI the operand to be shifted is read from register rs1.
RULE 2: In SRAI the shift amount is taken from the lower five bits of the immediate field (shamt[4:0]).
RULE 3: SRAI must produce a result equal to an arithmetic right shift of the rs1 value by shamt bits, with the original sign bit copied into all vacated upper bits.
RULE 4: SRAI reads the value to be shifted from register rs1 and writes the resulting value to register rd.
RULE 5: In an RV64I implementation, SRAI takes the shift amount from the low-order six bits (imm[5:0]) of the immediate field.
RULE 6: The arithmetic right-shift operation of SRAI is selected by bit 30 of the instruction encoding.
RULE 7: SRAI performs an arithmetic right shift, copying the original sign bit of the operand into all vacated upper bits of the result.
RULE 8: SRAI (SRAIW variant) operates on the low-order thirty-two bits of rs1 and applies an arithmetic right shift to those bits.
RULE 9: SRAI (SRAIW variant) sign-extends the thirty-two-bit shift result to sixty-four bits before writing it to rd.
RULE 10: SRAI (SRAIW variant) encodings with imm[5] not equal to zero are reserved and have undefined architectural behavior.
RULE 11: SRAI (SRAIW variant) is defined only for RV64I implementations and must not be provided in RV32I-only configurations.

INSTRUCTION: SRAIW
FORMAT: sraiw rd, rs1, shamt
RULE 1: SRAIW is defined only in RV64I implementations and operates on a 32-bit source operand.
RULE 2: SRAIW sign-extends the 32-bit result of the shift to produce a 64-bit destination register value.
RULE 3: The shift amount for SRAIW is taken from the lower six bits of the immediate field, and any encoding with imm[5] ≠ 0 is reserved.
RULE 4: SRAIW encodings with imm[5] ≠ 0 must not raise an illegal-instruction exception; their behavior is reserved and not defined by the architecture.
RULE 5: SRAIW performs an arithmetic right shift, copying the original sign bit of the 32-bit operand into all vacated upper bits of the 32-bit result before sign-extension.

INSTRUCTION: SRAW
FORMAT: sraw rd, rs1, rs2
RULE 1: SRAW uses the low 5 bits of rs2 (rs2[4:0]) as the shift amount for the arithmetic right shift.
RULE 2: SRAW applies an arithmetic right shift to the 32-bit value held in rs1 using the shift amount from rs2[4:0].
RULE 3: SRAW produces a 32-bit result from the shift and then sign-extends that result to 64 bits before writing it to the destination register rd.

INSTRUCTION: SRET
FORMAT: sret
RULE 1: Executing SRET sets the SIE bit to the value of the SPIE bit.
RULE 2: Executing SRET changes the current privilege mode to the mode encoded in the SPP field.
RULE 3: Executing SRET sets the SPIE bit to 1.
RULE 4: Executing SRET sets the SPP field to the least-privileged supported mode, which is U if U-mode is implemented, otherwise M.
RULE 5: If the mode restored by SRET is not M, then SRET clears the MPRV bit to 0.
RULE 6: When SRET is executed, the SPP field is a WARL field that may hold only S-mode and any implemented lower privilege mode.
RULE 7: If S-mode is not implemented, then the SPP field must be read-only zero for SRET.
RULE 8: When SRET is executed while the processor is in M-mode, the implementation MUST set the MDT bit to 0.
RULE 9: When the execution of SRET results in a new privilege mode of U, VS, or VU, the implementation MUST set sstatus.SDT to 0.
RULE 10: When the execution of SRET results in a new privilege mode of VU, the implementation MUST set vsstatus.SDT to 0.
RULE 11: If an SRET instruction changes the privilege mode to a mode that is less privileged than M, then the instruction must set MPRV to 0.
RULE 12: The TSR field is a WARL field that determines whether execution of the SRET instruction in S-mode is intercepted.
RULE 13: When TSR = 1, an attempt to execute SRET while executing in S-mode MUST raise an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 14: When TSR = 0, an attempt to execute SRET while executing in S-mode MUST NOT raise an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 15: If the implementation does not support S-mode, the TSR field that controls SRET interception is read-only zero.
RULE 16: SRET must be provided when supervisor mode is supported, and should raise an illegal-instruction exception when supervisor mode is not supported.
RULE 17: SRET should raise an illegal-instruction exception when the TSR bit in mstatus is 1.
RULE 18: Executing SRET in user mode (a privilege level lower than supervisor) must raise an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 19: SRET may be executed in privilege mode S or in privilege mode M.
RULE 20: When SRET is executed, it sets the program counter to the value stored in the sepc register after performing the privilege-stack manipulation.
RULE 21: The Smrnmi extension MUST NOT change the behavior of the SRET instruction.
RULE 22: The execution of SRET MUST NOT be affected by the value of the mnstatus.NMIE bit.
RULE 23: The execution of SRET MUST NOT modify the mnstatus.NMIE bit.
RULE 24: When an SRET instruction is executed, the current privilege level is set to user mode if the SPP bit is 0, and to supervisor mode if the SPP bit is 1.
RULE 25: When an SRET instruction is executed, the SPP bit is cleared to 0.
RULE 26: When an SRET instruction is executed, the SIE bit is loaded with the value of the SPIE bit.
RULE 27: When an SRET instruction is executed, the SPIE bit is set to 1.
RULE 28: SRET MUST set the SDT bit to 0.
RULE 29: The implementation must evaluate interrupt-trap conditions immediately after the execution of an SRET instruction.
RULE 30: Following an SRET instruction, any explicit write to a CSR on which interrupt-trap conditions depend (including sip, sie, or sstatus) must cause the interrupt-trap conditions to be evaluated immediately.
RULE 31: When IALIGN = 32, the implicit read of sepc performed by the SRET instruction must mask bit sepc[1] to zero, so that the read value appears with sepc[1] = 0.
RULE 32: If VTSR=1 and an attempt is made in VS-mode to execute SRET, the implementation MUST raise a virtual-instruction exception.
RULE 33: If SRET is executed while the V bit equals 0, the implementation MUST set V to the value of SPV.
RULE 34: In VU-mode, execution of the SRET instruction MUST cause a virtual-instruction exception when the mstatus.TW field is zero.
RULE 35: In VS-mode, execution of the SRET instruction MUST cause a virtual-instruction exception when the hstatus.VTSR field is one.
RULE 36: When a virtual-instruction exception is taken as a result of executing the SRET instruction, the trap-value register mtval (or stval if the trap is taken to S-mode) MUST be written with the same value that would be written for an illegal-instruction trap.
RULE 37: SRET MUST cause a return from a trap that was taken into HS-mode or VS-mode.
RULE 38: When SRET is executed while the current virtualization mode V field is 0 (i.e., in M-mode or HS-mode), SRET MUST set hstatus.SPV to 0.
RULE 39: When SRET is executed while the current virtualization mode V field is 0, SRET MUST set sstatus.SPP to 0.
RULE 40: When SRET is executed while the current virtualization mode V field is 0, SRET MUST copy the previous value of sstatus.SPIE into sstatus.SIE.
RULE 41: When SRET is executed while the current virtualization mode V field is 0, SRET MUST set sstatus.SPIE to 1.
RULE 42: When SRET is executed while the current virtualization mode V field is 0, SRET MUST restore the current privilege mode to the mode saved in sstatus.SPP prior to the trap.
RULE 43: When SRET is executed while the current virtualization mode V field is 0, SRET MUST set the program counter to the value contained in sepc.
RULE 44: If the Ssdbltrp extension is implemented and SRET is executed in HS-mode with the resulting privilege mode VU, SRET MUST set vsstatus.SDT to 0.
RULE 45: If the Ssdbltrp extension is implemented and SRET is executed while the current mode is VS-mode, SRET MUST set vsstatus.SDT to 0.
RULE 46: When SRET is executed and the privilege mode that becomes active after the return has its LPE bit equal to 1, the architectural register ELP is set to the value contained in the S-mode PELP field.
RULE 47: When SRET is executed and the privilege mode that becomes active after the return has its LPE bit equal to 0, the architectural register ELP is set to NO_LP_EXPECTED.
RULE 48: Execution of SRET sets the S-mode PELP field to NO_LP_EXPECTED.
RULE 49: SRET is recognized when bits [31:25] of the instruction are 0001000.
RULE 50: SRET is recognized when bits [24:20] of the instruction are 00010.
RULE 51: SRET is recognized when bits [19:15] of the instruction are 00000.
RULE 52: SRET is recognized when bits [14:12] of the instruction are 000.
RULE 53: SRET is recognized when bits [11:7] of the instruction are 00000.
RULE 54: SRET is recognized when bits [6:0] of the instruction are 1110011.
RULE 55: The complete 32-bit encoding of SRET is 0001000 00010 00000 000 00000 1110011.

INSTRUCTION: SRL
FORMAT: srl rd, rs1, rs2
RULE 1: SRL MUST be classified as an RV32I integer register-register R-type instruction.
RULE 2: SRL MUST read the contents of source registers rs1 and rs2 and write the computed result into destination register rd.
RULE 3: SRL MUST compute its result by logically shifting the 32-bit value in rs1 to the right by the shift amount specified by the low-order five bits of rs2.
RULE 4: SRL performs a logical right shift on the value in register rs1 by the shift amount specified in register rs2, inserting zeros into the vacated high-order bits.
RULE 5: In RV64I, SRL considers only the low 6 bits of register rs2 as the shift amount and ignores all higher bits.
RULE 6: SRL MUST perform a logical right shift that zero-extends the vacated bits.
RULE 7: The SRL form of a narrowing right-shift extracts a smaller field from a wider operand and zero-extends the extracted field to the destination width.
RULE 8: The SRL form may obtain its shift amount from a vector register group.
RULE 9: The SRL form may obtain its shift amount from a scalar x register.
RULE 10: The SRL form may obtain its shift amount from a zero-extended 5-bit immediate.
RULE 11: The SRL form uses only the low log2(2*SEW) bits of the provided shift-amount value when performing the shift.
RULE 12: When the scalar operand supplied to the SRL form is register x0, the operation halves the width of the integer value, producing a result that is half the original width.

INSTRUCTION: SRLI
FORMAT: srli rd, rs1, shamt
RULE 1: SRLI reads the 32-bit value from the source register rs1.
RULE 2: SRLI computes the result by logically shifting the value in rs1 right by shamt positions, inserting zeros into the vacated high-order bits.
RULE 3: SRLI writes the logical-right-shift result into the destination register rd.
RULE 4: SRLI does not modify any architectural state other than the destination register rd.
RULE 5: SRLI reads its source operand from register rs1.
RULE 6: In RV64I implementations SRLI takes the shift amount from the lower 6 bits of the immediate field.
RULE 7: SRLI performs a logical right shift, inserting zeros into the upper bits of the destination register.
RULE 8: SRLI, when executed immediately after an SLLI that shifts a 16-bit value left, produces a zero-extended 16-bit result.

INSTRUCTION: SRLIW
FORMAT: srliw rd, rs1, shamt
RULE 1: SRLIW operates on the 32-bit value in rs1, logically right-shifts it by the specified amount, and then sign-extends the 32-bit result to produce a 64-bit result written to rd.
RULE 2: The shift amount for SRLIW is taken from the lower six bits of the I-immediate field, with imm[5] representing the most-significant bit of the amount.
RULE 3: SRLIW performs a logical right shift, inserting zeros into the upper bits of the 32-bit intermediate result before sign-extension.

INSTRUCTION: SRLW
FORMAT: srlw rd, rs1, rs2
RULE 1: SRLW is defined only in the RV64I base ISA and is not defined for RV32I implementations.
RULE 2: SRLW logically right-shifts the 32-bit value taken from register rs1.
RULE 3: SRLW uses the low five bits of register rs2 (rs2[4:0]) as the shift amount.
RULE 4: SRLW sign-extends the 32-bit logical right-shift result to 64 bits before writing it to the destination register.
RULE 5: SRLW behaves analogously to the SRL instruction except that its operand and result are 32 bits and the result is sign-extended to 64 bits.

INSTRUCTION: SSAMOSWAP.D
FORMAT: ssamoswap.d rd, rs2, (rs1)
RULE 1: SSAMOSWAP.D atomically swaps a doubleword value with the contents of a shadow stack location.
RULE 2: If the current privilege mode is not Machine mode and the menvcfg.SSE field is zero, execution of SSAMOSWAP.D must raise an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 3: If the implementation does not provide S-mode, execution of SSAMOSWAP.D must raise an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 4: If the current privilege mode is User mode and the senvcfg.SSE field is zero, execution of SSAMOSWAP.D must raise an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 5: If the current privilege mode is Virtual Supervisor mode and the henvcfg.SSE field is zero, execution of SSAMOSWAP.D must raise a virtual-instruction exception.
RULE 6: If the current privilege mode is Virtual User mode and the senvcfg.SSE field is zero, execution of SSAMOSWAP.D must raise a virtual-instruction exception.
RULE 7: Otherwise, SSAMOSWAP.D atomically loads the 64-bit value from the memory address in rs1 into rd and stores the 64-bit value from rs2 to the same memory address.
RULE 8: The address held in rs1 for SSAMOSWAP.D must be naturally aligned to eight bytes, and if it is not, execution of SSAMOSWAP.D must raise the same exception that would be raised by the privilege-mode checks (illegal-instruction or virtual-instruction as appropriate).
RULE 9: If the aq bit of SSAMOSWAP.D is set to 1, the operation has acquire semantics.
RULE 10: If the rl bit of SSAMOSWAP.D is set to 1, the operation has release semantics.
RULE 11: When rd is x0, SSAMOSWAP.D still performs the atomic load and store but discards the loaded value.
RULE 12: If the SSE field in the menvcfg CSR is 0, execution of SSAMOSWAP.D must raise an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 13: When menvcfg.SSE is 1, execution of SSAMOSWAP.D in U-mode must raise an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 14: When menvcfg.SSE is 1, execution of SSAMOSWAP.D in VU-mode must raise a virtual-instruction exception.
RULE 15: If the menvcfg.SSE field is set to one, executing the SSAMOSWAP.D instruction must raise a virtual-instruction exception.
RULE 16: If satp.MODE (or vsatp.MODE when V=1) is Bare and the effective privilege mode is less than M, execution of SSAMOSWAP.D must raise a store/AMO access-fault exception.
RULE 17: If the effective privilege mode is M, execution of SSAMOSWAP.D must raise a store/AMO access-fault exception.
RULE 18: Writes to a memory region mapped as an SS page by any instruction other than SSAMOSWAP.D, SSPUSH, or C.SSPUSH must raise a store/AMO access-fault exception.
RULE 19: If SSAMOSWAP.D accesses a page whose pte.xwr field is neither 010b (shadow-stack) nor 001b (read-only), a store/AMO access-fault exception must be raised.
RULE 20: If SSAMOSWAP.D accesses a page whose pte.xwr field is 001b (read-only), a store/AMO page-fault exception must be raised.
RULE 21: If SSAMOSWAP.D accesses a page whose pte.xwr field is 011b, 111b, 101b, or 100b (read-write, read-write-execute, read-execute, or execute-only), a store/AMO access-fault exception must be raised.
RULE 22: When SSAMOSWAP.D accesses a read-only shadow-stack page, a store/AMO page-fault exception must be generated to support copy-on-write semantics.
RULE 23: If the memory location accessed by SSAMOSWAP.D is not idempotent, execution of SSAMOSWAP.D must raise a store/AMO access-fault exception.
RULE 24: If the PMA attributes for the address accessed by SSAMOSWAP.D do not provide AMOSwap level support, SSAMOSWAP.D must raise a store/AMO access-fault exception.
RULE 25: If PMP configuration does not grant read-write permission for the address accessed by SSAMOSWAP.D, execution of SSAMOSWAP.D must raise a store/AMO access-fault exception.
RULE 26: For SSAMOSWAP.D memory accesses, the U and SUM bits are enforced according to the standard privilege rules.
RULE 27: For SSAMOSWAP.D accesses to a shadow-stack page, the MXR bit does not affect read permission.
RULE 28: When G-stage page tables are active and the G-stage PTE for the address accessed by SSAMOSWAP.D lacks read-write permission, SSAMOSWAP.D must raise a store/AMO guest-page-fault exception.
RULE 29: When SSAMOSWAP.D triggers an exception, the reported exception cause code must be store/AMO access-fault (7), store/AMO page-fault (15), or store/AMO guest-page-fault (23) as appropriate, and the instruction type reported must be treated as a store/AMO operation.
RULE 30: If the Zicfiss extension is implemented, a trap caused by a store/AMO exception may be identified as a store/AMO/SS exception, indicating that SSAMOSWAP.D could be the trapping instruction.

INSTRUCTION: SSAMOSWAP.W
FORMAT: ssamoswap.w rd, rs2, (rs1)
RULE 1: SSAMOSWAP.W performs an atomic swap from a shadow stack location.
RULE 2: SSAMOSWAP.W must raise an illegal-instruction exception when the current privilege mode is not Machine mode and the menvcfg.SSE bit is zero.
RULE 3: SSAMOSWAP.W must raise an illegal-instruction exception when the current privilege mode is User mode and the senvcfg.SSE bit is zero.
RULE 4: SSAMOSWAP.W must raise a virtual-instruction exception when the current privilege mode is Virtual Supervisor mode and the henvcfg.SSE bit is zero.
RULE 5: SSAMOSWAP.W must raise a virtual-instruction exception when the current privilege mode is Virtual User mode and the senvcfg.SSE bit is zero.
RULE 6: SSAMOSWAP.W must atomically load a 32-bit word from the address in rs1, write the loaded value to rd, and store the 32-bit value from rs2 to the same address when all privilege-mode and configuration checks succeed on an RV32 implementation.
RULE 7: SSAMOSWAP.W must atomically load a 32-bit word from the address in rs1, sign-extend the loaded value to 64 bits, write the sign-extended value to rd, and store the lower 32 bits of rs2 to the same address when all privilege-mode and configuration checks succeed on an RV64 implementation.
RULE 8: SSAMOSWAP.W must require that the address in rs1 be naturally aligned to a four-byte boundary, and if it is not, the same exception behavior defined for AMO instructions applies.
RULE 9: SSAMOSWAP.W provides acquire semantics when its aq bit is set to 1.
RULE 10: SSAMOSWAP.W provides release semantics when its rl bit is set to 1.
RULE 11: If the menvcfg.SSE field is 0 and the current privilege mode is less privileged than M, execution of SSAMOSWAP.W MUST raise an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 12: If the menvcfg.SSE field is 1 and the current privilege mode is less privileged than M, execution of SSAMOSWAP.W MUST NOT raise an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 13: If the current privilege mode is M, execution of SSAMOSWAP.W MUST NOT raise an illegal-instruction exception regardless of the value of menvcfg.SSE.
RULE 14: If the SSE field in menvcfg is set to 1 and the processor is executing in U-mode, an execution of the SSAMOSWAP.W instruction must raise an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 15: If the SSE field in menvcfg is set to 1 and the processor is executing in VU-mode, an execution of the SSAMOSWAP.W instruction must raise a virtual-instruction exception.
RULE 16: If the menvcfg.SSE field is 1, execution of the SSAMOSWAP.W instruction MUST raise a virtual-instruction exception.
RULE 17: If satp.MODE (or vsatp.MODE when V=1) is Bare and the effective privilege mode is less than M, execution of SSAMOSWAP.W MUST raise a store/AMO access-fault exception.
RULE 18: If the effective privilege mode is M, execution of SSAMOSWAP.W MUST raise a store/AMO access-fault exception.
RULE 19: SSAMOSWAP.W is one of the only instructions permitted to write to a memory region mapped as a shadow-stack (SS) page, and writes by any other instruction to that region raise a store/AMO access-fault exception.
RULE 20: Stores performed by SSAMOSWAP.W to a shadow-stack page do NOT trigger a store/AMO access-fault exception, which is the fault type that other instructions would raise.
RULE 21: If SSAMOSWAP.W accesses a page whose PTE xwr field is neither 010b (shadow-stack) nor 001b (read-only), a store/AMO access-fault exception is raised; if the xwr field is 001b, a store/AMO page-fault exception is raised.
RULE 22: If SSAMOSWAP.W accesses a page whose PTE xwr field indicates read-write (110b), read-write-execute (111b), read-execute (100b), or execute-only (101b), a store/AMO access-fault exception is raised.
RULE 23: If the memory region accessed by SSAMOSWAP.W is not idempotent, execution of SSAMOSWAP.W MUST raise a store/AMO access-fault exception.
RULE 24: When G-stage page tables are active and the G-stage PTE for the address accessed by SSAMOSWAP.W does not grant read-write permission, a store/AMO guest-page-fault exception is raised.
RULE 25: If PMP configuration does not provide read-write permission for the memory accessed by SSAMOSWAP.W, execution of SSAMOSWAP.W MUST raise a store/AMO access-fault exception.
RULE 26: If the PMA attributes of the memory range accessed by SSAMOSWAP.W do not provide AMOSwap level support, execution of SSAMOSWAP.W MUST raise a store/AMO access-fault exception.
RULE 27: The SSAMOSWAP.W instruction is defined only when the Zicfiss extension is implemented.
RULE 28: The address used by SSAMOSWAP.W MUST be transformed by pointer masking.
RULE 29: The pointer-masked address for SSAMOSWAP.W MUST be computed according to the pointer-masking settings of the effective privilege mode.
RULE 30: The pointer-masking computation for SSAMOSWAP.W MUST incorporate the effects of MPRV, SPVP, and MXR as described for other explicit memory accesses.

INSTRUCTION: SSPOPCHK.X5
FORMAT: sspopchk.x5 sspopchk_x5
RULE 1: SSPOPCHK.X5 MUST use register x5 as its rs1 operand, and using any other register as rs1 causes a software-check exception.
RULE 2: When the CSR xSSE equals 1, SSPOPCHK.X5 performs a load of an XLEN-wide value from the address held in the shadow-stack pointer register ssp, using the same architectural semantics as a normal XLEN load with base ssp.
RULE 3: If the bitwise comparison performed by SSPOPCHK.X5 determines that the loaded value is not equal to the contents of register x5, SSPOPCHK.X5 raises a software-check exception with cause = 18 and xtval set to the string "shadow stack fault (code=3)".
RULE 4: If the bitwise comparison performed by SSPOPCHK.X5 determines that the loaded value is equal to the contents of register x5, SSPOPCHK.X5 increments the shadow-stack pointer register ssp by XLEN/8.
RULE 5: The increment of ssp performed by SSPOPCHK.X5 occurs only if the load from the shadow stack completes without raising any load/store/AMO access-fault exception and no software-check exception is raised.
RULE 6: The software-check exception generated by SSPOPCHK.X5 has lower priority than any load/store/AMO access-fault exception that may be triggered by the same load.
RULE 7: The compressed instruction C.SSPOPCHK x5 expands to SSPOPCHK.X5 and exhibits exactly the same architectural behavior as SSPOPCHK.X5.

INSTRUCTION: SSPUSH.X1
FORMAT: sspush.x1 sspush_x1
RULE 1: SSPUSH.X1 must have its rs2 field encode register x1.
RULE 2: The 16-bit C.SSPUSH x1 instruction has the same architectural effect as SSPUSH.X1.
RULE 3: When the architectural xSSE flag is 1, SSPUSH.X1 stores the XLEN-bit value of register x1 to memory at address ssp-(XLEN/8).
RULE 4: The store performed by SSPUSH.X1 uses the same semantics as a normal XLEN-bit store with an implicit base register of ssp and an implicit offset of –XLEN/8.
RULE 5: SSPUSH.X1 decrements the architectural ssp register by XLEN/8 only after the store to the shadow stack completes successfully.
RULE 6: If the architectural xSSE flag is 0, SSPUSH.X1 does not modify memory and does not change the ssp register.
RULE 7: SSPUSH.X1 writes the contents of the link register x1, thereby pushing the link register onto the shadow stack.
RULE 8: SSPUSH.X1 writes the XLEN-wide value contained in integer register x1 to the memory address specified by the current shadow-stack pointer (ssp).
RULE 9: After performing the XLEN-wide store, SSPUSH.X1 decrements the shadow-stack pointer (ssp) by XLEN/8.

INSTRUCTION: SSPUSH.X5
FORMAT: sspush.x5 sspush_x5
RULE 1: SSPUSH.X5 MUST push the value contained in register X5 onto the shadow stack.
RULE 2: When SSPUSH.X5 is executed and the architectural xSSE bit is 1, the implementation must store the XLEN-wide value of x5 to the memory address ssp−(XLEN/8).
RULE 3: The store performed by SSPUSH.X5 uses the same architectural semantics as an ordinary XLEN-wide store, with the base address implicitly equal to ssp.
RULE 4: If the store performed by SSPUSH.X5 completes successfully, the implementation must decrement ssp by XLEN/8.
RULE 5: If the store performed by SSPUSH.X5 does not complete successfully, the implementation must not modify ssp.

INSTRUCTION: SSRDP
FORMAT: ssrdp rd
RULE 1: SSRDP reads the current value of the ssp register and writes that value to the integer register specified as the destination operand of the instruction.
RULE 2: If the shadow-stack extension status bit xSSE is 1, executing SSRDP writes the current value of the ssp register into the destination integer register specified by rd.
RULE 3: If the shadow-stack extension status bit xSSE is 0, executing SSRDP writes zero into the destination integer register specified by rd.

INSTRUCTION: SUB
FORMAT: sub rd, rs1, rs2
RULE 1: SUB is an integer register-register R-type instruction defined in the RV32I base ISA.
RULE 2: SUB reads the architectural registers indicated by the rs1 and rs2 fields as its source operands.
RULE 3: SUB writes the architectural result of the operation into the register indicated by the rd field.
RULE 4: SUB is selected by the funct7 and funct3 bit-fields that match the encoding defined for the SUB operation in the R-type instruction format.
RULE 5: SUB computes the arithmetic subtraction of the value contained in rs2 from the value contained in rs1.
RULE 6: SUB ignores any overflow that occurs during the subtraction and keeps only the low XLEN bits of the mathematical result.
RULE 7: SUB stores those low XLEN bits into the destination register rd.
RULE 8: The SUB instruction computes the two's-complement XLEN-bit subtraction of the value in source register rs2 from the value in source register rs1 and writes the resulting integer to destination register rd.
RULE 9: When the destination register rd is the same as source register rs1, the SUB instruction replaces the original content of that register with its original value minus the value in rs2, thereby decrementing that register by the amount in rs2.
RULE 10: The SUB instruction MUST compute the value of the first source register minus the value of the second source register and write the XLEN-bit result to the destination register.
RULE 11: The SUB instruction MUST produce its result modulo 2^XLEN, discarding any overflow beyond the XLEN-bit width.
RULE 12: The SUB instruction MUST read the contents of its two source registers and MUST update only the destination register; no other architectural state may be changed.
RULE 13: The SUB instruction MUST NOT raise an exception for arithmetic overflow.
RULE 14: When the second source register holds the value 1, executing SUB on a register MUST decrement that register by one.
RULE 15: When the second source register holds a count of non-zero bytes, executing SUB on a register MUST subtract that count from the register.

INSTRUCTION: SUBW
FORMAT: subw rd, rs1, rs2
RULE 1: SUBW MUST ignore the upper 32 bits of each source operand when performing the subtraction.
RULE 2: SUBW MUST produce a 32-bit signed subtraction result and sign-extend it to the full XLEN width, so that bits XLEN-1 through 31 of the destination register equal bit 31 of the 32-bit result.
RULE 3: SUBW must be defined only for RV64I implementations and is not defined for RV32I or other base ISA variants.
RULE 4: SUBW must subtract the lower 32 bits of the second source register from the lower 32 bits of the first source register, interpreting both operands as signed 32-bit integers.
RULE 5: SUBW must ignore any overflow that results from the signed 32-bit subtraction.
RULE 6: SUBW must write the low 32 bits of the subtraction result, sign-extended to 64 bits, into the destination register.

INSTRUCTION: SW
FORMAT: sw rs2, imm12(rs1)
RULE 1: SW stores a 32-bit value taken from the low bits of register rs2 into memory.
RULE 2: The effective address used by an SW instruction is computed by adding the contents of register rs1 to the sign-extended 12-bit immediate offset.
RULE 3: In a little-endian configuration, an SW instruction writes the least-significant byte of rs2 to the lowest addressed byte and writes the remaining bytes in order of increasing significance.
RULE 4: In a big-endian configuration, an SW instruction writes the most-significant byte of rs2 to the lowest addressed byte and writes the remaining bytes in order of decreasing significance.
RULE 5: When the effective address of an SW instruction is naturally aligned to a 4-byte boundary, the instruction shall not raise an address-misaligned exception.
RULE 6: If an execution environment guarantees that misaligned stores are fully supported, an SW instruction with a misaligned effective address may be performed by hardware, by an invisible trap, or by a combination of hardware and invisible trap, and software shall never experience a contained or fatal address-misaligned trap.
RULE 7: If an execution environment does not guarantee invisible handling of misaligned stores, an SW instruction with a misaligned effective address may either complete successfully or raise an exception.
RULE 8: When an SW instruction raises an exception due to address misalignment in an environment that does not guarantee invisible handling, the exception may be either an address-misaligned exception or an access-fault exception.
RULE 9: Naturally aligned SW instructions are guaranteed to execute atomically.
RULE 10: SW must store the low-order 32 bits of the value in register rs2 to the addressed memory location.
RULE 11: The SW instruction atomically stores the low-order 2-byte value of register rs2 to the memory address contained in register rs1.
RULE 12: Encodings of the SW instruction that do not set the rl bit are RESERVED.
RULE 13: An encoding of the SW instruction with both the aq and rl bits cleared is not provided.
RULE 14: An encoding of the SW instruction with the aq bit set and the rl bit cleared is not provided.
RULE 15: The SW instruction MUST compute its effective address by zero-extending the offset immediate, multiplying the result by 4, and adding that value to the current contents of the stack pointer register x2.
RULE 16: The SW instruction MUST store the low-order 32 bits of register rs2 to the memory location whose address equals the effective address computed for the instruction.
RULE 17: SW must store the 32-bit value contained in source register rs2′ to the memory location whose effective address is computed for the instruction.
RULE 18: SW must compute its effective address by adding to the base address in register rs1′ the zero-extended offset field multiplied by 4.
RULE 19: C.SW must expand to the regular SW instruction sw rs2′, offset(rs1′) and therefore exhibit the same architectural behavior as SW.
RULE 20: SW may generate imprecise faults from the bus as a result of the store transaction.
RULE 21: In RV32, a store-word (SW) to the low 32-bit address of the 64-bit mtimecmp register MUST update only the low 32-bit half of the register and MUST leave the high 32-bit half unchanged.
RULE 22: In RV32, a store-word (SW) to the high 32-bit address of the 64-bit mtimecmp register MUST update only the high 32-bit half of the register and MUST leave the low 32-bit half unchanged.
RULE 23: The SW instruction must write a 32-bit word to the memory location whose effective address is the sum of the base register and the sign-extended immediate offset.
RULE 24: For a non-compressed standard SW instruction that traps, the transformed instruction must follow the transformed-store format and must preserve the rs2, funct3, and opcode fields of the original SW instruction.
RULE 25: When a basic SW instruction is transformed, its immediate offset fields must be replaced with zero.
RULE 26: In a transformed SW instruction, bits 19:15 (the Addr. Offset field) must contain the positive difference between the faulting virtual address (written to mtval or stval) and the original virtual address, and this difference is non-zero only if the SW caused a misaligned memory access.
RULE 27: If a compressed SW instruction traps, the transformed instruction must be produced by expanding the compressed encoding to its 32-bit equivalent, applying the SW transformation, and then forcing bit 1 to 0, which results in bits 1:0 equal to 01.
RULE 28: On a Store/AMO address-misaligned, access-fault, or page-fault exception generated by a standard SW instruction, the hardware may write a transformed SW encoding into mtinst or htinst.
RULE 29: Pointer masking does not affect software reads or writes of CSRs, but when SW uses an address obtained from a CSR, the address shall be masked if pointer masking is enabled for the executing privilege mode.

INSTRUCTION: UNZIP
FORMAT: unzip rd, rs1
RULE 1: UNZIP rd, rs reads the XLEN-bit value from source register rs and writes the resulting XLEN-bit value to destination register rd.
RULE 2: UNZIP performs a bit-deinterleave operation on the XLEN-bit input value from rs to produce the output value written to rd.
RULE 3: When an UNZIP instruction is executed, for each integer i from 0 to XLEN/2-1 the i-th bit of the destination register X(rd) is set to the bit 2·i of the source register X(rs1).
RULE 4: When an UNZIP instruction is executed, for each integer i from 0 to XLEN/2-1 the bit at position i+XLEN/2 of the destination register X(rd) is set to the bit 2·i+1 of the source register X(rs1).
RULE 5: Execution of an UNZIP instruction does not modify the contents of the source register X(rs1).
RULE 6: The UNZIP instruction is the inverse of the ZIP instruction, such that applying UNZIP to a value that was produced by ZIP of the same operand yields the original operand value.
RULE 7: The UNZIP rd, rs instruction places each even-numbered bit of X(rs) into the low half (bits 0 to XLEN/2-1) of X(rd) and each odd-numbered bit of X(rs) into the high half (bits XLEN/2 to XLEN-1) of X(rd).
RULE 8: The UNZIP operation assigns, for every index i from 0 to XLEN/2-1, X(rd)[i] = X(rs)[2*i] and X(rd)[i + XLEN/2] = X(rs)[2*i + 1].
RULE 9: The UNZIP instruction is defined only when XLEN equals 32; if XLEN is not 32 the encoding is reserved and its architectural effect is undefined.
RULE 10: For any 32-bit value V, executing UNZIP on a register containing V and then executing ZIP on the resulting register must produce V in the final destination register.
RULE 11: For any 32-bit value V, executing ZIP on a register containing V and then executing UNZIP on the resulting register must produce V in the final destination register.
RULE 12: UNZIP performs a bit-interleave (de-interleave) operation on the bits of the value in source register rs1 and writes the resulting value to destination register rd.

INSTRUCTION: VAADD.VV
FORMAT: vaadd.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VAADD.VV must, for each element i whose index is less than the active vector length and whose mask bit is true, compute the infinite-precision signed sum of vs2[i] and vs1[i], right-shift the sum by one bit, round the shifted value according to the current setting of the vector rounding-mode register vxrm, and write the rounded result to vd[i].
RULE 2: The rounding performed by VAADD.VV on the right-shifted sum must follow the rounding mode specified in vxrm.
RULE 3: VAADD.VV must perform the addition and averaging in infinite precision before any rounding or truncation to the destination element width.
RULE 4: VAADD.VV must never produce an overflow condition; the rounded result is guaranteed to fit within the destination element width.
RULE 5: VAADD.VV shares the same architectural permission and exception handling effects as the unsigned averaging add instruction VAADDU.VV.
RULE 6: For any element i whose mask bit is false, VAADD.VV must leave vd[i] unchanged.

INSTRUCTION: VAADDU.VV
FORMAT: vaaddu.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VAADDU.VV must add each unsigned integer element vs2[i] to the corresponding unsigned integer element vs1[i] using infinite-precision arithmetic.
RULE 2: VAADDU.VV must right-shift the infinite-precision sum of vs2[i] and vs1[i] by one bit before any rounding is applied.
RULE 3: VAADDU.VV must round the right-shifted value for each element i according to the current vxrm rounding mode and must write the rounded result to the destination element vd[i] when the corresponding mask bit vm[i] is 1.
RULE 4: VAADDU.VV must leave destination element vd[i] unchanged for any element i whose mask bit vm[i] is 0.
RULE 5: VAADDU.VV must never produce an overflow; the result is defined for all possible operand values.
RULE 6: VAADDU.VV must perform all arithmetic in infinite precision prior to the right shift and rounding steps.

INSTRUCTION: VAADDU.VX
FORMAT: vaaddu.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VAADDU.VX must read the unsigned integer scalar operand from integer register rs1, read each unsigned integer element of source vector register vs2, write the resulting unsigned integer elements to destination vector register vd, and apply the optional mask vm if it is present.
RULE 2: VAADDU.VX must right-shift the infinite-precision sum for each element i by one bit and then round the shifted value according to the current setting of the vxrm rounding-mode register.
RULE 3: VAADDU.VX must never cause an overflow in any result element, because overflow cannot occur for this instruction.

INSTRUCTION: VADC.VIM
FORMAT: vadc.vim vd, vs2, simm5, v0
RULE 1: VADC.VIM computes for each element i the result vd[i] = vs2[i] + imm + v0.mask[i].
RULE 2: VADC.VIM writes the computed result to every body element of the destination register even when the corresponding mask bit is clear.
RULE 3: VADC.VIM uses the mask bits of the implicit vector register v0 as the carry-in bits for each element addition.

INSTRUCTION: VADC.VVM
FORMAT: vadc.vvm vd, vs2, vs1, v0
RULE 1: For each element i within the current active vector length, VADC.VVM computes vd[i] as the sum of vs2[i], vs1[i], and the corresponding mask bit v0.mask[i].
RULE 2: VADC.VVM is defined only for encodings with the mask bit vm equal to 0, and any encoding with vm equal to 1 is reserved.
RULE 3: VADC.VVM obtains the carry-in for each element from the mask bits of the implicit vector register v0.
RULE 4: VADC.VVM must not use v0 as the destination register; an encoding that specifies v0 for vd is reserved.
RULE 5: VADC.VVM writes the computed result to every element of the destination register vd for the entire active vector length, regardless of any element mask.
RULE 6: VADC.VVM must obey the general vector register source/destination overlap restrictions, and any encoding that violates those restrictions is reserved.

INSTRUCTION: VADC.VXM
FORMAT: vadc.vxm vd, vs2, rs1, v0
RULE 1: VADC.VXM computes vd[i] = vs2[i] + x[rs1] + v0.mask[i].
RULE 2: Despite being encoded as a masked instruction, VADC.VXM produces a result for every element and writes back all elements of the destination vector register regardless of the mask bits.
RULE 3: Encodings of VADC.VXM with vm equal to 1 are reserved and have no defined architectural behavior.
RULE 4: The encoding of VADC.VXM is reserved when the destination vector register vd is v0.
RULE 5: The carry-in used by VADC.VXM for each element i must be taken from the mask bit v0.mask[i] of the implicit v0 register.
RULE 6: VADC.VXM may write its result to any vector register that satisfies the standard source/destination overlap restrictions.

INSTRUCTION: VADD.VV
FORMAT: vadd.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VADD.VV adds each element of the first source vector register group vs2 to the corresponding element of the second source vector register group vs1 and writes the integer sum to the destination vector register group vd.
RULE 2: When vm is present, VADD.VV performs the element-wise addition only for those element positions where the corresponding mask bit in vm is true, leaving other element positions of vd unchanged.

INSTRUCTION: VADD.VX
FORMAT: vadd.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VADD.VX performs integer addition of the scalar integer in integer register rs1 to each element of source vector register vs2 and writes the resulting elements to destination vector register vd.
RULE 2: When a mask register vm is supplied, VADD.VX updates only those elements of vd whose corresponding mask bit is true and leaves the other elements unchanged.

INSTRUCTION: VAESDF.VS
FORMAT: vaesdf.vs vd, vs2
RULE 1: For VAESDF.VS any SEW value other than 32 is a reserved encoding.
RULE 2: For VAESDF.VS the vd register group overlapping the vs2 scalar element group is a reserved encoding.
RULE 3: VAESDF.VS reads the round-state element groups from the vd register group as input.
RULE 4: VAESDF.VS reads the round-key scalar element group (element group 0) from the vs2 register group as input.
RULE 5: VAESDF.VS writes the new round state to the same vd register group as output.
RULE 6: VAESDF.VS performs a final-round AES block-cipher decryption.
RULE 7: VAESDF.VS raises an illegal-instruction exception and retires with RETIRE_FAIL when LMUL × VLEN < EGW.
RULE 8: VAESDF.VS retires with RETIRE_SUCCESS when execution completes without triggering an illegal-instruction exception.

INSTRUCTION: VAESDF.VV
FORMAT: vaesdf.vv vd, vs2
RULE 1: VAESDF.VV raises an illegal-instruction exception and does not retire when LMUL × VLEN is less than the element group width (EGW) of 128 bits.
RULE 2: VAESDF.VV writes the 128-bit result of the XOR back to element group i of the destination register vd, overwriting the original round-state element.

INSTRUCTION: VAESDM.VS
FORMAT: vaesdm.vs vd, vs2
RULE 1: If LMUL * VLEN is less than 128 bits (the element width EGW), executing VAESDM.VS raises an illegal-instruction exception and the instruction retires with failure.
RULE 2: VAESDM.VS writes the 128-bit result of the inverse MixColumns step back to the corresponding element group of vd, producing the new round state.
RULE 3: For each active element group, VAESDM.VS selects scalar element index 0 of vs2 as the round key used in the XOR operation.
RULE 4: The encoding in which the destination register group vd overlaps the scalar element group of vs2 for the .vs form of VAESDM.VS is reserved and its architectural behavior is undefined.

INSTRUCTION: VAESDM.VV
FORMAT: vaesdm.vv vd, vs2
RULE 1: If the SEW field of a VAESDM.VV encoding is any value other than 32, the encoding is reserved.
RULE 2: For the VAESDM.VS form only, if the vd register group overlaps the vs2 scalar element group, the encoding of VAESDM.VV is reserved.
RULE 3: VAESDM.VV raises an illegal-instruction exception and causes RETIRE_FAIL when LMUL multiplied by VLEN is less than 128 bits.
RULE 4: VAESDM.VV computes the number of element groups to process as the integer division of VL by 4 and the starting element group index as the integer division of VSTART by 4.
RULE 5: VAESDM.VV applies the AES inverse ShiftRows transformation to the round-state element read from Vd.
RULE 6: VAESDM.VV applies the AES inverse SubBytes transformation to the result of the inverse ShiftRows step.
RULE 7: VAESDM.VV XORs the output of the inverse SubBytes step with the round-key element read from Vs2.
RULE 8: VAESDM.VV applies the AES inverse MixColumns transformation to the XOR result.
RULE 9: VAESDM.VV writes the 128-bit result of the inverse MixColumns step back to the same element group i of Vd, replacing the original round state.

INSTRUCTION: VAESEF.VS
FORMAT: vaesef.vs vd, vs2
RULE 1: VAESEF.VS must be encoded with SEW equal to 32, and any encoding with a different SEW value is defined as a reserved encoding.
RULE 2: VAESEF.VS is a reserved encoding when the destination register group vd overlaps the scalar element group of the source register vs2.
RULE 3: VAESEF.VS performs a final-round AES encryption function on each 128-bit element group of the destination vector register vd.
RULE 4: VAESEF.VS applies the AES SubBytes forward transformation to the 128-bit state element obtained from vd for each element group.
RULE 5: VAESEF.VS applies the AES ShiftRows forward transformation to the result of the SubBytes step for each element group.
RULE 6: VAESEF.VS XORs the shifted-row state with the round key taken from the scalar element group of vs2 (element index 0) for each element group.
RULE 7: VAESEF.VS writes the XOR result back into the corresponding element group of vd, thereby updating the round state.
RULE 8: VAESEF.VS causes an illegal-instruction exception and retirement failure when LMUL × VLEN is less than the effective group width of 128 bits.
RULE 9: When LMUL × VLEN is at least 128 bits, VAESEF.VS computes the number of element groups as vl divided by the element-group size (4) and starts processing at vstart divided by the element-group size.
RULE 10: For each element-group index i from the start index up to (but not including) the computed length, VAESEF.VS uses the i-th element of vd as the state and the element 0 of vs2 as the round key.

INSTRUCTION: VAESEF.VV
FORMAT: vaesef.vv vd, vs2
RULE 1: VAESEF.VV encodings with a SEW field value other than 32 are reserved.
RULE 2: VAESEF.VV reads the source vector register vs2 as the round-key input.
RULE 3: VAESEF.VV reads the destination vector register vd as the round-state input before it is overwritten.
RULE 4: VAESEF.VV writes the resulting new round state to the destination vector register vd.
RULE 5: If the product of LMUL and VLEN is less than the effective group width of 128 bits, VAESEF.VV raises an illegal-instruction exception and does not retire successfully.
RULE 6: If LMUL × VLEN is at least 128 bits, VAESEF.VV processes each element group i from floor(vstart/EGS) to floor(vl/EGS)-1 by reading the 128-bit round-state from vd, reading the corresponding 128-bit round-key from vs2, applying the AES forward SubBytes transformation, then the AES forward ShiftRows transformation, XORing the shifted result with the round key, and writing the 128-bit result back to vd at the same element group index.
RULE 7: After all selected element groups have been processed, VAESEF.VV retires successfully.

INSTRUCTION: VAESEM.VS
FORMAT: vaesem.vs vd, vs2
RULE 1: VAESEM.VS encoding is reserved when the selected element width (SEW) is any value other than 32.
RULE 2: VAESEM.VS encoding is reserved for the .vs form when the destination register group vd overlaps the scalar element group of the source register vs2.
RULE 3: VAESEM.VS raises an illegal-instruction exception and results in RETIRE_FAIL if LMUL multiplied by VLEN is less than the effective group width of 128 bits.
RULE 4: VAESEM.VS applies the AES SubBytes forward transformation to the round-state value.
RULE 5: VAESEM.VS applies the AES ShiftRows forward transformation to the result of SubBytes.
RULE 6: VAESEM.VS applies the AES MixColumns forward transformation to the result of ShiftRows.
RULE 7: VAESEM.VS XORs the output of MixColumns with the round key to produce the new round state.
RULE 8: VAESEM.VS writes the new round state back to the corresponding element group i of vd.

INSTRUCTION: VAESEM.VV
FORMAT: vaesem.vv vd, vs2
RULE 1: VAESEM.VV raises an illegal-instruction exception and retires fail when LMUL multiplied by VLEN is less than 128 bits, because the vector register group cannot hold the required 128-bit element group.
RULE 2: When LMUL multiplied by VLEN is at least 128 bits, VAESEM.VV executes without raising an exception.
RULE 3: VAESEM.VV applies the AES SubBytes forward transformation to each 128-bit round-state element group taken from the input vector register vd.
RULE 4: VAESEM.VV applies the AES ShiftRows forward transformation to the output of the SubBytes step for each round-state element group.
RULE 5: VAESEM.VV applies the AES MixColumns forward transformation to the output of the ShiftRows step for each round-state element group.
RULE 6: For each element group i, VAESEM.VV XORs the result of the MixColumns step with the round-key element taken from element group i of vector register vs2.
RULE 7: VAESEM.VV writes the XOR result back to element group i of the destination vector register vd, replacing the original round-state element.
RULE 8: VAESEM.VV performs the AES middle-round encryption function on each 128-bit element group of the input round state.

INSTRUCTION: VAESKF1.VI
FORMAT: vaeskf1.vi vd, vs2, zimm5
RULE 1: If the selected element width (SEW) is not 32, the encoding for VAESKF1.VI is reserved.
RULE 2: The immediate field uimm[3:0] supplies the round number for VAESKF1.VI, and uimm[4] is ignored.
RULE 3: For VAESKF1.VI, if the round number extracted from uimm[3:0] is 0 or greater than 10, the effective round number is obtained by inverting bit 3 of uimm[3:0], mapping 0 to 8 and values 11-15 to 3-7.
RULE 4: The effective round number for VAESKF1.VI determines the round constant used in the first word generation via aes_decode_rcon.
RULE 5: If LMUL multiplied by VLEN is less than 128 bits, execution of VAESKF1.VI raises an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 6: For each element group processed by VAESKF1.VI, the most-significant 32-bit word of the current round key (word 3) is rotated, substituted with aes_subword_fwd, XORed with the round constant derived from (round-1), and XORed with the least-significant word (word 0) to produce the first word of the next round key.
RULE 7: For each element group processed by VAESKF1.VI, the second word of the next round key is computed as the XOR of the first generated word and the second word of the current round key.
RULE 8: For each element group processed by VAESKF1.VI, the third word of the next round key is computed as the XOR of the second generated word and the third word of the current round key.
RULE 9: For each element group processed by VAESKF1.VI, the fourth word of the next round key is computed as the XOR of the third generated word and the fourth word of the current round key.
RULE 10: For each element group processed by VAESKF1.VI, the four generated 32-bit words are written to the corresponding element group of the destination vector register vd.
RULE 11: When the pre-condition LMUL*VLEN >= 128 bits holds, VAESKF1.VI completes without raising an exception and retires successfully.

INSTRUCTION: VAESKF2.VI
FORMAT: vaeskf2.vi vd, vs2, zimm5
RULE 1: Encodings of VAESKF2.VI with SEW other than 32 are reserved.
RULE 2: If LMUL × VLEN is less than 128 (the EGW) then VAESKF2.VI MUST raise an illegal-instruction exception and retire as a failure.
RULE 3: VAESKF2.VI obtains the round number from the low 4 bits of the immediate (uimm[3:0]) and ignores uimm[4].
RULE 4: When the extracted 4-bit round number is less than 2 or greater than 14, VAESKF2.VI maps it into the valid range by inverting bit 3 of the immediate.
RULE 5: VAESKF2.VI maps out-of-range round numbers 0-1 to 8-9 and 15 to 7 by inverting bit 3 of the immediate.
RULE 6: VAESKF2.VI performs a single forward AES-256 key-schedule round producing the next round key from the previous round key supplied in vd and the current round key supplied in vs2.
RULE 7: For each element group, VAESKF2.VI computes the most-significant 32-bit word w[0] by applying aes_subword_fwd to the most-significant word of the current round key, optionally preceded by aes_rotword when the least-significant bit of the round number is 0, then XORing the result with the appropriate round constant and with the corresponding word from the previous round key.
RULE 8: When the least-significant bit of the round number is 1, VAESKF2.VI computes w[0] as aes_subword_fwd applied to the most-significant word of the current round key XORed with the corresponding word from the previous round key, without using a round constant.
RULE 9: When the least-significant bit of the round number is 0, VAESKF2.VI computes w[0] as aes_subword_fwd applied to aes_rotword of the most-significant word of the current round key XORed with the decoded round constant derived from (round number>>1)-1 and XORed with the corresponding word from the previous round key.
RULE 10: VAESKF2.VI computes w[1] as w[0] XOR the second 32-bit word of the previous round key.
RULE 11: VAESKF2.VI computes w[2] as w[1] XOR the third 32-bit word of the previous round key.
RULE 12: VAESKF2.VI computes w[3] as w[2] XOR the fourth 32-bit word of the previous round key.
RULE 13: VAESKF2.VI writes the four 32-bit words w[3:0] back to the destination vector register vd at the same element-group index from which the source elements were read.
RULE 14: VAESKF2.VI processes element groups starting at the index given by vstart divided by 4 up to (vl divided by 4) minus 1, inclusive.
RULE 15: VAESKF2.VI operates on 32-bit element width (EEW = 32) for all intermediate and final computations.
RULE 16: The VAESKF2.VI instruction MUST use opcode 0x77 and belongs to the Crypto Vector instruction category (OP-VE).
RULE 17: The VAESKF2.VI instruction MUST be encoded as a V-type vector instruction.
RULE 18: The VAESKF2.VI instruction MUST have its funct6 field equal to binary 101010.
RULE 19: The VAESKF2.VI instruction MUST be listed among integer-vector-immediate (OPIVI) instructions and therefore includes an immediate operand.

INSTRUCTION: VAESZ.VS
FORMAT: vaesz.vs vd, vs2
RULE 1: If the selected standard element width (SEW) for VAESZ.VS is not 32 bits, the encoding is reserved.
RULE 2: If the destination register group (vd) overlaps the source register group (vs2) for VAESZ.VS, the encoding is reserved.
RULE 3: For each 128-bit element group of vd, VAESZ.VS writes the bitwise XOR of that element group and the 128-bit round key from vs2 back to the same element group of vd.
RULE 4: If the current vector start index (vstart) is not a multiple of the element group size (EGS) for VAESZ.VS, an illegal-instruction exception is raised and the instruction does not retire successfully.
RULE 5: If the product of LMUL and VLEN is less than the effective group width (EGW) for VAESZ.VS, an illegal-instruction exception is raised and the instruction does not retire successfully.
RULE 6: VAESZ.VS processes element groups starting at index vstart/EGS up to (vl/EGS)−1, inclusive, applying the XOR operation to each group.
RULE 7: After successfully processing all applicable element groups, VAESZ.VS retires with a successful completion status.
RULE 8: For VAESZ.VS the effective element width (EEW) is 32 bits, the effective group width (EGW) is 128 bits, and the effective group size (EGS) is 4 elements.

INSTRUCTION: VAND.VI
FORMAT: vand.vi vd, vs2, simm5, vm
RULE 1: VAND.VI takes a destination vector register operand named vd and writes the result of the operation to vd.
RULE 2: VAND.VI takes a source vector register operand named vs2 whose elements are used as one operand of the logical operation.
RULE 3: VAND.VI takes an immediate operand named imm whose bit pattern is used as the other operand of the logical operation.
RULE 4: VAND.VI may include an optional mask register operand named vm that controls which elements are updated.
RULE 5: When a mask register vm is supplied, VAND.VI updates only those elements of vd for which the corresponding mask bit is set, leaving other elements of vd unchanged.
RULE 6: When the mask register vm is omitted, VAND.VI behaves as if all mask bits are set and updates every active element of vd.
RULE 7: For each active element, VAND.VI computes the bitwise logical AND of the element from vs2 and the immediate imm and writes the result to the corresponding element of vd.
RULE 8: Applying VAND.VI with an immediate operand that is the bitwise inversion of a given constant produces the same architectural effect as a ".vi" form of the VANDN instruction would have produced.

INSTRUCTION: VASUB.VV
FORMAT: vasub.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VASUB.VV MUST compute each destination element i as roundoff_signed(vs2[i] - vs1[i], 1), where roundoff_signed denotes a right-shift-by-one-bit followed by rounding according to the current vxrm rounding mode.
RULE 2: VASUB.VV MUST perform the subtraction vs2[i] - vs1[i] in infinite precision before any right-shift-by-one-bit or rounding is applied.
RULE 3: If the result of VASUB.VV exceeds the representable signed-integer range of the element, the overflow is ignored and the result wraps around modulo the element width.
RULE 4: Overflow for VASUB.VV can only occur when subtracting the smallest representable signed integer from the largest representable signed integer while vxrm is set to either rnu or rne.

INSTRUCTION: VASUB.VX
FORMAT: vasub.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VASUB.VX operates on signed integer elements of the source vector vs2 and the scalar register rs1.
RULE 2: VASUB.VX computes for each element i the infinite-precision value of vs2[i] minus the scalar value in rs1 before any rounding is applied.
RULE 3: VASUB.VX right-shifts the infinite-precision subtraction result for each element by one bit prior to rounding.
RULE 4: VASUB.VX rounds the one-bit-right-shifted result for each element according to the rounding mode specified in the vxrm CSR.
RULE 5: VASUB.VX stores the final rounded value for each element into the corresponding element of the destination vector vd.
RULE 6: VASUB.VX ignores arithmetic overflow and the resulting element value wraps around modulo the element width.
RULE 7: VASUB.VX generates overflow only when the subtraction operation subtracts the smallest representable signed value from the largest representable signed value while the rounding mode is rnu or rne.

INSTRUCTION: VASUBU.VX
FORMAT: vasubu.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VASUBU.VX must compute the subtraction of the unsigned integer value in scalar register rs1 from each unsigned element of source vector register vs2 using infinite-precision arithmetic before any rounding or truncation.
RULE 2: VASUBU.VX must right-shift the infinite-precision subtraction result by one bit and round the shifted value according to the unsigned rounding mode specified in the vxrm field.
RULE 3: VASUBU.VX must produce the final destination element by truncating the rounded value to the element width, with any overflow from the subtraction ignored and the result wrapping around modulo the element size.

INSTRUCTION: VBREV.V
FORMAT: vbrev.v vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VBREV.V reverses the order of bits within each element of the source vector and writes the reversed elements to the destination vector register.
RULE 2: VBREV.V must reverse the order of bits in each element of the source vector register vs2 and write the resulting elements to the destination vector register vd.
RULE 3: For each element index i from the current vector start index vstart up to vl-1, VBREV.V reads the SEW-bit element from vs2 and writes to the corresponding element of vd a value whose bit at position (SEW-1-b) equals the bit at position b of the source element for every bit position b in the range 0 to SEW-1.
RULE 4: VBREV.V must perform the bit-reversal operation independently for each element within the active vector length and must not modify elements outside the range vstart to vl-1.
RULE 5: After processing all active elements, VBREV.V signals successful retirement of the instruction.

INSTRUCTION: VBREV8.V
FORMAT: vbrev8.v vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VBREV8.V reverses the order of the eight bits in each byte element of the source vector and writes the resulting bytes to the corresponding elements of the destination vector.
RULE 2: VBREV8.V is defined only in the Zvkb extension; an implementation that does not implement Zvkb treats the VBREV8.V encoding as reserved.
RULE 3: VBREV8.V reads each vector element i from source register vs2 for indices i from vstart to the current vector length vl and writes a result to the corresponding element i of destination register vd.
RULE 4: VBREV8.V treats each processed element as SEW bits and reverses the bit order within each byte of the element while preserving the positions of the bytes.
RULE 5: VBREV8.V is defined for every supported standard element width (SEW), allowing the byte-wise bit-reversal operation to be applied regardless of the element size.
RULE 6: VBREV8.V does not modify any destination elements whose indices lie outside the active element range from vstart to vl.
RULE 7: VBREV8.V retires successfully without raising an exception when all architectural preconditions are satisfied.

INSTRUCTION: VCLMUL.VV
FORMAT: vclmul.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: Encodings of VCLMUL.VV where the selected element width (SEW) is not 64 are reserved.
RULE 2: For each active element index i from vstart to vl1, VCLMUL.VV reads the 64-bit element i of source register vs1 as the multiplier operand.
RULE 3: For each active element index i from vstart to vl1, VCLMUL.VV reads the 64-bit element i of source register vs2 as the multiplicand operand.
RULE 4: For each active element index i, VCLMUL.VV computes a 64-bit product by performing a carry-less multiplication of the two 64-bit operands using the algorithm: start with a zero result, and for each bit position j from 0 to 63, if the j-th bit of the multiplicand is 1 then XOR the result with the multiplier left-shifted by j.
RULE 5: The 64-bit product produced by VCLMUL.VV is the least-significant half of the full 128-bit carry-less product of the two 64-bit operands.
RULE 6: For each active element index i, VCLMUL.VV writes the computed 64-bit product into element i of destination register vd.
RULE 7: VCLMUL.VV completes execution and retires successfully without raising an exception when encoded with SEW equal to 64.

INSTRUCTION: VCLMUL.VX
FORMAT: vclmul.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VCLMUL.VX is only defined for a selected element width (SEW) of 64 bits; any encoding that specifies an SEW value other than 64 is reserved.
RULE 2: VCLMUL.VX uses the 64-bit integer register rs1 as the multiplier operand for the carry-less multiplication.
RULE 3: VCLMUL.VX uses each 64-bit element of the source vector register vs2 as a multiplicand operand for the carry-less multiplication.
RULE 4: VCLMUL.VX iterates over element indices i from the current vector start (vstart) to the active vector length minus one (vl-1) and performs the multiplication for each selected element.
RULE 5: VCLMUL.VX stores the least-significant 64 bits of the 128-bit carry-less product into the corresponding element of the destination vector register vd.
RULE 6: VCLMUL.VX leaves elements of vd whose indices are outside the range from vstart to vl-1 unchanged.
RULE 7: VCLMUL.VX completes execution without raising an exception and retires successfully.

INSTRUCTION: VCLMULH.VV
FORMAT: vclmulh.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: If the selected element width (SEW) for a VCLMULH.VV instruction is any value other than 64, the VCLMULH.VV encoding is reserved.
RULE 2: VCLMULH.VV reads the 64-bit element i from source vector register vs1 as the multiplier operand for each active element index i.
RULE 3: VCLMULH.VV reads the 64-bit element i from source vector register vs2 as the multiplicand operand for each active element index i.
RULE 4: VCLMULH.VV writes to destination vector register vd the 64-bit result of the carry-less high-half multiplication for each active element index i.
RULE 5: For each active element index i from vstart up to vl-1, VCLMULH.VV computes a 64-bit product by invoking the clmulh function with operands op1 = vs1[i], op2 = vs2[i], and width = 64.
RULE 6: The final value of the result after processing all bit positions 1 through 63 is the value stored by VCLMULH.VV into vd[i].
RULE 7: VCLMULH.VV produces the most significant 64 bits of the 128-bit carry-less product of the corresponding 64-bit elements of vs2 and vs1.
RULE 8: VCLMULH.VV completes execution without trapping and retires successfully when all active elements are processed according to the defined algorithm.

INSTRUCTION: VCLMULH.VX
FORMAT: vclmulh.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VCLMULH.VX MUST produce for each active element i the most-significant 64 bits of the 128-bit carry-less product of the 64-bit element from vs2 and the 64-bit integer value in rs1.
RULE 2: VCLMULH.VX reads the 64-bit scalar multiplier from integer register rs1 and zero-extends or truncates it to the selected element width (SEW) before using it in the multiplication.
RULE 3: VCLMULH.VX reads each 64-bit multiplicand element from the source vector register vs2 at index i for all indices i from vstart to the current vector length vl.
RULE 4: VCLMULH.VX writes the 64-bit result of the carry-less high-half multiplication into the destination vector register vd at the same element index i for all active elements.
RULE 5: VCLMULH.VX is defined only for SEW equal to 64; any encoding that selects an SEW other than 64 is reserved.
RULE 6: When VCLMULH.VX is encoded with a reserved SEW value, the architectural effect is undefined because the encoding is reserved.
RULE 7: VCLMULH.VX completes execution with a successful retirement status (RETIRE_SUCCESS) after processing all active elements.

INSTRUCTION: VCLZ.V
FORMAT: vclz.v vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VCLZ.V MUST count the number of leading zero bits in each element of the source vector register vs2 and write that count to the corresponding element of the destination vector register vd.
RULE 2: VCLZ.V MUST examine each element using the current SEW as the element width and scan bits from the most-significant bit (index SEW-1) down to the least-significant bit until a 1-bit is encountered.
RULE 3: If the scanned element of vs2 contains at least one 1-bit, VCLZ.V MUST set the destination element to SEW-1 minus the index of the first 1-bit encountered during the scan.
RULE 4: If the scanned element of vs2 is zero-valued, VCLZ.V MUST set the corresponding destination element to the value SEW.
RULE 5: VCLZ.V MUST process elements with indices i such that vstart ≤ i < vl and must not modify elements with indices i ≥ vl.
RULE 6: After completing the element-wise leading-zero count for all active elements, VCLZ.V MUST retire with a successful completion status.

INSTRUCTION: VCOMPRESS.VM
FORMAT: vcompress.vm vd, vs2, vs1
RULE 1: VCOMPRESS.VM compresses elements of the source vector register group selected by the mask register into contiguous elements at the beginning of the destination vector register group.
RULE 2: VCOMPRESS.VM uses the mask register specified by vs1 to select which of the first vl elements of the source vector register group vs2 are extracted and packed into the destination vector register vd.
RULE 3: VCOMPRESS.VM treats any elements of the destination vector register group that are not written by the compress operation as tail elements according to the current tail policy.
RULE 4: VCOMPRESS.VM is encoded with the vm field set to 1; an encoding of the same instruction with vm equal to 0 is reserved.
RULE 5: The destination vector register group must not overlap the source vector register group for VCOMPRESS.VM, otherwise the encoding is reserved.
RULE 6: The destination vector register group must not overlap the source mask register for VCOMPRESS.VM, otherwise the encoding is reserved.
RULE 7: A trap caused by VCOMPRESS.VM must report a vstart value of 0.
RULE 8: Executing VCOMPRESS.VM with a non-zero vstart must raise an illegal-instruction exception.

INSTRUCTION: VCPOP.M
FORMAT: vcpop.m rd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VCPOP.M reads a vector source register (vs2) that contains mask elements as defined in the vector mask register format.
RULE 2: VCPOP.M counts the number of active mask elements in the source register that have the value 1 and writes that count to the scalar integer register x[rd].
RULE 3: When a mask register (vm) is specified, VCPOP.M counts only those source mask elements for which the corresponding mask element in vm is true.
RULE 4: VCPOP.M writes the result to x[rd] even when the current vector length (vl) is zero, storing the value 0 in that case.
RULE 5: If VCPOP.M causes a trap, the value of the vstart CSR reported on the trap is always 0.
RULE 6: VCPOP.M raises an illegal-instruction exception if the vstart CSR is non-zero at the time the instruction is executed.
RULE 7: VCPOP.M operates on mask registers as a mask operation.
RULE 8: VCPOP.M must not be affected by the Zvkt extension.

INSTRUCTION: VCPOP.V
FORMAT: vcpop.v vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VCPOP.V reads the source vector register vs2 as the input elements for the population-count operation.
RULE 2: VCPOP.V writes the count of set bits for each element into the destination vector register vd, producing the output elements.
RULE 3: VCPOP.V performs a population count on each element of vs2 by counting the number of bits that are 1 in that element.
RULE 4: VCPOP.V processes element indices i starting at the current vstart value and continuing through the last active element index vl-1.
RULE 5: For each processed element i, VCPOP.V computes the output element as the sum of the SEW individual bit values of the input element and stores the result in vd element i with width SEW bits.
RULE 6: VCPOP.V completes execution with a successful retirement status (RETIRE_SUCCESS) and does not raise an architectural exception.

INSTRUCTION: VCTZ.V
FORMAT: vctz.v vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: The VCTZ.V instruction performs a vector count-trailing-zeros operation on each element of the source vector.
RULE 2: VCTZ.V counts the number of consecutive zero bits starting from the least-significant bit of each element in the source vector register vs2.
RULE 3: VCTZ.V processes elements with indices i from vstart through vl-1, reading each SEW-sized element of vs2.
RULE 4: For a given element, VCTZ.V increments a counter j from 0 upward while the j-th bit of the element is 0 and stops when a bit equal to 1 is encountered.
RULE 5: When VCTZ.V encounters a 1 bit at position j in an element, it writes the integer value j into the corresponding element of the destination vector register vd.
RULE 6: If VCTZ.V scans all SEW bits of an element without finding a 1 bit, it writes the integer value SEW into the corresponding element of vd.
RULE 7: VCTZ.V writes each result using the same element width (SEW) as the source element.
RULE 8: VCTZ.V retires successfully after completing the element-wise computation (RETIRE_SUCCESS).

INSTRUCTION: VDIV.VV
FORMAT: vdiv.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VDIV.VV is a signed divide vector-vector instruction that reads two source vector registers vs2 and vs1 and writes the elementwise results to a destination vector register vd, optionally applying a mask vm.
RULE 2: For each element whose corresponding mask vm bit is 1, VDIV.VV must compute the signed quotient of the vs2 element divided by the vs1 element, rounding toward zero, and must store that quotient into the corresponding element of vd.
RULE 3: For each element whose corresponding mask vm bit is 0, VDIV.VV must leave the corresponding element of vd unchanged.
RULE 4: The signed quotient produced by VDIV.VV for any active element must be identical to the result of the RISC-V scalar signed division operation on the same operand values, including extreme-input cases.
RULE 5: When the divisor element (vs1) is zero, VDIV.VV must produce the same quotient as the scalar signed division by zero, which is all 1s (i.e., -1) for the element width.
RULE 6: When the dividend element is the most-negative integer and the divisor element is -1, VDIV.VV must produce the same quotient as the scalar signed division, which is the most-negative integer (no overflow).

INSTRUCTION: VDIV.VX
FORMAT: vdiv.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VDIV.VX is a vector-scalar instruction that divides each element of source vector register vs2 by the signed integer value in scalar integer register rs1, stores the signed quotient into the corresponding element of destination vector register vd, and applies the vector mask vm to select active elements.
RULE 2: VDIV.VX performs signed integer division according to the RISC-V signed-division rules.
RULE 3: For every active element, VDIV.VX produces the identical quotient as a scalar signed-integer divide instruction executed with the same dividend element and the same divisor value, including all extreme input cases.
RULE 4: When rs1 contains zero, VDIV.VX writes the value –1 (all bits 1) to each active element of vd, matching the result of a scalar signed divide by zero.
RULE 5: When an active dividend element is the most-negative representable integer and rs1 contains –1, VDIV.VX writes the most-negative integer to that element of vd, matching the scalar signed-divide overflow result.

INSTRUCTION: VDIVU.VV
FORMAT: vdivu.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VDIVU.VV performs an element-wise unsigned integer division of the corresponding elements of the source vector register vs2 by the elements of source vector register vs1, producing a quotient for each element in the destination vector register vd.
RULE 2: For every element, the quotient produced by VDIVU.VV MUST be identical to the result of the scalar unsigned divide operation defined by the RISC-V integer divide instruction, including the results for extreme input values.

INSTRUCTION: VDIVU.VX
FORMAT: vdivu.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VDIVU.VX reads the unsigned integer elements of source vector register vs2 and the unsigned integer value in scalar register rs1, divides each element of vs2 by the value in rs1, and writes the resulting quotient to the corresponding element of destination vector register vd while applying the element-mask vm so that only elements with a mask bit set are updated.
RULE 2: VDIVU.VX must produce exactly the same quotient results as the RISC-V scalar unsigned integer divide instruction for every element, including the defined results for extreme cases such as division by zero or overflow.

INSTRUCTION: VFADD.VV
FORMAT: vfadd.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: In the VFADD.VV syntax, vs2 and vs1 specify the two source vector registers whose elements are added element-wise.
RULE 2: In the VFADD.VV syntax, vm specifies the mask register that controls which destination elements are written.

INSTRUCTION: VFCVT.F.X.V
FORMAT: vfcvt.f.x.v vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFCVT.F.X.V converts each signed integer element in the source vector register vs2 to a floating-point element in the destination vector register vd, with both source and destination elements having the same SEW-wide size.
RULE 2: The conversion performed by VFCVT.F.X.V follows the same exceptional-condition handling rules as the corresponding scalar signed-integer-to-floating-point conversion instruction.
RULE 3: VFCVT.F.X.V uses the dynamic rounding mode specified in the frm field for all element conversions.

INSTRUCTION: VFCVT.F.XU.V
FORMAT: vfcvt.f.xu.v vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFCVT.F.XU.V converts each unsigned integer element in the source vector register vs2 to a floating-point element in the destination vector register vd.
RULE 2: VFCVT.F.XU.V operates on vector elements whose size equals the current configured SEW, so source and destination elements are SEW-wide.
RULE 3: VFCVT.F.XU.V uses the dynamic rounding mode specified in the frm field to round the conversion result.
RULE 4: VFCVT.F.XU.V has no rounding-to-zero variant; the rounding mode is always taken from the frm field.
RULE 5: VFCVT.F.XU.V raises the same floating-point exception flags (invalid, overflow, underflow, inexact) as the equivalent scalar unsigned-integer-to-float conversion when those conditions occur.

INSTRUCTION: VFCVT.RTZ.X.F.V
FORMAT: vfcvt.rtz.x.f.v vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFCVT.RTZ.X.F.V converts each floating-point element of the source vector register vs2 to a signed integer element and writes the result to the corresponding element of the destination vector register vd.
RULE 2: VFCVT.RTZ.X.F.V may be executed with an optional mask register vm, and for each element whose mask bit is zero the destination element is not written and retains its previous value.
RULE 3: VFCVT.RTZ.X.F.V must be used only when the element width (SEW) of the source vector register vs2 and the destination vector register vd are identical.
RULE 4: VFCVT.RTZ.X.F.V must round each conversion toward zero, ignoring the dynamic rounding mode specified in frm.
RULE 5: VFCVT.RTZ.X.F.V must handle exceptional conditions (such as overflow, NaN, or inexact results) using the same architectural rules as the corresponding scalar floating-point-to-integer conversion instructions.

INSTRUCTION: VFCVT.RTZ.XU.F.V
FORMAT: vfcvt.rtz.xu.f.v vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFCVT.RTZ.XU.F.V must convert each floating-point element in source vector register vs2 to an unsigned integer element in destination vector register vd by truncating the value toward zero.
RULE 2: VFCVT.RTZ.XU.F.V must round all conversions toward zero and must ignore the dynamic rounding mode specified in the frm field.
RULE 3: VFCVT.RTZ.XU.F.V must apply the same architectural handling of exceptional conditions (such as overflow, NaN, or out-of-range values) as defined for the equivalent scalar floating-point-to-unsigned-integer conversion instructions.

INSTRUCTION: VFCVT.X.F.V
FORMAT: vfcvt.x.f.v vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFCVT.X.F.V must convert each element of the source floating-point vector to a signed integer element in the destination vector.
RULE 2: VFCVT.X.F.V must treat each source and destination element as SEW-wide (single-width) vector elements.
RULE 3: VFCVT.X.F.V must apply the same exceptional-condition handling rules to each element conversion as are defined for the scalar floating-point-to-signed-integer conversion instructions.
