RULE 4: VFCVT.X.F.V must use the dynamic rounding mode currently encoded in the floating-point rounding-mode register (frm) for all element conversions.

INSTRUCTION: VFCVT.XU.F.V
FORMAT: vfcvt.xu.f.v vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFCVT.XU.F.V converts each SEW-wide floating-point element in the source vector register vs2 to an unsigned integer of the same SEW and writes the result to the corresponding element of the destination vector register vd.
RULE 2: VFCVT.XU.F.V must use the dynamic rounding mode specified in the frm field for all element conversions.
RULE 3: VFCVT.XU.F.V must handle exceptional conditions (such as NaN, overflow, or inexact) in exactly the same way as the scalar floating-point-to-unsigned-integer conversion instructions.

INSTRUCTION: VFDIV.VF
FORMAT: vfdiv.vf vd, vs2, fs1, vm
RULE 1: VFDIV.VF divides each element of the source vector register vs2 by the scalar floating-point value held in the scalar register rs1 and writes the resulting elements to the destination vector register vd.
RULE 2: VFDIV.VF uses the optional mask register vm to determine which element positions are active; for each element whose mask bit is clear, VFDIV.VF leaves the corresponding element of vd unchanged.
RULE 3: If the optional mask register vm is not specified for VFDIV.VF, the instruction treats all element positions as active and writes a result to every element of vd.
RULE 4: VFDIV.VF is classified as a vector-scalar floating-point divide instruction.

INSTRUCTION: VFDIV.VV
FORMAT: vfdiv.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VFDIV.VV MUST perform element-wise floating-point division of each element of the source vector register vs2 (dividend) by the corresponding element of the source vector register vs1 (divisor).
RULE 2: VFDIV.VV MUST write the result of each element-wise division into the corresponding element of the destination vector register vd.
RULE 3: VFDIV.VV may be encoded with an optional mask register vm that, when present, applies the standard vector mask semantics to each element of the operation.

INSTRUCTION: VFIRST.M
FORMAT: vfirst.m rd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFIRST.M rd, vs2, vm must locate the lowest-numbered active element of the source mask vector vs2 that has the value 1 and must write that element's index to the integer register x[rd].
RULE 2: VFIRST.M must write the integer value -1 to x[rd] when no active element of vs2 has the value 1.
RULE 3: VFIRST.M must write a value to x[rd] even when the current vector length vl is zero, and the value written in that case must be -1 because no mask elements are active.
RULE 4: When VFIRST.M triggers a trap, the architectural vstart reported with the trap must be zero.
RULE 5: VFIRST.M must raise an illegal-instruction exception if the architectural register vstart is non-zero at the time the instruction is executed.
RULE 6: VFIRST.M is not affected by Zvkt.

INSTRUCTION: VFMACC.VF
FORMAT: vfmacc.vf vd, fs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: If a mask register vm is supplied to VFMACC.VF, only the elements i for which vm[i] is true are considered active, and elements with vm[i] false leave the corresponding destination element unchanged.
RULE 2: If no mask register vm is supplied to VFMACC.VF, all element positions of the destination vector are treated as active.
RULE 3: For each active element i, VFMACC.VF computes the product of the scalar source value T1 and the vector source element T2[i], adds the existing destination element vd[i], and writes the rounded result back to vd[i] using the current floating-point rounding mode frm.
RULE 4: The rounding performed by VFMACC.VF after the fused multiply-accumulate follows the current frm rounding mode and is applied only once per active element.
RULE 5: VFMACC.VF may raise the floating-point exceptions Overflow, Underflow, Inexact, or Invalid as defined for fused multiply-accumulate operations.
RULE 6: VFMACC.VF computes for each element i the product of the scalar floating-point value held in integer register rs1 and the i-th element of source vector register vs2, adds the original i-th element of destination vector register vd, and writes the resulting sum back to vd[i].
RULE 7: VFMACC.VF uses as its scalar operand the floating-point value represented in integer register rs1.
RULE 8: VFMACC.VF overwrites each destination element vd[i] with the fused multiply-add result, replacing the previous addend value in vd[i].

INSTRUCTION: VFMACC.VV
FORMAT: vfmacc.vv vd, vs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFMACC.VV performs a fused multiply-accumulate operation on corresponding elements of two source vectors and an accumulator vector.
RULE 2: VFMACC.VV takes a destination/accumulator vector vd, source vectors T1 and T2 that contain widened FP32 values, and a mask vm, and writes the result to vd respecting the mask.
RULE 3: For each element index i where the mask vm bit is true, VFMACC.VV multiplies T1[i] by T2[i] to produce an unrounded product, adds that product to the original value of vd[i], rounds the sum according to the current frm rounding mode, and stores the rounded result in vd[i].
RULE 4: For each element index i where the mask vm bit is false, VFMACC.VV leaves the element vd[i] unchanged.
RULE 5: The rounding of the sum performed by VFMACC.VV follows the rounding mode specified in the frm register.
RULE 6: VFMACC.VV must raise the same floating-point exceptions (Overflow, Underflow, Inexact, Invalid) as defined for vfwmaccbf16.vv when the rounded result or the operation meets the corresponding exception conditions.
RULE 7: VFMACC.VV overwrites each destination element vd[i] with the result of (vs1[i] * vs2[i]) + vd[i] for every element i that is processed.
RULE 8: VFMACC.VV updates a destination element vd[i] only when the corresponding mask bit in vm is true; when the mask bit is false, vd[i] retains its previous value.
RULE 9: VFMACC.VV performs the element-wise operation only for element indices i that are less than the current vector length, leaving elements with indices i ≥ VL unchanged.

INSTRUCTION: VFMADD.VF
FORMAT: vfmadd.vf vd, fs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFMADD.VF, when executed, replaces each element i of the destination vector register vd with the result of a fused multiply-add operation that multiplies the scalar floating-point value in integer register rs1 by the original value of vd[i] and then adds the element i of source vector register vs2, using the element's floating-point format.
RULE 2: VFMADD.VF performs the multiply and add as a single-width fused operation, meaning the intermediate product and sum are computed without intermediate rounding and the final result is rounded only once to the element's format.
RULE 3: VFMADD.VF overwrites the multiplicand, so the original value of each destination element vd[i] is used as the multiplicand in the fused multiply-add before being replaced by the computed result.

INSTRUCTION: VFMAX.VF
FORMAT: vfmax.vf vd, vs2, fs1, vm
RULE 1: VFMAX.VF vd, vs2, rs1, vm performs the maximumNumber operation between each active element of the source vector register vs2 and the floating-point value held in the scalar register rs1, writing the result to the corresponding element of the destination vector register vd.
RULE 2: VFMAX.VF follows the definition of the maximumNumber operation as defined for the scalar floating-point maximum instructions in version 2.2 of the RISC-V F/D/Q extensions.
RULE 3: VFMAX.VF applies the maximumNumber operation only to those vector elements that are active according to the current vector length setting.
RULE 4: VFMAX.VF uses the optional mask operand vm to control element activity, making an element active only if the corresponding mask bit is set.
RULE 5: VFMAX.VF treats the scalar register rs1 as the single floating-point operand that is compared with each active element of vs2.
RULE 6: VFMAX.VF is a vector-scalar instruction whose operands are ordered as destination vector register vd, source vector register vs2, source scalar register rs1, and optional mask register vm.

INSTRUCTION: VFMAX.VV
FORMAT: vfmax.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VFMAX.VV MUST be recognized as a vector-floating-point maximum instruction.
RULE 2: VFMAX.VV MUST perform the maximumNumber operation on each active element of the vector.
RULE 3: VFMAX.VV MUST exhibit the same behavior as the scalar floating-point maximum instruction defined in version 2.2 of the RISC-V F/D/Q extension.
RULE 4: For each active element, VFMAX.VV MUST compare the corresponding elements of source vector registers vs2 and vs1 and select the greater value according to the maximumNumber rule.
RULE 5: VFMAX.VV MUST write the selected maximumNumber value for each active element into the corresponding element of destination vector register vd.
RULE 6: When a mask vm is provided, VFMAX.VV MUST treat only the elements whose mask bit is true as active and must leave the elements of vd unchanged for which the mask bit is false.

INSTRUCTION: VFMERGE.VFM
FORMAT: vfmerge.vfm vd, vs2, fs1, v0
RULE 1: VFMERGE.VFM must perform its operation on every element index i such that i ≥ vstart and i < vl, independent of the value of the mask bit for that element.
RULE 2: VFMERGE.VFM is encoded as a masked instruction with the vm field set to zero, which causes the instruction to ignore mask-controlled element-wise execution and apply its operation to all active elements.
RULE 3: For each element i processed by VFMERGE.VFM, if the mask bit v0.mask[i] is zero, VFMERGE.VFM must copy the corresponding element vs2[i] into the destination element vd[i].
RULE 4: For each element i processed by VFMERGE.VFM, if the mask bit v0.mask[i] is one, VFMERGE.VFM must copy the scalar floating-point register specified by rs1 into the destination element vd[i].
RULE 5: VFMERGE.VFM must have a vm field value that is not 1 and a vs2 field value that is not v0.

INSTRUCTION: VFMIN.VF
FORMAT: vfmin.vf vd, vs2, fs1, vm
RULE 1: VFMIN.VF reads a scalar floating-point value from integer register rs1 and uses it as the second operand of the minimumNumber operation.
RULE 2: VFMIN.VF reads each active element of the source vector register group vs2 as the first operand of the minimumNumber operation.
RULE 3: For every active element, VFMIN.VF computes the minimumNumber of the element and the scalar value and writes the resulting floating-point value to the corresponding element of the destination vector register group vd.
RULE 4: For every inactive element, VFMIN.VF leaves the corresponding element of the destination vector register group vd unchanged.
RULE 5: VFMIN.VF applies the architectural definition of the scalar floating-point minimumNumber instruction from version 2.2 of the RISC-V F/D/Q extension to each active element-wise computation.

INSTRUCTION: VFMIN.VV
FORMAT: vfmin.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VFMIN.VV uses the instruction format vd, vs2, vs1, vm where vd, vs2, and vs1 are vector registers and vm is a mask register.
RULE 2: VFMIN.VV MUST compute the floating-point minimum of each pair of corresponding elements from vs2 and vs1 and write the result to the matching element of vd for every element that is active according to the mask vm.
RULE 3: VFMIN.VV MUST not modify any element of vd for which the corresponding mask bit in vm is inactive.
RULE 4: VFMIN.VV MUST perform the minimumNumber operation with the same handling of special cases (such as NaNs, signed zeros, and infinities) as the scalar floating-point minimum instruction defined in version 2.2 of the RISC-V F/D/Q extension.

INSTRUCTION: VFMSAC.VV
FORMAT: vfmsac.vv vd, vs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFMSAC.VV performs a floating-point multiply-subtract-accumulator operation that for each element i computes (vs1[i] * vs2[i]) – vd[i] and writes the result to vd[i].
RULE 2: VFMSAC.VV overwrites the subtrahend element in the destination vector register with the computed result for each element i.

INSTRUCTION: VFMSUB.VF
FORMAT: vfmsub.vf vd, fs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFMSUB.VF reads the scalar floating-point register f[rs1] and uses its value as the multiplicand for all element operations of the instruction.
RULE 2: For each element index i that is less than the current vector length, VFMSUB.VF computes the destination element vd[i] as (f[rs1] * vd[i]) minus vs2[i].
RULE 3: When the mask bit for element i is false, VFMSUB.VF leaves the destination element vd[i] unchanged.
RULE 4: VFMSUB.VF updates only those destination elements vd[i] for which the mask bit is true and the element index i is within the current vector length; all other elements of vd remain unmodified.
RULE 5: The multiplication and subtraction performed by VFMSUB.VF are executed as a single fused multiply-subtract operation with no intermediate rounding.
RULE 6: The result of the fused operation performed by VFMSUB.VF is rounded according to the currently active floating-point rounding mode.
RULE 7: If the fused operation of VFMSUB.VF yields a NaN for an element, the NaN is propagated to the corresponding destination element in accordance with IEEE 754 rules.

INSTRUCTION: VFMUL.VF
FORMAT: vfmul.vf vd, vs2, fs1, vm
RULE 1: VFMUL.VF must compute the product of each element of source vector register vs2 and the scalar floating-point value in register rs1, writing the results to destination vector register vd.
RULE 2: VFMUL.VF must operate on single-width floating-point elements, requiring that the element width of vd and vs2 matches the width of the scalar in rs1.
RULE 3: VFMUL.VF must respect the optional mask register specified by vm, performing the multiplication only for elements whose mask bit is set and leaving the remaining elements of vd unchanged.

INSTRUCTION: VFMUL.VV
FORMAT: vfmul.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VFMUL.VV is a vector-vector instruction that multiplies each element of the first source vector register vs2 by the corresponding element of the second source vector register vs1 using floating-point arithmetic.
RULE 2: VFMUL.VV writes the result of each elementwise floating-point multiplication to the destination vector register vd for elements for which the corresponding mask bit in the mask register vm is true.
RULE 3: VFMUL.VV leaves unchanged the elements of the destination vector register vd for which the corresponding mask bit in the mask register vm is false.

INSTRUCTION: VFMV.F.S
FORMAT: vfmv.f.s fd, vs2
RULE 1: VFMV.F.S transfers a single SEW-wide element from element 0 of the source vector register to the destination scalar floating-point register, and it ignores LMUL and any vector register grouping.
RULE 2: VFMV.F.S copies the value of element 0 of vector register vs2 into scalar floating-point register f[rd].
RULE 3: VFMV.F.S must perform its operation and write the destination scalar register even when vstart ≥ vl or when vl = 0.
RULE 4: Encodings of VFMV.F.S with the mask disabled (vm = 0) are reserved.

INSTRUCTION: VFMV.S.F
FORMAT: vfmv.s.f vd, fs1
RULE 1: VFMV.S.F copies the value from the specified scalar floating-point register into element 0 of the destination vector register.
RULE 2: VFMV.S.F ignores the LMUL setting and any vector register grouping when performing the copy.
RULE 3: VFMV.S.F treats all destination vector elements with index greater than 0 as tail elements according to the currently selected tail-agnostic or tail-undisturbed policy.
RULE 4: VFMV.S.F performs no write to the destination register when the architectural register vstart is greater than or equal to the current vector length vl.
RULE 5: VFMV.S.F does not update any element of the destination vector register group when the current vector length vl is zero, irrespective of the value of vstart.

INSTRUCTION: VFMV.V.F
FORMAT: vfmv.v.f vd, fs1
RULE 1: VFMV.V.F must copy the floating-point value held in the scalar register identified by rs1 into every element of the destination vector register group specified by vd.
RULE 2: VFMV.V.F is defined as an unmasked operation, therefore the vm field of its encoding must be 1 and all elements of the destination vector register group are treated as active.
RULE 3: VFMV.V.F must have the vs2 field of its encoding set to the register identifier v0; any encoding with a vs2 value other than v0 is reserved.

INSTRUCTION: VFNCVT.F.F.W
FORMAT: vfncvt.f.f.w vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFNCVT.F.F.W converts each double-width floating-point element in the source vector register group vs2 to a single-width floating-point element in the corresponding position of the destination vector register group vd.
RULE 2: VFNCVT.F.F.W writes a converted element to a destination element only when the corresponding mask bit in vm is 1; when the mask bit is 0 the destination element retains its previous value.
RULE 3: For a given vector length, VFNCVT.F.F.W produces one single-width result element for each active element index up to the configured vector length, reading the double-width source element at the same index from vs2.
RULE 4: When the Zvfhmin extension is implemented, VFNCVT.F.F.W is defined only for SEW=16.
RULE 5: For VFNCVT.F.F.W, EEW=16 floating-point operands are interpreted using the binary16 format.

INSTRUCTION: VFNCVT.F.X.W
FORMAT: vfncvt.f.x.w vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFNCVT.F.X.W must convert each double-width signed integer element in the source vector register vs2 to a single-width floating-point element and write the converted element to the corresponding element of the destination vector register vd.
RULE 2: VFNCVT.F.X.W performs a narrowing conversion, so the width of each destination floating-point element is half the width of the source signed-integer element.
RULE 3: VFNCVT.F.X.W must obey the same vector register overlap constraints that apply to other narrowing instructions.
RULE 4: VFNCVT.F.X.W does not define a specific rounding mode, therefore the result is produced according to the implementation's default rounding behavior for narrowing conversions.

INSTRUCTION: VFNCVT.F.XU.W
FORMAT: vfncvt.f.xu.w vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFNCVT.F.XU.W converts each double-width unsigned integer element in the source vector register vs2 to a single-width floating-point element and writes the result to the corresponding element of the destination vector register vd.
RULE 2: VFNCVT.F.XU.W treats the elements of vs2 as unsigned integers whose width is twice the width of the floating-point elements produced in vd.
RULE 3: If a mask register vm is provided, VFNCVT.F.XU.W updates only those elements of vd for which the corresponding mask bit in vm is true, and leaves all other elements of vd unchanged.
RULE 4: VFNCVT.F.XU.W MUST obey the same vector register overlap constraints as other narrowing instructions, so the destination register vd may not overlap the source register vs2 in any configuration that is prohibited for narrowing operations.

INSTRUCTION: VFNCVT.ROD.F.F.W
FORMAT: vfncvt.rod.f.f.w vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFNCVT.ROD.F.F.W MUST perform a vector narrowing conversion from a wider floating-point element type to a narrower floating-point element type using the round-towards-odd rounding mode.
RULE 2: VFNCVT.ROD.F.F.W narrows each element from a larger floating-point format (such as FP32) to the BF16 format as a halving step in a sequence of conversions.
RULE 3: VFNCVT.ROD.F.F.W applies the round-towards-odd rounding mode to every element it converts, regardless of any other rounding-mode configuration.
RULE 4: After one or more executions of VFNCVT.ROD.F.F.W, a final conversion from FP32 to BF16 may be performed with a rounding mode different from round-towards-odd, and VFNCVT.ROD.F.F.W does not dictate that final rounding mode.
RULE 5: VFNCVT.ROD.F.F.W converts each element from a double-width floating-point format to a single-width floating-point format using rounding toward odd.
RULE 6: VFNCVT.ROD.F.F.W must observe the same vector-register overlap constraints as other narrowing instructions.
RULE 7: If a finite source element exceeds the representable range of the single-width destination format, VFNCVT.ROD.F.F.W converts it to the largest finite destination value having the same sign.
RULE 8: When a conversion is implemented as a sequence of halving steps, using VFNCVT.ROD.F.F.W for all steps except the final step yields results that are equivalently rounded and raises the same exception flags as a direct conversion.

INSTRUCTION: VFNCVT.RTZ.X.F.W
FORMAT: vfncvt.rtz.x.f.w vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFNCVT.RTZ.X.F.W converts each double-width floating-point element in source vector register vs2 to a signed integer element of half the width and writes the converted element to the corresponding element of destination vector register vd.
RULE 2: VFNCVT.RTZ.X.F.W performs the conversion using truncating rounding, i.e., round-toward-zero.
RULE 3: VFNCVT.RTZ.X.F.W must obey the vector-register overlap constraints that apply to all narrowing instructions, allowing vd and vs2 to overlap only in ways permitted for narrowing operations.

INSTRUCTION: VFNCVT.RTZ.XU.F.W
FORMAT: vfncvt.rtz.xu.f.w vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFNCVT.RTZ.XU.F.W must convert each double-width floating-point element in the source vector register vs2 to an unsigned integer element of half the width and write the converted element to the corresponding position in the destination vector register vd.
RULE 2: VFNCVT.RTZ.XU.F.W must perform the conversion using truncating rounding, i.e., round-toward-zero.
RULE 3: VFNCVT.RTZ.XU.F.W must obey the same vector register overlap constraints that apply to other narrowing instructions.

INSTRUCTION: VFNCVT.XU.F.W
FORMAT: vfncvt.xu.f.w vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFNCVT.XU.F.W MUST convert each element of the source vector register from double-width floating-point format to an unsigned integer of the destination element width, placing the converted elements into the destination vector register.
RULE 2: VFNCVT.XU.F.W MUST satisfy the same vector register overlap constraints that apply to other narrowing vector instructions.

INSTRUCTION: VFNCVTBF16.F.F.W
FORMAT: vfncvtbf16.f.f.w vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFNCVTBF16.F.F.W must read each element of the source vector as an IEEE-754 binary32 value, convert it to the bfloat16 format, and write the converted value to the corresponding element of the destination vector.
RULE 2: VFNCVTBF16.F.F.W is available only when the Zvfbfmin vector BF16 conversion extension is implemented.
RULE 3: VFNCVTBF16.F.F.W requires the Zve32f vector extension; the instruction is not defined on implementations that do not provide Zve32f.
RULE 4: VFNCVTBF16.F.F.W converts each 32-bit floating-point element in the source vector register vs2 to a 16-bit bfloat16 element in the destination vector register vd, applying the rounding mode specified in the frm register.
RULE 5: VFNCVTBF16.F.F.W must be executed with a selected element width (SEW) of 16 bits; any encoding that specifies an SEW other than 16 is reserved.
RULE 6: VFNCVTBF16.F.F.W may raise the floating-point overflow exception if a converted value exceeds the maximum representable bfloat16 magnitude.
RULE 7: VFNCVTBF16.F.F.W may raise the floating-point underflow exception if a converted value is non-zero but smaller than the minimum positive normal bfloat16 magnitude after rounding.
RULE 8: VFNCVTBF16.F.F.W may raise the floating-point inexact exception when the conversion of a 32-bit floating-point element to bfloat16 does not produce an exact representation.
RULE 9: VFNCVTBF16.F.F.W may raise the floating-point invalid exception for source elements that are NaN or otherwise undefined for conversion to bfloat16.

INSTRUCTION: VFNMACC.VF
FORMAT: vfnmacc.vf vd, fs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFNMACC.VF computes each active element i as vd[i] = -(f[rs1] * vs2[i]) - vd[i].
RULE 2: For each element i, VFNMACC.VF updates vd[i] with the computed result only if the corresponding mask bit vm[i] is true; otherwise vd[i] retains its prior value.
RULE 3: In VFNMACC.VF the scalar floating-point register rs1 supplies the first multiplicand f[rs1] that is used for all vector elements.
RULE 4: In VFNMACC.VF the source vector register vs2 supplies the second multiplicand vs2[i] for each element i.
RULE 5: In VFNMACC.VF the destination vector register vd serves both as the subtrahend operand and as the register that receives the result, overwriting its previous contents for active elements.
RULE 6: VFNMACC.VF performs the fused multiply-add operation using the floating-point format (single-precision or double-precision) defined by the element type of the vector registers, and the operation is single-width.
RULE 7: VFNMACC.VF does not introduce any additional permission checks, exception behavior, or rounding-mode specifications beyond those defined by the standard floating-point arithmetic for the given element type.

INSTRUCTION: VFNMADD.VF
FORMAT: vfnmadd.vf vd, fs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFNMADD.VF computes each element i of the destination vector register as the negated product of the floating-point scalar register rs1 and the original value of the destination element, minus the corresponding element of source vector register vs2, i.e., vd[i] = -(f[rs1] * vd[i]) - vs2[i].
RULE 2: VFNMADD.VF reads the floating-point scalar register rs1 as the first multiplicand for the fused operation.
RULE 3: VFNMADD.VF reads the original content of the destination vector register vd as the second multiplicand that is overwritten by the result.
RULE 4: VFNMADD.VF reads each element of source vector register vs2 as the addend that is subtracted after the negated product.
RULE 5: VFNMADD.VF is a floating-point negate-multiply-add instruction that overwrites the first multiplicand operand.

INSTRUCTION: VFNMADD.VV
FORMAT: vfnmadd.vv vd, vs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFNMADD.VV computes for each element index i the value vd[i] = -(vs1[i] * vd[i]) - vs2[i].
RULE 2: VFNMADD.VV overwrites the first multiplicand operand, meaning the original contents of vd are replaced by the computed result for each element.
RULE 3: VFNMADD.VV performs the computation element-wise across all elements up to the current vector length.

INSTRUCTION: VFNMSAC.VV
FORMAT: vfnmsac.vv vd, vs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFNMSAC.VV must, for each element i whose mask bit is set, compute -(vs1[i] * vs2[i]) + vd[i] using the element's floating-point format and write the result to vd[i].
RULE 2: VFNMSAC.VV must leave vd[i] unchanged for any element i whose mask bit is not set.
RULE 3: VFNMSAC.VV overwrites the subtrahend operand vd with the computed result for each active element.

INSTRUCTION: VFNMSUB.VF
FORMAT: vfnmsub.vf vd, fs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFNMSUB.VF reads the scalar floating-point value from floating-point register rs1 as operand f[rs1].
RULE 2: VFNMSUB.VF reads each element i of the source vector register vs2 as operand vs2[i].
RULE 3: VFNMSUB.VF reads each element i of the destination vector register vd as operand vd[i] before the instruction writes the result.
RULE 4: For every element i for which the corresponding mask bit in vm is 1, VFNMSUB.VF computes the product of f[rs1] and the original vd[i], negates the product, adds vs2[i], and writes the resulting value to element i of vd.
RULE 5: For every element i for which the corresponding mask bit in vm is 0, VFNMSUB.VF leaves element i of vd unchanged.
RULE 6: VFNMSUB.VF performs the arithmetic using the current floating-point rounding mode and updates the floating-point exception flags according to the result of each element's computation.
RULE 7: VFNMSUB.VF updates only the elements of the destination register vd that are written and does not modify any other architectural registers or memory locations.
RULE 8: VFNMSUB.VF operates on all vector elements up to the current vector length as defined by the vector configuration.

INSTRUCTION: VFNMSUB.VV
FORMAT: vfnmsub.vv vd, vs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFNMSUB.VV MUST compute for each element i that is selected by the mask (or for all elements if no mask is provided) the result vd[i] = -(vs1[i] * original_vd[i]) + vs2[i].
RULE 2: VFNMSUB.VV uses the original value of the destination register vd as the first multiplicand in the fused operation before vd is overwritten.
RULE 3: VFNMSUB.VV uses the source register vs1 as the second multiplicand of the fused operation.
RULE 4: VFNMSUB.VV uses the source register vs2 as the addend of the fused operation.
RULE 5: VFNMSUB.VV MUST leave vd[i] unchanged for any element i whose corresponding mask bit is zero when a mask register is supplied.

INSTRUCTION: VFREC7.V
FORMAT: vfrec7.v vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFREC7.V produces an output element whose sign bit is identical to the sign bit of the corresponding input element.
RULE 2: If the input element x to VFREC7.V is negative infinity, VFREC7.V returns negative zero and raises no exception.
RULE 3: If the input element x to VFREC7.V satisfies –2^(B+1) < x ≤ –2^B and x is normal, VFREC7.V returns a subnormal result y such that –2^(B+1) ≥ y > –2^B and raises no exception.
RULE 4: If the input element x to VFREC7.V satisfies –2^B < x ≤ –2^(B-1) and x is normal, VFREC7.V returns a subnormal result y such that –2^B ≥ y > –2^(B-1) and raises no exception.
RULE 5: If the input element x to VFREC7.V satisfies –2^(B-1) < x ≤ –2^(–B+1) and x is normal, VFREC7.V returns a normal result y such that –2^(–B+1) ≥ y > –2^(B-1) and raises no exception.
RULE 6: If the input element x to VFREC7.V satisfies –2^(–B+1) < x ≤ –2^(–B) and x is subnormal with leading-significand bits "1…", VFREC7.V returns a normal result y such that –2^(B-1) ≥ y > –2^B and raises no exception.
RULE 7: If the input element x to VFREC7.V satisfies –2^(–B) < x ≤ –2^(–(B+1)) and x is subnormal with leading-significand bits "01…", VFREC7.V returns a normal result y such that –2^B ≥ y > –2^(B+1) and raises no exception.
RULE 8: If the input element x to VFREC7.V satisfies –2^(–(B+1)) < x < –0.0, the input is subnormal with leading-significand bits "00…", and the current rounding mode is RUP or RTZ, VFREC7.V returns the greatest-magnitude negative finite value, and raises the inexact (NX) and overflow (OF) exceptions.
RULE 9: If the input element x to VFREC7.V satisfies –2^(–(B+1)) < x < –0.0, the input is subnormal with leading-significand bits "00…", and the current rounding mode is RDN, RNE or RMM, VFREC7.V returns negative infinity and raises the inexact (NX) and overflow (OF) exceptions.
RULE 10: If the input element x to VFREC7.V is –0.0, VFREC7.V returns negative infinity and raises the divide-by-zero (DZ) exception.
RULE 11: If the input element x to VFREC7.V is +0.0, VFREC7.V returns positive infinity and raises the divide-by-zero (DZ) exception.
RULE 12: If the input element x to VFREC7.V satisfies +0.0 < x < 2^(–(B+1)), the input is subnormal with leading-significand bits "00…", and the current rounding mode is RUP, RNE or RMM, VFREC7.V returns positive infinity and raises the inexact (NX) and overflow (OF) exceptions.
RULE 13: If the input element x to VFREC7.V satisfies +0.0 < x < 2^(–(B+1)), the input is subnormal with leading-significand bits "00…", and the current rounding mode is RDN or RTZ, VFREC7.V returns the greatest finite positive value and raises the inexact (NX) and overflow (OF) exceptions.
RULE 14: If the input element x to VFREC7.V satisfies 2^(–(B+1)) ≤ x < 2^(–B) and the input is subnormal with leading-significand bits "01…", VFREC7.V returns a normal result y such that 2^(B+1) > y ≥ 2^B and raises no exception.
RULE 15: If the input element x to VFREC7.V satisfies 2^(–B) ≤ x < 2^(–B+1) and the input is subnormal with leading-significand bits "1…", VFREC7.V returns a normal result y such that 2^B > y ≥ 2^(B-1) and raises no exception.
RULE 16: If the input element x to VFREC7.V satisfies 2^(B-1) ≤ x < 2^B and x is normal, VFREC7.V returns a subnormal result y such that 2^(–B+1) > y ≥ 2^(–B) (subnormal with leading-significand bits "1…") and raises no exception.
RULE 17: If the input element x to VFREC7.V satisfies 2^B ≤ x < 2^(B+1) and x is normal, VFREC7.V returns a subnormal result y such that 2^(–B) > y ≥ 2^(–(B+1)) (subnormal with leading-significand bits "01…") and raises no exception.
RULE 18: If the input element x to VFREC7.V is positive infinity, VFREC7.V returns positive zero and raises no exception.
RULE 19: If the input element x to VFREC7.V is a quiet NaN, VFREC7.V returns the canonical NaN representation and raises no exception.
RULE 20: If the input element x to VFREC7.V is a signalling NaN, VFREC7.V returns the canonical NaN representation and raises the invalid operation (NV) exception.
RULE 21: Subnormal inputs whose magnitude is at least 2^(–(B+1)) cause VFREC7.V to produce normal outputs, whereas other subnormal inputs cause VFREC7.V to produce infinite outputs.
RULE 22: Normal inputs whose magnitude is at least 2^(B-1) cause VFREC7.V to produce subnormal outputs, whereas other normal inputs cause VFREC7.V to produce normal outputs.
RULE 23: When VFREC7.V raises an overflow exception, the exact output value is selected according to the dynamic rounding mode that is in effect at the time of the exception.

INSTRUCTION: VFREDMAX.VS
FORMAT: vfredmax.vs vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VFREDMAX.VS performs a reduction that yields the maximum value of the elements specified by the source vectors.

INSTRUCTION: VFREDMIN.VS
FORMAT: vfredmin.vs vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VFREDMIN.VS MUST compute the minimum of all active elements of source vector register group vs2, using the scalar value held in the first element of source vector register vs1 as the initial accumulator value.
RULE 2: VFREDMIN.VS MUST write the final minimum value to the first element of destination vector register vd and MUST leave all other elements of vd unchanged.
RULE 3: VFREDMIN.VS MUST consider only elements with indices less than the current vector length (VL) when performing the reduction.
RULE 4: VFREDMIN.VS MUST include an element of vs2 in the reduction only if the corresponding mask bit in vm is set to one; elements with a mask bit of zero are excluded.
RULE 5: VFREDMIN.VS MUST perform the comparison and produce the result using the element data type of vs2 (and vs1).
RULE 6: VFREDMIN.VS MUST raise an illegal-instruction exception if the element type is not a supported floating-point type for the minimum operation.

INSTRUCTION: VFREDOSUM.VS
FORMAT: vfredosum.vs vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VFREDOSUM.VS must compute an ordered sum reduction of the elements contained in the source vector groups.
RULE 2: VFREDOSUM.VS writes the result of the ordered sum reduction to the destination register operand vd.
RULE 3: VFREDOSUM.VS uses the source vector register operand vs2 as the primary source of the elements that are reduced.
RULE 4: VFREDOSUM.VS includes the source vector register operand vs1 as an additional source operand as defined by the instruction encoding.
RULE 5: VFREDOSUM.VS includes a mask operand vm.

INSTRUCTION: VFRSQRT7.V
FORMAT: vfrsqrt7.v vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: When the source element of VFRSQRT7.V is a finite negative value (-∞ ≤ x < -0.0), VFRSQRT7.V produces a canonical NaN and raises the invalid operation (NV) exception.
RULE 2: When the source element of VFRSQRT7.V is –0.0, VFRSQRT7.V produces –∞ and raises the divide-by-zero (DZ) exception.
RULE 3: When the source element of VFRSQRT7.V is +0.0, VFRSQRT7.V produces +∞ and raises the divide-by-zero (DZ) exception.
RULE 4: When the source element of VFRSQRT7.V is a positive finite value (+0.0 < x < +∞), VFRSQRT7.V produces an estimate of 1 / √x and raises no floating-point exception.
RULE 5: When the source element of VFRSQRT7.V is +∞, VFRSQRT7.V produces +0.0 and raises no floating-point exception.
RULE 6: When the source element of VFRSQRT7.V is a quiet NaN (qNaN), VFRSQRT7.V produces a canonical NaN and raises no floating-point exception.
RULE 7: When the source element of VFRSQRT7.V is a signaling NaN (sNaN), VFRSQRT7.V produces a canonical NaN and raises the invalid operation (NV) exception.
RULE 8: For all positive normal and subnormal source elements, VFRSQRT7.V produces a normal (non-subnormal, non-special) result.
RULE 9: The result produced by VFRSQRT7.V is independent of the current dynamic rounding mode.
RULE 10: VFRSQRT7.V places the seven-bit table output as the seven most-significant bits of the result significand (after the leading one) and sets all remaining lower bits of the result significand to zero.
RULE 11: VFRSQRT7.V computes the result exponent as floor((3 * B - 1 - normalized exponent) / 2), where B is the exponent bias of the element width.
RULE 12: VFRSQRT7.V copies the sign of the source element to the result element.
RULE 13: When VFRSQRT7.V is executed with SEW = 32, the element value 0x00718ABC yields the result 0x5F080000, and the element value 0x7F765432 yields the result 0x1F820000.

INSTRUCTION: VFSGNJ.VF
FORMAT: vfsgnj.vf vd, vs2, fs1, vm
RULE 1: For each element i that is active (the mask vm is absent or its mask bit is set), VFSGNJ.VF must write to vd[i] a value whose sign bit is the sign bit of the scalar floating-point register rs1 and whose remaining bits are copied from the corresponding bits of vs2[i].
RULE 2: For each element i that is inactive because the mask vm is present and its mask bit is clear, VFSGNJ.VF must leave vd[i] unchanged.
RULE 3: If the optional mask register vm is omitted, VFSGNJ.VF must treat all vector elements as active and update every element of the destination vector vd.
RULE 4: VFSGNJ.VF must not raise any architecturally defined exceptions or cause side-effects beyond the normal vector-scalar register reads and the destination write.

INSTRUCTION: VFSGNJN.VF
FORMAT: vfsgnjn.vf vd, vs2, fs1, vm
RULE 1: VFSGNJN.VF must compute each active element's result by copying all bits except the sign bit from the corresponding element of source vector register vs2.
RULE 2: VFSGNJN.VF must set the sign bit of each active element of the result to the logical negation of the sign bit of scalar floating-point register rs1.
RULE 3: VFSGNJN.VF must write the computed result into destination vector register vd for every element whose mask bit in vm is true.
RULE 4: VFSGNJN.VF must leave unchanged the elements of destination vector register vd for which the corresponding mask bit in vm is false.

INSTRUCTION: VFSGNJN.VV
FORMAT: vfsgnjn.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VFSGNJN.VV must copy all bits except the sign bit from each element *i* of source vector **vs2** into the corresponding element *i* of the destination vector.
RULE 2: VFSGNJN.VV must set the sign bit of each destination element *i* to the logical complement of the sign bit of element *i* of source vector **vs1**.
RULE 3: VFSGNJN.VV must produce element-wise negation of a vector when the two source vectors **vs1** and **vs2** are the same, because the magnitude bits are copied from **vs2** and the sign bit is complemented from **vs1**.

INSTRUCTION: VFSGNJX.VF
FORMAT: vfsgnjx.vf vd, vs2, fs1, vm
RULE 1: VFSGNJX.VF must produce each active element of the destination vector by copying the sign bit from the scalar floating-point register rs1 and copying all remaining bits from the corresponding element of the source vector register vs2.
RULE 2: VFSGNJX.VF must leave any destination element unchanged for which the corresponding mask bit in vm is clear.
RULE 3: VFSGNJX.VF must apply the sign-injection operation using the element width (SEW) currently configured for the vector unit, where the sign bit is the most-significant bit of that element format.

INSTRUCTION: VFSGNJX.VV
FORMAT: vfsgnjx.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VFSGNJX.VV produces each element of the destination vector by copying all bits except the sign bit from the corresponding element of the vs2 source operand.
RULE 2: VFSGNJX.VV yields the absolute value of a vector when the two source operands are the same register, because the resulting elements contain the magnitude of the source elements with a positive sign.

INSTRUCTION: VFSLIDE1DOWN.VF
FORMAT: vfslide1down.vf vd, vs2, fs1, vm
RULE 1: VFSLIDE1DOWN.VF copies the element at index i+1 of the source vector register group vs2 into the destination vector register vd at index i for every index i satisfying vstart ≤ i < vl-1, provided that the mask element v0.mask[i] is enabled.
RULE 2: VFSLIDE1DOWN.VF leaves the destination element vd[i] unchanged for every index i where i < vstart.
RULE 3: VFSLIDE1DOWN.VF writes the floating-point register f[rs1] into the destination element vd[vl-1] when the mask element v0.mask[vl-1] is enabled.
RULE 4: VFSLIDE1DOWN.VF applies the mask-agnostic or undisturbed policy to any destination element vd[i] whose corresponding mask element v0.mask[i] is disabled.
RULE 5: VFSLIDE1DOWN.VF applies the tail policy to every destination element vd[i] where i ≥ vl and i < VLMAX.
RULE 6: VFSLIDE1DOWN.VF does not modify any destination elements beyond the architectural limit VLMAX.
RULE 7: VFSLIDE1DOWN.VF places the scalar argument taken from floating-point register f[rs1] at location vl-1 in the destination vector register only if that element is active; otherwise the update follows the mask-agnostic or undisturbed policy.
RULE 8: VFSLIDE1DOWN.VF exhibits element-wise behavior identical to the integer-register version of VFSLIDE1DOWN, applied to the floating-point scalar argument.
RULE 9: VFSLIDE1DOWN.VF obtains its scalar argument from the floating-point register identified by rs1 (f[rs1]).

INSTRUCTION: VFSQRT.V
FORMAT: vfsqrt.v vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: For each element position where the mask vm is true (or when no mask is applied), VFSQRT.V computes the square root of the corresponding element in vs2 and stores the result in the same element position of vd.
RULE 2: For each element position where the mask vm is false (or masked off), VFSQRT.V leaves the corresponding element of vd unchanged.

INSTRUCTION: VFSUB.VF
FORMAT: vfsub.vf vd, vs2, fs1, vm
RULE 1: VFSUB.VF computes for each element i of the destination vector register vd the value vs2[i] minus the floating-point value in register f[rs1] and writes the result to vd[i].

INSTRUCTION: VFWADD.VF
FORMAT: vfwadd.vf vd, vs2, fs1, vm
RULE 1: VFWADD.VF MUST treat the bits in integer register rs1 as an SEW-wide floating-point scalar value.
RULE 2: VFWADD.VF MUST read each SEW-wide element from source vector register vs2 and add it to the scalar value from rs1 using floating-point addition.
RULE 3: VFWADD.VF MUST write the result of each element-wise addition into the corresponding element of destination vector register vd.
RULE 4: VFWADD.VF MUST produce each result element with a width of 2 × SEW bits, implementing a widening floating-point addition.
RULE 5: VFWADD.VF MUST apply the element-wise addition only to elements for which the mask vm is true, leaving masked-off elements unchanged.

INSTRUCTION: VFWADD.WF
FORMAT: vfwadd.wf vd, vs2, fs1, vm
RULE 1: VFWADD.WF must be a vector widening floating-point add instruction whose result element width is twice the standard element width (2 × SEW).
RULE 2: VFWADD.WF must compute each 2 × SEW result element as the sum of a SEW-wide element from vs2 and the SEW-wide scalar in rs1.
RULE 3: VFWADD.WF must write its results to a destination vector register group vd that holds elements of width 2 × SEW.
RULE 4: VFWADD.WF must read its source vector register group vs2 as containing elements of width SEW.
RULE 5: VFWADD.WF must read the scalar floating-point register rs1 as a SEW-wide operand that is added to every element of vs2.
RULE 6: VFWADD.WF may be executed with an optional vector mask vm; for each element index where the mask bit is 1, VFWADD.WF must perform the addition and write the 2 × SEW result to vd, and for mask bits equal to 0 VFWADD.WF must leave the corresponding element of vd unchanged.
RULE 7: VFWADD.WF must perform the operation element-wise, producing vd[i] = (vs2[i] + rs1) widened to 2 × SEW precision for each active element i.

INSTRUCTION: VFWADD.WV
FORMAT: vfwadd.wv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VFWADD.WV must add each pair of corresponding SEW-wide floating-point elements from source vectors vs2 and vs1 and must write the sum as a 2 × SEW-wide element to the matching element position of destination vector vd.
RULE 2: VFWADD.WV must produce a destination vector vd whose element width is exactly twice the element width of the source vectors vs2 and vs1 (2 × SEW).
RULE 3: VFWADD.WV must operate as a vector-vector instruction, processing all active elements up to the current vector length.

INSTRUCTION: VFWCVT.F.F.V
FORMAT: vfwcvt.f.f.v vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFWCVT.F.F.V must obey the same vector register overlap constraints as other widening vector instructions.
RULE 2: VFWCVT.F.F.V converts each element of the source single-width IEEE floating-point vector to a double-width IEEE floating-point element that exactly represents the original value.
RULE 3: VFWCVT.F.F.V may be implemented as a sequence of one or more doubling steps and must produce results equivalent to those steps without raising any additional floating-point exception flags.
RULE 4: When VFWCVT.F.F.V executes, each source element of effective element width (EEW) 16 is interpreted as a binary16 floating-point value.
RULE 5: VFWCVT.F.F.V converts each binary16 source element to a binary32 destination element.

INSTRUCTION: VFWCVT.F.X.V
FORMAT: vfwcvt.f.x.v vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFWCVT.F.X.V must convert each signed integer element of the source vector register vs2 into a double-width IEEE floating-point element placed in the corresponding element position of the destination vector register vd.
RULE 2: VFWCVT.F.X.V must be executed only when the destination register vd and the source register vs2 satisfy the same vector register overlap constraints that apply to all widening vector instructions.
RULE 3: VFWCVT.F.X.V must produce a double-width floating-point result that exactly represents the original single-width signed integer value for every element, because a double-width IEEE floating-point format can always represent a single-width integer exactly.

INSTRUCTION: VFWCVT.F.XU.V
FORMAT: vfwcvt.f.xu.v vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFWCVT.F.XU.V converts each unsigned integer element in the source vector register vs2 to a double-width IEEE floating-point element and writes the result to the corresponding element of the destination vector register vd, applying the optional vector mask vm.
RULE 2: VFWCVT.F.XU.V must satisfy the same vector register overlap constraints as other widening instructions.
RULE 3: VFWCVT.F.XU.V must produce a double-width IEEE floating-point result that exactly represents the unsigned integer input value for every element.
RULE 4: VFWCVT.F.XU.V must not raise any additional floating-point exception flags.

INSTRUCTION: VFWCVT.RTZ.X.F.V
FORMAT: vfwcvt.rtz.x.f.v vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFWCVT.RTZ.X.F.V converts each element of the source vector register vs2 from a single-width floating-point value to a double-width signed integer value in the destination vector register vd by applying round-toward-zero truncation to the fractional part.
RULE 2: VFWCVT.RTZ.X.F.V uses the round-toward-zero (RTZ) rounding mode, which discards the fractional portion of each floating-point source element during conversion.
RULE 3: VFWCVT.RTZ.X.F.V must obey the same vector-register overlap constraints that apply to other widening vector instructions.

INSTRUCTION: VFWCVT.RTZ.XU.F.V
FORMAT: vfwcvt.rtz.xu.f.v vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFWCVT.RTZ.XU.F.V MUST convert each floating-point element in source vector register vs2 to an unsigned integer element of double the width, rounding toward zero (truncation), and write the result to the corresponding element in destination vector register vd.
RULE 2: VFWCVT.RTZ.XU.F.V performs a widening conversion such that the destination element type is exactly twice the bit-width of the source floating-point element type.

INSTRUCTION: VFWCVT.X.F.V
FORMAT: vfwcvt.x.f.v vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFWCVT.X.F.V MUST convert each element of the source vector register vs2 from a floating-point format to a signed integer of double the element width and store the converted elements in the corresponding positions of the destination vector register vd.
RULE 2: VFWCVT.X.F.V MUST obey the same vector register overlap constraints that apply to other widening vector instructions.
RULE 3: VFWCVT.X.F.V may be implemented as a sequence of one or more widening steps that double the element width at each step, and such an implementation must produce results equivalent to a single-step conversion and must not raise any additional exception flags beyond those defined for the conversion.

INSTRUCTION: VFWCVT.XU.F.V
FORMAT: vfwcvt.xu.f.v vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFWCVT.XU.F.V converts each element in the source vector register vs2 from a floating-point format to an unsigned integer whose element width is double the width of the source element and writes the converted elements into the destination vector register vd.
RULE 2: VFWCVT.XU.F.V must obey the same vector register overlap constraints as other widening vector instructions, prohibiting any overlapping of destination and source registers that is disallowed for widening operations.

INSTRUCTION: VFWCVTBF16.F.F.V
FORMAT: vfwcvtbf16.f.f.v vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFWCVTBF16.F.F.V converts each BF16 element in the source vector to a corresponding FP32 element in the destination vector.
RULE 2: VFWCVTBF16.F.F.V produces a destination vector whose elements are 32-bit floating-point values that represent the same numeric value as the original BF16 elements.
RULE 3: VFWCVTBF16.F.F.V is a reserved encoding when the selected element width (SEW) is any value other than 16.
RULE 4: VFWCVTBF16.F.F.V must perform an exact widening conversion from a BF16 source element to an FP32 result element.
RULE 5: VFWCVTBF16.F.F.V, if the BF16 input element is normal or infinity, must produce an FP32 result whose bit pattern equals the BF16 bits shifted left by 16 positions with the lower 16 bits set to zero.
RULE 6: VFWCVTBF16.F.F.V performs a widening conversion of each BF16 element in the source vector to a corresponding FP32 element in the destination vector.

INSTRUCTION: VFWMACC.VF
FORMAT: vfwmacc.vf vd, fs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFWMACC.VF computes for each element index i selected by the mask vm the destination element as vd[i] = (f[rs1] * vs2[i]) + vd[i].
RULE 2: VFWMACC.VF treats the multiplier inputs f[rs1] and vs2[i] as SEW-wide values while the addend vd[i] and the destination element are 2 × SEW-wide values.
RULE 3: VFWMACC.VF overwrites the 2 × SEW-wide addend with the result of the fused multiply-add operation.

INSTRUCTION: VFWMACC.VV
FORMAT: vfwmacc.vv vd, vs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFWMACC.VV computes for each element i the product of the SEW-wide floating-point values in source registers vs1[i] and vs2[i].
RULE 2: VFWMACC.VV adds the product computed for element i to the current 2×SEW-wide floating-point value stored in destination register vd[i] and writes the sum back to vd[i].
RULE 3: VFWMACC.VV overwrites the original wide addend in vd with the result of the fused multiply-add for each element.
RULE 4: VFWMACC.VV uses SEW-wide inputs for the multiplier operands and a 2×SEW-wide destination and addend width.

INSTRUCTION: VFWMACCBF16.VF
FORMAT: vfwmaccbf16.vf vd, fs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: If the selected element width (SEW) for VFWMACCBF16.VF is any value other than 16, the encoding is reserved.
RULE 2: VFWMACCBF16.VF reads a scalar BF16 value from the floating-point register rs1.
RULE 3: VFWMACCBF16.VF reads each BF16 element from the source vector register vs2 according to the active vector length.
RULE 4: VFWMACCBF16.VF multiplies the scalar BF16 value from rs1 with each BF16 element from vs2 to produce an unrounded product for each element.
RULE 5: VFWMACCBF16.VF adds each unrounded product to the corresponding 32-bit floating-point element of the destination vector register vd.
RULE 6: VFWMACCBF16.VF rounds each sum according to the rounding mode specified in the frm register before writing the result to the corresponding element of vd.
RULE 7: VFWMACCBF16.VF may raise overflow, underflow, inexact, or invalid floating-point exceptions as defined by the IEEE-754 semantics of the rounding operation.
RULE 8: VFWMACCBF16.VF updates only those elements for which the vector mask vm is true; elements with vm false remain unchanged in vd.
RULE 9: VFWMACCBF16.VF has the same architectural effect as executing the sequence: fcvt.s.bf16 T1, rs1; vfwcvtbf16.f.f.v T2, vs2, vm; vfmacc.vf vd, T1, T2, vm.

INSTRUCTION: VFWMACCBF16.VV
FORMAT: vfwmaccbf16.vv vd, vs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFWMACCBF16.VV encoding is reserved when the selected SEW is any value other than 16.
RULE 2: VFWMACCBF16.VV performs a widening fused multiply-accumulate where each pair of BF16 elements from the source registers is multiplied to produce an unrounded product that is added to the corresponding FP32 accumulator element from the destination register.
RULE 3: VFWMACCBF16.VV rounds the result of the unrounded product plus accumulator according to the current rounding mode specified in the frm register.
RULE 4: In the vector-vector form VFWMACCBF16.VV reads BF16 elements from source registers vs1 and vs2, reads the FP32 accumulator element from destination register vd, and writes the rounded FP32 result back to the same destination register vd.
RULE 5: VFWMACCBF16.VV may raise any of the floating-point exception flags Overflow, Underflow, Inexact, or Invalid.
RULE 6: VFWMACCBF16.VV is architecturally equivalent to converting BF16 elements in vs1 to FP32 with vfwcvtbf16.f.f.v, converting BF16 elements in vs2 to FP32 with vfwcvtbf16.f.f.v, and then performing a vector fused multiply-add of the two FP32 vectors into the accumulator vector vd with vfmacc.vv, all under the same mask vm.

INSTRUCTION: VFWMSAC.VF
FORMAT: vfwmsac.vf vd, fs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFWMSAC.VF reads the scalar floating-point value from integer register rs1 and treats it as a SEW-wide operand for the multiply operation.
RULE 2: VFWMSAC.VF reads each element i of source vector register vs2 as a SEW-wide operand for the multiply operation.
RULE 3: VFWMSAC.VF reads each element i of destination vector register vd as a 2 × SEW-wide addend operand.
RULE 4: VFWMSAC.VF computes for each element i the expression (f[rs1] * vs2[i]) − vd[i] using SEW-wide multiplication and 2 × SEW-wide subtraction, producing a 2 × SEW-wide result.
RULE 5: VFWMSAC.VF writes the 2 × SEW-wide result back to element i of destination vector register vd, overwriting the original value.

INSTRUCTION: VFWMSAC.VV
FORMAT: vfwmsac.vv vd, vs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFWMSAC.VV is a widening floating-point fused multiply-add instruction that overwrites the wide addend with the computed result.
RULE 2: VFWMSAC.VV reads each multiplier element vs1[i] and vs2[i] as SEW-wide floating-point values.
RULE 3: VFWMSAC.VV treats each addend element vd[i] and each destination element as 2 × SEW-wide floating-point values.
RULE 4: VFWMSAC.VV computes for every element i the value (vs1[i] * vs2[i]) − vd[i] and stores the 2 × SEW result into vd[i], replacing the previous content.

INSTRUCTION: VFWMUL.VF
FORMAT: vfwmul.vf vd, vs2, fs1, vm
RULE 1: VFWMUL.VF must multiply each element of the source vector register vs2 by the scalar floating-point value held in the scalar floating-point register rs1 and must write the resulting widened product into the corresponding element of the destination vector register vd.
RULE 2: VFWMUL.VF must produce each product in a floating-point format that is twice the precision of the elements of vs2, thereby widening the result before it is stored in vd.
RULE 3: If the optional mask register vm is supplied, VFWMUL.VF must perform the multiplication only for those element positions whose mask bit is set, and must leave the destination element unchanged for positions whose mask bit is clear.
RULE 4: VFWMUL.VF must read its scalar operand from a floating-point register rs1 and must read its vector multiplicand elements from the vector register vs2, while the destination vector register vd receives the results.

INSTRUCTION: VFWMUL.VV
FORMAT: vfwmul.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VFWMUL.VV is defined as a widening floating-point multiply vector-vector operation.

INSTRUCTION: VFWNMSAC.VF
FORMAT: vfwnmsac.vf vd, fs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFWNMSAC.VF computes for every element index i the value -(f[rs1] * vs2[i]) + vd[i] and stores that value into the corresponding element of vd.
RULE 2: VFWNMSAC.VF overwrites the wide addend element in vd with the result of the fused multiply-negate-add operation, so the previous addend value is not retained.
RULE 3: VFWNMSAC.VF uses SEW-wide floating-point values for the scalar register rs1 and the vector source vs2, while each element of the destination vector vd is 2 × SEW bits wide.

INSTRUCTION: VFWNMSAC.VV
FORMAT: vfwnmsac.vv vd, vs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFWNMSAC.VV operates on vector registers vd, vs1, and vs2 and may use an optional mask register vm to control element-wise execution.
RULE 2: VFWNMSAC.VV treats the elements of vs1 and vs2 as SEW-wide floating-point values for the multiplication operand.
RULE 3: VFWNMSAC.VV treats the elements of vd as 2 × SEW-wide floating-point values for the addend and destination.
RULE 4: VFWNMSAC.VV overwrites the wide addend in vd with the result of the fused operation for each element that is active.
RULE 5: For each active element i, VFWNMSAC.VV computes the fused operation vd[i] = -(vs1[i] * vs2[i]) + vd[i] using floating-point arithmetic.
RULE 6: For each element i for which the mask vm is false, VFWNMSAC.VV leaves the corresponding element of vd unchanged.

INSTRUCTION: VFWREDOSUM.VS
FORMAT: vfwredosum.vs vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VFWREDOSUM.VS produces a reduction result whose element width is twice the source element width (2*SEW) and writes that double-width result to the destination vector register.
RULE 2: VFWREDOSUM.VS promotes each SEW-width element in the source vector vs2 to 2*SEW bits before adding it to a 2*SEW-bit accumulator, performing the reduction in the same manner as the single-width reduction case.
RULE 3: VFWREDOSUM.VS ignores any element of vs2 that is masked off by the vector mask vm, handling inactive elements in the same way as vfredosum.vs.
RULE 4: VFWREDOSUM.VS propagates NaN payloads in the same manner as vfredosum.vs, so that if any contributing element is a NaN the final reduction result is a NaN with the appropriate payload.

INSTRUCTION: VFWSUB.VF
FORMAT: vfwsub.vf vd, vs2, fs1, vm
RULE 1: VFWSUB.VF computes each element of the destination vector register vd as the floating-point subtraction of the corresponding SEW-width element from source vector register vs2 and the SEW-width scalar value from source floating-point register rs1, producing a result element of width 2 × SEW.
RULE 2: VFWSUB.VF requires that the destination vector register vd be configured for element width 2 × SEW while the source vector register vs2 and the source floating-point register rs1 are configured for element width SEW.
RULE 3: If a mask register vm is specified with VFWSUB.VF, the instruction updates only those elements of vd whose corresponding mask bit is true; elements whose mask bit is false retain their prior values.
RULE 4: If no mask register is supplied, VFWSUB.VF treats the operation as if all mask bits are true and updates every element of vd.
RULE 5: VFWSUB.VF follows the vector-scalar form, taking a vector source operand vs2, a scalar floating-point source operand rs1, and producing a vector destination vd.

INSTRUCTION: VFWSUB.VV
FORMAT: vfwsub.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VFWSUB.VV computes each destination element as the floating-point subtraction of the corresponding element of source vector register vs1 from the corresponding element of source vector register vs2, producing a result element whose width is twice the original SEW (2 × SEW).
RULE 2: VFWSUB.VV stores each computed widened result element into the corresponding element of destination vector register vd.
RULE 3: VFWSUB.VV may be executed with a mask register vm; for any element whose mask bit is clear, VFWSUB.VV leaves the corresponding element of vd unchanged.
RULE 4: VFWSUB.VV operates in vector-vector mode, requiring both source operands to be provided by vector registers vs2 and vs1.

INSTRUCTION: VFWSUB.WV
FORMAT: vfwsub.wv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VFWSUB.WV performs an element-wise subtraction of each element in source vector register vs1 from the corresponding element in source vector register vs2, producing a result element whose width is twice the standard element width (2 × SEW).
RULE 2: VFWSUB.WV stores each widened-precision subtraction result into the corresponding element of destination vector register vd.
RULE 3: VFWSUB.WV uses an optional vector mask register vm to control element-wise activation, and for any element whose mask bit is clear the destination element in vd is not written and retains its previous value.
RULE 4: VFWSUB.WV takes its first source operand from vector register vs2, which provides the minuend values, and its second source operand from vector register vs1, which provides the subtrahend values.
RULE 5: If the vector mask operand vm is omitted in the VFWSUB.WV instruction, all vector elements are treated as active and the subtraction is performed on every element.

INSTRUCTION: VGHSH.VV
FORMAT: vghsh.vv vd, vs2, vs1
RULE 1: For VGHSH.VV any encoding with SEW other than 32 is reserved.
RULE 2: VGHSH.VV must raise an illegal-instruction exception and cause RETIRE_FAIL when LMUL × VLEN is less than EGW (128).
RULE 3: VGHSH.VV treats each input byte of the source registers as having its bits reversed before any arithmetic is performed.
RULE 4: VGHSH.VV bit-reverses each output byte after the polynomial multiplication to produce the final result in standard GCM ordering.
RULE 5: VGHSH.VV interprets the 128-bit values from the source registers as coefficients of a polynomial over GF(2) with the lowest-addressed byte containing the least-significant coefficients.
RULE 6: For each element VGHSH.VV computes Yi+1 = ((Yi XOR Xi) multiplied by H) in GF(2¹²⁸) modulo the irreducible polynomial x¹²⁸ + x⁷ + x² + x + 1.
RULE 7: VGHSH.VV performs the GF(2¹²⁸) multiplication as a carry-less multiply of two 128-bit polynomials followed by reduction modulo the GHASH irreducible polynomial.
RULE 8: During the multiplication loop VGHSH.VV reduces the intermediate product by XORing with the constant 0x87 whenever the most-significant bit of the shifted H value is 1.
RULE 9: VGHSH.VV processes vector elements from index floor(vstart / EGS) up to floor(vl / EGS) − 1, inclusive, where each element has an effective element width of 128 bits.
RULE 10: For each processed element VGHSH.VV obtains Yi from the destination register, Xi from the first source register, and H from the second source register after applying a byte-wise bit reversal.
RULE 11: VGHSH.VV writes the bit-reversed accumulator Z back to the destination register element as the next partial hash Yi+1.
RULE 12: VGHSH.VV operates with an effective element width of 128 bits, an element group size of 4, and a selected element width of 32 bits for each vector register.
RULE 13: VGHSH.VV is defined as the operation of VGMUL.VV with the first source operand (vs1) hard-wired to zero.
RULE 14: All architectural behavior specified for VGMUL.VV applies to VGHSH.VV when the first source operand is hard-wired to zero.

INSTRUCTION: VGMUL.VV
FORMAT: vgmul.vv vd, vs2
RULE 1: VGMUL.VV operates on 128-bit element groups that consist of four 32-bit elements.
RULE 2: VGMUL.VV requires that the vector length (vl) be set to the number of SEW=32 elements to be processed, and therefore vl must be a multiple of four.
RULE 3: VGMUL.VV requires that the vstart register value be a multiple of four.
RULE 4: VGMUL.VV with a selected element width (SEW) other than 32 is a reserved encoding and its architectural behavior is undefined.
RULE 5: VGMUL.VV treats each 128-bit element of its source operands as a polynomial over GF(2) and produces a 128-bit polynomial result equal to the carry-less product of the two operands reduced modulo the GHASH irreducible polynomial x^128 + x^7 + x^2 + x + 1.
RULE 6: Before the carry-less multiplication, VGMUL.VV reverses the order of bits within each byte of the multiplier element read from Vd and the multiplicand element read from Vs2.
RULE 7: After the multiplication and reduction, VGMUL.VV reverses the order of bits within each byte of the 128-bit product before writing the result back to Vd.
RULE 8: VGMUL.VV raises an illegal-instruction exception and retires with failure if LMUL × VLEN is less than 128 bits.
RULE 9: For each 128-bit element index i from floor(VSTART/EGS) to floor(VL/EGS) − 1, VGMUL.VV reads the i-th element of Vd, applies byte-wise bit reversal, treats the result as the multiplier Y, reads the i-th element of Vs2, applies byte-wise bit reversal, treats the result as the multiplicand H, computes the carry-less product Z, applies byte-wise bit reversal to Z, and writes the final 128-bit value to the i-th element of Vd.
RULE 10: VGMUL.VV does not modify any vector register other than the destination register Vd.
RULE 11: VGMUL.VV is functionally identical to the vghsh.vv instruction when the first source operand of vghsh.vv (vs1) is set to zero.

INSTRUCTION: VID.V
FORMAT: vid.v vd, vm
RULE 1: VID.V writes the index of each element, starting at 0 and incrementing by one for each subsequent element, into the corresponding element of the destination vector register group for element positions 0 through VL-1.
RULE 2: VID.V may be executed with a mask and the mask does not alter the index value that is written to any element that is active under the mask.
RULE 3: VID.V must have the VS2 field set to register v0, and any encoding with VS2 not equal to v0 is reserved.
RULE 4: When the destination element's SEW is wider than the index value produced by VID.V, the index value is zero-extended to fill the entire destination element.
RULE 5: When the index value produced by VID.V would exceed the width of the destination element's SEW, the result is truncated to retain only the least-significant SEW bits.
RULE 6: VID.V must not be affected by the Zvkt extension.

INSTRUCTION: VIOTA.M
FORMAT: viota.m vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VIOTA.M reads a source vector mask register and writes to each element of the destination vector register group the count of mask bits in source elements whose indices are less than that element's index.
RULE 2: If VIOTA.M is masked, only those source mask elements whose corresponding mask bit is enabled are counted in the prefix sum.
RULE 3: If the destination SEW is larger than the number of bits needed to represent the sum, VIOTA.M zero-extends the sum to fill the destination element.
RULE 4: If the sum produced by VIOTA.M exceeds the destination SEW, VIOTA.M retains only the least-significant SEW bits of the sum.
RULE 5: When a trap occurs on VIOTA.M, the trap reports vstart=0 and execution restarts from the first element upon resumption.
RULE 6: If VIOTA.M is executed with vstart nonzero, an illegal-instruction exception MUST be raised.
RULE 7: VIOTA.M is not affected by the Zvkt extension.

INSTRUCTION: VL1RE16.V
FORMAT: vl1re16.v vd, (rs1)
RULE 1: VL1RE16.V loads a whole vector register group consisting of a single vector register from memory.
RULE 2: VL1RE16.V operates like an unmasked unit-stride load with the base address supplied in scalar integer register rs1.
RULE 3: VL1RE16.V computes its effective vector length as evl = NFIELDS × VLEN / EEW, which for VL1RE16.V evaluates to evl = VLEN / 16.
RULE 4: VL1RE16.V does not suppress element writes when vstart ≥ vl; the usual rule that no elements are written if vstart ≥ vl does not apply.
RULE 5: VL1RE16.V is reserved (behavior undefined) if vstart ≥ evl.
RULE 6: VL1RE16.V may raise a misaligned address exception if the base address is not naturally aligned to the larger of 2 bytes (EEW/8) or SEWMIN/8 bytes.
RULE 7: VL1RE16.V transfers VLEN/16 halfwords (i.e., VLEN/16 × 2 bytes) from the memory address in the base register into the destination vector register.

INSTRUCTION: VL1RE32.V
FORMAT: vl1re32.v vd, (rs1)
RULE 1: VL1RE32.V loads a vector register group with VLEN/32 32-bit elements from the memory address contained in the scalar register rs1.
RULE 2: The EEW field of VL1RE32.V MUST be encoded as 32.
RULE 3: The nf field of VL1RE32.V MUST encode NFIELDS = 1.
RULE 4: VL1RE32.V MUST use a destination vector register number that is aligned as a vector register group, and the instruction encoding is reserved if this alignment is not satisfied.
RULE 5: Only NFIELDS values of 1, 2, 4, or 8 are defined for VL1RE32.V; encodings with other NFIELDS values are reserved.
RULE 6: VL1RE32.V computes its effective vector length evl as VLEN / 32, regardless of the current vtype and vl settings.
RULE 7: VL1RE32.V is reserved when vstart ≥ VLEN / 32.
RULE 8: VL1RE32.V writes the raw byte contents from memory directly into the destination register group without any byte-reordering.
RULE 9: VL1RE32.V may raise a misaligned address exception if the base address is not naturally aligned to the greater of 4 bytes (EEW/8) or SEWMIN/8.
RULE 10: VL1RE32.V operates with the base address taken from the scalar x register specified by rs1, analogous to an unmasked unit-stride load.

INSTRUCTION: VL1RE64.V
FORMAT: vl1re64.v vd, (rs1)
RULE 1: VL1RE64.V performs a whole-register load using the base address contained in the scalar integer register rs1.
RULE 2: VL1RE64.V is reserved if the destination vector register numbers are not aligned as a vector register group.
RULE 3: VL1RE64.V is reserved when the architectural register vstart holds a value greater than or equal to evl.
RULE 4: VL1RE64.V loads VLEN/64 doublewords (VLEN/8 bytes) from the memory address in rs1 into the destination vector register.
RULE 5: VL1RE64.V writes the loaded data to the destination register group using the same byte layout as in memory, independent of the element width EEW.
RULE 6: VL1RE64.V may raise a misaligned address exception if the base address is not naturally aligned to the larger of 8 bytes (EEW/8) or the implementation's minimum SEW in bytes.
RULE 7: VL1RE64.V obtains the base address from the scalar integer register specified by rs1, exactly as unmasked unit-stride load instructions do.
RULE 8: VL1RE64.V transfers a number of bytes equal to the current value of the vlenb register.

INSTRUCTION: VL1RE8.V
FORMAT: vl1re8.v vd, (rs1)
RULE 1: VL1RE8.V v3,(a0) loads VLEN/8 bytes from the memory address held in the scalar register a0 into the destination vector register group v3.
RULE 2: The nf field of VL1RE8.V must encode a power-of-2 number of registers and the destination register numbers must be aligned to a vector register group, otherwise the encoding is reserved.
RULE 3: VL1RE8.V must be encoded with NFIELDS equal to 1, and any VL1RE8.V encoding that specifies an NFIELDS value other than 1 results in a reserved encoding.
RULE 4: VL1RE8.V computes its effective vector length as evl = VLEN/8, independent of the current vtype and vl settings.
RULE 5: VL1RE8.V may write vector elements even when vstart ≥ vl, because the usual restriction based on vl does not apply to this instruction.
RULE 6: VL1RE8.V is reserved when vstart ≥ evl (i.e., when vstart ≥ VLEN/8).
RULE 7: VL1RE8.V uses the scalar integer register specified by rs1 as the base address for the memory operation.
RULE 8: An implementation may raise a misaligned address exception for VL1RE8.V if the base address is not naturally aligned to max(1 byte, SEWMIN/8).
RULE 9: VL1RE8.V stores the loaded bytes into the destination register group in the same order as they appear in memory, without any byte-layout transformation.

INSTRUCTION: VL2RE32.V
FORMAT: vl2re32.v vd, (rs1)
RULE 1: VL2RE32.V loads the vector register group v2-v3 with 2 × VLEN/32 words from the memory address contained in scalar register a0.
RULE 2: VL2RE32.V must have the encoded number of registers be a power of 2 and the vector register numbers be aligned as required for a vector register group, otherwise the encoding is reserved.
RULE 3: VL2RE32.V supports only NFIELDS values of 1, 2, 4, or 8; any other NFIELDS value makes the encoding reserved.
RULE 4: VL2RE32.V does not obey the usual rule that no elements are written when vstart ≥ vl.
RULE 5: VL2RE32.V does not treat the condition that vstart exceeds the largest element index for the current vtype as a reserved case.
RULE 6: VL2RE32.V is reserved if vstart ≥ evl.
RULE 7: VL2RE32.V operates like an unmasked unit-stride load, using the base address supplied in the scalar x register specified by rs1.
RULE 8: Implementations may raise a misaligned address exception on VL2RE32.V if the base address is not naturally aligned to the larger of EEW/8 bytes or the implementation's smallest supported SEW size in bytes (SEWMIN/8).
RULE 9: The number of bytes transferred by VL2RE32.V equals 2 × VLEN / 8 bytes and can be determined by reading the vlenb register.

INSTRUCTION: VL2RE64.V
FORMAT: vl2re64.v vd, (rs1)
RULE 1: VL2RE64.V v2, (a0) loads the vector registers v2-v3 with 2 * VLEN / 64 doublewords taken from the memory address contained in a0.
RULE 2: VL2RE64.V supports only NFIELDS values of 1, 2, 4, or 8; any other NFIELDS value is reserved.
RULE 3: VL2RE64.V requires that the destination vector register numbers be aligned as a vector register group, and mis-aligned register numbers make the encoding reserved.
RULE 4: VL2RE64.V operates with an effective vector length evl equal to NFIELDS * VLEN / EEW, independent of the current vtype and vl settings.
RULE 5: VL2RE64.V does not apply the usual rule that no elements are written when vstart >= vl.
RULE 6: VL2RE64.V does not treat vstart exceeding the largest element index for the current vtype as a reserved condition.
RULE 7: VL2RE64.V is reserved if vstart >= evl.
RULE 8: VL2RE64.V may raise a misaligned address exception when the base address in rs1 is not naturally aligned to the larger of EEW / 8 bytes or the implementation's smallest supported SEW size in bytes (SEWMIN / 8).
RULE 9: VL2RE64.V performs memory accesses with the same ordering semantics as unmasked unit-stride load instructions.
RULE 10: VL2RE64.V stores the data for the lowest-numbered destination register at the lowest-numbered memory addresses and places successive vector registers contiguously in increasing memory order.
RULE 11: VL2RE64.V enables software to determine the total number of bytes transferred by reading the vlenb CSR.
RULE 12: VL2RE64.V writes the same byte layout to the destination register group as exists in memory regardless of EEW, allowing the encoded EEW to serve as a hint for implementations that may rearrange data internally.

INSTRUCTION: VL2RE8.V
FORMAT: vl2re8.v vd, (rs1)
RULE 1: VL2RE8.V v2,(a0) loads vector registers v2 and v3 with 2 × VLEN/8 bytes from the memory address held in scalar register a0.
RULE 2: When VL2RE8.V transfers multiple registers, the lowest-numbered destination register receives data from the lowest-addressed memory locations and each subsequent register receives the next contiguous block of memory.
RULE 3: VL2RE8.V loads vector register v2 from the base address and loads vector register v3 from the address offset by VLEN/8 bytes.
RULE 4: The usual rule that no elements are written when vstart ≥ vl does not apply to VL2RE8.V; VL2RE8.V is reserved if vstart ≥ evl.
RULE 5: VL2RE8.V obtains its base address from the scalar integer register specified by rs1, the same register used by unmasked unit-stride load instructions.
RULE 6: Implementations may raise a misaligned address exception for VL2RE8.V when the base address is not naturally aligned to the larger of EEW/8 bytes and SEWMIN/8 bytes; for VL2RE8.V the required alignment is at least SEWMIN/8 bytes because EEW/8 = 1 byte.
RULE 7: The number of bytes transferred by VL2RE8.V can be obtained from the vlenb CSR and equals 2 × VLEN/8 bytes.

INSTRUCTION: VL4RE16.V
FORMAT: vl4re16.v vd, (rs1)
RULE 1: VL4RE16.V loads a whole vector register group consisting of four consecutive vector registers.
RULE 2: VL4RE16.V requires that the NFIELDS field encode a power-of-two number of registers and that the destination vector registers be aligned as a vector register group, otherwise the encoding is reserved.
RULE 3: VL4RE16.V supports only NFIELDS values of 1, 2, 4, or 8, and any other NFIELDS value makes the encoding reserved.
RULE 4: When VL4RE16.V transfers multiple registers, the lowest-numbered destination register receives data from the lowest-numbered memory addresses and each subsequent register receives the next contiguous block of memory.
RULE 5: VL4RE16.V operates with an effective vector length evl equal to NFIELDS multiplied by VLEN divided by EEW, independent of the current settings in vtype and vl.
RULE 6: VL4RE16.V does not apply the usual property that no elements are written when vstart ≥ vl.
RULE 7: VL4RE16.V does not treat the condition that vstart exceeds the largest element index for the current vtype as reserved.
RULE 8: VL4RE16.V is reserved if vstart ≥ evl.
RULE 9: VL4RE16.V uses the scalar x register rs1 as the base address for unit-stride memory accesses, similar to unmasked unit-stride load and store instructions.
RULE 10: Implementations may raise a misaligned address exception for VL4RE16.V if the base address is not naturally aligned to the larger of EEW/8 bytes or the implementation's smallest supported SEW size in bytes.
RULE 11: The byte layout of data loaded by VL4RE16.V into the destination register group is identical to the in-memory byte layout, so the same data is written regardless of the EEW value.

INSTRUCTION: VL4RE32.V
FORMAT: vl4re32.v vd, (rs1)
RULE 1: VL4RE32.V loads a whole vector register group consisting of four consecutive vector registers starting at the specified base register.
RULE 2: VL4RE32.V uses an element width (EEW) of 32 bits as encoded in the mew and width fields of the instruction.
RULE 3: VL4RE32.V has NFIELDS = 4, and encodings with an NFIELDS value other than 1, 2, 4, or 8 are reserved.
RULE 4: VL4RE32.V requires that the encoded number of registers (four) be a power of 2 and that the destination vector registers be aligned as a vector register group, otherwise the encoding is reserved.
RULE 5: VL4RE32.V writes to the destination register group using the same byte layout as the source memory layout, so the data stored in the registers is identical to the memory representation regardless of EEW.
RULE 6: VL4RE32.V operates with an effective vector length evl equal to NFIELDS × VLEN / EEW, which for this instruction evaluates to 4 × VLEN / 32, independent of the current vtype and vl settings.
RULE 7: VL4RE32.V is reserved if the architectural register vstart is greater than or equal to evl, and the usual condition that no elements are written when vstart ≥ vl does not apply.
RULE 8: VL4RE32.V obtains its base memory address from the scalar integer register specified by rs1.
RULE 9: Implementations may raise a misaligned address exception for VL4RE32.V when the base address is not naturally aligned to the larger of EEW/8 bytes or the implementation's minimum supported SEW size in bytes (SEWMIN/8).
RULE 10: VL4RE32.V transfers a total of 4 × VLEN/8 bytes (four 32-bit words) from memory to the vector register group.

INSTRUCTION: VL4RE64.V
FORMAT: vl4re64.v vd, (rs1)
RULE 1: VL4RE64.V loads the vector registers v4 through v7 with doubleword elements from consecutive memory locations starting at the address held in the scalar register specified by rs1.
RULE 2: VL4RE64.V has NFIELDS equal to 4, indicating that four consecutive vector registers are loaded.
RULE 3: VL4RE64.V aligns the vector registers as a register group such that the lowest-numbered register receives the lowest-numbered memory addresses and successive registers are placed contiguously in memory.
RULE 4: VL4RE64.V is reserved when the architectural vstart register holds a value greater than or equal to evl.
RULE 5: The usual rule that no elements are written when vstart ≥ vl does not apply to VL4RE64.V.
