RULE 6: VL4RE64.V uses the scalar x register given by rs1 as the base address for its unit-stride memory accesses.
RULE 7: An implementation may raise a misaligned address exception for VL4RE64.V if the base address is not naturally aligned to the larger of 8 bytes (EEW/8) or SEWMIN/8.
RULE 8: VL4RE64.V transfers a total of 4 × vlenb bytes from memory to the destination vector register group.

INSTRUCTION: VL4RE8.V
FORMAT: vl4re8.v vd, (rs1)
RULE 1: VL4RE8.V loads vector registers v4 through v7 (four consecutive registers) from memory starting at the address held in the scalar register specified as the base address (e.g., a0).
RULE 2: VL4RE8.V requires that the NFIELDS field be a power of two and be one of the values 1, 2, 4, or 8; any other NFIELDS value is reserved.
RULE 3: VL4RE8.V ignores the current vtype and vl settings; these settings do not affect its operation.
RULE 4: VL4RE8.V is reserved when the architectural register vstart is greater than or equal to the computed evl.
RULE 5: VL4RE8.V does not apply the usual rule that no elements are written when vstart is greater than or equal to vl.
RULE 6: VL4RE8.V places the contents of the lowest-numbered destination register (v4) at the lowest-addressed memory location and stores the contents of successive registers (v5, v6, v7) contiguously at increasing addresses.
RULE 7: VL4RE8.V obtains its base address from the scalar integer register specified as rs1 (for example, a0).
RULE 8: VL4RE8.V implementations may raise a misaligned address exception if the base address is not naturally aligned to the larger of the EEW size in bytes (1 byte for EEW=8) and the implementation's smallest supported SEW size in bytes (SEWMIN/8).
RULE 9: VL4RE8.V writes the same byte data to the destination register group regardless of the EEW value because in-register byte layouts are identical to in-memory byte layouts.

INSTRUCTION: VL8RE16.V
FORMAT: vl8re16.v vd, (rs1)
RULE 1: VL8RE16.V loads 8 × VLEN / 16 halfword elements from the memory address held in the scalar register rs1 into the consecutive vector registers starting at the destination register specified.
RULE 2: VL8RE16.V writes the same data to the destination register group regardless of the encoded EEW value.
RULE 3: VL8RE16.V uses an NFIELDS value of 8, which is a supported NFIELDS value; any other NFIELDS value for VL8RE16.V is reserved.
RULE 4: VL8RE16.V computes its effective vector length (evl) as 8 × VLEN / EEW, and this evl is used independently of the current vtype and vl settings.
RULE 5: VL8RE16.V is reserved when the architectural vstart register is greater than or equal to the computed evl.
RULE 6: The usual architectural rule that no elements are written when vstart ≥ vl does not apply to VL8RE16.V.
RULE 7: VL8RE16.V behaves like an unmasked unit-stride load, using the scalar register rs1 as the base address for the memory operation.
RULE 8: VL8RE16.V may raise a misaligned address exception if the base address is not naturally aligned to the larger of EEW/8 bytes or the implementation's minimum supported SEW size in bytes (SEWMIN/8).

INSTRUCTION: VL8RE32.V
FORMAT: vl8re32.v vd, (rs1)
RULE 1: VL8RE32.V loads the vector register group v8-v15 from memory starting at the address contained in the scalar integer register specified as rs1.
RULE 2: VL8RE32.V is reserved if the encoded number of registers is not a power of two.
RULE 3: VL8RE32.V is reserved if the vector register numbers are not aligned as a vector register group.
RULE 4: VL8RE32.V is reserved for NFIELDS values other than 1, 2, 4, or 8.
RULE 5: VL8RE32.V places the bytes for the lowest-numbered vector register (v8) at the lowest-numbered memory addresses and stores each subsequent register's bytes in the next contiguous VLEN/8 bytes.
RULE 6: VL8RE32.V computes its effective vector length evl as NFIELDS × VLEN ÷ EEW, which for VL8RE32.V equals 8 × VLEN ÷ 32.
RULE 7: VL8RE32.V uses the computed evl instead of the current vl and vtype settings.
RULE 8: VL8RE32.V does not apply the usual rule that no elements are written when vstart ≥ vl.
RULE 9: VL8RE32.V is reserved if vstart ≥ evl.
RULE 10: VL8RE32.V obtains its base address from the scalar integer register rs1.
RULE 11: VL8RE32.V may raise a misaligned address exception if the base address is not naturally aligned to the larger of EEW/8 (4 bytes) or the implementation's smallest supported SEW size in bytes (SEWMIN/8).
RULE 12: VL8RE32.V may raise a misaligned address exception if the base address is not aligned to the implementation's smallest supported SEW even when that SEW is larger than the encoded EEW.

INSTRUCTION: VL8RE64.V
FORMAT: vl8re64.v vd, (rs1)
RULE 1: For VL8RE64.V the nf field must encode a power-of-2 number of registers and the destination vector register numbers must be aligned as a vector register group, otherwise the encoding is reserved.
RULE 2: For VL8RE64.V only NFIELDS values of 1, 2, 4, or 8 are supported; any other NFIELDS value makes the encoding reserved.
RULE 3: For VL8RE64.V the NFIELDS value is 8, causing the instruction to load vector registers v8 through v15.
RULE 4: For VL8RE64.V the instruction is reserved when the architectural register vstart is greater than or equal to evl.
RULE 5: For VL8RE64.V the usual property that no elements are written when vstart ≥ vl does not apply.
RULE 6: For VL8RE64.V the usual property that the instruction is reserved when vstart exceeds the largest element index for the current vtype does not apply.
RULE 7: For VL8RE64.V the byte layout of the loaded data in the destination registers matches the byte layout of the data in memory, independent of the EEW.
RULE 8: For VL8RE64.V the base address is taken from the scalar integer register specified by rs1, and the instruction behaves like an unmasked unit-stride load.
RULE 9: For VL8RE64.V an implementation may raise a misaligned address exception if the base address is not naturally aligned to the larger of 8 bytes (EEW/8) or SEWMIN/8 bytes.
RULE 10: For VL8RE64.V v8,(a0) the instruction loads vector registers v8 through v15 with 8 × VLEN / 64 doubleword elements from the memory address held in a0.

INSTRUCTION: VL8RE8.V
FORMAT: vl8re8.v vd, (rs1)
RULE 1: VL8RE8.V must have its nf field set to 8, and any encoding whose nf field is not one of 1, 2, 4, or 8 is reserved.
RULE 2: VL8RE8.V requires that the encoded number of registers be a power of two and that the vector register numbers be aligned as a vector register group, and the encoding is reserved if either condition is violated.
RULE 3: VL8RE8.V loads eight consecutive vector registers v8-v15 from memory, placing the contents of the lowest-numbered register at the lowest-addressed memory location and storing successive registers contiguously.
RULE 4: VL8RE8.V ignores the current vtype and vl settings and always uses the effective vector length calculated in RULE 4.
RULE 5: VL8RE8.V does not apply the usual rule that no elements are written when vstart ≥ vl.
RULE 6: VL8RE8.V is reserved if vstart ≥ evl (i.e., if vstart ≥ VLEN).
RULE 7: VL8RE8.V obtains its base address from the scalar integer register rs1.
RULE 8: VL8RE8.V performs an unmasked unit-stride load, applying no mask to the memory operation.
RULE 9: VL8RE8.V may raise a misaligned address exception on a whole-register load if the base address in rs1 is not naturally aligned to the larger of EEW/8 bytes or SEWMIN/8 bytes; for VL8RE8.V the required alignment is at least SEWMIN/8 bytes because EEW/8 equals 1 byte.
RULE 10: VL8RE8.V writes to the destination register group the same byte layout that exists in memory, so the in-register byte arrangement is identical to the in-memory byte arrangement regardless of EEW.
RULE 11: VL8RE8.V does not modify the architectural registers vl or vtype.
RULE 12: VL8RE8.V loads exactly 8 × VLEN/8 bytes from memory into the vector registers v8 through v15.

INSTRUCTION: VLE16.V
FORMAT: vle16.v vd, (rs1), vm
RULE 1: VLE16.V must load 16-bit elements from memory starting at the address contained in rs1 and increment the address by 2 bytes for each successive element (unit-stride) into the destination vector register vd.
RULE 2: VLE16.V must use the mask encoding vm to control element-wise writes, writing a loaded element to vd only when the corresponding mask bit is 1 and leaving the element in vd unchanged when the mask bit is 0.
RULE 3: VLE16.V specifies that the rs1 operand provides the base memory address from which the first 16-bit element is loaded.
RULE 4: VLE16.V specifies that the vd operand identifies the destination vector register that receives the loaded 16-bit elements.

INSTRUCTION: VLE16FF.V
FORMAT: vle16ff.v vd, (rs1), vm
RULE 1: VLE16FF.V MUST execute as a regular load and MUST only take a trap caused by a synchronous exception on element 0.
RULE 2: If element 0 raises an exception during execution of VLE16FF.V, vl MUST remain unchanged and the trap MUST be taken.
RULE 3: If an element with index greater than 0 raises an exception during VLE16FF.V, the trap MUST NOT be taken and vl MUST be reduced to that element's index.
RULE 4: If no exception is raised during VLE16FF.V, the implementation MAY process fewer than vl elements and reduce vl accordingly, but MUST process at least one element when vstart = 0 and vl > 0.

INSTRUCTION: VLE32.V
FORMAT: vle32.v vd, (rs1), vm
RULE 1: VLE32.V must read a sequence of 32-bit elements from memory starting at the address contained in integer register rs1 and using a unit stride of four bytes for successive elements.
RULE 2: VLE32.V must write each 32-bit element that is successfully read into the corresponding element of the destination vector register group vd for which the element's mask bit in vm is 1.
RULE 3: VLE32.V must leave the destination element unchanged for any element whose mask bit in vm is 0.
RULE 4: VLE32.V must not modify any integer register, including rs1, as part of its normal operation.
RULE 5: VLE32.V may raise a load-address-misaligned or load-access-fault exception if any computed memory address is misaligned or inaccessible.

INSTRUCTION: VLE32FF.V
FORMAT: vle32ff.v vd, (rs1), vm
RULE 1: VLE32FF.V MUST execute as a regular load except that it will only take a trap caused by a synchronous exception on element 0.
RULE 2: If element 0 raises an exception during VLE32FF.V, the trap is taken and vl MUST NOT be modified.
RULE 3: If any element greater than 0 raises an exception during VLE32FF.V, the trap MUST NOT be taken and vl MUST be reduced to the index of that element.
RULE 4: VLE32FF.V MUST access non-idempotent memory locations only when it is known that the corresponding element load operation will not be restarted due to a trap or vector-length trimming.
RULE 5: If vstart = 0 and vl > 0 for VLE32FF.V, then at least one element MUST be processed.

INSTRUCTION: VLE64.V
FORMAT: vle64.v vd, (rs1), vm
RULE 1: VLE64.V loads a sequence of 64-bit elements from memory starting at the address contained in integer register rs1 and stores the loaded elements into the destination vector register group specified by vd.
RULE 2: VLE64.V uses a unit stride of 8 bytes, so the effective address for element i is rs1 plus i × 8 bytes.
RULE 3: VLE64.V applies the vector mask vm such that for each element i, if the corresponding mask bit is 1 the element is loaded from memory, and if the mask bit is 0 the element in vd is left unchanged.
RULE 4: VLE64.V writes only the elements selected by the mask vm; elements not selected retain their previous values in the destination vector register group.
RULE 5: VLE64.V operates on a 64-bit element width irrespective of other vector configuration settings.

INSTRUCTION: VLE8.V
FORMAT: vle8.v vd, (rs1), vm
RULE 1: VLE8.V loads an 8-bit element from memory for each active vector element, using a unit-stride address that increments by one byte for successive elements starting from the byte address held in the scalar register rs1.
RULE 2: VLE8.V stores each loaded 8-bit value into the corresponding element position of the destination vector register vd without any additional widening or sign-extension.
RULE 3: VLE8.V applies the mask operand; when the mask encoding is v0.t, only those element positions whose mask bit is true are loaded, and element positions with a false mask bit retain their previous value in vd.
RULE 4: VLE8.V operates as an unmasked load when the mask operand is omitted, loading all element positions of vd.

INSTRUCTION: VLE8FF.V
FORMAT: vle8ff.v vd, (rs1), vm
RULE 1: VLE8FF.V executes as a normal vector load but takes a trap only for a synchronous exception that occurs on element 0.
RULE 2: If a synchronous exception occurs on element 0 during execution of VLE8FF.V, the vector length register vl is not modified and the trap is taken.
RULE 3: If a synchronous exception would occur on any element with index greater than 0 during execution of VLE8FF.V, the trap is not taken and vl is reduced to the index of that element.
RULE 4: The values written by VLE8FF.V to spurious updated elements do not have to match the contents of the corresponding memory locations.
RULE 5: VLE8FF.V must not access a non-idempotent memory location for an element if the load of that element could be restarted because of a trap or because vl may be trimmed.
RULE 6: When VLE8FF.V executes without raising an exception and vstart = 0 with vl > 0, the implementation must process at least one element and may reduce vl to reflect processing fewer than the original vl elements.
RULE 7: If VLE8FF.V would cause a debug data-watchpoint trap on any element after the first, the implementation should not reduce vl and must deliver the debug trap.

INSTRUCTION: VLM.V
FORMAT: vlm.v vd, (rs1)
RULE 1: VLM.V transfers mask values from memory to the destination vector register.
RULE 2: VLM.V performs a unit-stride load of bytes and operates like an unmasked byte load with EEW = 8.
RULE 3: VLM.V computes its effective vector length as evl = ceil(vl / 8), which corresponds to EMUL = 1.
RULE 4: VLM.V always writes the destination register using a tail-agnostic policy.
RULE 5: VLM.V interprets the vstart register in units of bytes for this instruction.
RULE 6: VLM.V respects the vill field in vtype because vill influences the constraints on vl that VLM.V depends on.

INSTRUCTION: VLOXEI16.V
FORMAT: vloxei16.v vd, (rs1), vs2, vm
RULE 1: VLOXEI16.V loads each vector element using a 16-bit index taken from the corresponding element of source vector register vs2 and a base address held in integer register rs1.
RULE 2: VLOXEI16.V stores each loaded element into the corresponding element of destination vector register vd.
RULE 3: VLOXEI16.V loads data whose width equals the currently configured standard element width (SEW).
RULE 4: VLOXEI16.V performs the indexed loads in element order, ensuring that memory accesses occur sequentially from the lowest to the highest element index.
RULE 5: VLOXEI16.V respects the element-wise mask vm, leaving the destination element unchanged and suppressing the memory access for any element whose mask bit is clear.

INSTRUCTION: VLOXEI32.V
FORMAT: vloxei32.v vd, (rs1), vs2, vm
RULE 1: VLOXEI32.V MUST perform an ordered 32-bit indexed load of SEW (Standard-Element-Width) data.
RULE 2: VLOXEI32.V MUST use the statically encoded EEW that corresponds to the index element width, not the width of the loaded data.
RULE 3: VLOXEI32.V uses vd as the destination vector register that receives the loaded elements.
RULE 4: VLOXEI32.V uses rs1 as the base address register for the memory accesses.
RULE 5: VLOXEI32.V uses vs2 to provide the byte-offsets applied to the base address for each element.
RULE 6: VLOXEI32.V uses vm as the mask register that determines which elements are loaded.

INSTRUCTION: VLOXEI64.V
FORMAT: vloxei64.v vd, (rs1), vs2, vm
RULE 1: VLOXEI64.V MUST execute an ordered load that reads vector elements of standard element width (SEW) from memory using 64-bit index values.
RULE 2: VLOXEI64.V MUST interpret the statically encoded EEW field as the element width for the index vector and must not apply that EEW to the width of the loaded data elements.

INSTRUCTION: VLOXEI8.V
FORMAT: vloxei8.v vd, (rs1), vs2, vm
RULE 1: VLOXEI8.V performs an ordered indexed load of vector elements whose size is defined by the current SEW.
RULE 2: VLOXEI8.V uses the operand order vd, (rs1), vs2, vm where vd is the destination vector register, rs1 supplies the base address, vs2 supplies byte offsets, and vm supplies an optional mask.
RULE 3: VLOXEI8.V executes an ordered 8-bit indexed load, using 8-bit indices to address SEW-sized data elements.
RULE 4: VSXEI8.V is an alias for VLOXEI8.V and therefore exhibits identical architectural behavior.

INSTRUCTION: VLSE16.V
FORMAT: vlse16.v vd, (rs1), rs2, vm
RULE 1: VLSE16.V supports negative and zero stride values.
RULE 2: VLSE16.V element accesses within a constant-stride instruction are unordered with respect to each other.
RULE 3: When rs2 = x0, VLSE16.V implementation may perform fewer memory operations than the number of active elements, and the number of memory operations may differ between dynamic executions of the same static instruction.
RULE 4: When rs2 ≠ x0 and the register specified by rs2 contains zero, VLSE16.V must perform one memory access for each active element, although the accesses are unordered.
RULE 5: VLSE16.V must appear to perform each memory access dictated by its architectural semantics.
RULE 6: VLSE16.V implementations may eliminate memory accesses that cannot be observed by any agent in idempotent memory regions that obey RVWMO.
RULE 7: VLSE16.V implementations must not eliminate memory accesses in non-idempotent memory regions where each access can be observed by a device.

INSTRUCTION: VLSE32.V
FORMAT: vlse32.v vd, (rs1), rs2, vm
RULE 1: VLSE32.V performs element memory accesses in an unordered manner with respect to each other.
RULE 2: When rs2 is not x0 and the value held in register x[rs2] is zero, a VLSE32.V execution must perform one memory access for each active element, although the accesses remain unordered.
RULE 3: VLSE32.V implementations must appear to perform each memory access required by the architectural specification.
RULE 4: In non-idempotent memory regions, a VLSE32.V implementation must not eliminate memory accesses because each access can be observed by a device.

INSTRUCTION: VLSE64.V
FORMAT: vlse64.v vd, (rs1), rs2, vm
RULE 1: VLSE64.V must support both negative and zero stride values.
RULE 2: VLSE64.V element accesses within a constant-stride instruction are unordered with respect to each other.
RULE 3: When VLSE64.V is executed with rs2 ≠ x0 and the value in register x[rs2] equals zero, the implementation must perform one memory access for each active element.
RULE 4: When VLSE64.V is executed with rs2 ≠ x0 and the value in register x[rs2] equals zero, the memory accesses are not ordered.
RULE 5: Implementations of VLSE64.V must appear to perform each memory access prescribed by the instruction.
RULE 6: When VLSE64.V accesses a non-idempotent memory region, implementations must not optimize away any of the accesses because each access can be observed by a device.

INSTRUCTION: VLSE8.V
FORMAT: vlse8.v vd, (rs1), rs2, vm
RULE 1: VLSE8.V must support negative and zero stride values.
RULE 2: VLSE8.V element accesses within a constant-stride instruction are unordered with respect to each other.
RULE 3: When rs2 is not x0 and the stride value loaded from x[rs2] equals zero, VLSE8.V must perform one memory access for each active element (the accesses remain unordered).
RULE 4: VLSE8.V implementations must appear to perform each memory access.
RULE 5: VLSE8.V implementations must not optimize away memory accesses in non-idempotent memory regions because each access can be observed by a device.

INSTRUCTION: VLSEG2E32.V
FORMAT: vlseg2e32.v vd, (rs1), vm
RULE 1: VLSEG2E32.V MUST be implemented as a unit-stride segment load that transfers packed contiguous memory segments into a group of destination vector registers.
RULE 2: For VLSEG2E32.V the number of fields (NFIELDS) MUST be 2 and the element width (EEW) MUST be 32 bits.
RULE 3: When VLSEG2E32.V performs a load, the destination register specified as vd MUST receive the first field of each segment, and the next vector register in the same register group MUST receive the second field of each segment.
RULE 4: VLSEG2E32.V MUST use the current vector length (VL) and element width configuration that were established by the most recent vsetvli instruction.

INSTRUCTION: VLSEG8E8.V
FORMAT: vlseg8e8.v vd, (rs1), vm
RULE 1: When VLSEG8E8.V executes, the destination register specified by vd receives the first field of the loaded segment.
RULE 2: VLSEG8E8.V loads eight vector registers, forming a destination register group that begins with vd.
RULE 3: Each of the eight vector registers loaded by VLSEG8E8.V receives an 8-bit (byte) field from memory.
RULE 4: VLSEG8E8.V uses the address in rs1 as a unit-stride base address for memory accesses.
RULE 5: VLSEG8E8.V applies the optional mask vm to each element load operation.
RULE 6: VLSEG8E8.V has NFIELDS equal to 8 and an element width of 8 bits.

INSTRUCTION: VLSSEG3E8.V
FORMAT: vlsseg3e8.v vd, (rs1), rs2, vm
RULE 1: VLSSEG3E8.V loads three 8-bit elements per segment, storing the first element in the vector register group beginning at vd, the second element in the group beginning at vd+1, and the third element in the group beginning at vd+2.
RULE 2: For each element index i, VLSSEG3E8.V reads a byte from the memory address rs1 + i × rs2 and writes that byte to element i of the first segment (vd[i]).
RULE 3: For each element index i, VLSSEG3E8.V reads a byte from the memory address rs1 + i × rs2 + 1 and writes that byte to element i of the second segment (vd+1[i]).
RULE 4: For each element index i, VLSSEG3E8.V reads a byte from the memory address rs1 + i × rs2 + 2 and writes that byte to element i of the third segment (vd+2[i]).
RULE 5: The stride supplied in rs2 for VLSSEG3E8.V is a signed byte-stride, therefore negative, zero, and positive stride values are all supported.
RULE 6: VLSSEG3E8.V may perform the three field accesses of each segment in any order, and the architectural result is unaffected even when the stride causes the segments to overlap in memory.

INSTRUCTION: VLUXEI16.V
FORMAT: vluxei16.v vd, (rs1), vs2, vm
RULE 1: VLUXEI16.V MUST load 16-bit SEW elements from memory into the destination vector register specified by vd.
RULE 2: VLUXEI16.V computes the memory address for each element i by adding the byte offset contained in element i of vs2 to the base address held in rs1.
RULE 3: VLUXEI16.V performs the loads in an unordered manner, so the architectural result does not depend on the order in which individual element loads are performed.
RULE 4: VLUXEI16.V updates element i of vd only if the corresponding mask bit i in vm is set; if the mask bit is clear, element i of vd retains its previous value.

INSTRUCTION: VLUXEI32.V
FORMAT: vluxei32.v vd, (rs1), vs2, vm
RULE 1: VLUXEI32.V MUST perform an unordered indexed load of SEW data using a 32-bit element index.
RULE 2: VLUXEI32.V MUST use a statically encoded EEW that applies to the index rather than to the loaded data elements.

INSTRUCTION: VLUXEI64.V
FORMAT: vluxei64.v vd, (rs1), vs2, vm
RULE 1: VLUXEI64.V must perform an unordered indexed load that uses 64-bit index elements to compute memory addresses and loads vector elements of the current SEW.
RULE 2: In VLUXEI64.V the EEW field of the instruction encodes the width of the index elements, and the assembler syntax specifies the index operand with the "ei x" form rather than "ex".

INSTRUCTION: VLUXEI8.V
FORMAT: vluxei8.v vd, (rs1), vs2, vm
RULE 1: VLUXEI8.V loads SEW-sized elements from memory into the destination vector register vd using 8-bit indices taken from the source vector register vs2.
RULE 2: For each element i, VLUXEI8.V computes the effective address by adding the sign-extended 8-bit index value from vs2[i] to the base address held in integer register rs1.
RULE 3: VLUXEI8.V updates element i of vd with the memory byte retrieved from the effective address only if the corresponding mask bit vm[i] is 1, and leaves element i of vd unchanged when vm[i] is 0.

INSTRUCTION: VLUXSEG3EI8.V
FORMAT: vluxseg3ei8.v vd, (rs1), vs2, vm
RULE 1: VLUXSEG3EI8.V executed as vluxseg3ei8.v v4, (x5), v3 loads the byte at address x5+v3[i] into element i of v4, the byte at address x5+v3[i]+1 into element i of v5, and the byte at address x5+v3[i]+2 into element i of v6 for each active element i.
RULE 2: VLUXSEG3EI8.V computes each segment base address by adding the scalar base address in rs1 to the corresponding byte-offset element in the index vector vs2.
RULE 3: VLUXSEG3EI8.V does not order the memory accesses to the individual fields within a segment, so the accesses may occur in any order.
RULE 4: VLUXSEG3EI8.V requires that the data vector register group have EEW equal to SEW and EMUL equal to LMUL.
RULE 5: VLUXSEG3EI8.V requires that the index vector register group encode EEW in the instruction and have EMUL equal to (EEW/SEW) × LMUL.
RULE 6: VLUXSEG3EI8.V requires that EMUL multiplied by NFIELDS be less than or equal to 8 for the data vector register group.
RULE 7: VLUXSEG3EI8.V requires that the destination vector register group must not overlap the source index vector register group; overlapping makes the encoding reserved.
RULE 8: VLUXSEG3EI8.V allows restart of an indexed segment load that raises an exception partway through loading a structure because the EMUL × NFIELDS ≤ 8 constraint is satisfied.

INSTRUCTION: VMACC.VV
FORMAT: vmacc.vv vd, vs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VMACC.VV performs an integer multiply-add operation that overwrites the addend vector register.
RULE 2: For each element index i where the mask bit vm[i] is 1, VMACC.VV computes the product of the corresponding elements vs1[i] and vs2[i].
RULE 3: For each active element i, VMACC.VV adds the low half of the product vs1[i] * vs2[i] to the original value of vd[i] and writes the sum to vd[i].
RULE 4: For each element index i where the mask bit vm[i] is 0, VMACC.VV leaves the element vd[i] unchanged.
RULE 5: The low half of the product generated by VMACC.VV is the portion used in the addition; the high half is discarded.
RULE 6: VMACC.VV is classified as a destructive overwrite-addend instruction, meaning the original contents of vd are replaced for active elements.
RULE 7: The specification does not define any additional privilege checks, traps, or exceptions specific to VMACC.VV beyond those applicable to standard integer vector instructions.

INSTRUCTION: VMACC.VX
FORMAT: vmacc.vx vd, rs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VMACC.VX computes, for each element i whose mask vm bit is 1, vd[i] = (low-half(x[rs1] * vs2[i])) + vd[i].
RULE 2: VMACC.VX leaves vd[i] unchanged for each element i whose mask vm bit is 0.
RULE 3: The low-half used by VMACC.VX is defined as the least-significant XLEN bits of the full integer product of x[rs1] and vs2[i].
RULE 4: VMACC.VX overwrites the destination vector register vd with the result of the multiply-add operation, destroying the original addend values.

INSTRUCTION: VMADC.VI
FORMAT: vmadc.vi vd, vs2, simm5
RULE 1: VMADC.VI MUST compute, for each element i of the active vector length, the carry-out of the addition of vs2[i] and the immediate operand imm with no carry-in added.
RULE 2: VMADC.VI MUST write the carry-out bit produced for element i into the mask field of the destination vector register vd.
RULE 3: VMADC.VI MUST operate on and write back all elements of the active vector length even if the corresponding mask bit in v0.mask is zero.
RULE 4: VMADC.VI is the unmasked form of the instruction (equivalent to vm = 1) and therefore MUST NOT use a carry-in.
RULE 5: VMADC.VI MUST NOT read the destination mask register as a source operand; only vs2 and the immediate are source operands.

INSTRUCTION: VMADC.VIM
FORMAT: vmadc.vim vd, vs2, simm5, v0
RULE 1: VMADC.VIM computes each destination mask bit vd.mask[i] as the carry-out of the addition of the corresponding element vs2[i], the encoded immediate, and the corresponding v0 mask bit v0.mask[i].
RULE 2: VMADC.VIM is encoded with vm=0 and therefore must operate on and write back all body elements regardless of the element mask.
RULE 3: VMADC.VIM must apply a tail-agnostic policy for elements beyond the current vector length because it produces a mask value.
RULE 4: For each element i, VMADC.VIM takes the carry-in from the implicit register v0 mask bit v0.mask[i].
RULE 5: VMADC.VIM writes the resulting per-element carry-out into the destination register vd in mask-register format.
RULE 6: VMADC.VIM may write its result to any destination vector register that does not violate the architectural source/destination overlap restrictions.

INSTRUCTION: VMADC.VV
FORMAT: vmadc.vv vd, vs2, vs1
RULE 1: VMADC.VV adds each active element i of vs2 and vs1 using the element width defined by the current SEW, does not add a carry-in, and writes the resulting carry-out to mask bit i of vd.
RULE 2: VMADC.VV processes and writes back every body element of the destination mask register regardless of any element-wise masking.
RULE 3: VMADC.VV always uses a tail-agnostic policy because its result is a mask value.
RULE 4: VMADC.VV is defined only for the unmasked vm field value of 1, and in this form no carry-in is added to the sum.
RULE 5: The encoding of VMADC.VV does not reserve the case where the destination register vd is v0, so vd may be v0.
RULE 6: VMADC.VV does not treat the encoding with destination register v0 as reserved; that reservation applies only to vadc and vsbc.

INSTRUCTION: VMADC.VX
FORMAT: vmadc.vx vd, vs2, rs1
RULE 1: VMADC.VX computes for each element i within the active vector length the carry-out of the addition of vs2[i] and the scalar integer value in rs1, and stores that carry-out into mask bit i of the destination vector register vd.
RULE 2: VMADC.VX always uses a tail-agnostic policy, so elements beyond the active vector length have no defined architectural effect.
RULE 3: VMADC.VX includes no carry-in to the per-element addition because it is the unmasked variant (vm = 1).
RULE 4: VMADC.VX processes and writes back mask bits for all body elements of the active vector length even when a mask register is present.
RULE 5: VMADC.VX writes the resulting mask bits into the mask field of the destination vector register vd.

INSTRUCTION: VMADC.VXM
FORMAT: vmadc.vxm vd, vs2, rs1, v0
RULE 1: VMADC.VXM with vm=0 (masked) adds each element of source vector vs2, the scalar value broadcast from integer register rs1, and the corresponding mask bit from the implicit vector register v0, and writes the resulting carry-out into the mask bit of destination register vd for every element i.
RULE 2: VMADC.VXM with vm=1 (unmasked) adds each element of source vector vs2 and the scalar value broadcast from integer register rs1 without any carry-in, and writes the resulting carry-out into the mask bit of destination register vd for every element i.
RULE 3: VMADC.VXM operates on and writes back all body elements of the destination register regardless of whether the instruction is masked or unmasked.
RULE 4: VMADC.VXM always follows a tail-agnostic policy because its result is a mask value.
RULE 5: VMADC.VXM obtains its carry-in bits exclusively from the mask layout of the implicit vector register v0.
RULE 6: VMADC.VXM stores its carry-out result in the mask layout of the destination vector register vd.

INSTRUCTION: VMADD.VV
FORMAT: vmadd.vv vd, vs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VMADD.VV must read the vector registers vs1, vd, and vs2 and the mask register vm as its operands.
RULE 2: For each element index i where the corresponding mask element vm[i] is true, VMADD.VV must compute the low half of the product of vs1[i] and the original vd[i] and then add vs2[i] to produce the result written to vd[i].
RULE 3: For each element index i where the corresponding mask element vm[i] is false, VMADD.VV must leave the original value of vd[i] unchanged.
RULE 4: The low half of the product used by VMADD.VV consists of the least significant XLEN bits of the full-width integer multiplication result.
RULE 5: VMADD.VV must overwrite the destination register vd with the computed results, thereby destroying the original multiplicand values.

INSTRUCTION: VMADD.VX
FORMAT: vmadd.vx vd, rs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VMADD.VX must, for each element i, multiply the integer value in scalar register x[rs1] by the original value of vd[i], take the low half of the full-width product, add that low half to the corresponding element vs2[i], and write the resulting value back to vd[i].
RULE 2: VMADD.VX must be destructive by overwriting the original contents of the destination vector register vd with the computed results, thereby replacing the first multiplicand.

INSTRUCTION: VMAND.MM
FORMAT: vmand.mm vd, vs2, vs1
RULE 1: VMAND.MM performs an element-wise logical AND of two source mask registers and writes the result to a destination mask register.
RULE 2: For each mask element, VMAND.MM sets the destination mask bit only when the corresponding bits of both source mask registers are set, and clears it otherwise.
RULE 3: Omitting VMAND.MM and masking the second vmfeq instruction causes the invalid-operation exception to be incorrectly not raised when an element of the first operand contains a quiet NaN and the corresponding element of the second operand contains a signaling NaN.
RULE 4: Executing VMAND.MM after the two vmfeq.vv instructions causes the invalid-operation exception to be raised as prescribed for the combination of a quiet NaN in the first operand and a signaling NaN in the second operand.
RULE 5: VMAND.MM computes each mask element of the destination register as the logical AND of the corresponding mask elements of the two source mask registers, i.e., vd.mask[i] = vs2.mask[i] && vs1.mask[i].
RULE 6: Execution of VMAND.MM must not modify the vlmul field in vtype.
RULE 7: Elements of the mask registers with indices less than the current vstart value are left unchanged by VMAND.MM.
RULE 8: After VMAND.MM completes, the architectural vstart register is reset to zero.
RULE 9: VMAND.MM is always executed unmasked, meaning that no elements are considered inactive regardless of any masking configuration.
RULE 10: Encodings of VMAND.MM that set the vm bit to 0 are reserved.
RULE 11: For VMAND.MM, mask elements with indices greater than or equal to the current vector length (vl) are updated according to the tail-agnostic policy.

INSTRUCTION: VMANDN.MM
FORMAT: vmandn.mm vd, vs2, vs1
RULE 1: VMANDN.MM must compute for each element i that vd.mask[i] = vs2.mask[i] && !vs1.mask[i].
RULE 2: VMANDN.MM may use the destination register vd that is the same physical register as either source register vs2 or vs1.
RULE 3: For VMANDN.MM execution, elements with indices less than the current vstart value must remain unchanged.
RULE 4: After VMANDN.MM completes, the architectural register vstart must be reset to zero.
RULE 5: Execution of VMANDN.MM must not modify the architectural register vlmul.
RULE 6: For VMANDN.MM, elements with indices greater than or equal to the current vl value (tail elements) must be updated according to the tail-agnostic policy.
RULE 7: VMANDN.MM is always executed unmasked; there are no inactive elements for this instruction.
RULE 8: Encodings of VMANDN.MM with the vm field equal to zero are reserved.
RULE 9: The form VMANDN.MM vd, src2, src1 implements the logical function src2 && !src1 for each mask element.
RULE 10: The form VMANDN.MM vd, src1, src2 implements the logical function src1 && !src2 for each mask element.

INSTRUCTION: VMAX.VV
FORMAT: vmax.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VMAX.VV is a signed integer maximum instruction that selects the greater of two signed integer operands.
RULE 2: For each element position i, VMAX.VV computes the signed maximum of the i-th element of source register vs2 and the i-th element of source register vs1 and writes the result to the i-th element of destination register vd when the corresponding mask bit is 1 or when no mask is supplied.
RULE 3: When a mask bit for element i is 0 in the mask register vm, VMAX.VV leaves the i-th element of destination register vd unchanged.
RULE 4: VMAX.VV operates on vector-vector data, meaning it processes corresponding elements of the two source vector registers versus each other.

INSTRUCTION: VMAX.VX
FORMAT: vmax.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VMAX.VX is a signed integer maximum instruction that computes the signed integer maximum of the source operands.

INSTRUCTION: VMAXU.VV
FORMAT: vmaxu.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VMAXU.VV MUST compute the element-wise unsigned maximum of the corresponding elements of source vector registers vs2 and vs1 and store each result element into the corresponding element of destination vector register vd.
RULE 2: VMAXU.VV MUST operate in vector-vector mode, requiring both source operands to be vector registers and producing a vector result in a vector register.

INSTRUCTION: VMAXU.VX
FORMAT: vmaxu.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VMAXU.VX computes, for each element index i, the unsigned maximum of the unsigned value of the source vector element vs2[i] and the unsigned scalar value read from integer register rs1.
RULE 2: VMAXU.VX writes the unsigned maximum result into destination vector element vd[i] when the corresponding mask bit in vm is 1, and preserves the prior value of vd[i] when that mask bit is 0.
RULE 3: VMAXU.VX treats the scalar operand obtained from integer register rs1 as an unsigned integer for the maximum comparison with each element of vs2.

INSTRUCTION: VMERGE.VIM
FORMAT: vmerge.vim vd, vs2, simm5, v0
RULE 1: VMERGE.VIM must read its first source operand as the vector register group specified by the vs2 field.
RULE 2: VMERGE.VIM must read its second source operand as the 5-bit immediate encoded in the instruction and sign-extend it to the element width.
RULE 3: VMERGE.VIM must obtain the mask bits for each element i from the mask register v0 (v0.mask[i]).
RULE 4: For each element i with index satisfying vstart ≤ i < vl, VMERGE.VIM must write vd[i] = imm when v0.mask[i] is one, and must write vd[i] = vs2[i] when v0.mask[i] is zero.
RULE 5: For each element i that does not satisfy vstart ≤ i < vl, VMERGE.VIM must leave the destination element vd[i] unchanged.
RULE 6: VMERGE.VIM must operate only on the body elements, i.e., the set of elements from vstart up to the current vector length vl.
RULE 7: VMERGE.VIM must be encoded as a masked instruction with the vm field set to zero, causing the mask bits from v0 to be applied to the operation.

INSTRUCTION: VMERGE.VVM
FORMAT: vmerge.vvm vd, vs2, vs1, v0
RULE 1: VMERGE.VVM operates on all body elements, i.e., the element indices from vstart up to the current vector length vl.
RULE 2: In VMERGE.VVM the first source operand is the vector register group specified by vs2.
RULE 3: In VMERGE.VVM the second source operand is the vector register group specified by vs1.
RULE 4: In VMERGE.VVM the mask operand is supplied by a mask register such as v0.
RULE 5: For each element index i processed by VMERGE.VVM, if the mask bit v0.mask[i] is zero then the destination element vd[i] is set to the element vs2[i].
RULE 6: For each element index i processed by VMERGE.VVM, if the mask bit v0.mask[i] is one then the destination element vd[i] is set to the element vs1[i].

INSTRUCTION: VMFEQ.VF
FORMAT: vmfeq.vf vd, vs2, fs1, vm
RULE 1: VMFEQ.VF compares, for each element i of the source vector register vs2, the floating-point value in element i with the scalar floating-point value held in integer register rs1 and writes a result bit to the corresponding element i of the destination mask register vd.
RULE 2: VMFEQ.VF writes a 1 to mask element i of vd when the compared floating-point value from vs2 element i is equal to the scalar floating-point value from rs1 and neither operand is NaN.
RULE 3: VMFEQ.VF writes a 0 to mask element i of vd when the compared floating-point value from vs2 element i is not equal to the scalar floating-point value from rs1 or when either operand is NaN.
RULE 4: VMFEQ.VF raises an invalid-operation exception if and only if a signaling NaN is supplied as either the vector element or the scalar operand during the comparison.
RULE 5: VMFEQ.VF implements the comparison semantics defined for the scalar floating-point equal-compare instruction for each element of the vector.
RULE 6: VMFEQ.VF stores the destination mask vector in a single vector register according to the element layout defined for mask registers.
RULE 7: VMFEQ.VF may use the same register for the destination mask register vd and the source mask register v0.
RULE 8: VMFEQ.VF performs mask-register writes using a tail-agnostic policy, so any elements beyond the effective vector length are written according to that policy.

INSTRUCTION: VMFEQ.VV
FORMAT: vmfeq.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VMFEQ.VV writes its result to the destination vector register specified by vd.
RULE 2: VMFEQ.VV reads the first source vector register specified by vs2.
RULE 3: VMFEQ.VV reads the second source vector register specified by vs1.
RULE 4: VMFEQ.VV uses the mask register specified by vm to predicate the element-wise operation.

INSTRUCTION: VMFGE.VF
FORMAT: vmfge.vf vd, vs2, fs1, vm
RULE 1: VMFGE.VF writes its result to a single destination mask register (vd) that holds one mask bit per element as defined for mask vectors.
RULE 2: VMFGE.VF permits the destination mask register (vd) to be the same register as the source mask register v0.
RULE 3: VMFGE.VF compares each active element of the source vector register vs2 with the scalar floating-point value in rs1 using the greater-than-or-equal relation.
RULE 4: VMFGE.VF may use an optional mask register (vm) to enable or disable element-wise operation, and elements with a mask bit of zero are not written to the destination.
RULE 5: For each active element, VMFGE.VF sets the corresponding destination mask bit to 1 if the vs2 element is greater than or equal to the scalar rs1, otherwise it sets the bit to 0.
RULE 6: VMFGE.VF raises the invalid-operation exception when either the vs2 element or the scalar rs1 is a signaling or quiet NaN.
RULE 7: When either operand is NaN, VMFGE.VF writes 0 to the corresponding destination mask element.

INSTRUCTION: VMFGT.VF
FORMAT: vmfgt.vf vd, vs2, fs1, vm
RULE 1: VMFGT.VF performs an element-wise greater-than comparison between each element of the source vector register vs2 and the scalar floating-point value in rs1.
RULE 2: VMFGT.VF writes the result of each element comparison to a single destination mask register vd.
RULE 3: VMFGT.VF may use the same physical register for the destination mask register vd as the source mask register v0.
RULE 4: VMFGT.VF operates under a tail-agnostic policy because it writes to a mask register.
RULE 5: VMFGT.VF updates only those elements of vd whose corresponding mask bit in the mask register vm is active; elements with an inactive mask bit are not written.
RULE 6: VMFGT.VF writes a 1 to an element of vd when the element of vs2 is greater than the scalar in rs1 and neither operand is NaN.
RULE 7: VMFGT.VF writes a 0 to an element of vd when the element of vs2 is not greater than the scalar in rs1 or when either operand is NaN.
RULE 8: VMFGT.VF raises the invalid-operation floating-point exception when any operand involved in a comparison is a signaling NaN.
RULE 9: VMFGT.VF raises the invalid-operation floating-point exception when any operand involved in a comparison is a quiet NaN.

INSTRUCTION: VMFLE.VF
FORMAT: vmfle.vf vd, vs2, fs1, vm
RULE 1: VMFLE.VF writes its result to a single destination mask register, and the destination mask register may be the same register as source mask register v0.
RULE 2: VMFLE.VF operates under a tail-agnostic policy, causing mask elements beyond the effective vector length to be written as 0.
RULE 3: VMFLE.VF compares each active element of the source vector register versus the scalar floating-point register using the scalar floating-point compare-less-than-or-equal operation.
RULE 4: VMFLE.VF raises the invalid-operation exception for any element where either the vector element or the scalar operand is a NaN, regardless of whether the NaN is signaling or quiet.
RULE 5: VMFLE.VF writes a 0 to the corresponding mask element for any element where either operand is a NaN.
RULE 6: VMFLE.VF writes a 1 to the corresponding mask element only when the vector element is ordered (neither operand is NaN) and the vector element is less-than-or-equal to the scalar operand.
RULE 7: VMFLE.VF writes a 0 to the corresponding mask element when both operands are numbers and the vector element is greater than the scalar operand.
RULE 8: VMFLE.VF updates mask elements only for those elements for which the mask register vm is true; mask elements with vm false remain unchanged.

INSTRUCTION: VMFLE.VV
FORMAT: vmfle.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VMFLE.VV performs an element-wise comparison for each element i evaluating whether vs2[i] ≤ vs1[i] and writes the Boolean result to the corresponding element of the destination mask register vd.
RULE 2: VMFLE.VV writes a mask element value of 1 when the comparison vs2[i] ≤ vs1[i] is true and writes a mask element value of 0 when the comparison is false.
RULE 3: VMFLE.VV always writes its result to a single mask vector register, and the element layout of that mask register follows the vector register element layout defined for mask vectors.
RULE 4: VMFLE.VV permits the destination mask register to be the same physical register as the source mask register v0.
RULE 5: VMFLE.VV operates under a tail-agnostic policy, meaning that elements beyond the active vector length do not affect the result.
RULE 6: VMFLE.VV raises an invalid-operation exception when either operand of any element is a signaling NaN or a quiet NaN.
RULE 7: When either operand of an element is NaN, VMFLE.VV writes 0 to the corresponding destination mask element.

INSTRUCTION: VMFLT.VF
FORMAT: vmflt.vf vd, vs2, fs1, vm
RULE 1: VMFLT.VF MUST compare each element i of the source vector register vs2 with the scalar floating-point value held in integer register rs1.
RULE 2: VMFLT.VF MUST set destination mask element vd[i] to 1 if and only if the compared element vs2[i] is less than rs1.
RULE 3: VMFLT.VF MUST set destination mask element vd[i] to 0 when vs2[i] is not less than rs1, including any case where either operand is NaN.
RULE 4: VMFLT.VF MUST raise the invalid-operation exception whenever a signaling or quiet NaN appears in either operand of the comparison.
RULE 5: VMFLT.VF MUST write a 0 to the destination mask element corresponding to any element for which a NaN is present in either operand.
RULE 6: VMFLT.VF MUST store the resulting mask vector in a single vector register whose element layout follows the definition in Section 31.4.5.
RULE 7: VMFLT.VF MAY include an optional mask operand vm that controls element activity, and VMFLT.VF MUST leave inactive elements unchanged.
RULE 8: VMFLT.VF MUST operate under a tail-agnostic policy for all compare operations.

INSTRUCTION: VMFLT.VV
FORMAT: vmflt.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VMFLT.VV compares each pair of corresponding elements from source vectors vs2 and vs1 and writes the per-element comparison result to the mask register specified by vd.
RULE 2: VMFLT.VV stores the mask result in a single vector register whose element layout follows the defined layout for mask vectors.
RULE 3: VMFLT.VV always operates under a tail-agnostic policy because it writes to a mask register.
RULE 4: VMFLT.VV raises the invalid-operation exception when any element of either source operand is a signaling NaN or a quiet NaN.
RULE 5: VMFLT.VV writes a zero to a destination mask element when either corresponding source element is NaN.
RULE 6: VMFLT.VV writes a one to a destination mask element when the vs2 element is less than the vs1 element and neither operand is NaN.

INSTRUCTION: VMFNE.VV
FORMAT: vmfne.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VMFNE.VV must write the comparison result to each element of the destination mask register vd, using the mask register vm to control element-wise activity.
RULE 2: VMFNE.VV must set a destination mask element to 1 when the corresponding element of either source vector register (vs2 or vs1) is NaN.
RULE 3: VMFNE.VV must set a destination mask element to 1 when the corresponding elements of the two source vectors are not equal and neither element is NaN.
RULE 4: VMFNE.VV must set a destination mask element to 0 when the corresponding elements of the two source vectors are equal and neither element is NaN.
RULE 5: VMFNE.VV must raise an invalid-operation exception if any source element is a signaling NaN, and must not raise that exception for quiet NaNs or non-NaN values.
RULE 6: VMFNE.VV must store its result mask in a single vector register whose element layout follows the standard vector mask layout.
RULE 7: VMFNE.VV may use the same vector register for the destination mask register and the source mask register v0.
RULE 8: VMFNE.VV must operate under a tail-agnostic policy for all elements beyond the active vector length.

INSTRUCTION: VMIN.VV
FORMAT: vmin.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VMIN.VV computes the element-wise signed integer minimum of the corresponding elements of vs2 and vs1 and writes the result to the corresponding element of vd for each element that is enabled by the mask.
RULE 2: If the vm field is omitted, VMIN.VV writes the result to every element of vd.
RULE 3: When a mask register vm is present, VMIN.VV updates only those elements of vd whose corresponding mask bit is 1, leaving other elements unchanged.

INSTRUCTION: VMIN.VX
FORMAT: vmin.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VMIN.VX MUST be classified as a signed integer minimum operation.
RULE 2: VMIN.VX MUST read a scalar integer operand from integer register rs1.
RULE 3: VMIN.VX MUST read a vector source operand from vector register vs2.
RULE 4: For each active element index i, VMIN.VX MUST compute the signed minimum of the scalar value in rs1 and the element vs2[i] and produce that value as result element i.
RULE 5: VMIN.VX MUST write each result element to the corresponding element of destination vector register vd for all element positions for which the mask vm is true, or for all elements when the mask is omitted.
RULE 6: VMIN.VX MUST leave unchanged any destination element for which the mask vm is false when a mask is provided.
RULE 7: VMIN.VX MUST treat the mask operand vm as optional, and when it is not provided all vector elements are considered active.

INSTRUCTION: VMINU.VV
FORMAT: vminu.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VMINU.VV must compute for each element the smaller of the two unsigned integer values taken from the corresponding elements of source vector registers vs2 and vs1.
RULE 2: VMINU.VV applies the mask register vm to predicate the element-wise operation, updating only those elements for which the corresponding mask bit is true.

INSTRUCTION: VMNAND.MM
FORMAT: vmnand.mm vd, vs2, vs1
RULE 1: VMNAND.MM vd, vs2, vs1 computes each mask element vd.mask[i] as the logical NAND of the corresponding mask elements vs2.mask[i] and vs1.mask[i], i.e., vd.mask[i] = !(vs2.mask[i] && vs1.mask[i]).
RULE 2: VMNAND.MM may write its result to a destination vector register that is the same register as either source vector register.
RULE 3: VMNAND.MM leaves mask elements with indices less than the current vstart unchanged and resets vstart to zero after the instruction completes.
RULE 4: VMNAND.MM is always executed unmasked, therefore no element is considered inactive and any encoding with the vm field equal to zero is reserved.
RULE 5: VMNAND.MM updates mask elements whose indices are greater than or equal to the current vl according to the tail-agnostic policy.
RULE 6: VMNAND.MM operates on a single vector register irrespective of the vlmul setting in vtype.
RULE 7: VMNAND.MM does not modify the vlmul field in vtype.
RULE 8: When the two source operands of VMNAND.MM refer to the same mask register, the instruction produces the logical NOT of that mask register.
RULE 9: When the two source operands of VMNAND.MM refer to different mask registers, the instruction produces the logical NAND of the two source mask registers.

INSTRUCTION: VMNOR.MM
FORMAT: vmnor.mm vd, vs2, vs1
RULE 1: VMNOR.MM computes each mask element as the logical NOR of the corresponding bits of the two source mask registers, setting vd.mask[i] to the negation of (vs2.mask[i] OR vs1.mask[i]).
RULE 2: VMNOR.MM operates on mask registers where each element is a single bit.
RULE 3: VMNOR.MM does not modify the vlmul field.
RULE 4: VMNOR.MM permits the destination register vd to be the same register as either source register vs1 or vs2.
RULE 5: VMNOR.MM leaves mask elements with indices less than vstart unchanged.
RULE 6: VMNOR.MM resets vstart to zero after execution.
RULE 7: VMNOR.MM is always executed unmasked, meaning there are no inactive elements during its operation.
RULE 8: VMNOR.MM treats encodings with the vm field equal to zero as reserved.
RULE 9: VMNOR.MM updates mask elements with indices greater than or equal to vl according to the tail-agnostic policy.

INSTRUCTION: VMOR.MM
FORMAT: vmor.mm vd, vs2, vs1
RULE 1: VMOR.MM does not modify the vlmul architectural register.
RULE 2: VMOR.MM permits the destination register to be the same as either source register.
RULE 3: For every element index i, VMOR.MM sets vd.mask[i] to the logical OR of vs2.mask[i] and vs1.mask[i].
RULE 4: VMOR.MM leaves mask elements with indices less than the current vstart unchanged and resets vstart to zero after the instruction completes.
RULE 5: VMOR.MM is always executed unmasked, so no elements are inactive during its execution.
RULE 6: Encodings of VMOR.MM with the vm field equal to zero are reserved.
RULE 7: VMOR.MM updates mask elements whose indices are greater than or equal to vl (tail elements) according to the tail-agnostic policy.

INSTRUCTION: VMORN.MM
FORMAT: vmorn.mm vd, vs2, vs1
RULE 1: VMORN.MM computes each mask element of the destination register vd as the logical OR of the corresponding element of source register vs2 and the logical NOT of the corresponding element of source register vs1.
RULE 2: VMORN.MM treats each element of a mask register as a single-bit value.
RULE 3: VMORN.MM always uses a single vector register width regardless of the vlmul setting in vtype, and the execution of VMORN.MM does not change the architectural value of vlmul.
RULE 4: VMORN.MM permits the destination mask register vd to be the same architectural register as either source register vs1 or source register vs2.
RULE 5: VMORN.MM leaves vd.mask[i] unchanged for every element index i that is less than the current value of vstart.
RULE 6: VMORN.MM resets the architectural register vstart to zero after execution.
RULE 7: VMORN.MM is defined only for encodings with the mask bit vm set to one; encodings with vm equal to zero are reserved.
RULE 8: VMORN.MM has no inactive elements; all element positions are treated as active during the operation.
RULE 9: VMORN.MM updates vd.mask[i] for every element index i that is greater than or equal to the current vector length vl according to the tail-agnostic policy, writing the computed logical result to the destination element.
RULE 10: VMORN.MM always applies the NOT operation to the second source operand and ORs the first source operand with that negated value, regardless of which source register appears first in the instruction syntax.

INSTRUCTION: VMSBC.VV
FORMAT: vmsbc.vv vd, vs2, vs1
RULE 1: VMSBC.VV takes a destination vector register vd and source vector registers vs2 and vs1 and performs a vector-vector subtraction without using any borrow-in.
RULE 2: For each element index i, VMSBC.VV sets vd.mask[i] to the borrow-out resulting from subtracting the i-th element of vs1 from the i-th element of vs2.
RULE 3: The borrow-out produced by VMSBC.VV for an element is 1 if and only if the full-precision difference (vs2[i] – vs1[i]) is negative before any truncation, otherwise the borrow-out is 0.
RULE 4: VMSBC.VV always follows a tail-agnostic policy, meaning that tail elements are not written and do not affect the execution of the instruction.
RULE 5: VMSBC.VV operates on and writes back mask bits for all body elements of the vector register group, even when element masking is enabled.
RULE 6: The mask bits written by VMSBC.VV are stored in the mask-register layout of the destination vector register vd.
RULE 7: When the vector mask register vm is set to 1 (unmasked execution), VMSBC.VV does not use any borrow-in and does not read the mask bits of v0.

INSTRUCTION: VMSBC.VVM
FORMAT: vmsbc.vvm vd, vs2, vs1, v0
RULE 1: VMSBC.VVM subtracts each element of source vector register vs1 from the corresponding element of source vector register vs2 and also subtracts the borrow-in bit supplied by the mask field of the implicit v0 register for each element.
RULE 2: VMSBC.VVM must obtain its borrow-in (carry-in) for each element exclusively from the mask bit v0.mask[i] of the implicit v0 register.
RULE 3: VMSBC.VVM writes the borrow-out (carry-out) for each element to the mask field of the destination vector register vd, and the destination register may be any vector register that satisfies the architectural source/destination overlap restrictions.
RULE 4: VMSBC.VVM sets vd.mask[i] to 1 if and only if the untruncated subtraction result vs2[i] – vs1[i] – v0.mask[i] is negative; otherwise vd.mask[i] is set to 0.
RULE 5: VMSBC.VVM is a masked operation with vm = 0, meaning the mask register does not inhibit element processing.
RULE 6: VMSBC.VVM processes and writes back a result for every element in the active vector length even when the corresponding mask bit is cleared.
RULE 7: VMSBC.VVM always applies a tail-agnostic policy because it produces a mask value as its result.

INSTRUCTION: VMSBC.VX
FORMAT: vmsbc.vx vd, vs2, rs1
RULE 1: VMSBC.VX MUST read the scalar integer value from integer register rs1, sign-extend it to the element width, and subtract it from each element of source vector register vs2 to compute a borrow output for each element.
RULE 2: VMSBC.VX MUST write the borrow output for element i as a single-bit mask value to the corresponding mask bit vd.mask[i] of the destination vector register vd.
RULE 3: VMSBC.VX defines the borrow output to be 1 if and only if the untruncated difference vs2[i] − x[rs1] is negative; otherwise the borrow output is 0.
RULE 4: VMSBC.VX MUST write mask bits for all body elements of the destination register even if the operation is masked.
RULE 5: VMSBC.VX produces a mask register as its architectural result and does NOT produce any SEW-width data result.
RULE 6: VMSBC.VX MUST store the mask bits in the destination register using the mask-register layout defined for mask registers.

INSTRUCTION: VMSBC.VXM
FORMAT: vmsbc.vxm vd, vs2, rs1, v0
RULE 1: VMSBC.VXM performs a vector-scalar masked subtraction that writes the borrow-out to the mask field of the destination vector register vd.
RULE 2: For each element index i within the active vector length, VMSBC.VXM computes difference = vs2[i] – x[rs1] – v0.mask[i].
RULE 3: VMSBC.VXM sets vd.mask[i] to 1 if the pre-truncation difference for element i is negative, otherwise it sets vd.mask[i] to 0.
RULE 4: The borrow-in used by VMSBC.VXM for each element i is taken from the mask bit v0.mask[i] of the implicit v0 register.
RULE 5: The borrow-out produced by VMSBC.VXM for each element i is stored as the mask bit vd.mask[i] of the destination register.
RULE 6: VMSBC.VXM processes all body elements of the vector, even for elements that are masked off.
RULE 7: VMSBC.VXM always operates with a tail-agnostic policy because it produces a mask result.
RULE 8: VMSBC.VXM must obey the same source-destination overlap restrictions as other masked vector instructions that write a mask result, and no special exception is provided for it.

INSTRUCTION: VMSBF.M
FORMAT: vmsbf.m vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VMSBF.M may write any element in the tail of the result with the mask value that would be produced by the mask-producing operation using vl = VLEN, SEW = 8, and LMUL = 8, allowing all bits of the mask register to be overwritten.
RULE 2: VMSBF.M always treats mask destination tail elements as tail-agnostic regardless of the setting of the vta bit.
RULE 3: VMSBF.M reads a mask register as its source operand and writes the result to a mask register destination operand.
RULE 4: VMSBF.M writes a 1 to each active destination mask element that precedes the first active source mask element containing a 1, and writes a 0 to that first 1 element and to all subsequent active destination elements.
RULE 5: If no active source mask element contains a 1, VMSBF.M writes a 1 to every active destination mask element.
RULE 6: When VMSBF.M causes a trap, the vstart field in the trap record is reported as 0.
RULE 7: VMSBF.M raises an illegal-instruction exception if it is executed with vstart not equal to 0.
RULE 8: VMSBF.M requires that the destination register be different from the source register, and if masking is active the destination register must also be different from the mask register v0.
RULE 9: VMSBF.M vd, vs2 MUST set each mask bit in the destination register vd to true for vector element positions that are earlier than the position of the first true mask bit in the source register vs2, and to false for that position and all later positions.
RULE 10: When the source mask register vs2 is generated by vmseq.vi to flag zero-byte flags, VMSBF.M vd, vs2 produces a destination mask vd that has true bits for all elements preceding the first zero-byte flag in vs2 and false bits for the element containing the zero-byte flag and all subsequent elements.

INSTRUCTION: VMSEQ.VI
FORMAT: vmseq.vi vd, vs2, simm5, vm
RULE 1: VMSEQ.VI writes a 1 to each element of the destination mask register if the element-wise comparison of the source vector register vs2 with the immediate value evaluates to true, and writes a 0 otherwise.
RULE 2: VMSEQ.VI always writes to a mask register and therefore operates under a tail-agnostic policy, meaning the contents of elements beyond the effective vector length are not architecturally defined.
RULE 3: VMSEQ.VI may specify the same vector register for the destination mask register (vd) and the source mask register (v0) without violating architectural rules.
RULE 4: When the destination mask register of VMSEQ.VI is v0, each resulting mask element equals the logical AND of the newly computed comparison result and the original value of the corresponding element in v0, implementing a mask-undisturbed policy.

INSTRUCTION: VMSEQ.VV
FORMAT: vmseq.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VMSEQ.VV MUST write a 1 to each destination mask-register element when the element-wise equality comparison evaluates to true and MUST write a 0 to each destination mask-register element when the comparison evaluates to false.
RULE 2: VMSEQ.VV writes its result to a single vector register that holds the destination mask vector.
RULE 3: When the destination register of VMSEQ.VV is v0, the instruction ANDs the newly computed mask bits with the existing bits of v0, thereby preserving bits that were previously zero (mask-undisturbed policy).
RULE 4: VMSEQ.VV always writes all elements of the destination mask register and therefore operates under a tail-agnostic policy.

INSTRUCTION: VMSEQ.VX
FORMAT: vmseq.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VMSEQ.VX must write a 1 to each element of the destination mask register vd for which the corresponding element of source vector register vs2 is equal to the scalar integer in integer register rs1, and must write a 0 to each element for which the comparison is false.
RULE 2: VMSEQ.VX must store the result mask in a single vector register whose element layout follows the mask vector layout defined for vector registers.
RULE 3: VMSEQ.VX must operate under a tail-agnostic policy, meaning that elements beyond the current vector length are not required to be written.
RULE 4: When the destination register vd of VMSEQ.VX is the mask register v0, VMSEQ.VX must combine the newly computed mask with the prior contents of v0 by performing a logical AND, thereby preserving mask bits that are zero (mask-undisturbed behavior).

INSTRUCTION: VMSGT.VI
FORMAT: vmsgt.vi vd, vs2, simm5, vm
RULE 1: VMSGT.VI writes a mask element value of 1 to each element of the destination mask register when the signed value in the corresponding element of the source vector register is greater than the signed immediate operand, and writes a mask element value of 0 otherwise.
RULE 2: VMSGT.VI stores the destination mask vector in a single vector register whose element layout follows the layout defined in Section 31.4.5.
RULE 3: VMSGT.VI permits the destination mask register to be the same register as the source vector mask register v0.
RULE 4: VMSGT.VI evaluates a signed-greater-than comparison between each element of the source vector register and the signed immediate operand.
RULE 5: VMSGT.VI includes an optional mask operand vm that governs element-wise execution according to the standard mask semantics for vector instructions.
RULE 6: VMSGT.VI operates under a tail-agnostic policy, so elements beyond the current vector length do not affect architectural state.

INSTRUCTION: VMSGT.VX
FORMAT: vmsgt.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VMSGT.VX writes a 1 to each destination mask register element when the signed greater-than comparison between the corresponding element of the source vector register vs2 and the scalar integer in rs1 evaluates true, and writes a 0 otherwise.
RULE 2: VMSGT.VX stores its result mask elements in a single destination vector register whose element layout follows the standard vector mask element layout.
RULE 3: When VMSGT.VX writes to destination register v0, the operation effectively ANDs the newly computed mask bits with the existing bits in v0, thereby preserving a mask-undisturbed policy.

INSTRUCTION: VMSGTU.VX
FORMAT: vmsgtu.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VMSGTU.VX writes a mask element value of 1 to each destination mask register element when the unsigned-greater-than comparison between the corresponding element of vs2 and the scalar integer rs1 evaluates to true, and writes 0 otherwise.
RULE 2: VMSGTU.VX stores its destination mask vector in a single vector register, and that register may be the same register as the source vector mask register v0.
RULE 3: VMSGTU.VX performs an unsigned-greater-than comparison of each element of the source vector register vs2 against the scalar integer register rs1.
RULE 4: VMSGTU.VX operates under a tail-agnostic policy, so the architectural state of mask register elements beyond the effective vector length is not defined by the instruction.
RULE 5: When the destination register of VMSGTU.VX is v0, the instruction updates only those mask elements enabled by the active mask, leaving disabled elements unchanged, thereby implementing mask-undisturbed behavior.

INSTRUCTION: VMSIF.M
FORMAT: vmsif.m vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: For VMSIF.M, any element in the tail of the result may be written with the value that the mask-producing operation would calculate using vl = VLEN, SEW = 8, and LMUL = 8, allowing all bits of the mask register to be overwritten.
RULE 2: For VMSIF.M, the tail elements of a mask result are treated as agnostic and may be left unchanged, may be overwritten with all 1s, or may be written with any value permitted by the agnostic policy.
RULE 3: VMSIF.M sets each element in the destination mask register up to and including the first element whose corresponding source mask bit is set.
RULE 4: VMSIF.M updates the tail elements of the destination mask register according to the tail-agnostic policy.
RULE 5: If VMSIF.M causes a trap, the architectural register vstart must be 0 at trap entry.
RULE 6: VMSIF.M must raise an illegal-instruction exception when executed with vstart not equal to 0.
RULE 7: VMSIF.M v0, v1 sets each element of the destination mask v0 to true for every element from the start of the vector through the first element whose corresponding element in the source mask v1 is true, and clears all subsequent elements.
RULE 8: When the source mask v1 is produced by a preceding vmseq.vi that flags zero-byte, VMSIF.M v0, v1 creates a destination mask v0 that is true for all elements up to and including the first zero-byte element indicated by v1.

INSTRUCTION: VMSLE.VI
FORMAT: vmsle.vi vd, vs2, simm5, vm
RULE 1: VMSLE.VI writes a 1 to each element of the destination mask register when the signed less-than-or-equal comparison of the corresponding source vector element with the sign-extended immediate operand evaluates true, and writes a 0 otherwise.
RULE 2: VMSLE.VI stores the resulting mask vector in a single vector register using the standard vector element layout.
RULE 3: VMSLE.VI may use the same vector register as both the destination mask register and the source mask register (v0).
RULE 4: VMSLE.VI's 5-bit immediate field is sign-extended and the immediate value must be in the range –16 to +15.

INSTRUCTION: VMSLE.VV
FORMAT: vmsle.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VMSLE.VV writes a 1 to each destination mask register element vd[i] when the signed integer comparison vs2[i] ≤ vs1[i] evaluates true for that element, and writes a 0 to vd[i] when the comparison evaluates false.
RULE 2: VMSLE.VV always operates under a tail-agnostic policy, meaning that elements beyond the current vector length are treated as inactive and do not affect the result.
RULE 3: When the destination register of VMSLE.VV is v0, the instruction updates each active element by AND-ing the newly computed mask bit with the existing bit in v0, preserving bits of v0 that are masked off according to the mask-undisturbed policy.
RULE 4: VMSLE.VV does not generate any exceptions or require special permissions beyond those defined for generic integer compare instructions.

INSTRUCTION: VMSLEU.VI
FORMAT: vmsleu.vi vd, vs2, simm5, vm
RULE 1: VMSLEU.VI writes a 1 to each element of the destination mask register when the unsigned less-or-equal comparison between the corresponding element of the source vector register and the immediate evaluates true, and writes a 0 otherwise.
RULE 2: VMSLEU.VI may use the same vector register for both the destination mask register and the source mask register v0.
RULE 3: VMSLEU.VI stores its result mask in a single vector register whose element layout follows the standard vector element layout.
RULE 4: VMSLEU.VI immediate operand must be a 5-bit signed value in the range 0 to 15; encodings with an immediate outside this range are reserved.
RULE 5: For an allowed immediate N (0 ≤ N ≤ 15), VMSLEU.VI performs the same comparison as VMSLTU.VI with immediate N+1, giving an effective VMSLTU.VI range of 1 to 16.
RULE 6: When the sign-extended 5-bit immediate has its high bit set, VMSLEU.VI supports unsigned immediate values in the range (2 × SEW − 16) to (2 × SEW − 1), enabling comparisons against those unsigned values.
RULE 7: The unsigned immediate range supported by VMSLEU.VI when the high bit is set allows corresponding VMSLTU.VI comparisons against unsigned immediates in the range (2 × SEW − 15) to (2 × SEW).
RULE 8: VMSLEU.VI operates under a tail-agnostic mask policy, so elements beyond the current vector length are treated as masked off.
RULE 9: If the destination register of VMSLEU.VI is v0, the instruction updates the mask by ANDing the newly computed mask bits with the existing mask bits, preserving other bits under a mask-undisturbed policy.

INSTRUCTION: VMSLEU.VV
FORMAT: vmsleu.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VMSLEU.VV writes a mask element value of 1 to the destination register for each element index i when the unsigned integer value in source vector register vs2 at element i is less than or equal to the unsigned integer value in source vector register vs1 at element i, and writes a mask element value of 0 otherwise.
RULE 2: VMSLEU.VV uses the elements of source vector register vs2 as the left operand and the elements of source vector register vs1 as the right operand in the unsigned less-or-equal comparison.
RULE 3: VMSLEU.VV stores the resulting mask vector in a single destination vector register.
RULE 4: VMSLEU.VV permits the destination mask register to be the same register as source mask register v0.
RULE 5: VMSLEU.VV always operates under a tail-agnostic policy, meaning that elements beyond the current effective vector length do not affect the architectural state of the destination mask.
RULE 6: When the destination register of VMSLEU.VV is v0, the instruction updates only those mask bits that are already set, leaving mask bits that are clear unchanged (mask-undisturbed policy).

INSTRUCTION: VMSLEU.VX
FORMAT: vmsleu.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VMSLEU.VX writes a 1 to each element of the destination mask register element when the unsigned comparison of the corresponding element of source vector register vs2 to the scalar integer register rs1 evaluates true, and writes a 0 otherwise.
RULE 2: VMSLEU.VX stores the destination mask vector in a single vector register whose element layout follows the mask vector element format.
RULE 3: VMSLEU.VX may use the same vector register for both the destination mask and the source vector mask register (v0).
RULE 4: VMSLEU.VX always operates under a tail-agnostic policy because it writes a mask register.
RULE 5: If the destination register of VMSLEU.VX is v0, the instruction effectively ANDs the newly generated mask with the existing mask bits, preserving previously set mask bits (mask-undisturbed policy).
RULE 6: VMSLEU.VX implements the unsigned "≤" comparison of each element of source vector register vs2 against scalar integer register rs1 and produces the resulting mask in the destination register.

INSTRUCTION: VMSLT.VV
FORMAT: vmslt.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VMSLT.VV writes a 1 to each element of the destination mask register when the signed less-than comparison between the corresponding elements of the two source vector registers evaluates to true, and writes a 0 otherwise.
RULE 2: VMSLT.VV stores its result mask in a single vector register, never spanning multiple registers.
RULE 3: VMSLT.VV permits the destination mask register to be the same register used as a source mask register (v0).
RULE 4: VMSLT.VV performs the signed less-than comparison as "source-register-1 element < source-register-2 element" for each element position.
RULE 5: VMSLT.VV respects the mask operand vm, updating only those elements for which the mask bit is true and leaving other elements unchanged.
RULE 6: When the destination register of VMSLT.VV is v0, the instruction combines the newly computed mask with the existing mask in v0 using a logical AND, so that each element of v0 becomes 1 only if the prior mask element was 1 and the signed less-than comparison is true.

INSTRUCTION: VMSLT.VX
FORMAT: vmslt.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VMSLT.VX must perform a signed less-than comparison between each element of the source vector register vs2 and the scalar integer register rs1.
RULE 2: VMSLT.VX must write a mask element value of 1 to the destination mask register for each element where the signed comparison evaluates to true, and a value of 0 otherwise.
RULE 3: VMSLT.VX stores the mask result in a single vector register whose element layout follows the standard mask vector layout.
RULE 4: VMSLT.VX may use the same register for the destination mask and the source mask register, allowing vd to be v0.
RULE 5: VMSLT.VX respects an optional mask operand vm by updating destination mask elements only for elements where vm is true and leaving other elements unchanged.
RULE 6: When the destination register of VMSLT.VX is v0, the instruction must apply a mask-undisturbed policy, effectively ANDing the newly computed mask with the existing bits of v0 for elements where the mask vm is false.

INSTRUCTION: VMSLTU.VV
FORMAT: vmsltu.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VMSLTU.VV compares each element i of the source vector register vs2 with the corresponding element i of the source vector register vs1 as unsigned integers and writes the value 1 to the destination mask register element i if the comparison vs2[i] < vs1[i] is true, otherwise writes the value 0.
RULE 2: VMSLTU.VV writes a result to destination mask element i only when the corresponding mask bit i in the mask register vm is true; when the mask bit i is false, VMSLTU.VV leaves destination mask element i unchanged.
RULE 3: VMSLTU.VV permits the destination mask register vd to be the same physical register as the mask source register v0.
RULE 4: When the destination mask register vd of VMSLTU.VV is v0, the instruction logically ANDs each computed result bit with the prior value of v0, thereby preserving any mask bits that are not set by the unsigned comparison.

INSTRUCTION: VMSLTU.VX
FORMAT: vmsltu.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VMSLTU.VX performs an unsigned less-than comparison between each element of source vector register vs2 and the scalar integer register rs1, writing 1 to the corresponding element of destination mask register vd when the comparison is true and 0 otherwise.
RULE 2: The destination mask vector produced by VMSLTU.VX is stored in a single vector register whose element layout follows the definition given in Section 31.4.5.
RULE 3: VMSLTU.VX may use the same vector register for the destination mask register vd and the source vector register vs2 (i.e., register overlap is permitted).
RULE 4: VMSLTU.VX is subject to the same permission checks, exception conditions, and other architectural effects that apply to the other integer compare instructions that write mask registers, such as VMSLT.VX and VMSLEU.VX.

INSTRUCTION: VMSNE.VI
FORMAT: vmsne.vi vd, vs2, simm5, vm
RULE 1: VMSNE.VI performs a not-equal comparison between each element of source vector register vs2 and the sign-extended immediate operand.
RULE 2: VMSNE.VI must write a 1 to each element of the destination mask register when the corresponding not-equal comparison is true and must write a 0 when the comparison is false.
RULE 3: VMSNE.VI stores its result mask vector in a single vector register.
RULE 4: VMSNE.VI may use the same vector register (v0) for both the destination mask register and the source mask register.
RULE 5: VMSNE.VI operates under a tail-agnostic policy, meaning that elements beyond the current vector length do not affect the architectural result.
RULE 6: If VMSNE.VI's destination register is v0, the instruction updates only the mask elements that are set, preserving mask elements that are cleared (mask-undisturbed policy).

INSTRUCTION: VMSNE.VV
FORMAT: vmsne.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VMSNE.VV writes a 1 to each element of its destination mask register when the integer not-equal comparison for that element evaluates true, and writes a 0 otherwise.
RULE 2: VMSNE.VV stores its result mask in a single vector register, and the element layout of that mask follows the standard vector element width definition.
RULE 3: VMSNE.VV may use the same register for the destination mask and the source mask register v0.
RULE 4: VMSNE.VV, like all integer-compare instructions that write mask registers, operates under a tail-agnostic policy.
RULE 5: When VMSNE.VV writes its result to the mask register v0, elements that are not written retain their previous mask bits, effectively performing an AND with the existing mask under a mask-undisturbed policy.

INSTRUCTION: VMSNE.VX
FORMAT: vmsne.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VMSNE.VX must compare each active element of source vector register vs2 with the integer value in scalar register rs1 and determine true when the two values are not equal.
