RULE 2: VMSNE.VX must write the integer 1 to the corresponding element of destination mask register vd when the not-equal comparison for that element is true, and must write the integer 0 when the comparison is false.
RULE 3: VMSNE.VX must store the destination mask vector in a single vector register whose element layout follows the mask-register layout defined for vector masks.
RULE 4: If the destination register vd is v0, VMSNE.VX must update each element of v0 by logically ANDing the previously stored mask bit with the newly computed not-equal result, effectively preserving mask bits that were previously zero.
RULE 5: VMSNE.VX must respect the optional mask operand vm; for any element whose corresponding mask bit in vm is zero, VMSNE.VX must leave the destination element unchanged.

INSTRUCTION: VMSOF.M
FORMAT: vmsof.m vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: In VMSOF.M, mask-destination tail elements are always treated as tail-agnostic regardless of the vta setting.
RULE 2: In VMSOF.M, any tail element of the result may be written with the value that the mask-producing operation would have calculated using vl = VLMAX.
RULE 3: In VMSOF.M, any tail element of the result may be written with the value that the mask-producing operation would have calculated using vl = VLEN, SEW = 8, and LMUL = 8 (allowing all bits of the mask register to be overwritten).
RULE 4: In VMSOF.M, the selection of which permitted value is written to each tail element is not required to be deterministic, and any combination of the allowed values may be used for different tail elements within a single execution.
RULE 5: In VMSOF.M, mask tail elements are always treated as agnostic, allowing them to be written at bit granularity without architectural significance.
RULE 6: When a trap is taken on VMSOF.M, the architectural vstart field is reported as zero.
RULE 7: VMSOF.M raises an illegal-instruction exception when the architectural vstart field is non-zero.
RULE 8: If the source mask register contains at least one set bit, VMSOF.M sets only the first (lowest-index) element of the destination mask register to one and clears all other elements; if the source mask register contains no set bits, VMSOF.M clears all elements of the destination mask register.

INSTRUCTION: VMUL.VV
FORMAT: vmul.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VMUL.VV must perform, for each element selected, a signed multiplication of two SEW-bit operands to produce a 2 × SEW-bit product.
RULE 2: VMUL.VV must write the low SEW-bit half of each 2 × SEW-bit product to the corresponding element of the destination vector register.
RULE 3: VMUL.VV must select elements for operation according to the mask register vm when it is present, and must operate on all elements when the mask register is not present.
RULE 4: VMUL.VV must not apply any rounding, scaling, or saturation to the multiplication result.
RULE 5: VMUL.VV must not use any rounding-mode fields in its operation.

INSTRUCTION: VMUL.VX
FORMAT: vmul.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VMUL.VX is a vector-scalar signed multiply instruction that computes a signed SEW-bit × SEW-bit product for each element of the source vector register vs2 with the SEW-bit scalar contained in integer register rs1.
RULE 2: VMUL.VX generates a 2 × SEW-bit product for each element as a result of the signed multiplication.
RULE 3: VMUL.VX returns one half of the 2 × SEW-bit product in the SEW-bit-wide destination register vd.
RULE 4: VMUL.VX writes the low SEW-bit word of each 2 × SEW-bit product into the corresponding element of the destination vector register vd.

INSTRUCTION: VMULH.VV
FORMAT: vmulh.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VMULH.VV MUST multiply each SEW-bit signed element of source vector register vs2 by the corresponding SEW-bit signed element of source vector register vs1 to produce a 2 × SEW-bit signed product and MUST write the upper SEW bits of each product to the corresponding element of the destination vector register vd.
RULE 2: The value written by VMULH.VV to each element of vd is SEW-bit-wide and MUST contain exactly the high half of the 2 × SEW-bit signed product of the paired source elements.
RULE 3: VMULH.VV MUST perform a simple fractional multiply with no scaling, rounding, or saturation applied to the high-half result.
RULE 4: VMULH.VV MUST NOT signal an overflow condition because the instruction discards the low half of the product.
RULE 5: VMULH.VV shares the same high-word-write behavior as the scalar-variant vmulh.vx, meaning both return the high bits of a signed multiplication.
RULE 6: VMULH.VV returns the high word of the product of each pair of corresponding elements of its two source vectors.
RULE 7: VMULH.VV is defined for element widths of 8, 16, and 32 bits in every Zve* extension.
RULE 8: VMULH.VV is not defined for element width 64 bits in any Zve64* extension.
RULE 9: Any trap taken by VMULH.VV in a Zve* extension MUST be a precise trap.

INSTRUCTION: VMULH.VX
FORMAT: vmulh.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VMULH.VX must multiply each element of source vector register vs2 by the signed integer scalar in rs1, treating both operands as SEW-bit signed values.
RULE 2: VMULH.VX must compute a 2 × SEW-bit product for each element and must write the most-significant SEW bits of that product to the corresponding element of destination vector register vd.
RULE 3: VMULH.VX must apply the vector mask vm so that for each element whose mask bit is clear the destination element is left unchanged, and for each element whose mask bit is set the high-SEW-bit result is written to vd.
RULE 4: VMULH.VX must perform a simple fractional signed multiply without any scaling, rounding, or saturation of the result.
RULE 5: VMULH.VX belongs to the mulh class and therefore must write the high word of the product to the destination register rather than the low word.
RULE 6: VMULH.VX computes the product of each vector element and the scalar operand and writes the high word of each product to the corresponding destination vector element.
RULE 7: VMULH.VX raises precise traps for all trap conditions that occur during its execution.

INSTRUCTION: VMULHSU.VV
FORMAT: vmulhsu.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VMULHSU.VV multiplies each element of the source vector register vs2 interpreted as signed with the corresponding element of the source vector register vs1 interpreted as unsigned, producing a 2 × SEW-bit product for each element.
RULE 2: VMULHSU.VV writes the most-significant SEW-bit half of each 2 × SEW-bit product into the corresponding element of the destination vector register vd.
RULE 3: VMULHSU.VV does not apply any scaling, rounding, or saturation to the multiplication result; it returns the high bits directly.
RULE 4: VMULHSU.VV never signals overflow because the operation returns the high half of the product without overflow detection.
RULE 5: VMULHSU.VV returns the high word of the product of each pair of vector elements.
RULE 6: VMULHSU.VV is available for element widths of 8, 16, and 32 bits in every Zve* extension.
RULE 7: VMULHSU.VV is not defined for an element width of 64 bits in any Zve64* extension.
RULE 8: Any trap taken by VMULHSU.VV is a precise trap.

INSTRUCTION: VMULHSU.VX
FORMAT: vmulhsu.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VMULHSU.VX multiplies each SEW-bit element of the vector source operand vs2, interpreted as signed, by the SEW-bit unsigned integer in scalar register rs1, generating a 2*SEW-bit product for each element.
RULE 2: VMULHSU.VX writes the upper SEW-bit half of each 2*SEW-bit product into the corresponding element of the destination vector register vd.
RULE 3: VMULHSU.VX performs a simple fractional multiply with no option to scale, round, or saturate the result.
RULE 4: VMULHSU.VX never raises an overflow condition because overflow cannot occur in this operation.
RULE 5: All implementations that include any Zve* extension MUST implement the VMULHSU.VX instruction.
RULE 6: In Zve64* extensions, the VMULHSU.VX instruction is not defined for element width EEW = 64.
RULE 7: When a trap condition occurs during execution of VMULHSU.VX, the trap is raised precisely in accordance with the Zve* extensions' trap model.

INSTRUCTION: VMULHU.VV
FORMAT: vmulhu.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VMULHU.VV multiplies each SEW-bit element of source vector vs2 with the corresponding SEW-bit element of source vector vs1 as unsigned integers, generates a 2 × SEW-bit product, and writes the high SEW-bit portion of each product to the corresponding element of destination vector vd.
RULE 2: The value written by VMULHU.VV for each element is the most-significant SEW bits of the 2 × SEW-bit product of the unsigned multiplication.
RULE 3: VMULHU.VV performs a simple fractional multiply and does not apply any scaling, rounding, or saturation to the result.
RULE 4: VMULHU.VV never produces an overflow condition, and therefore no overflow exception or flag is generated by the instruction.
RULE 5: VMULHU.VV is a vector-vector instruction with the form vmulhu.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm, operating element-wise on the vectors.
RULE 6: VMULHU.VV MUST be supported by every Zve* extension except when the element width (EEW) is 64 in the Zve64x, Zve64f, or Zve64d extensions, where it is not included.
RULE 7: In the Zve64x, Zve64f, and Zve64d extensions, VMULHU.VV MUST NOT be available for EEW = 64.
RULE 8: Any trap generated by VMULHU.VV is a precise trap.

INSTRUCTION: VMULHU.VX
FORMAT: vmulhu.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VMULHU.VX must perform an unsigned SEW-bit × SEW-bit multiplication for each element of the source vector register versus the scalar integer register, producing a 2 × SEW-bit product per element.
RULE 2: VMULHU.VX must write the upper SEW-bit half of each 2 × SEW-bit product into the corresponding element of the destination vector register.
RULE 3: VMULHU.VX provides no option to scale, round, or saturate the multiplication result and returns the high bits directly.
RULE 4: VMULHU.VX must never raise an overflow exception because overflow is not possible for this unsigned high-multiply operation.
RULE 5: VMULHU.VX returns the high word of the product of each element of the source vector and the scalar integer operand.
RULE 6: VMULHU.VX is supported in every Zve* extension for each element width (EEW) that the extension implements, except where the specification explicitly excludes it.
RULE 7: In the Zve64x extension, VMULHU.VX is not available when the element width (EEW) is 64 bits.
RULE 8: In the Zve64f extension, VMULHU.VX is not available when the element width (EEW) is 64 bits.
RULE 9: In the Zve64d extension, VMULHU.VX is not available when the element width (EEW) is 64 bits.
RULE 10: In the Zve64x extension, VMULHU.VX is available for element widths of 8, 16, and 32 bits.
RULE 11: In the Zve64f extension, VMULHU.VX is available for element widths of 8, 16, and 32 bits.
RULE 12: In the Zve64d extension, VMULHU.VX is available for element widths of 8, 16, and 32 bits.
RULE 13: In the Zve32x extension, VMULHU.VX is supported for element widths of 8, 16, and 32 bits.
RULE 14: In the Zve32f extension, VMULHU.VX is supported for element widths of 8, 16, and 32 bits.

INSTRUCTION: VMV.S.X
FORMAT: vmv.s.x vd, rs1
RULE 1: VMV.S.X copies the scalar integer register to element 0 of the destination vector register.
RULE 2: If SEW < XLEN, VMV.S.X copies the least-significant bits of the scalar integer register to element 0 and ignores the upper XLEN-SEW bits.
RULE 3: If SEW > XLEN, VMV.S.X sign-extends the scalar integer register value to SEW bits before writing to element 0.
RULE 4: For each element with index greater than 0 and less than VLEN/SEW, VMV.S.X treats the element as a tail element according to the current tail-agnostic or tail-undisturbed policy.
RULE 5: If vstart ≥ vl, VMV.S.X performs no operation and does not update the destination register.
RULE 6: When vl = 0, VMV.S.X does not update any elements in the destination vector register group regardless of the value of vstart.

INSTRUCTION: VMV.V.I
FORMAT: vmv.v.i vd, simm5
RULE 1: The VMV.V.I instruction must write the specified immediate value to each element of the destination vector register.
RULE 2: When the immediate value in a VMV.V.I instruction is zero, the destination vector register must contain zero in every element after execution.

INSTRUCTION: VMV.X.S
FORMAT: vmv.x.s rd, vs2
RULE 1: VMV.X.S transfers a single scalar value between an integer x register and element 0 of a vector register while ignoring LMUL and vector register groups.
RULE 2: VMV.X.S copies the SEW-wide element at index 0 of the source vector register into the destination integer register.
RULE 3: If SEW > XLEN, VMV.X.S transfers only the least-significant XLEN bits of the source element and discards the upper SEW-XLEN bits.
RULE 4: If SEW < XLEN, VMV.X.S sign-extends the source element to XLEN bits before writing it to the integer register.
RULE 5: VMV.X.S performs its copy operation even when vstart ≥ vl.
RULE 6: VMV.X.S performs its copy operation even when vl = 0.

INSTRUCTION: VMV1R.V
FORMAT: vmv1r.v vd, vs2
RULE 1: VMV1R.V copies all VLEN bits from source vector register vs2 to destination vector register vd, behaving as if EEW equals SEW, EMUL equals NREG, and effective length evl equals EMUL × VLEN/SEW with NREG set to 1.
RULE 2: The usual property that no elements are written when vstart ≥ vl does not apply to VMV1R.V.
RULE 3: VMV1R.V is not reserved when vstart exceeds the largest element index for the current vtype setting.
RULE 4: VMV1R.V is reserved if vstart ≥ evl.
RULE 5: When vd is equal to vs2, VMV1R.V does not change any vector register state.
RULE 6: For VMV1R.V the immediate field simm[2:0] must be 0, representing NREG = 1.
RULE 7: Any encoding of VMV1R.V with simm[2:0] ≠ 0 is reserved.
RULE 8: The source and destination vector register numbers used by VMV1R.V must be aligned to the vector register group size, and encodings that violate this alignment are reserved.
RULE 9: VMV1R.V is defined only for the unmasked variant where vm = 1; masked variants are not defined.

INSTRUCTION: VMV4R.V
FORMAT: vmv4r.v vd, vs2
RULE 1: VMV4R.V copies the entire contents of each whole vector register, i.e., all VLEN bits, and can copy whole vector register groups.
RULE 2: VMV4R.V has an nr field value of 4, which defines NREG = 4.
RULE 3: VMV4R.V operates as if EEW = SEW, EMUL = NREG, and its effective length evl is calculated as EMUL × VLEN / SEW.
RULE 4: VMV4R.V does not observe the usual property that no elements are written when vstart ≥ vl.
RULE 5: VMV4R.V does not observe the usual property that the instruction is reserved when vstart exceeds the largest element index for the current vtype setting.
RULE 6: VMV4R.V is reserved if vstart ≥ evl.
RULE 7: VMV4R.V does not modify any vector register state when the destination register vd is the same as the source register vs2.
RULE 8: VMV4R.V requires that the low three bits of the immediate field (simm[2:0]) equal NREG-1, therefore for VMV4R.V simm[2:0] must be 3.
RULE 9: VMV4R.V must use an NREG value of 1, 2, 4, or 8, and any encoding with simm[4:0] other than 0, 1, 3, or 7 is reserved.
RULE 10: VMV4R.V requires source and destination vector register numbers to be aligned to the vector register group size, and any encoding with misaligned register numbers is reserved.

INSTRUCTION: VMV8R.V
FORMAT: vmv8r.v vd, vs2
RULE 1: VMV8R.V copies all VLEN bits of each of eight consecutive source vector registers to the corresponding eight consecutive destination vector registers.
RULE 2: VMV8R.V operates with element width equal to SEW, an EMUL field equal to 8, and an effective vector length evl equal to EMUL multiplied by VLEN divided by SEW.
RULE 3: VMV8R.V is reserved (behavior undefined) if the current vstart value is greater than or equal to the effective length evl.
RULE 4: VMV8R.V does not modify any architectural state when the destination register number equals the source register number.
RULE 5: VMV8R.V requires that the source and destination register numbers be aligned on an eight-register boundary appropriate for the register group size; encodings that violate this alignment are reserved.
RULE 6: VMV8R.V encodings whose low three bits of the immediate field (simm[2:0]) do not equal 7 (i.e., NREG-1) are reserved.
RULE 7: VMV8R.V encodes the number of registers to copy as NREG-1 in the low three bits of the immediate field, and for VMV8R.V the encoded value must represent NREG equal to 8.
RULE 8: VMV8R.V may write destination elements even when vstart is greater than or equal to the current vl, provided that vstart is less than the effective length evl.

INSTRUCTION: VMXNOR.MM
FORMAT: vmxnor.mm vd, vs2, vs1
RULE 1: VMXNOR.MM computes each mask element as the logical NOT of the exclusive-OR of the corresponding mask elements of the two source registers, storing the result in the destination mask register.
RULE 2: VMXNOR.MM permits the destination mask register to be the same register as either source mask register.
RULE 3: VMXNOR.MM operates on a single vector register irrespective of the vlmul field setting in vtype.
RULE 4: VMXNOR.MM does not change the architectural register vlmul.
RULE 5: During execution of VMXNOR.MM, mask elements with indices less than vstart remain unchanged and vstart is reset to zero after the instruction finishes.
RULE 6: VMXNOR.MM is always unmasked, there are no inactive elements, and any encoding with vm=0 is reserved.
RULE 7: VMXNOR.MM updates mask elements whose indices are greater than or equal to vl according to the tail-agnostic policy, meaning those tail elements are always written.
RULE 8: The pseudoinstruction vmset.m vd is defined as an alias for VMXNOR.MM vd, vd, vd, which sets all mask bits in vd to 1.

INSTRUCTION: VMXOR.MM
FORMAT: vmxor.mm vd, vs2, vs1
RULE 1: The VMXOR.MM instruction performs a bitwise exclusive-OR of the two source mask registers and writes the result to the destination mask register.
RULE 2: The VMXOR.MM instruction always uses the same register for its destination operand and its first source operand.
RULE 3: VMXOR.MM computes each mask element as the exclusive-or of the corresponding mask elements of the two source mask registers, storing the result in the destination mask register (vd.mask[i] = vs2.mask[i] XOR vs1.mask[i]).
RULE 4: VMXOR.MM operates on mask registers, each mask element being a single bit, and therefore the instruction always reads and writes a single vector register irrespective of the vlmul setting in vtype.
RULE 5: VMXOR.MM does not modify the architectural value of the vlmul field in vtype.
RULE 6: VMXOR.MM permits the destination mask register to be the same physical register as either source mask register.
RULE 7: For VMXOR.MM, mask elements whose indices are less than the current value of vstart are left unchanged by the instruction.
RULE 8: After completing execution of VMXOR.MM, the architectural register vstart is reset to zero.
RULE 9: VMXOR.MM is defined as an unmasked vector mask logical instruction, so no elements are considered inactive during its execution.
RULE 10: VMXOR.MM updates mask elements with indices greater than or equal to the current vector length (vl) according to the tail-agnostic policy, meaning the tail elements are written regardless of their previous values.
RULE 11: When VMXOR.MM is used with both source registers identical to the destination register (vd, vd, vd), the resulting destination mask register is cleared, i.e., all mask bits become zero.

INSTRUCTION: VNCLIP.WI
FORMAT: vnclip.wi vd, vs2, zimm5, vm
RULE 1: VNCLIP.WI sets the vxsat bit in the vxsat register if any destination element is saturated.

INSTRUCTION: VNCLIP.WV
FORMAT: vnclip.wv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VNCLIP.WV selects the rounding mode from the vxrm CSR for all element computations.
RULE 2: VNCLIP.WV sets the vxsat bit in the vxsat register if any destination element is saturated by the operation.

INSTRUCTION: VNCLIP.WX
FORMAT: vnclip.wx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VNCLIP.WX computes each destination element vd[i] as the clipped result of rounding the signed fixed-point value obtained from source element vs2[i] after it is right-shifted by a shift amount taken from integer register rs1.
RULE 2: VNCLIP.WX uses the low lg₂(2 × SEW) bits of integer register rs1 as the right-shift amount that provides the scaling for each source element.
RULE 3: VNCLIP.WX narrows each source element to a destination element whose width is half that of the source element, discarding any bits beyond the destination element width after clipping.
RULE 4: VNCLIP.WX rounds each scaled source element according to the rounding mode specified in the vxrm CSR, rounding around the least-significant bit of the destination element.
RULE 5: VNCLIP.WX performs rounding before applying saturation to the destination element.
RULE 6: VNCLIP.WX treats the rounded, shifted source value as a signed integer and saturates it to the representable range of the signed destination element if overflow would occur.
RULE 7: VNCLIP.WX sets the vxsat bit in the vxsat register when any destination element is saturated as a result of the operation.

INSTRUCTION: VNCLIPU.WI
FORMAT: vnclipu.wi vd, vs2, zimm5, vm
RULE 1: VNCLIPU.WI reads each element of the source vector register group vs2 and writes a corresponding element to the narrower unsigned destination vector register group vd that is the clipped, rounded, and scaled version of the source element.
RULE 2: VNCLIPU.WI uses a zero-extended 5-bit immediate uimm and takes the low lg2(2*SEW) bits of that immediate as the right-shift amount applied to each source element for scaling.
RULE 3: VNCLIPU.WI, when performing a SEW = 64-bit to SEW = 32-bit narrowing operation, uses the low 6 bits of the immediate as the shift amount.
RULE 4: VNCLIPU.WI obtains the rounding mode from the vxrm CSR and applies rounding around the least-significant bit of the destination element before any saturation is performed.
RULE 5: VNCLIPU.WI treats the result after rounding and shifting as an unsigned integer and, if that value exceeds the maximum unsigned value representable in the destination element width, saturates the result to that maximum.
RULE 6: VNCLIPU.WI sets the vxsat bit in the vxsat CSR if any destination element is saturated.

INSTRUCTION: VNCLIPU.WV
FORMAT: vnclipu.wv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VNCLIPU.WV reads each source element from the vector register group specified by vs2.
RULE 2: VNCLIPU.WV obtains each shift-amount element from the vector register group specified by vs1.
RULE 3: VNCLIPU.WV uses the low lg2(2 × SEW) bits of each vs1 element to determine the right-shift amount applied to the corresponding vs2 element.
RULE 4: For a SEW of 64 bits narrowing to a SEW of 32 bits, VNCLIPU.WV uses the low 6 bits of each vs1 element as the shift amount.
RULE 5: VNCLIPU.WV rounds the shifted value using the rounding mode encoded in the vxrm CSR, rounding around the least-significant bit of the destination element.
RULE 6: VNCLIPU.WV performs rounding before any saturation is applied.
RULE 7: VNCLIPU.WV treats the rounded, shifted source value as an unsigned integer for the purpose of clipping to the destination element width.
RULE 8: VNCLIPU.WV saturates a destination element to the maximum unsigned value representable in the destination element if the unsigned result would overflow that width.
RULE 9: VNCLIPU.WV writes each destination element as the clipped unsigned result of the rounding and possible saturation operation.
RULE 10: VNCLIPU.WV sets the vxsat flag in the vxsat register if any destination element is saturated.
RULE 11: VNCLIPU.WV implements the element-wise operation vd[i] = clip(roundoff_unsigned(vs2[i], vs1[i])) for all vector elements i.

INSTRUCTION: VNCLIPU.WX
FORMAT: vnclipu.wx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VNCLIPU.WX rounds each source element vs2[i] to an unsigned integer using the rounding mode specified in the vxrm CSR, with rounding performed around the least-significant bit of the destination element and before any saturation.
RULE 2: VNCLIPU.WX sets the vxsat bit in the vxsat register if any element of the destination vector register group is saturated during the operation.

INSTRUCTION: VNMSAC.VV
FORMAT: vnmsac.vv vd, vs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VNMSAC.VV takes three vector registers (vd, vs1, vs2) and an optional mask operand vm and performs a vector-vector integer multiply-sub operation.
RULE 2: VNMSAC.VV must operate only on elements up to the current active vector length.
RULE 3: For each active element i, VNMSAC.VV must compute the low half of the product of vs1[i] and vs2[i], negate that low-half product, and add the result to the original value of vd[i].
RULE 4: VNMSAC.VV must write the computed value back to vd[i], thereby overwriting the original contents of the destination register (destructive behavior).
RULE 5: VNMSAC.VV must use only the low half of the 2×XLEN product of vs1[i] and vs2[i] in the computation and must not use the high half.
RULE 6: If the mask operand vm is present, VNMSAC.VV must update only those elements whose corresponding mask bit is set and must leave unchanged the elements whose mask bit is clear.

INSTRUCTION: VNMSAC.VX
FORMAT: vnmsac.vx vd, rs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VNMSAC.VX computes each element i as the negated product of the scalar integer in rs1 and the i-th element of vs2 added to the original i-th element of vd, storing the result into vd[i].
RULE 2: VNMSAC.VX is a destructive integer multiply-sub instruction that overwrites the minuend operand, replacing the original value of each destination element with the computed result.
RULE 3: VNMSAC.VX uses only the low half of the product of x[rs1] and vs2[i] when performing the addition with the original vd[i].
RULE 4: VNMSAC.VX belongs to the integer multiply-sub category and performs a multiply-subtract operation where the destination vector acts as the minuend.

INSTRUCTION: VNMSUB.VV
FORMAT: vnmsub.vv vd, vs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VNMSUB.VV MUST be a destructive integer multiply-add instruction that overwrites the first multiplicand operand with the computed result.
RULE 2: VNMSUB.VV MUST accept a destination register vd, source registers vs1 and vs2, and a mask register vm as its operands.
RULE 3: For each element i selected by the mask vm, VNMSUB.VV MUST write to vd[i] the value obtained by negating the low half of the product of vs1[i] and vd[i] and then adding vs2[i].
RULE 4: For each element i not selected by the mask vm, VNMSUB.VV MUST leave vd[i] unchanged.

INSTRUCTION: VNMSUB.VX
FORMAT: vnmsub.vx vd, rs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VNMSUB.VX is a destructive instruction that overwrites the destination vector register with the computed result for each active element.
RULE 2: VNMSUB.VX computes for each element i up to the active vector length the value vd[i] = -(x[rs1] * vd[i]) + vs2[i] using the scalar integer from integer register rs1, the original destination element, and the i-th element of source vector register vs2.
RULE 3: VNMSUB.VX uses only the low-order half of the integer product x[rs1] * vd[i] when negating the product before adding the corresponding vs2 element.
RULE 4: When a mask operand vm is supplied, VNMSUB.VX updates only those elements whose mask bit is true and leaves the other destination elements unchanged.
RULE 5: If no mask operand is present, VNMSUB.VX updates all elements up to the current active vector length.

INSTRUCTION: VNSRA.WI
FORMAT: vnsra.wi vd, vs2, zimm5, vm
RULE 1: VNSRA.WI extracts a half-width field from each 2*SEW-wide element of the source vector vs2 and produces a SEW-wide result in the destination vector vd.
RULE 2: VNSRA.WI uses a zero-extended 5-bit immediate uimm encoded in the instruction as the source of the shift amount.
RULE 3: VNSRA.WI determines the effective shift amount by selecting the low lg2(2*SEW) bits of the zero-extended immediate value.
RULE 4: When SEW equals 64 bits, VNSRA.WI selects the low 6 bits of uimm as the effective shift amount.
RULE 5: VNSRA.WI right-shifts the extracted half-width field arithmetically by the effective shift amount and then sign-extends the shifted value to fill a SEW-wide destination element.

INSTRUCTION: VNSRA.WV
FORMAT: vnsra.wv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VNSRA.WV must use a double-width source vector register group as indicated by the w suffix in the source operand.
RULE 2: VNSRA.WV must convert a double-width source vector register group specified by vs2 whose EEW/EMUL equals 2 × SEW / 2 × LMUL into a result vector register group whose element width and group multiplier are the current SEW and LMUL settings.
RULE 3: If a second source vector register group vs1 is present for VNSRA.WV, the element width of vs1 must equal the element width of the result (EEW = SEW).
RULE 4: In the vector-vector form of VNSRA.WV (vnop.wv vd, vs2, vs1, vm), each result element vd[i] must be the arithmetic right-shift of the double-width element vs2[i] by the shift amount contained in the corresponding element vs1[i].
RULE 5: In the vector-scalar form of VNSRA.WV (vnop.wx vd, vs2, rs1, vm), each result element vd[i] must be the arithmetic right-shift of the double-width element vs2[i] by the shift amount held in the scalar register x[rs1].
RULE 6: VNSRA.WV must produce a single-width result vector register group vd whose element width equals the current SEW setting.
RULE 7: VNSRA.WV produces destination elements whose width (SEW) is exactly one half of the width of the corresponding source elements.
RULE 8: VNSRA.WV extracts a smaller field from each wider source element before performing the shift operation.
RULE 9: VNSRA.WV sign-extends the result of the arithmetic right shift to fill the destination element width.
RULE 10: VNSRA.WV obtains each shift-amount value from the corresponding element of the vector register group specified as vs1 in the vector-vector form.
RULE 11: VNSRA.WV uses only the low lg₂(2 × SEW) bits of each shift-amount element, discarding any higher-order bits.

INSTRUCTION: VNSRA.WX
FORMAT: vnsra.wx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VNSRA.WX extracts each element of the source vector register group vs2, whose element width is twice the destination element width SEW, and treats it as a signed value.
RULE 2: VNSRA.WX takes the shift amount from scalar register rs1 and uses only the low lg2(2 × SEW) bits of that value as the arithmetic right-shift count.
RULE 3: For a narrowing from 64-bit source elements to 32-bit destination elements, VNSRA.WX uses the low 6 bits of rs1 as the shift amount.
RULE 4: VNSRA.WX performs a sign-extending arithmetic right shift of each selected 2 × SEW element of vs2 by the shift amount and writes the low SEW bits of the shifted result into the corresponding element of destination register group vd.
RULE 5: VNSRA.WX respects the vector mask vm such that elements for which the mask bit is clear are not written to vd and retain their prior values.

INSTRUCTION: VNSRL.WI
FORMAT: vnsrl.wi vd, vs2, zimm5, vm
RULE 1: VNSRL.WI may allow the destination vector register group to overlap the source vector register group only when the destination EEW equals the source EEW.
RULE 2: VNSRL.WI may allow the destination vector register group to overlap the source vector register group when the destination EEW is smaller than the source EEW and the lowest-numbered register of the destination group is identical to the lowest-numbered register of the source group.
RULE 3: VNSRL.WI may allow the destination vector register group to overlap the source vector register group when the destination EEW is greater than the source EEW, the source EMUL is at least 1, and the highest-numbered register of the destination group is identical to the highest-numbered register of the source group.
RULE 4: Any VNSRL.WI instruction encoding in which the destination vector register group overlaps the source vector register group while the overlap does not satisfy any of the three conditions listed in RULE 1, RULE 2, or RULE 3 is reserved.
RULE 5: Any VNSRL.WI instruction encoding that specifies a vector register group using a register other than the lowest-numbered register of that group is reserved.
RULE 6: When source and destination registers of VNSRL.WI overlap and have different EEW, the instruction executes with mask-agnostic and tail-agnostic behavior regardless of the vta and vma bits in vtype.
RULE 7: VNSRL.WI must not use a single vector register as a source operand that provides more than one EEW within the same instruction.
RULE 8: For the single-EEW source operand constraint of VNSRL.WI, a mask-register source is considered to have EEW equal to 1.
RULE 9: VNSRL.WI performs a logical right shift on each element of the source vector register group vs2 using a shift amount derived from the immediate operand.
RULE 10: VNSRL.WI zero-extends the result of the logical right shift and writes the lower SEW bits into the corresponding elements of the destination vector register group vd.
RULE 11: VNSRL.WI requires that each source element width is exactly twice the destination element width (SEW).
RULE 12: VNSRL.WI uses the low lg2(2*SEW) bits of the zero-extended 5-bit immediate operand uimm as the shift amount.
RULE 13: When the source element width is 64 bits and the destination element width is 32 bits, VNSRL.WI uses the low 6 bits of the immediate as the shift amount.
RULE 14: VNSRL.WI applies the vector mask vm to predicate each element operation in the same manner as other vector instructions.

INSTRUCTION: VNSRL.WV
FORMAT: vnsrl.wv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VNSRL.WV is the zero-extending (logical) variant of the narrowing right-shift operation.
RULE 2: VNSRL.WV obtains each shift-amount value from the corresponding element of the vector register group vs1; scalar registers or immediates are not used for this instruction.
RULE 3: VNSRL.WV uses the low lg2(2 * SEW) bits of each shift-amount element from vs1 as the effective shift amount for the corresponding element of vs2.
RULE 4: VNSRL.WV performs a logical right shift of each 2 * SEW element of the source vector register group vs2 by the effective shift amount, inserting zeros into the vacated high bits.
RULE 5: VNSRL.WV extracts the low SEW bits of the shifted 2 * SEW result and zero-extends them to form a SEW-wide destination element.
RULE 6: VNSRL.WV writes the zero-extended SEW result to the corresponding element of destination vector register group vd for each element whose mask bit in vm is true.
RULE 7: VNSRL.WV leaves the destination element unchanged for each element whose mask bit in vm is false.
RULE 8: VNSRL.WV has no additional architectural permission checks, requirements, or exception conditions defined beyond those inherent to vector operations.

INSTRUCTION: VNSRL.WX
FORMAT: vnsrl.wx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VNSRL.WX extracts a smaller field from each element of the wider operand vector and produces a zero-extended result after performing a logical right shift.
RULE 2: VNSRL.WX obtains the shift amount from the scalar integer register rs1.
RULE 3: VNSRL.WX uses only the low lg2(2*SEW) bits of the value in rs1 as the shift amount, ignoring higher bits.
RULE 4: When SEW is 64 bits and the destination element width is 32 bits, VNSRL.WX uses the low 6 bits of rs1 as the shift amount.
RULE 5: VNSRL.WX processes each element of the source vector register group vs2 and writes the resulting elements to the destination vector register group vd.
RULE 6: VNSRL.WX respects the optional mask register vm, leaving destination elements unchanged for which the corresponding mask bit is clear.
RULE 7: If rs1 is the zero register x0, VNSRL.WX halves the integer width of each source element, converting it to a destination element of half the source width.

INSTRUCTION: VREDAND.VS
FORMAT: vredand.vs vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: All operands and the result of VREDAND.VS must have identical SEW width.
RULE 2: VREDAND.VS uses element 0 of source vector register vs1 as the scalar operand for the reduction.
RULE 3: VREDAND.VS considers only the elements of vs2 whose corresponding mask bits are active in the current vector mask vm when performing the reduction.
RULE 4: VREDAND.VS computes the logical AND of the scalar operand vs1[0] with each active element of vs2 and reduces these values to a single element.
RULE 5: VREDAND.VS writes the single reduction result to element 0 of destination vector register vd.

INSTRUCTION: VREDMAX.VS
FORMAT: vredmax.vs vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VREDMAX.VS requires that the selected element width (SEW) of the source scalar register vs1, the source vector register vs2, and the destination register vd are identical.
RULE 2: VREDMAX.VS specifies that any arithmetic sum that overflows wraps around modulo 2^SEW.
RULE 3: VREDMAX.VS writes the maximum of the scalar element vs1[0] and all active elements of vector register vs2 into element 0 of the destination register vd.
RULE 4: VREDMAX.VS defines only element 0 of the destination register vd after execution; the contents of all other elements of vd are unspecified.
RULE 5: VREDMAX.VS considers an element of vs2 active if the corresponding mask bit in vm is true, and only those active elements participate in the maximum computation.
RULE 6: VREDMAX.VS ignores elements of vs2 whose corresponding mask bit in vm is false when computing the maximum.

INSTRUCTION: VREDMAXU.VS
FORMAT: vredmaxu.vs vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VREDMAXU.VS MUST use a single SEW width such that the SEW of vd, the SEW of vs2, and the SEW of the element vs1[0] are all identical.
RULE 2: VREDMAXU.VS computes vd[0] as the unsigned maximum of the scalar element vs1[0] and every element of vs2.
RULE 3: VREDMAXU.VS MUST store the computed maximum in vd[0] using the same SEW width as the source operands.
RULE 4: VREDMAXU.VS MUST wrap around on any arithmetic sum overflow that occurs during its execution.

INSTRUCTION: VREDMIN.VS
FORMAT: vredmin.vs vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VREDMIN.VS requires that the selected element width (SEW) of the source registers vs1 and vs2 and the destination register vd are identical.
RULE 2: VREDMIN.VS reduces the active elements of vector register vs2 together with the scalar element vs1[0].
RULE 3: VREDMIN.VS writes the reduction result to element 0 of the destination vector register vd.
RULE 4: VREDMIN.VS computes vd[0] as the minimum of the scalar element vs1[0] and each active element of vs2 selected by the mask vm.
RULE 5: VREDMIN.VS considers an element of vs2 active only if the corresponding mask bit in vm is set.
RULE 6: VREDMIN.VS does not modify any elements of vd other than element 0.

INSTRUCTION: VREDMINU.VS
FORMAT: vredminu.vs vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VREDMINU.VS requires that all source operands and the destination element have the same standard element width (SEW).
RULE 2: VREDMINU.VS follows the overflow behavior of single-width reduction instructions, whereby any arithmetic overflow wraps around.
RULE 3: VREDMINU.VS computes vd[0] as the unsigned minimum of the scalar element vs1[0] and each active element of vs2.
RULE 4: VREDMINU.VS treats an element of vs2 as active if the corresponding mask bit in vm is set, and excludes elements with a cleared mask bit from the reduction.
RULE 5: VREDMINU.VS writes the reduction result only to element 0 of the destination register vd, leaving all other elements of vd unchanged.

INSTRUCTION: VREDOR.VS
FORMAT: vredor.vs vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VREDOR.VS computes vd[0] as the bitwise OR of vs1[0] and all active elements of vs2 selected by the mask vm.
RULE 2: VREDOR.VS uses the mask vm to determine which elements of vs2 are active, and only those active elements contribute to the OR reduction.
RULE 3: VREDOR.VS requires that vd, vs2, vs1, and the result all have the same standard element width (SEW) as defined for single-width reduction instructions.
RULE 4: VREDOR.VS writes the reduction result only to element 0 of the destination vector register vd and leaves all other elements of vd unchanged.

INSTRUCTION: VREDSUM.VS
FORMAT: vredsum.vs vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VREDSUM.VS requires that the standard element width (SEW) of the destination register vd, source register vs1, and source register vs2 are identical.
RULE 2: VREDSUM.VS treats the element vs1[0] as the initial accumulator value for the reduction.
RULE 3: VREDSUM.VS adds each active element of vs2 to the accumulator, where an element of vs2 is active if the corresponding mask bit in vm is true.
RULE 4: VREDSUM.VS performs the additions using integer arithmetic defined for the instruction's SEW, and any overflow wraps around modulo 2^SEW.
RULE 5: After all active elements of vs2 have been added, VREDSUM.VS writes the final accumulator value to element 0 of the destination register vd.
RULE 6: VREDSUM.VS does not modify any element of vd other than element 0.

INSTRUCTION: VREDXOR.VS
FORMAT: vredxor.vs vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VREDXOR.VS MUST use a selected element width (SEW) that is identical for both source vector registers and the destination vector register, and the result element width is that same SEW.
RULE 2: VREDXOR.VS MUST wrap around on arithmetic sum overflow, producing a result reduced modulo 2^SEW.
RULE 3: VREDXOR.VS sets the element zero of the destination vector register (vd[0]) to the bitwise XOR of the element zero of the first source vector register (vs1[0]) with the XOR of all active elements of the second source vector register (vs2).

INSTRUCTION: VREM.VV
FORMAT: vrem.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VREM.VV must compute the signed remainder of each corresponding element of the source vector vs2 divided by the corresponding element of the source vector vs1 and place the result in the matching element of the destination vector vd.
RULE 2: The signed remainder produced by VREM.VV for each element must be identical to the result of the scalar signed remainder operation applied to the same operand values.
RULE 3: The equivalence between the result of VREM.VV and the scalar signed remainder operation must hold for extreme input cases.

INSTRUCTION: VREM.VX
FORMAT: vrem.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VREM.VX reads a signed scalar integer from integer register rs1 and a vector of signed integers from vector register vs2, computes the signed remainder of each element of vs2 divided by the scalar value, and writes the resulting signed integers to the corresponding elements of destination vector register vd.
RULE 2: VREM.VX must produce, for every element, a signed remainder value that is identical to the result of the scalar signed remainder operation defined by the base RISC-V ISA for the same dividend and divisor, even when the operands are extreme edge-case values.

INSTRUCTION: VREMU.VV
FORMAT: vremu.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VREMU.VV is a vector-vector instruction that computes the element-wise unsigned integer remainder of each element of source vector register vs2 divided by the corresponding element of source vector register vs1.
RULE 2: VREMU.VV writes each computed remainder element into the corresponding element of destination vector register vd only for elements whose mask bit in vm is active.
RULE 3: VREMU.VV does not modify the corresponding element of vd for any element whose mask bit in vm is inactive.
RULE 4: VREMU.VV produces the same remainder result as the scalar unsigned remainder operation for every possible input value, including extreme cases.

INSTRUCTION: VREMU.VX
FORMAT: vremu.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VREMU.VX must compute for each element i of the source vector register vs2 the unsigned remainder of vs2[i] divided by the scalar integer in rs1 and must write that result to element i of the destination vector register vd.
RULE 2: VREMU.VX must apply the vector mask register vm so that if the mask bit for element i is clear, the instruction must leave vd[i] unchanged.
RULE 3: The unsigned remainder result produced by VREMU.VX for any element i must be identical to the result that would be produced by the scalar unsigned remainder instruction applied to the same operand values.
RULE 4: For extreme input values, including division by zero and overflow cases, VREMU.VX must produce the same architectural result or exception as the scalar unsigned remainder instruction would produce for those values.
RULE 5: Any permissions, exceptions, or architectural side-effects that apply to the scalar unsigned remainder instruction must also apply to each element operation performed by VREMU.VX.

INSTRUCTION: VREV8.V
FORMAT: vrev8.v vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VREV8.V reverses the order of bytes within each element of the source vector register and writes the resulting elements to the destination vector register.
RULE 2: For each element, the byte at position i (where 0 ≤ i < SEW/8) in the source element becomes the byte at position (SEW/8-1-i) in the corresponding destination element when VREV8.V is executed.
RULE 3: When VREV8.V is executed with a mask, elements for which the mask bit is clear are left unchanged in the destination vector register.
RULE 4: Elements with an index greater than or equal to the current vector length (VL) are not written by VREV8.V and retain their previous values.
RULE 5: VREV8.V is defined for any standard element width (SEW) that is a multiple of eight bits, and when SEW equals eight bits the instruction leaves each element unchanged because there is only a single byte.
RULE 6: VREV8.V reverses the order of bytes within each element of the source vector and writes the resulting elements to the destination vector.
RULE 7: VREV8.V performs a byte-reversal (endian swap) on each element of the source vector register vs2 and writes the resulting element to the destination vector register vd.
RULE 8: For each element index i from vstart to the current vector length minus one, VREV8.V reads the SEW-wide element from vs2, reverses the order of its bytes, and writes the SEW-wide result to vd at the same index.
RULE 9: Within each element, VREV8.V assigns output bits k..k+7 the value of input bits (j-1)..j for k = 0, 8, …, SEW-8 while j is initialized to SEW and decremented by 8 after each assignment.
RULE 10: VREV8.V does not modify any architectural state other than the destination vector register vd.

INSTRUCTION: VRGATHER.VI
FORMAT: vrgather.vi vd, vs2, zimm5, vm
RULE 1: VRGATHER.VI zero-extends the immediate uimm to XLEN bits and treats the resulting value as an unsigned integer index.
RULE 2: VRGATHER.VI does not truncate the zero-extended uimm index to SEW bits when XLEN is greater than SEW.
RULE 3: For each element i, VRGATHER.VI writes vd[i] equal to 0 if the unsigned uimm index is greater than or equal to VLMAX; otherwise it writes vd[i] equal to the element vs2[uimm].
RULE 4: VRGATHER.VI may be executed with a mask register vm and follows the mask-undisturbed/agnostic policy for elements where the mask bit is inactive.
RULE 5: VRGATHER.VI can read the source vector element at any index less than VLMAX regardless of the current vector length vl.
RULE 6: VRGATHER.VI writes results only to destination elements i such that i is less than the current vector length vl; elements with i ≥ vl are handled according to the current tail policy.
RULE 7: VRGATHER.VI applies the current tail policy to destination elements beyond vl.
RULE 8: VRGATHER.VI encoding is reserved if the destination register group vd overlaps with the source register group vs2.

INSTRUCTION: VRGATHER.VV
FORMAT: vrgather.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VRGATHER.VV MUST read each element from the first source vector register group at the element position specified by the corresponding index element of the second source vector register group.
RULE 2: VRGATHER.VV MUST interpret each index element from the second source vector register group as an unsigned integer.
RULE 3: VRGATHER.VV MUST be able to read a source element at any index that is less than VLMAX regardless of the current active vector length (vl).
RULE 4: VRGATHER.VV MUST write result elements for all element positions i where i < vl, and for positions i ≥ vl the architectural effect MUST follow the currently configured tail policy.
RULE 5: When a mask is supplied, VRGATHER.VV MUST update only elements whose mask bit is true and MUST leave inactive elements undisturbed or set them according to the configured mask-undisturbed or mask-agnostic policy.
RULE 6: For each element i, VRGATHER.VV MUST write zero to the destination element if the index value vs1[i] is greater than or equal to VLMAX; otherwise it MUST write the element from the first source vector register group located at index vs1[i].
RULE 7: VRGATHER.VV MUST use the same element width (SEW) and vector grouping (LMUL) for both the data source vector register group and the index source vector register group.
RULE 8: If SEW is 8 bits, VRGATHER.VV MUST reference only index values in the range 0 to 255 inclusive.
RULE 9: VRGATHER.VV MUST return zero for any destination element whose corresponding index value is greater than or equal to VLMAX.
RULE 10: VRGATHER.VV MUST have a destination vector register group that does not overlap any source vector register group; encodings that cause overlap are reserved.
RULE 11: In an execution of VRGATHER.VV v11, v1, v10, v0.t the instruction expands elements from source vector v1 into destination vector v11 according to the indices supplied by vector v10, and writes each element only if the corresponding mask bit in v0.t is true.

INSTRUCTION: VRGATHER.VX
FORMAT: vrgather.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VRGATHER.VX treats the index value from the scalar register (or immediate) as an unsigned XLEN-bit integer and does not truncate the index when XLEN is greater than SEW.
RULE 2: VRGATHER.VX writes a zero to destination element i when the unsigned index is greater than or equal to VLMAX; otherwise it writes the element from the source vector at that index.
RULE 3: VRGATHER.VX reads a single element from the source vector at the computed index and writes that value to each destination element whose mask bit is active.
RULE 4: VRGATHER.VX leaves destination elements unchanged for which the mask bit is false, following the mask-agnostic policy.
RULE 5: VRGATHER.VX writes results only to the first vl elements of the destination vector and handles elements beyond vl according to the current tail policy.
RULE 6: VRGATHER.VX encoding is reserved when the destination vector register group overlaps any source vector register group.

INSTRUCTION: VRGATHEREI16.VV
FORMAT: vrgatherei16.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VRGATHEREI16.VV computes each destination element vd[i] as 0 if the corresponding index element vs1[i] is greater than or equal to VLMAX, otherwise as the element vs2[vs1[i]].
RULE 2: VRGATHEREI16.VV treats index values in vs1 as unsigned integers.
RULE 3: VRGATHEREI16.VV reads elements from vs2 at locations given by the unsigned index values in vs1.
RULE 4: VRGATHEREI16.VV may read any element of vs2 whose index is less than VLMAX regardless of the current vl setting.
RULE 5: VRGATHEREI16.VV writes at most vl elements to the destination register group vd; elements beyond vl are handled according to the current tail policy.
RULE 6: VRGATHEREI16.VV supports masking, and for elements where the mask is inactive the mask-undisturbed or mask-agnostic policy applies.
RULE 7: VRGATHEREI16.VV must not have the destination register group vd overlap with either source register group vs2 or vs1, and any such overlap makes the encoding reserved.
RULE 8: VRGATHEREI16.VV must treat the values in vs1 and rs1 as control values and must exempt them from DIEL.

INSTRUCTION: VROL.VV
FORMAT: vrol.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VROL.VV MUST perform a bitwise left rotation on each element of the source vector vs2.
RULE 2: VROL.VV MUST use the corresponding element of vs1 as the rotate amount for each element of vs2.
RULE 3: VROL.VV MUST mask the rotate amount with (SEW-1), using only the low log2(SEW) bits of each vs1 element and ignoring all higher bits.
RULE 4: For each element index i from vstart to vl-1, VROL.VV MUST set the destination element vd[i] to the value of vs2[i] rotated left by (vs1[i] & (SEW-1)).
RULE 5: VROL.VV MUST NOT modify destination elements for indices i outside the active vector range [vstart, vl).
RULE 6: VROL.VV MUST retire with a successful completion status after executing the element-wise rotations.

INSTRUCTION: VROL.VX
FORMAT: vrol.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VROL.VX must perform a bitwise left rotation on each element of the source vector register vs2.
RULE 2: VROL.VX must obtain the rotate amount from integer register rs1 and apply the same amount to all vector elements.
RULE 3: VROL.VX must mask the value read from rs1 with the low log2(SEW) bits, ignoring all higher-order bits of rs1.
RULE 4: VROL.VX must, for each element index i from vstart to vl-1, write to destination vector register vd element i the result of left-rotating the corresponding element of vs2 by the masked rotate amount.
RULE 5: VROL.VX must leave unchanged any element of vd whose index is outside the active range vstart to vl-1.
RULE 6: VROL.VX must rotate bits within each element modulo the element width SEW, so that bits shifted out of the most-significant position reappear at the least-significant position.
RULE 7: VROL.VX must retire successfully without raising an architectural exception when all active elements have been processed.

INSTRUCTION: VROR.VI
FORMAT: vror.vi vd, vs2, zimm6, vm
RULE 1: A VROR.VI instruction executed with an immediate rotate amount equal to the modular negation of a desired left-rotate amount produces the same architectural result as a rotate-left immediate operation would have produced, because no VROL.VI immediate form is defined.

INSTRUCTION: VROR.VV
FORMAT: vror.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VROR.VV performs a bitwise right rotation on each element of the source vector register vs2.
RULE 2: VROR.VV takes the rotation amount for each element from the corresponding element of the source vector register vs1.
RULE 3: VROR.VV uses only the low log2(SEW) bits of the rotation-amount value from vs1 and ignores all higher bits.
RULE 4: For every active element index i from vstart to vl-1, VROR.VV sets element i of the destination vector register vd to the result of a logical right shift of element i of vs2 by the value (get_velem(vs1,i) & (SEW1)).

INSTRUCTION: VROR.VX
FORMAT: vror.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VROR.VX must rotate each element of the source vector register vs2 right by a rotate amount derived from the low log2(SEW) bits of the integer register rs1.
RULE 2: VROR.VX must ignore all bits of rs1 above the low log2(SEW) bits when determining the rotate amount.
RULE 3: VROR.VX must iterate over element indices i from the current vstart up to vl-1 inclusive.
RULE 4: For each element i, VROR.VX must write to the destination vector register vd the result of right-rotating the i-th element of vs2 by the masked rotate amount.
RULE 5: VROR.VX must use an element width equal to the selected SEW (EEW = SEW) for all element operations.
RULE 6: After processing all elements in the active range, VROR.VX must retire with success (RETIRE_SUCCESS) and must not raise an exception.

INSTRUCTION: VRSUB.VI
FORMAT: vrsub.vi vd, vs2, simm5, vm
RULE 1: VRSUB.VI must treat the immediate operand as a scalar value that is used as the left operand of the subtraction for every element of the operation.
RULE 2: VRSUB.VI must compute for each element index i within the current vector length the result imm − vs2[i] and write that result to the destination element vd[i] when the mask bit for that element is true.
RULE 3: VRSUB.VI must leave the destination element vd[i] unchanged for any element index i within the current vector length when the mask bit for that element is false.
RULE 4: VRSUB.VI must perform the element-wise computation for all element indices i from 0 up to the current vector length minus one.
RULE 5: VRSUB.VI must use the vector-scalar reverse-subtract immediate form, meaning the operation subtracts each source vector element from the immediate scalar.
RULE 6: VRSUB.VI must, in the absence of a mask register, execute the subtraction for all elements of the current vector length.

INSTRUCTION: VS1R.V
FORMAT: vs1r.v vs3, (rs1)
RULE 1: VS1R.V stores the entire contents of the single destination vector register to memory starting at the address contained in scalar integer register rs1.
RULE 2: VS1R.V transfers exactly one vector register (NFIELDS = 1).
RULE 3: VS1R.V computes its effective vector length evl as VLEN divided by EEW and uses this evl regardless of the current vtype configuration and the current vl setting.
RULE 4: VS1R.V is reserved when the architectural register vstart is greater than or equal to the computed evl, and the architectural effect of the instruction is undefined in that case.
RULE 5: The usual property that no elements are written when vstart ≥ vl does not apply to VS1R.V.
RULE 6: The usual property that the instruction is reserved when vstart exceeds the largest element index for the current vtype does not apply to VS1R.V.
RULE 7: VS1R.V stores the lowest-numbered vector register at the lowest-numbered memory addresses beginning at the address in rs1.
RULE 8: An implementation may raise a misaligned address exception for VS1R.V if the base address in rs1 is not naturally aligned to the larger of the EEW size in bytes (EEW/8) and the implementation's smallest supported SEW size in bytes (SEWMIN/8).

INSTRUCTION: VS2R.V
FORMAT: vs2r.v vs3, (rs1)
RULE 1: VS2R.V encoding is reserved when the nf field encodes a number of registers that is not a power of two.
RULE 2: VS2R.V encoding is reserved when the starting vector register number is not aligned to the vector register group size defined by NFIELDS.
RULE 3: VS2R.V encoding is reserved for NFIELDS values other than 1, 2, 4, or 8.
RULE 4: VS2R.V computes its effective vector length as evl = NFIELDS × VLEN ÷ EEW independent of the current vtype and vl settings.
RULE 5: VS2R.V does not suppress element stores when vstart ≥ vl, so stores may occur even if vstart is greater than or equal to vl.
RULE 6: VS2R.V encoding is reserved when the architectural vstart is greater than or equal to the computed evl.
RULE 7: VS2R.V uses the scalar register specified as rs1 to supply the base address for the memory store.
RULE 8: VS2R.V may raise a misaligned address exception if the base address is not naturally aligned to the larger of EEW/8 bytes and SEWMIN/8 bytes.
RULE 9: VS2R.V operates as an unmasked unit-stride store of elements whose effective element width is 8 bits.
RULE 10: VS2R.V stores the lowest-numbered vector register at the lowest memory address and stores successive vector registers contiguously in increasing address order.

INSTRUCTION: VS8R.V
FORMAT: vs8r.v vs3, (rs1)
RULE 1: VS8R.V stores the contents of the eight vector registers v8 through v15 to memory.
RULE 2: VS8R.V writes the contents of vector register v8 to the lowest-addressed memory location and writes the contents of registers v9 through v15 to consecutive higher addresses in ascending register order.
RULE 3: VS8R.V uses the scalar integer register specified as rs1 as the base address for the memory store.
RULE 4: VS8R.V has an effective vector length evl equal to VLEN and operates with this evl regardless of the current vtype and vl settings.
RULE 5: VS8R.V is reserved if the value of vstart is greater than or equal to evl (i.e., if vstart ≥ VLEN).
RULE 6: VS8R.V does not suppress stores when vstart ≥ vl; instead it is reserved when vstart ≥ VLEN.
RULE 7: VS8R.V is reserved if the eight registers are not aligned as a vector-register group.
RULE 8: Implementations may raise a misaligned address exception for VS8R.V when the base address in rs1 is not naturally aligned to the larger of EEW/8 bytes (1 byte) or SEWMIN/8 bytes.
RULE 9: VS8R.V writes the register contents to memory exactly as they appear in the registers because the in-register byte layout is identical to the in-memory byte layout, making the stored data independent of the EEW value.
RULE 10: VS8R.V transfers eight whole vector registers as indicated by its nf field.

INSTRUCTION: VSADD.VI
FORMAT: vsadd.vi vd, vs2, simm5, vm
RULE 1: VSADD.VI adds a signed immediate operand to each signed integer element of the source vector vs2 and writes the resulting saturated values to the destination vector vd.
RULE 2: VSADD.VI produces each element of the destination vector vd with the same element width as the corresponding elements of the source vector vs2.
RULE 3: VSADD.VI saturates on overflow by replacing any element result that exceeds the representable range of the destination element type with the nearest representable signed integer value.
RULE 4: VSADD.VI sets the vxsat bit in the vector status register when any element addition overflows the destination element type.

INSTRUCTION: VSADD.VV
FORMAT: vsadd.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VSADD.VV adds the signed integer value of each element in source vector register vs2 to the signed integer value of the corresponding element in source vector register vs1 and writes the result to the corresponding element of destination vector register vd.
RULE 2: VSADD.VV saturates an element result to the nearest representable value when the signed addition of the corresponding elements of vs2 and vs1 would overflow the destination element type.
RULE 3: VSADD.VV sets the vxsat status flag bit whenever any element result is saturated due to overflow.

INSTRUCTION: VSADD.VX
FORMAT: vsadd.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VSADD.VX MUST take a destination vector register vd, a source vector register vs2, a scalar integer register rs1, and may optionally use a mask register vm.
RULE 2: VSADD.VX MUST perform a signed integer addition of each element in vs2 with the scalar value in rs1 to produce an element result.
RULE 3: VSADD.VX MUST write each element result into the corresponding element of the destination vector register vd for elements whose mask bit is true, or for all elements when no mask is present.
RULE 4: VSADD.VX MUST leave each destination element unchanged for elements whose mask bit is false when a mask register vm is present.
RULE 5: VSADD.VX MUST replace the result with the nearest representable value when the signed addition of a pair of elements would exceed the destination element's representable range.
RULE 6: VSADD.VX MUST set the vxsat bit in the vector status register whenever a saturation occurs because of overflow.

INSTRUCTION: VSADDU.VI
FORMAT: vsaddu.vi vd, vs2, simm5, vm
RULE 1: VSADDU.VI MUST add, for each element, the unsigned integer in the corresponding element of vs2 to the sign-extended immediate imm and write the result to the corresponding element of vd.
RULE 2: If the result of the element-wise addition performed by VSADDU.VI would overflow the destination element, VSADDU.VI MUST replace the result with the closest representable unsigned value for that element and set the vxsat bit.

INSTRUCTION: VSADDU.VV
FORMAT: vsaddu.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VSADDU.VV takes a destination vector register vd, source vector registers vs2 and vs1, and an optional mask register vm as operands.
RULE 2: VSADDU.VV performs element-wise addition of unsigned integer elements from vs2 and vs1 and writes the results to the corresponding elements of vd.
RULE 3: VSADDU.VV saturates each element result to the largest representable unsigned integer value when the addition would overflow the element's width.
RULE 4: VSADDU.VV sets the vxsat status bit whenever any element result is saturated due to overflow.
RULE 5: VSADDU.VV writes an element of vd only if the corresponding mask bit in vm is true; otherwise the element of vd remains unchanged.

INSTRUCTION: VSADDU.VX
FORMAT: vsaddu.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VSADDU.VX must perform an element-wise unsigned integer addition of each element of source vector register vs2 with the unsigned integer value in integer register rs1, storing each result in the corresponding element of destination vector register vd.
RULE 2: VSADDU.VX must saturate any element result that would exceed the maximum unsigned integer representable for the element width to that maximum value.
RULE 3: VSADDU.VX must set the vxsat status flag when any element result is saturated due to overflow.

INSTRUCTION: VSBC.VVM
FORMAT: vsbc.vvm vd, vs2, vs1, v0
RULE 1: For each element index i, VSBC.VVM computes vd[i] equal to vs2[i] minus vs1[i] minus v0.mask[i].
RULE 2: VSBC.VVM writes the computed element results to the destination vector register vd.
RULE 3: Despite being encoded as a masked instruction, VSBC.VVM operates on all element positions and writes back all body elements regardless of the mask bits.
RULE 4: Encodings of VSBC.VVM with vm set to 1 are reserved and have no defined architectural behavior.
RULE 5: The VSBC.VVM encoding is reserved when the destination vector register vd is register v0.

INSTRUCTION: VSBC.VXM
FORMAT: vsbc.vxm vd, vs2, rs1, v0
RULE 1: VSBC.VXM MUST compute each element i of the active vector length as vd[i] = vs2[i] - x[rs1] - v0.mask[i], where x[rs1] is the scalar value read from integer register rs1 and v0.mask[i] provides the borrow-in bit.
RULE 2: VSBC.VXM with vm set to 1 (unmasked form) is reserved.
RULE 3: VSBC.VXM MUST write the result to every element i of the destination vector register vd regardless of the mask bits.
RULE 4: VSBC.VXM encoding is reserved when the destination vector register is v0.
RULE 5: VSBC.VXM MUST obtain the borrow-in (carry-in) bits exclusively from the implicit mask register v0.

INSTRUCTION: VSE16.V
FORMAT: vse16.v vs3, (rs1), vm
RULE 1: VSE16.V stores each 16-bit element of the source vector register vs3 to memory using unit-stride addressing starting at the address held in integer register rs1.
RULE 2: VSE16.V computes the memory address for the i-th element as rs1 plus i multiplied by 2 bytes, where i is the element index within the active vector length.
RULE 3: VSE16.V uses the mask register specified by the vm field to control element-wise storage, storing an element only when the corresponding mask bit is true and leaving memory unchanged when the mask bit is false.
RULE 4: VSE16.V reads the mask encoding vm, which may be a mask register such as v0.t or may be omitted, and the presence or absence of a mask determines whether masked store semantics are applied.
RULE 5: VSE16.V does not modify any integer or floating-point registers; it only writes to memory and may read the mask register.

INSTRUCTION: VSE32.V
FORMAT: vse32.v vs3, (rs1), vm
RULE 1: VSE32.V stores a vector of 32-bit elements from the specified source vector register to consecutive memory locations beginning at the address held in the specified base integer register.
RULE 2: In the form "vse32.v v8, (a2)", VSE32.V stores the 32-bit element vector contained in vector register v8 to the memory address referenced by integer register a2.
RULE 3: VSE32.V stores each 32-bit element of the source vector register group specified by vs3 to memory addresses beginning at the address contained in rs1.
RULE 4: For element index i, VSE32.V computes the target address as rs1 plus i multiplied by 4 bytes, thereby using a unit-stride of one element.
RULE 5: If the mask operand vm is present and is v0.t, VSE32.V stores only those elements whose corresponding mask bit is true and does not modify memory for elements whose mask bit is false.
RULE 6: If the mask operand vm is omitted, VSE32.V stores all elements of vs3 without applying any mask.
RULE 7: The operand vs3 is interpreted as a vector register group that provides the 32-bit elements to be written by VSE32.V.
RULE 8: The operand rs1 provides the base address from which VSE32.V begins the unit-stride store.

INSTRUCTION: VSE64.V
FORMAT: vse64.v vs3, (rs1), vm
RULE 1: VSE64.V treats the vector source operand vs3 as the register group that provides the 64-bit elements to be stored.
RULE 2: VSE64.V uses the scalar register rs1 as the base address for the first 64-bit element to be written to memory.
RULE 3: VSE64.V stores each 64-bit element from vs3 to consecutive memory locations with a unit stride of eight bytes, starting at the address held in rs1.
RULE 4: VSE64.V respects the mask encoding vm; when vm is specified as v0.t, only elements whose corresponding mask bit is true are written to memory, and elements with a false mask bit are not stored.
RULE 5: VSE64.V treats a missing vm field as an unconditional store, effectively as if all mask bits are true.

INSTRUCTION: VSE8.V
FORMAT: vse8.v vs3, (rs1), vm
RULE 1: VSE8.V stores a vector of 8-bit elements from the source register group vs3 to memory starting at the address contained in integer register rs1.
RULE 2: VSE8.V may be masked using the mask register vm, and the mask encoding used by VSE8.V is the same as that used by other vector unit-stride operations.
RULE 3: VSE8.V with a mask register and the .t qualifier must store each 8-bit element from the specified source vector register to successive memory addresses beginning at the address held in the base integer register, and it must write an element only when the corresponding mask bit is true, leaving the destination memory unchanged for elements whose mask bit is false.
RULE 4: VSE8.V with a mask register and the .t qualifier must omit writing any byte whose value is zero even when the corresponding mask bit is true, so that only non-zero bytes are stored to memory.
RULE 5: VSE8.V without a mask operand must perform an unmasked store that writes every 8-bit element from the source vector register to successive memory locations starting at the base address, regardless of any mask state.
RULE 6: VSE8.V without a mask operand must write a zero byte to each destination memory location when the corresponding element in the source vector register is zero.

INSTRUCTION: VSETIVLI
FORMAT: vsetivli rd, uimm, vtypei
RULE 1: VSETIVLI must update the vtype CSR to the value encoded in its vtypei operand.
RULE 2: VSETIVLI must compute a new vector length (vl) from the unsigned immediate operand (uimm) according to the vector length calculation algorithm and store that result in the vl CSR.
RULE 3: VSETIVLI must write the newly stored vl CSR value into the integer register specified by its rd operand.
RULE 4: If VSETIVLI sets the vill bit (bit XLEN-1) of the vtype register to 1, the resulting vtype value is illegal.
RULE 5: If any of the bits XLEN-2 down to 8 of the vtype register written by VSETIVLI are non-zero, the behavior is reserved.
RULE 6: VSETIVLI sets bit 7 (vma) of the vtype register to indicate the vector mask-agnostic attribute.
RULE 7: VSETIVLI sets bit 6 (vta) of the vtype register to indicate the vector tail-agnostic attribute.
RULE 8: VSETIVLI sets bits 5 down to 3 (vsew[2:0]) of the vtype register to specify the selected element width (SEW) setting.
RULE 9: VSETIVLI sets bits 2 down to 0 (vlmul[2:0]) of the vtype register to specify the vector register group multiplier (LMUL) setting.
RULE 10: VSETIVLI must execute with the same architectural behavior regardless of whether the Zvkt extension is implemented, because it is not affected by Zvkt.

INSTRUCTION: VSETVL
FORMAT: vsetvl rd, rs1, rs2
RULE 1: VSETVL MUST write the signed vlmul field in vtype and the architectural LMUL value is defined as LMUL = 2^vlmul[2:0].
RULE 2: Encodings of vtype that would result in an LMUL value less than SEWMIN/ELEN are reserved for VSETVL.
RULE 3: Implementations may set the vill bit on VSETVL when they do not support configurations where the resulting LMUL is less than SEWMIN/ELEN.
RULE 4: After reset, the vector extension must be in a state such that the values of vtype and vl can be read and subsequently restored by a single VSETVL instruction.
RULE 5: Executing a VSETVL instruction resets the architectural register vstart to zero.
RULE 6: VSETVL writes the value supplied in source register rs2 to the vtype CSR.
RULE 7: VSETVL sets the vl CSR to the hardware-determined vector length that results from the given AVL and the newly written vtype.
RULE 8: VSETVL writes the value of the vl CSR, after it has been determined, into destination register rd.
RULE 9: VSETVL updates both the vtype CSR and the vl CSR as part of its execution.
RULE 10: VSETVL reads the vtype value from the integer register rs2 when the instruction is executed.
RULE 11: VSETVL causes an illegal-instruction exception if the vill bit (bit XLEN-1) of the vtype value supplied in rs2 is 1.
RULE 12: If any bits of the vtype value in positions XLEN-2 down to 8 are non-zero, the behavior of VSETVL is reserved.
RULE 13: VSETVL sets the vector-mask-agnostic attribute to the value of bit 7 (vma) of the vtype value supplied in rs2.
RULE 14: VSETVL sets the vector-tail-agnostic attribute to the value of bit 6 (vta) of the vtype value supplied in rs2.
RULE 15: VSETVL selects the element width (SEW) for subsequent vector operations according to the vsew[2:0] field (bits 5-3) of the vtype value supplied in rs2.
RULE 16: VSETVL selects the vector register-group multiplier (LMUL) for subsequent vector operations according to the vlmul[2:0] field (bits 2-0) of the vtype value supplied in rs2.
RULE 17: When the vtype value supplied in rs2 is neither illegal nor reserved, VSETVL writes that value into the architectural vtype CSR.
RULE 18: VSETVL determines the new vector length (VL) using the same algorithm as VSETVLI, applying that algorithm to the vtype value taken from rs2.
RULE 19: If any unsupported value is present in the vtype argument of VSETVL, the vill bit in the resulting vtype MUST be set.
RULE 20: If any unsupported value is present in the vtype argument of VSETVL, all remaining bits in the resulting vtype MUST be cleared to zero.
RULE 21: If any unsupported value is present in the vtype argument of VSETVL, the vl register MUST be written with zero.
RULE 22: Implementations of VSETVL MUST consider every bit of the vtype argument when determining support, and an unsupported value in any bit position MUST result in the vill bit being set.
RULE 23: When executing VSETVL with rs1 not x0, the instruction must treat AVL as the unsigned integer value held in the integer register specified by rs1 and must write the resulting vector length to the integer register specified by rd.
RULE 24: When executing VSETVL with rs1 = x0 and rd ≠ x0, the instruction must treat AVL as the maximum unsigned integer value (~0) and must write the resulting VLMAX to both the vector length register and the integer register specified by rd.
RULE 25: When executing VSETVL with rs1 = x0 and rd = x0, the instruction must treat AVL as the current vector length value read from the vl CSR and must write the resulting vector length back to the vl CSR without writing to any integer register.
RULE 26: VSETVL must set vl to an integer multiple of the element-group size when the operand whose vl is being set is a vector of element groups.
RULE 27: VSETVL values that are not integer multiples of the element-group size are reserved when the operand is a vector of element groups.
RULE 28: VSETVL must set vl to either VLMAX or to a positive integer multiple of EGSMAX when element-group instructions are present and the supplied AVL value is greater than VLMAX.
RULE 29: VSETVL must select an appropriate SEW when EEW is encoded statically in the instruction or when an instruction has multiple operands containing vectors of element groups with differing EEW.
RULE 30: VSETVL must not be affected by the Zvkt extension.

INSTRUCTION: VSETVLI
FORMAT: vsetvli rd, rs1, vtypei
RULE 1: A VSETVLI instruction sets the LMUL field in the architectural vtype register to 2^{vlmul[2:0]}, where vlmul[2:0] is the signed vlmul field encoded in the instruction.
RULE 2: An implementation may raise the vill (illegal instruction) flag for a VSETVLI instruction that writes a vtype encoding with LMUL less than SEWMIN/ELEN if the implementation does not support that configuration.
RULE 3: When the ta flag is specified on VSETVLI, subsequent vector instructions treat tail elements as agnostic.
RULE 4: When the tu flag is specified on VSETVLI, subsequent vector instructions leave tail elements undisturbed.
RULE 5: When the ma flag is specified on VSETVLI, subsequent vector instructions treat mask bits as agnostic.
RULE 6: When the mu flag is specified on VSETVLI, subsequent vector instructions leave mask bits undisturbed.
RULE 7: In implementations prior to version 0.9, a VSETVLI instruction that omits both tail and mask flags behaves as if the tu and mu flags were specified.
RULE 8: VSETVLI must set the vtype CSR to the value supplied by its vtypei operand.
RULE 9: VSETVLI must compute the vector length (vl) from the AVL supplied by its rs1 operand and the newly written vtype CSR, and must set the vl CSR to that computed value.
RULE 10: VSETVLI must write the resulting value of the vl CSR into the destination register rd.
RULE 11: The immediate fields of VSETVLI provide bits for the vtype CSR as follows: bit XLEN-1 (vill), bits XLEN-2:8, bit 7 (vma), bit 6 (vta), bits 5:3 (vsew[2:0]), and bits 2:0 (vlmul[2:0]).
RULE 12: If the vill bit (XLEN-1) supplied by the immediate fields of VSETVLI is 1, the resulting vtype value MUST be considered illegal.
RULE 13: If any of the bits XLEN-2 through 8 supplied by the immediate fields of VSETVLI are non-zero, the resulting vtype value is reserved.
RULE 14: VSETVLI MUST set the vma bit (bit 7) of the vtype CSR to the value specified by its immediate field.
RULE 15: VSETVLI MUST set the vta bit (bit 6) of the vtype CSR to the value specified by its immediate field.
RULE 16: VSETVLI MUST set the vsew[2:0] bits (bits 5:3) of the vtype CSR according to the SEW mnemonic operand, where e8 selects SEW = 8 bits, e16 selects SEW = 16 bits, e32 selects SEW = 32 bits, and e64 selects SEW = 64 bits.
RULE 17: VSETVLI MUST set the vlmul[2:0] bits (bits 2:0) of the vtype CSR according to the LMUL mnemonic operand, where mf8 selects LMUL = 1/8, mf4 selects LMUL = 1/4, mf2 selects LMUL = 1/2, m1 selects LMUL = 1, m2 selects LMUL = 2, m4 selects LMUL = 4, and m8 selects LMUL = 8.
RULE 18: VSETVLI MUST update the architectural vtype CSR to the value represented by its immediate fields (SEW, LMUL, vta, vma).
RULE 19: VSETVLI must treat the AVL as the unsigned integer value contained in the integer register specified by rs1 when rs1 is not x0.
RULE 20: VSETVLI must write the newly computed vl value to the integer register specified by rd when rs1 is not x0.
RULE 21: VSETVLI must use the maximum unsigned integer value (~0) as the AVL when rs1 is x0 and rd is not x0.
RULE 22: VSETVLI must write the resulting VLMAX to both the vl register and the integer register specified by rd when rs1 is x0 and rd is not x0.
RULE 23: VSETVLI must use the current vl register value as the AVL when both rs1 and rd are x0.
RULE 24: VSETVLI must write the resulting vl value to the vl register but not to any integer register when both rs1 and rd are x0.
RULE 25: Implementations may set vill when executing VSETVLI with rs1 equal to x0 and rd equal to x0, regardless of the prior vill state.
RULE 26: VSETVLI with rs1 equal to x0 and rd equal to x0 allows the vtype register to be changed while maintaining the current vl, provided VLMAX is not reduced.
RULE 27: The current vl value can be read from the vl CSR after executing VSETVLI.
RULE 28: Implementations may assume that vl is not changed by VSETVLI with rs1 equal to x0 and rd equal to x0 when VLMAX is not reduced.
RULE 29: VSETVLI a3, a0, e16, m4, ta, ma sets the vector element width to 16 bits and the vector length multiplier to 4, establishing a 16-bit integer vector type.
RULE 30: VSETVLI a3, a0, e16, m4, ta, ma writes the computed VL value, which is the number of elements that can be processed for this iteration, into destination register a3.
RULE 31: VSETVLI x0, x0, e32, m8, ta, ma sets the vector element width to 32 bits and the vector length multiplier to 8, establishing a 32-bit integer vector type.

INSTRUCTION: VSEXT.VF2
FORMAT: vsext.vf2 vd, vs2, vm
