RULE 1: VSEXT.VF2 sign-extends each element of the source vector whose element width (EEW) is half of the destination element width (SEW) to produce SEW-sized elements in the destination vector.
RULE 2: VSEXT.VF2 requires the source element width (EEW) to be exactly one-half of the selected SEW; any other EEW is not a supported width for this instruction.
RULE 3: For VSEXT.VF2 the source vector's EMUL value must equal (EEW/SEW) × LMUL, which for the required EEW of SEW/2 yields a source EMUL of LMUL/2.
RULE 4: VSEXT.VF2 produces a destination vector whose element width (EEW) equals the selected SEW and whose EMUL equals the current LMUL.

INSTRUCTION: VSEXT.VF4
FORMAT: vsext.vf4 vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VSEXT.VF4 must sign-extend each source vector element whose element width is one-fourth of the destination element width to produce a destination element of size SEW.
RULE 2: VSEXT.VF4 requires that the source element width EEW equals SEW divided by 4 and that this EEW is a supported width.
RULE 3: VSEXT.VF4 defines the destination element width EEW to be equal to SEW.
RULE 4: If the source EEW for VSEXT.VF4 is not a supported width, the encoding of VSEXT.VF4 is reserved.
RULE 5: If the source EMUL calculated for VSEXT.VF4 would be below the minimum legal LMUL, the encoding of VSEXT.VF4 is reserved.
RULE 6: When a mask operand vm is supplied to VSEXT.VF4, the sign-extension operation is performed only for elements whose mask bit is true, and destination elements whose mask bit is false are not written.
RULE 7: An instruction whose vs1 field value is binary 00101 must be decoded as the VSEXT.VF4 instruction.

INSTRUCTION: VSEXT.VF8
FORMAT: vsext.vf8 vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VSEXT.VF8 must sign-extend each source element whose element width is one-eighth of the destination element width, producing destination elements of size SEW.
RULE 2: The source operand element width for VSEXT.VF8 must be equal to SEW divided by 8.
RULE 3: For VSEXT.VF8 the source operand EMUL is defined as (EEW/SEW) multiplied by LMUL, which equals one-eighth of LMUL.
RULE 4: If the source EEW for VSEXT.VF8 is not a supported width or the computed source EMUL is less than the minimum legal LMUL, then the VSEXT.VF8 encoding is reserved.
RULE 5: The destination operand element width for VSEXT.VF8 equals SEW.
RULE 6: The destination operand EMUL for VSEXT.VF8 equals LMUL.
RULE 7: VSEXT.VF8 performs sign-extension of a source vector integer operand whose EEW is less than SEW, filling the destination vector with SEW-sized elements.
RULE 8: VSEXT.VF8 implements the sign-extend variant of the vector integer extension instructions, not the zero-extend variant.
RULE 9: If the vs1 field of an instruction encoding is 00011, the instruction must be decoded as VSEXT.VF8.

INSTRUCTION: VSHA2CH.VV
FORMAT: vsha2ch.vv vd, vs2, vs1
RULE 1: VSHA2CH.VV causes an illegal-instruction exception RETIRE_FAIL when the product LMUL × VLEN is less than the element group width EGW.
RULE 2: VSHA2CH.VV is reserved when the selected SEW is not 32 for the zvknha encoding.
RULE 3: VSHA2CH.VV is reserved when the selected SEW is neither 32 nor 64 for the zvknhb encoding.
RULE 4: VSHA2CH.VV is reserved when the destination register group overlaps any source register group.

INSTRUCTION: VSHA2CL.VV
FORMAT: vsha2cl.vv vd, vs2, vs1
RULE 1: VSHA2CL.VV in a Zvknha implementation is reserved when the selected SEW is any value other than 32.
RULE 2: VSHA2CL.VV in a Zvknhb implementation is reserved when the selected SEW is any value other than 32 or 64.
RULE 3: VSHA2CL.VV encoding is reserved when the destination register group overlaps with either source register group vs1 or vs2.
RULE 4: VSHA2CL.VV writes 4 × SEW bits to destination register vd as the concatenation {a @ b @ e @ f} after processing each element group.
RULE 5: When SEW equals 32, VSHA2CL.VV performs two rounds of SHA-256 compression.
RULE 6: When SEW equals 64, VSHA2CL.VV performs two rounds of SHA-512 compression.
RULE 7: VSHA2CL.VV raises an illegal-instruction exception with RETIRE_FAIL when LMUL × VLEN is less than EGW.
RULE 8: After completing both rounds for element i, VSHA2CL.VV stores the concatenation {a @ b @ e @ f} into vd for that element.
RULE 9: When the execution completes without encountering the LMUL × VLEN < EGW condition, VSHA2CL.VV retires successfully.

INSTRUCTION: VSHA2MS.VV
FORMAT: vsha2ms.vv vd, vs2, vs1
RULE 1: VSHA2MS.VV operates on SHA-256 when the selected SEW is 32 bits and operates on SHA-512 when the selected SEW is 64 bits.
RULE 2: VSHA2MS.VV processes element groups that consist of four SEW-sized elements, yielding a 128-bit group for SEW = 32 and a 256-bit group for SEW = 64.
RULE 3: VSHA2MS.VV processes a number of element groups equal to vl divided by 4.
RULE 4: VSHA2MS.VV encodings in the zvknha extension are reserved when the selected element width (SEW) is not 32.
RULE 5: VSHA2MS.VV encodings in the zvknhb extension are reserved when the selected element width (SEW) is neither 32 nor 64.
RULE 6: VSHA2MS.VV encodings are reserved when the destination register group (vd) overlaps any register in the source groups vs1 or vs2.
RULE 7: VSHA2MS.VV must raise an illegal-instruction exception and cause retire failure if the product of LMUL and VLEN is less than the element group width (EGW).
RULE 8: VSHA2MS.VV processes element groups indexed from floor(vstart/EGS) up to floor(vl/EGS)-1 for each active element group.
RULE 9: For each processed element group, VSHA2MS.VV reads four SEW-sized elements from vd and treats them as message words W[3], W[2], W[1], and W[0].
RULE 10: For each processed element group, VSHA2MS.VV reads four SEW-sized elements from vs2 and treats them as message words W[11], W[10], W[9], and W[4].
RULE 11: For each processed element group, VSHA2MS.VV reads four SEW-sized elements from vs1 and treats them as message words W[15], W[14], W[13], and W[12].
RULE 12: VSHA2MS.VV computes W[16] as sig1(W[14]) plus W[9] plus sig0(W[1]) plus W[0] using the SEW-dependent definitions of sig0 and sig1.
RULE 13: VSHA2MS.VV computes W[17] as sig1(W[15]) plus W[10] plus sig0(W[2]) plus W[1] using the SEW-dependent definitions of sig0 and sig1.
RULE 14: VSHA2MS.VV computes W[18] as sig1(W[16]) plus W[11] plus sig0(W[3]) plus W[2] using the SEW-dependent definitions of sig0 and sig1.
RULE 15: VSHA2MS.VV computes W[19] as sig1(W[17]) plus W[12] plus sig0(W[4]) plus W[3] using the SEW-dependent definitions of sig0 and sig1.
RULE 16: After computing W[16] through W[19], VSHA2MS.VV writes four SEW-sized elements to vd containing the concatenation of W[19], W[18], W[17], and W[16] for the current element group.
RULE 17: When SEW is 32, VSHA2MS.VV defines sig0(x) as ROTR(x,7) XOR ROTR(x,18) XOR SHR(x,3) and sig1(x) as ROTR(x,17) XOR ROTR(x,19) XOR SHR(x,10).
RULE 18: When SEW is 64, VSHA2MS.VV defines sig0(x) as ROTR(x,1) XOR ROTR(x,8) XOR SHR(x,7) and sig1(x) as ROTR(x,19) XOR ROTR(x,61) XOR SHR(x,6).
RULE 19: VSHA2MS.VV defines ROTR(x,n) as (x right-shifted by n) OR (x left-shifted by (SEW-n)), where the shifts are performed on SEW-bit values.
RULE 20: VSHA2MS.VV defines SHR(x,n) as the logical right shift of the SEW-bit value x by n bits.

INSTRUCTION: VSLIDE1DOWN.VX
FORMAT: vslide1down.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: For each element index i where i < vstart, VSLIDE1DOWN.VX must leave vd[i] unchanged.
RULE 2: For each element index i with vstart ≤ i < vl-1, VSLIDE1DOWN.VX must set vd[i] to the value of vs2[i+1] if the corresponding mask bit v0.mask[i] is enabled.
RULE 3: For element index i = vl-1, VSLIDE1DOWN.VX must write the scalar integer register x[rs1] (after XLEN/SEW conversion) to vd[vl-1] if the mask bit v0.mask[vl-1] is enabled.
RULE 4: If the mask bit for element index vl-1 is not enabled, VSLIDE1DOWN.VX must not modify vd[vl-1] and must follow the current mask-agnostic/undisturbed policy.
RULE 5: For each element index i where vl ≤ i < VLMAX, VSLIDE1DOWN.VX must handle the element according to the current tail policy.
RULE 6: When XLEN < SEW, VSLIDE1DOWN.VX must sign-extend the value from x[rs1] to SEW bits before writing it to the destination element.
RULE 7: When XLEN > SEW, VSLIDE1DOWN.VX must copy only the least-significant SEW bits of x[rs1] to the destination element and ignore the remaining high bits.
RULE 8: VSLIDE1DOWN.VX must not access memory during its execution.
RULE 9: VSLIDE1DOWN.VX must not alter any vector register other than the destination register group vd.

INSTRUCTION: VSLIDE1UP.VX
FORMAT: vslide1up.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VSLIDE1UP.VX must place the scalar integer register rs1 value into element 0 of the destination vector register group vd when element 0 is active, otherwise element 0 is updated according to the current mask-agnostic/undisturbed policy.
RULE 2: VSLIDE1UP.VX sign-extends the rs1 value to SEW bits before writing to vd[0] when XLEN < SEW, and copies the least-significant SEW bits of rs1 to vd[0] while ignoring the high XLEN-SEW bits when XLEN > SEW.
RULE 3: For each active element i satisfying 0 ≤ i < vl-1, VSLIDE1UP.VX copies the source vector element vs2[i] into the destination element vd[i+1], implementing a one-element upward shift for the remaining active elements.
RULE 4: VSLIDE1UP.VX does not update any destination elements with index i ≥ vl; such elements are handled according to the current tail policy.
RULE 5: VSLIDE1UP.VX requires that the destination vector register group vd does not overlap the source vector register group vs2, and if overlap occurs the encoding of VSLIDE1UP.VX is reserved.

INSTRUCTION: VSLIDEDOWN.VI
FORMAT: vslidedown.vi vd, vs2, zimm5, vm
RULE 1: VSLIDEDOWN.VI writes at most the number of destination elements indicated by the current vl register.
RULE 2: VSLIDEDOWN.VI treats destination elements with index greater than or equal to vl according to the current tail policy.
RULE 3: VSLIDEDOWN.VI forms the source start index OFFSET by zero-extending the 5-bit immediate to XLEN bits, and does not truncate OFFSET to SEW bits when XLEN is larger than SEW.
RULE 4: For VSLIDEDOWN.VI, when vstart is less than vl and an element index i satisfies 0 ≤ i+OFFSET < VLMAX, the source element src[i] is taken from vs2 element i+OFFSET.
RULE 5: For VSLIDEDOWN.VI, when vstart is less than vl and an element index i satisfies i+OFFSET ≥ VLMAX, the source element src[i] is zero.
RULE 6: For VSLIDEDOWN.VI, when vstart is less than vl and an element index i satisfies 0 ≤ i < vstart, the destination element vd[i] remains unchanged.
RULE 7: For VSLIDEDOWN.VI, when vstart is less than vl and an element index i satisfies vstart ≤ i < vl, the destination element vd[i] is written with src[i] only if the corresponding mask bit v0.mask[i] is enabled.
RULE 8: For VSLIDEDOWN.VI, when vstart is less than vl and an element index i satisfies vl ≤ i < VLMAX, the destination element follows the current tail policy.

INSTRUCTION: VSLIDEDOWN.VX
FORMAT: vslidedown.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VSLIDEDOWN.VX computes each destination element i as vd[i] = vs2[i + OFFSET] where OFFSET is the unsigned integer value read from integer register rs1.
RULE 2: VSLIDEDOWN.VX uses the full XLEN-wide unsigned value in rs1 as OFFSET and does not truncate OFFSET to SEW bits even when XLEN > SEW.
RULE 3: VSLIDEDOWN.VX limits the number of destination elements that may be written to the current value of vl; elements with indices i ≥ vl are not written directly by the instruction.
RULE 4: For each element i with vstart ≤ i < vl and 0 ≤ i + OFFSET < VLMAX, VSLIDEDOWN.VX sets src[i] = vs2[i + OFFSET].
RULE 5: For each element i with vstart ≤ i < vl and i + OFFSET ≥ VLMAX, VSLIDEDOWN.VX sets src[i] = 0.
RULE 6: For each element i where 0 ≤ i < vstart, VSLIDEDOWN.VX leaves vd[i] unchanged.
RULE 7: For each element i where vstart ≤ i < vl and the mask bit v0.mask[i] is enabled, VSLIDEDOWN.VX writes vd[i] = src[i]; if the mask bit is disabled, vd[i] remains unchanged.
RULE 8: For each element i where vl ≤ i < VLMAX, VSLIDEDOWN.VX processes vd[i] according to the current tail policy.
RULE 9: If vstart ≥ vl, VSLIDEDOWN.VX performs no element writes to vd and applies only the tail policy to elements i ≥ vl.

INSTRUCTION: VSLIDEUP.VI
FORMAT: vslideup.vi vd, vs2, zimm5, vm
RULE 1: The immediate operand uimm for VSLIDEUP.VI is a 5-bit unsigned value that is zero-extended to XLEN bits.
RULE 2: For VSLIDEUP.VI, if XLEN is greater than SEW, the OFFSET derived from uimm is not truncated to SEW bits.
RULE 3: For VSLIDEUP.VI the architectural value of vl determines the maximum number of destination elements that may be written.
RULE 4: For VSLIDEUP.VI destination elements with indices from OFFSET through vl-1 are written only if the corresponding mask bit is enabled and OFFSET < vl.
RULE 5: For VSLIDEUP.VI when vstart < vl, each element i satisfying 0 ≤ i < min(vl, max(vstart, OFFSET)) is written as vd[i] = vs2[i-OFFSET] provided the mask for element i is enabled.
RULE 6: For VSLIDEUP.VI when vstart < vl, each element i satisfying max(vstart, OFFSET) ≤ i < vl remains unchanged.
RULE 7: For VSLIDEUP.VI each element i satisfying vl ≤ i < VLMAX is processed according to the vector tail-agnostic policy.
RULE 8: The destination vector register group used by VSLIDEUP.VI must not overlap the source vector register group; overlapping encodings are reserved.
RULE 9: For VSLIDEUP.VI if the mask bit for a destination element is disabled, that element is not written and retains its previous value.

INSTRUCTION: VSLIDEUP.VX
FORMAT: vslideup.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VSLIDEUP.VX uses the unsigned integer value in the integer register specified by rs1 as the start index OFFSET for the destination.
RULE 2: If XLEN is greater than SEW, VSLIDEUP.VX does not truncate the OFFSET value to SEW bits.
RULE 3: VSLIDEUP.VX writes at most the number of elements specified by the current vl value.
RULE 4: VSLIDEUP.VX writes destination elements with indices from OFFSET up to vl-1 when those elements are unmasked and OFFSET is less than vl.
RULE 5: When vstart < vl, VSLIDEUP.VX sets each destination element with index i satisfying 0 ≤ i < min(vl, max(vstart, OFFSET)) to the value of the source element at index i-OFFSET if the mask bit for element i is enabled.
RULE 6: For indices i satisfying max(vstart, OFFSET) ≤ i < vl, VSLIDEUP.VX leaves the destination element unchanged.
RULE 7: For indices i satisfying vl ≤ i < VLMAX, VSLIDEUP.VX applies the tail-policy to the destination element.
RULE 8: The destination vector register group for VSLIDEUP.VX must not overlap the source vector register group; overlapping encodings are reserved.

INSTRUCTION: VSLL.VI
FORMAT: vsll.vi vd, vs2, zimm5, vm
RULE 1: VSLL.VI shifts each element of the vector register group identified by the vs2 operand left by a number of bit positions and writes the shifted result to the vector destination register group identified by the vd operand.
RULE 2: VSLL.VI obtains the shift-amount value from the 5-bit immediate field uimm, which is zero-extended before use.
RULE 3: VSLL.VI uses only the low lg2(SEW) bits of the zero-extended immediate value to control the element shift amount, where SEW is the configured element width.

INSTRUCTION: VSLL.VV
FORMAT: vsll.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VSLL.VV uses only the low lg2(SEW) bits of each shift-amount value to determine the amount by which the corresponding element of vs2 is shifted.
RULE 2: VSLL.VV performs a logical shift left on each element of vs2 by the element-wise shift amount taken from vs1 and writes the resulting elements to the destination vector register group vd.
RULE 3: VSLL.VV does not perform sign-extension or zero-extension of the shifted data; the shift is purely logical.

INSTRUCTION: VSLL.VX
FORMAT: vsll.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VSLL.VX reads the vector operand to be shifted from the vector register group identified by the vs2 field.
RULE 2: VSLL.VX obtains the shift-amount value from the scalar integer register identified by the rs1 field.
RULE 3: VSLL.VX uses only the low lg2(SEW) bits of the value read from rs1 as the effective shift amount for the logical left shift.
RULE 4: VSLL.VX logically shifts each element of the source vector vs2 left by the effective shift amount and writes the resulting elements to the destination vector register group specified by vd.

INSTRUCTION: VSM.V
FORMAT: vsm.v vs3, (rs1)
RULE 1: VSM.V stores a byte vector of length ceil(vl/8) elements to the memory address specified by rs1, using an element width of 8 bits (EEW=8).
RULE 2: VSM.V operates with the same semantics as an unmasked byte store (EEW=8) for the computed effective vector length.
RULE 3: VSM.V always writes the destination vector register (vs3) using a tail-agnostic policy regardless of masking.
RULE 4: VSM.V interprets the architectural vstart value as a byte count, so vstart advances by one for each byte element processed.
RULE 5: VSM.V respects the vill field of vtype, and execution of VSM.V is illegal when vtype.vill is set.
RULE 6: For VSM.V the effective vector length is evl = ceil(vl/8), corresponding to EMUL = 1.

INSTRUCTION: VSM3C.VI
FORMAT: vsm3c.vi vd, vs2, zimm5
RULE 1: VSM3C.VI performs an SM3 compression operation.
RULE 2: VSM3C.VI operates on 256-bit element groups that consist of eight 32-bit elements.
RULE 3: VSM3C.VI processes a number of element groups equal to vl divided by the element-group size (EGS).
RULE 4: VSM3C.VI must have the vector length register vl be a multiple of the element-group size (8) because vl must represent the number of SEW=32 elements to be processed.
RULE 5: VSM3C.VI must have the vector start register vstart be a multiple of the element-group size (8).
RULE 6: VSM3C.VI is reserved when the selected element width (SEW) is not 32.
RULE 7: VSM3C.VI is reserved when the destination register group (vd) overlaps the source register group (vs2).
RULE 8: VSM3C.VI causes an illegal-instruction exception with RETIRE_FAIL if LMUL × VLEN is less than the element group width (EGW) of 256.
RULE 9: VSM3C.VI reads eight 32-bit state words from the destination register group vd as an element group.
RULE 10: VSM3C.VI reads eight 32-bit message words from the source register group vs2 as an element group but uses only the words w0, w1, w4, and w5; the other four words are ignored.
RULE 11: VSM3C.VI byte-swaps each 32-bit input word from big-endian to little-endian before any SM3 compression computation.
RULE 12: VSM3C.VI uses the 5-bit unsigned immediate rnds (values 0-31) to select the pair of SM3 rounds numbered 2 × rnds and 2 × rnds + 1.
RULE 13: VSM3C.VI computes the constant T_j using the function T_j(j) which returns 0x79CC4519 for j ≤ 15 and 0x7A879D8A for j > 15.
RULE 14: VSM3C.VI selects the Boolean function FF_j for round j using FF1 for j ≤ 15 and FF2 for j > 15.
RULE 15: VSM3C.VI selects the Boolean function GG_j for round j using GG1 for j ≤ 15 and GG2 for j > 15.
RULE 16: VSM3C.VI computes the intermediate values ss1, ss2, tt1, tt2, and the updated state words exactly as defined in the pseudo-code, applying the specified rotations and the functions FF_j, GG_j, P_0, and ROL32.
RULE 17: VSM3C.VI writes the eight resulting 32-bit state words to vd after byte-swapping each word from little-endian back to big-endian.
RULE 18: VSM3C.VI must process each element group i from eg_start to eg_len − 1, where eg_len = vl/EGS and eg_start = vstart/EGS.

INSTRUCTION: VSM3ME.VV
FORMAT: vsm3me.vv vd, vs2, vs1
RULE 1: VSM3ME.VV must raise an illegal-instruction exception and retire with failure when LMUL multiplied by VLEN is less than the effective group width of 256 bits.
RULE 2: VSM3ME.VV must retire successfully when LMUL multiplied by VLEN is greater than or equal to the effective group width of 256 bits.
RULE 3: VSM3ME.VV encoding is reserved when the selected standard element width (SEW) is any value other than 32 bits.
RULE 4: VSM3ME.VV encoding is reserved when the destination register group overlaps the source register group vs2.

INSTRUCTION: VSM4K.VI
FORMAT: vsm4k.vi vd, vs2, zimm5
RULE 1: VSM4K.VI operates on 128-bit element groups each comprising four 32-bit elements.
RULE 2: VSM4K.VI processes a number of element groups equal to vl divided by the element-group size (EGS).
RULE 3: VSM4K.VI requires that vl be a multiple of the element-group size (EGS=4).
RULE 4: VSM4K.VI requires that vstart be a multiple of the element-group size (EGS=4).

INSTRUCTION: VSM4R.VS
FORMAT: vsm4r.vs vd, vs2
RULE 1: VSM4R.VS requires the scalar element group operand to have EMUL equal to ceil(128 / VLEN).
RULE 2: VSM4R.VS requires that the destination register group vd does not overlap the register group containing vs2.
RULE 3: VSM4R.VS belongs to the vsm4* group whose Element Group Size is 4, therefore the value in vl must be an integer multiple of 4.
RULE 4: VSM4R.VS belongs to the vsm4* group whose Element Group Size is 4, therefore the value in vstart must be an integer multiple of 4.
RULE 5: If vl or vstart is not an integer multiple of 4 for VSM4R.VS, the instruction encoding is reserved.
RULE 6: VSM4R.VS requires SEW to be 32; any other SEW value makes the VSM4R.VS encoding reserved.
RULE 7: For VSM4R.VS, LMUL multiplied by VLEN must be at least 128, otherwise an illegal-instruction exception is raised even when vl = 0.
RULE 8: For implementations with VLEN ≥ 128, VSM4R.VS does not require its scalar element group operand to be aligned to LMUL.
RULE 9: If the selected element width (SEW) for VSM4R.VS is any value other than 32, the encoding of VSM4R.VS is reserved.
RULE 10: If the destination register group (vd) overlaps the source register group (vs2) for the .vs form of VSM4R.VS, the encoding of VSM4R.VS is reserved.
RULE 11: If LMUL × VLEN is less than the effective group width (EGW) of 128, VSM4R.VS raises an illegal-instruction exception and retires with failure.
RULE 12: For each active element index i, VSM4R.VS reads four 32-bit words x0, x1, x2, x3 from element i of the destination register group vd.
RULE 13: For the .vs form of VSM4R.VS, the round-key group {rk3 @ rk2 @ rk1 @ rk0} is taken from element 0 of the scalar element group in vs2 for every element index i.
RULE 14: For each element index i, VSM4R.VS computes B = x1 ^ x2 ^ x3 ^ rk0, applies the SM4 sub-word transformation to B producing S, and then computes x4 = sm4_round(x0, S).
RULE 15: For each element index i, VSM4R.VS computes B = x2 ^ x3 ^ x4 ^ rk1, applies the SM4 sub-word transformation to B producing S, and then computes x5 = sm4_round(x1, S).
RULE 16: For each element index i, VSM4R.VS computes B = x3 ^ x4 ^ x5 ^ rk2, applies the SM4 sub-word transformation to B producing S, and then computes x6 = sm4_round(x2, S).
RULE 17: For each element index i, VSM4R.VS computes B = x4 ^ x5 ^ x6 ^ rk3, applies the SM4 sub-word transformation to B producing S, and then computes x7 = sm4_round(x3, S).
RULE 18: After computing x4, x5, x6, and x7 for element index i, VSM4R.VS writes the concatenated 128-bit value (x7 @ x6 @ x5 @ x4) back to element i of the destination register group vd.

INSTRUCTION: VSM4R.VV
FORMAT: vsm4r.vv vd, vs2
RULE 1: VSM4R.VV must raise an illegal-instruction exception and cause RETIRE_FAIL when LMUL multiplied by VLEN is less than 128 bits.
RULE 2: For each element-group index i processed by VSM4R.VV, the instruction writes the 128-bit vector composed of x7, x6, x5, and x4 back to Vd element group i.
RULE 3: After VSM4R.VV has processed all element groups, the instruction must retire successfully (RETIRE_SUCCESS).
RULE 4: In the VSM4R.VV .vv form, the round keys are consumed from Vs2 in the natural order rk0, rk1, rk2, rk3, which distinguishes encryption from decryption.

INSTRUCTION: VSMUL.VV
FORMAT: vsmul.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VSMUL.VV sets the vxsat flag if any element's result is saturated during the operation.
RULE 2: VSMUL.VV MUST NOT be included for EEW = 64 in any Zve64* extension.
RULE 3: VSMUL.VV MUST be available for EEW = 8, 16, and 32 in every Zve* extension.
RULE 4: VSMUL.VV MUST raise precise traps on any trap condition in all implementations that include the instruction.

INSTRUCTION: VSMUL.VX
FORMAT: vsmul.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VSMUL.VX operates on signed SEW-width elements of the vector source operand vs2 and the scalar source register x[rs1].
RULE 2: VSMUL.VX produces a 2 × SEW-bit intermediate product for each element by multiplying the signed SEW-width element vs2[i] with the signed SEW-width scalar x[rs1].
RULE 3: VSMUL.VX shifts each 2 × SEW intermediate product right by SEW-1 bits.
RULE 4: VSMUL.VX rounds the bits shifted out by SEW-1 according to the current rounding mode specified by vxrm.
RULE 5: VSMUL.VX saturates the rounded value so that the final result fits into SEW bits.
RULE 6: VSMUL.VX sets the vxsat bit when saturation occurs for any element.
RULE 7: VSMUL.VX writes each destination element vd[i] as clip(roundoff_signed(vs2[i] * x[rs1], SEW-1)), which is the saturated, rounded result of the signed multiplication.
RULE 8: VSMUL.VX is supported by every Zve* extension except that it is not included when the effective element width (EEW) is 64 in a Zve64* implementation.
RULE 9: VSMUL.VX executes with precise traps on all Zve* extensions.

INSTRUCTION: VSOXEI16.V
FORMAT: vsoxei16.v vs3, (rs1), vs2, vm
RULE 1: VSOXEI16.V stores each element of vector register vs3 to the memory address obtained by adding the 16-bit index element from the corresponding element of vector register vs2 to the base address held in integer register rs1.
RULE 2: VSOXEI16.V interprets the statically encoded EEW field as the element width of the index (16 bits) rather than as the width of the stored data.
RULE 3: VSOXEI16.V performs the stores in element order, guaranteeing that the store of element i completes before the store of element i+1 for all valid element indices.

INSTRUCTION: VSOXEI32.V
FORMAT: vsoxei32.v vs3, (rs1), vs2, vm
RULE 1: VSOXEI32.V stores each element of the source vector register group vs2 to memory using an address formed by adding a 32-bit index element taken from the index vector vs3 to the base address held in rs1, and the store of each element must use the current SEW as the data width.
RULE 2: VSOXEI32.V performs its stores as ordered operations, so the stores must become globally visible in program order relative to other memory accesses.

INSTRUCTION: VSOXEI64.V
FORMAT: vsoxei64.v vs3, (rs1), vs2, vm
RULE 1: VSOXEI64.V must perform an ordered store of 64-bit elements whose data come from the vector register vs3 to memory addresses formed by adding the base address in integer register rs1 to the byte-offsets taken from the corresponding elements of the index vector vs2.

INSTRUCTION: VSOXEI8.V
FORMAT: vsoxei8.v vs3, (rs1), vs2, vm
RULE 1: VSOXEI8.V performs an ordered indexed store of data elements whose width is the current standard-element-width (SEW).
RULE 2: VSOXEI8.V must use an 8-bit element width for the index as defined by the statically encoded EEW of 8 bits, independent of the data element width.
RULE 3: VSOXEI8.V must preserve program order of its store operations, meaning the stores are executed in element order and are not reordered with respect to each other.

INSTRUCTION: VSRA.VI
FORMAT: vsra.vi vd, vs2, zimm5, vm
RULE 1: VSRA.VI MUST perform an arithmetic (sign-extending) right shift on each element of the source vector register group.
RULE 2: VSRA.VI MUST obtain its shift amount from a zero-extended 5-bit immediate field (uimm).
RULE 3: VSRA.VI MUST use only the low lg2(SEW) bits of the shift-amount value to determine the effective shift amount.
RULE 4: VSRA.VI MUST shift each element of the source vector right by the effective shift amount and must sign-extend the vacated bits.

INSTRUCTION: VSRA.VV
FORMAT: vsra.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VSRA.VV MUST perform an arithmetic right shift with sign-extension on each element of the source vector.
RULE 2: VSRA.VV MUST obtain the data to be shifted from the vector register group specified by vs2.
RULE 3: VSRA.VV MUST obtain the shift-amount values from the vector register group specified by vs1.
RULE 4: VSRA.VV MUST use only the low lg2(SEW) bits of each shift-amount element to determine the shift amount applied to the corresponding element.

INSTRUCTION: VSRA.VX
FORMAT: vsra.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VSRA.VX performs a sign-extending arithmetic right shift on each element of the source vector register group vs2.
RULE 2: VSRA.VX obtains the shift-amount value from the scalar integer register rs1.
RULE 3: VSRA.VX uses only the low lg2(SEW) bits of the rs1 value to control the shift amount, and ignores all higher bits.
RULE 4: VSRA.VX writes the shifted result of each element into the corresponding element of the destination vector register group vd.

INSTRUCTION: VSRL.VI
FORMAT: vsrl.vi vd, vs2, zimm5, vm
RULE 1: VSRL.VI performs a logical right shift on each element of the source vector register group vs2, zero-extending the vacated bits, and writes the result to the destination vector register group vd.
RULE 2: VSRL.VI obtains its shift-amount value from a zero-extended 5-bit immediate field named uimm.
RULE 3: VSRL.VI masks the shift-amount value by using only the low lg2(SEW) bits of the zero-extended immediate to determine the actual shift amount for each element.
RULE 4: VSRL.VI applies the vector mask vm such that the shift operation is performed only on elements whose mask bit is true, while elements with a false mask bit are left unchanged.
RULE 5: VSRL.VI reads the element values to be shifted from the vector register group specified by vs2.
RULE 6: VSRL.VI writes the shifted element results to the vector register group specified by vd.

INSTRUCTION: VSRL.VV
FORMAT: vsrl.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VSRL.VV obtains each element's shift amount from the corresponding element of the vs1 vector register group.
RULE 2: VSRL.VV obtains each element's data to be shifted from the corresponding element of the vs2 vector register group.
RULE 3: VSRL.VV uses only the low lg2(SEW) bits of each shift-amount element to control the shift amount for that element.
RULE 4: VSRL.VV performs a logical right shift on each element and fills the vacated bit positions with zeros (zero-extension).

INSTRUCTION: VSRL.VX
FORMAT: vsrl.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VSRL.VX performs a zero-extending logical right shift on each element of the source vector register group specified by vs2.
RULE 2: VSRL.VX writes the result of the shift to the destination vector register group specified by vd.
RULE 3: VSRL.VX obtains the shift-amount value from the scalar integer register specified by rs1.
RULE 4: VSRL.VX uses only the low lg2(SEW) bits of the value read from rs1 to determine the shift amount applied to each element.
RULE 5: VSRL.VX treats the shift operation as logical, inserting zeros into the most-significant bits vacated by the shift.

INSTRUCTION: VSSE16.V
FORMAT: vsse16.v vs3, (rs1), rs2, vm
RULE 1: VSSE16.V supports negative stride values and also a stride value of zero.
RULE 2: VSSE16.V performs element memory accesses in an unordered manner with respect to each other.
RULE 3: When rs2 is x0, VSSE16.V is allowed to perform fewer memory operations than the number of active elements.
RULE 4: When rs2 is x0, VSSE16.V may perform a different number of memory operations across different dynamic executions of the same static instruction.
RULE 5: When rs2 is not x0 and the indexed stride value read from x[rs2] equals zero, VSSE16.V must perform one memory access for each active element, and these accesses are unordered.
RULE 6: VSSE16.V must appear to perform each memory access to other agents, although implementations may internally eliminate accesses that are not observable, such as accesses to idempotent memory regions obeying RVWMO.

INSTRUCTION: VSSE32.V
FORMAT: vsse32.v vs3, (rs1), rs2, vm
RULE 1: VSSE32.V supports negative stride values and a stride value of zero.
RULE 2: VSSE32.V element accesses generated by a constant-stride store are unordered with respect to each other.
RULE 3: If the rs2 operand of VSSE32.V is x0, an implementation may perform fewer memory operations than the number of active elements and may vary the number of memory operations between different dynamic executions of the same static instruction.
RULE 4: If rs2 is not x0 and the integer register indexed by rs2 contains zero, VSSE32.V must perform one memory access for each active element, and these accesses are unordered.
RULE 5: VSSE32.V implementations must appear to perform each memory access mandated by the instruction semantics.
RULE 6: VSSE32.V microarchitectures may eliminate memory accesses that are not observable by any other agent when the accesses target an idempotent memory region that follows the RVWMO memory model.
RULE 7: VSSE32.V implementations cannot eliminate memory accesses when the target memory region is non-idempotent, because each access can be observed by a device.

INSTRUCTION: VSSE64.V
FORMAT: vsse64.v vs3, (rs1), rs2, vm
RULE 1: VSSE64.V supports negative and zero strides.
RULE 2: VSSE64.V element accesses within a constant-stride store are unordered with respect to each other.
RULE 3: When rs2 != x0 for VSSE64.V and the value in the stride register x[rs2] equals zero, the implementation must perform one memory access for each active element, although the accesses remain unordered.
RULE 4: VSSE64.V implementations must appear to perform each memory access.
RULE 5: VSSE64.V accesses to nonidempotent memory regions cannot be optimized away because each access can be observed by a device.

INSTRUCTION: VSSE8.V
FORMAT: vsse8.v vs3, (rs1), rs2, vm
RULE 1: VSSE8.V supports negative stride values.
RULE 2: VSSE8.V supports a stride value of zero.
RULE 3: VSSE8.V performs element accesses in an unordered manner relative to each other.
RULE 4: If the rs2 operand of VSSE8.V is x0, an implementation may perform fewer memory operations than the number of active elements and may vary the number of memory operations across dynamic executions of the same static instruction.
RULE 5: If rs2 is not x0 and the integer register x[rs2] contains zero, VSSE8.V must perform one memory access for each active element, though the accesses remain unordered.
RULE 6: VSSE8.V must appear to perform each memory access architecturally, but microarchitectures may omit accesses that cannot be observed by any agent in idempotent memory regions complying with RVWMO.
RULE 7: In non-idempotent memory regions, VSSE8.V implementations cannot omit any memory access because each access can be observed by a device.

INSTRUCTION: VSSEG3E32.V
FORMAT: vsseg3e32.v vs3, (rs1), vm
RULE 1: VSSEG3E32.V stores a packed vector consisting of three 4-byte segments to memory.
RULE 2: VSSEG3E32.V writes the packed segments to memory beginning at the address held in rs1.

INSTRUCTION: VSSRA.VI
FORMAT: vssra.vi vd, vs2, zimm5, vm
RULE 1: VSSRA.VI uses a zero-extended 5-bit immediate (uimm) as the shift-amount operand.
RULE 2: VSSRA.VI determines the effective shift amount by taking the low lg2(SEW) bits of the immediate uimm and ignoring any higher bits.
RULE 3: For each element i of the source vector register group vs2 that is enabled by the vector mask vm, VSSRA.VI computes vd[i] as the result of a sign-extending arithmetic right shift of vs2[i] by the effective shift amount, with the bits shifted out rounded according to the current rounding mode vxrm.
RULE 4: VSSRA.VI updates only those destination elements vd[i] for which the corresponding mask bit vm[i] is true; elements with vm[i] false are left unchanged.
RULE 5: The rounding of the bits shifted out by VSSRA.VI follows the rounding mode specified by the vector rounding mode register vxrm.
RULE 6: The sign-extending arithmetic right shift performed by VSSRA.VI replicates the sign bit of the original element into the vacated high-order bits.

INSTRUCTION: VSSRA.VV
FORMAT: vssra.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VSSRA.VV performs a scaling right arithmetic shift that sign-extends the shifted value.
RULE 2: VSSRA.VV takes the data to be shifted from each element of the vector register group specified by vs2.
RULE 3: VSSRA.VV takes the shift-amount value for each element from the corresponding element of the vector register group specified by vs1.
RULE 4: VSSRA.VV uses only the low lg2(SEW) bits of each element of the vs1 shift-amount value to determine the shift amount for that element.
RULE 5: VSSRA.VV rounds the bits shifted out of each element according to the current vxrm rounding mode.
RULE 6: VSSRA.VV computes vd[i] = roundoff_signed(vs2[i], vs1[i]) for each element i, where roundoff_signed denotes a sign-extending arithmetic right shift of vs2[i] by the low lg2(SEW) bits of vs1[i] with rounding applied as defined by vxrm.

INSTRUCTION: VSSRL.VI
FORMAT: vssrl.vi vd, vs2, zimm5, vm
RULE 1: VSSRL.VI vd, vs2, uimm, vm must compute each destination element as vd[i] = roundoff_unsigned(vs2[i], uimm).
RULE 2: VSSRL.VI must shift each source element right by a shift amount equal to the low lg2(SEW) bits of the zero-extended 5-bit immediate uimm.
RULE 3: VSSRL.VI must round the bits shifted out according to the current rounding mode specified by vxrm.
RULE 4: VSSRL.VI must produce a zero-extending logical shift right result for each element.
RULE 5: VSSRL.VI must take the data to be shifted from the vector register group specified by vs2.
RULE 6: VSSRL.VI must apply element-wise masking as indicated by the vm field, leaving masked-off destination elements unchanged.
RULE 7: VSSRL.VI must obtain the shift amount from a zero-extended 5-bit immediate field named uimm.

INSTRUCTION: VSSRL.VV
FORMAT: vssrl.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VSSRL.VV must shift each element of the source vector register group specified by vs2 right by a per-element amount taken from the corresponding element of the vector register group specified by vs1 and must round the shifted-out bits according to the current rounding mode (vxrm).
RULE 2: VSSRL.VV must produce zero-extended results, i.e., the high bits of each destination element are filled with zeros after the shift-right logical operation.
RULE 3: VSSRL.VV must obtain the data to be shifted from the vector register group identified by the vs2 operand.
RULE 4: VSSRL.VV must obtain the shift-amount value for each element from the vector register group identified by the vs1 operand.
RULE 5: VSSRL.VV must use only the low lg2(SEW) bits of each shift-amount element to determine the effective shift amount for the corresponding element.
RULE 6: VSSRL.VV must write each resulting element to the destination vector register group vd, and the write must be masked by the optional mask vm so that elements for which vm is false remain unchanged.
RULE 7: For every element index i, VSSRL.VV must set vd[i] equal to roundoff_unsigned(vs2[i], vs1[i]), where roundoff_unsigned implements a logical right shift of vs2[i] by the effective shift amount derived from vs1[i] and applies rounding according to vxrm.

INSTRUCTION: VSSRL.VX
FORMAT: vssrl.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VSSRL.VX writes each element of the destination vector register group vd with the result of the scaling shift right logical operation.
RULE 2: VSSRL.VX uses the elements of the source vector register group vs2 as the data to be shifted.
RULE 3: VSSRL.VX obtains the shift-amount value from scalar integer register rs1 and uses only the low lg2(SEW) bits of that value to control the shift amount.
RULE 4: VSSRL.VX performs a logical right shift on each element of vs2 by the amount specified by the low lg2(SEW) bits of rs1, producing a zero-extended unsigned intermediate result.
RULE 5: VSSRL.VX rounds the bits shifted out of each element according to the current vxrm rounding mode.
RULE 6: VSSRL.VX stores into each element vd[i] the value resulting from applying the unsigned rounding operation defined by the current vxrm setting to the logically right-shifted value of vs2[i] using the shift amount x[rs1].

INSTRUCTION: VSSSEG2E32.V
FORMAT: vssseg2e32.v vs3, (rs1), rs2, vm
RULE 1: VSSSEG2E32.V must support a byte-stride offset provided by rs2 that can be negative, zero, or positive.
RULE 2: For each element index i where 0 ≤ i < VL, VSSSEG2E32.V stores the element of the first segment (vector register v2[i]) to the memory address computed as rs1 + i * rs2.
RULE 3: For each element index i where 0 ≤ i < VL, VSSSEG2E32.V stores the element of the second segment (vector register v3[i]) to the memory address computed as rs1 + i * rs2 + 4 bytes.
RULE 4: For segment s of VSSSEG2E32.V where s is 0 or 1, the store address for element i is rs1 + i * rs2 + s * 4 bytes.

INSTRUCTION: VSSUB.VV
FORMAT: vssub.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VSSUB.VV subtracts the signed integer element in source vector register vs2 from the signed integer element in source vector register vs1 for each element position and writes the resulting element to the corresponding position of destination vector register vd.
RULE 2: VSSUB.VV replaces the result of an element subtraction with the nearest representable signed integer value when the true difference exceeds the destination element's signed integer range.
RULE 3: VSSUB.VV sets the architectural vxsat flag when any element subtraction overflows and is saturated to the closest representable value.

INSTRUCTION: VSSUB.VX
FORMAT: vssub.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VSSUB.VX reads the signed integer value from scalar register rs1 and treats it as the scalar operand for the subtraction.
RULE 2: VSSUB.VX reads each element of source vector register vs2 as a signed integer.
RULE 3: For each element index i where the corresponding mask bit in vm is true, VSSUB.VX computes the signed subtraction result = vs2[i] − rs1.
RULE 4: For each element index i where the corresponding mask bit in vm is false, VSSUB.VX does not modify the destination element vd[i].
RULE 5: If the signed subtraction result for an element would exceed the representable range of the destination element type, VSSUB.VX saturates that element to the nearest representable value of that type.
RULE 6: When any element saturation occurs, VSSUB.VX sets the vxsat status bit.
RULE 7: If no element overflow occurs, VSSUB.VX leaves the vxsat status bit unchanged.

INSTRUCTION: VSSUBU.VV
FORMAT: vssubu.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VSSUBU.VV performs a saturating subtraction of unsigned integer elements, subtracting each element of source vector register vs1 from the corresponding element of source vector register vs2 and writing the element result to destination vector register vd.
RULE 2: If an elementwise unsigned subtraction performed by VSSUBU.VV would produce a value outside the representable range of the destination element type, VSSUBU.VV replaces that element result with the nearest representable value and sets the vxsat bit in the vector status register.

INSTRUCTION: VSSUBU.VX
FORMAT: vssubu.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VSSUBU.VX MUST subtract the unsigned integer scalar in integer register rs1 from each unsigned integer element of source vector register vs2 and write the unsigned integer results to destination vector register vd.
RULE 2: VSSUBU.VX MUST replace an element result with the closest representable unsigned integer value when the subtraction would overflow the destination element width.
RULE 3: VSSUBU.VX MUST set the vxsat flag when any element result is replaced because of overflow.
RULE 4: VSSUBU.VX MUST NOT set the vxsat flag when no element result overflows the destination element width.

INSTRUCTION: VSUXEI16.V
FORMAT: vsuxei16.v vs3, (rs1), vs2, vm
RULE 1: VSUXEI16.V performs an unordered 16-bit indexed store of data elements whose size is the current SEW.
RULE 2: In VSUXEI16.V the suffix `ei` specifies that the statically encoded EEW applies to the index elements rather than to the stored data elements.

INSTRUCTION: VSUXEI32.V
FORMAT: vsuxei32.v vs3, (rs1), vs2, vm
RULE 1: VSUXEI32.V is a vector indexed-unordered store instruction that stores the elements of vector register group vs3 to memory.
RULE 2: VSUXEI32.V stores each element of vector register group vs3 to a memory address computed by adding the scalar register rs1 base address to a byte-offset taken from the corresponding element of vector register group vs2.
RULE 3: VSUXEI32.V uses the EEW field encoded in the instruction to define the width of the index, and for VSUXEI32.V the statically encoded EEW is 32 bits.
RULE 4: VSUXEI32.V is classified as a memory scatter (indexed store) instruction.
RULE 5: VSUXEI32.V scatters elements from a source vector to memory locations whose addresses are computed by adding a base address to offset values supplied by an index vector, which must be appropriately scaled before the instruction executes.
RULE 6: In the form VSUXEI32.V v8, (a2), v16, v0.t, the vector register v8 provides the elements that are stored to memory.
RULE 7: In the form VSUXEI32.V v8, (a2), v16, v0.t, the register a2 supplies the base address used for address calculation.
RULE 8: In the form VSUXEI32.V v8, (a2), v16, v0.t, the vector register v16 supplies the offset values that are added to the base address to form the destination address for each element.
RULE 9: In the form VSUXEI32.V v8, (a2), v16, v0.t, the mask register v0.t causes VSUXEI32.V to store an element only when the corresponding mask bit is true.
RULE 10: VSUXEI32.V leaves memory unchanged for elements whose corresponding mask bit in the mask register is false.

INSTRUCTION: VSUXEI64.V
FORMAT: vsuxei64.v vs3, (rs1), vs2, vm
RULE 1: VSUXEI64.V is a vector indexed-unordered store instruction that writes SEW data to memory.
RULE 2: VSUXEI64.V takes vs3 as the vector register containing the data to be stored, rs1 as the scalar base address register, vs2 as the vector register holding byte-offset indices, and vm as the vector mask register controlling element-wise activity.
RULE 3: VSUXEI64.V stores 64-bit elements from vs3 to memory addresses computed by adding the base address in rs1 to each byte-offset element in vs2.
RULE 4: VSUXEI64.V uses the statically-encoded EEW field to define the element width of the index vector vs2, not the width of the stored data elements.
RULE 5: VSUXEI64.V applies the mask vm such that only elements with a true mask bit are written to memory, while elements with a false mask bit are not stored.
RULE 6: VSUXEI64.V is classified as an unordered store, meaning that the architectural effect does not impose any ordering constraint on the memory writes of different elements.
RULE 7: The same opcode may be accepted as an alias written as VSXEI64.V, and executing that alias must produce the identical architectural effect as VSUXEI64.V.

INSTRUCTION: VSUXEI8.V
FORMAT: vsuxei8.v vs3, (rs1), vs2, vm
RULE 1: VSUXEI8.V stores 8-bit elements, where the element width equals the standard element width (SEW) of 8 bits.
RULE 2: VSUXEI8.V uses the scalar register rs1 as the base address for all memory stores.
RULE 3: VSUXEI8.V uses the vector register vs2 to provide a byte offset for each element's store address.
RULE 4: VSUXEI8.V uses the vector mask register vm to determine which elements are active for storing.
RULE 5: For each element i where vm[i] is true, VSUXEI8.V stores the 8-bit element from vs3[i] to the memory address computed as rs1 plus the byte offset vs2[i].
RULE 6: For each element i where vm[i] is false, VSUXEI8.V does not perform a store to memory for that element.
RULE 7: VSUXEI8.V may perform the stores for active elements in any order because the accesses are unordered.

INSTRUCTION: VSUXSEG2EI32.V
FORMAT: vsuxseg2ei32.v vs3, (rs1), vs2, vm
RULE 1: VSUXSEG2EI32.V stores a 32-bit word from the first element of the destination data register group (v2[i]) to the memory address obtained by adding the scalar base address in rs1 to the byte offset contained in the corresponding element of the index register group (v5[i]).
RULE 2: VSUXSEG2EI32.V stores a 32-bit word from the second element of the destination data register group (v3[i]) to the memory address obtained by adding the scalar base address in rs1 to the byte offset contained in the corresponding element of the index register group (v5[i]) plus 4 bytes.
RULE 3: VSUXSEG2EI32.V computes each target address by adding the scalar base address in rs1 to the byte offset given by the corresponding element of the index vector register group and, for subsequent fields within a segment, adds an additional offset equal to the field index multiplied by the element width (32 bits).
RULE 4: VSUXSEG2EI32.V uses an index vector register group whose element width EEW is encoded in the instruction and whose group multiplier EMUL equals (EEW / SEW) × LMUL.
RULE 5: VSUXSEG2EI32.V must satisfy the register-group size constraint that EMUL × NFIELDS (where NFIELDS = 2) does not exceed 8 for the data vector register group.
RULE 6: VSUXSEG2EI32.V permits the destination data register groups to overlap the source index register group because the overlap constraint that applies to indexed segment loads does not apply to stores.

INSTRUCTION: VWADD.VV
FORMAT: vwadd.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VWADD.VV adds each pair of corresponding SEW-wide elements from source vector registers vs2 and vs1 after sign-extending both operands to 2 × SEW bits, producing a 2 × SEW-wide signed sum for each element.
RULE 2: VWADD.VV writes each 2 × SEW-wide sum result to the corresponding element of the destination vector register vd.
RULE 3: VWADD.VV uses the mask specified by the vm field to control element-wise activation of the addition operation.

INSTRUCTION: VWADD.VX
FORMAT: vwadd.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: vwadd.vx sign-extends each SEW-wide element of the source vector register vs2 and the SEW-wide scalar element in rs1 to double-width (2*SEW) before performing the addition.
RULE 2: For every element position where the mask vm is true, vwadd.vx adds the sign-extended vs2 element to the sign-extended rs1 element, producing a double-width result.
RULE 3: vwadd.vx writes the double-width result of each active element to the corresponding element of the destination vector register vd.
RULE 4: vwadd.vx leaves the elements of vd unchanged for element positions where the mask vm is false.
RULE 5: The result width produced by vwadd.vx is exactly twice the standard element width of the source operands (2*SEW).
RULE 6: When the scalar register rs1 is x0, vwadd.vx sign-extends the vs2 elements to double-width, adds zero, and therefore stores a double-width copy of the sign-extended vs2 elements in vd.

INSTRUCTION: VWADD.WX
FORMAT: vwadd.wx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VWADD.WX adds a signed SEW-width scalar from integer register rs1 to each SEW-width element of the vector source vs2 and stores the 2*SEW-width results in the destination vector vd.
RULE 2: The destination element width of VWADD.WX is defined to be twice the standard element width (2*SEW).
RULE 3: VWADD.WX treats both the scalar operand and the vector source elements as signed integers during the addition.
RULE 4: When the scalar register rs1 is x0, VWADD.WX produces a result whose width is twice that of the original integer value.
RULE 5: If rs1 is x0, the scalar value used by VWADD.WX is zero, causing the operation to effectively sign-extend each vector element to double width.

INSTRUCTION: VWADDU.VV
FORMAT: vwaddu.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VWADDU.VV performs an element-wise unsigned integer addition of the corresponding elements of the two source vector registers vs2 and vs1.
RULE 2: VWADDU.VV widens each unsigned addition result to an element width of 2 × SEW before writing it to the destination vector register vd.
RULE 3: VWADDU.VV follows the vector-vector form, taking two source vector registers vs2 and vs1 and a destination vector register vd as operands.
RULE 4: VWADDU.VV may include an optional vector mask operand vm, and when vm is present the mask determines which elements of vd are updated.
RULE 5: VWADDU.VV updates all elements of vd when the optional mask operand vm is omitted.

INSTRUCTION: VWADDU.VX
FORMAT: vwaddu.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: When VWADDU.VX is executed with the scalar operand x0, each integer element of the source vector is widened to double its original width by adding zero.

INSTRUCTION: VWADDU.WX
FORMAT: vwaddu.wx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VWADDU.WX widens unsigned integer elements from the source element width (SEW) to twice that width (2*SEW) by performing an unsigned addition with the unsigned scalar register operand.
RULE 2: For each element i whose mask bit in vm is set, VWADDU.WX computes the result as (zero-extend(vs2[i]) + zero-extend(rs1)) modulo 2^(2*SEW) and writes the result to vd[i].
RULE 3: For each element i whose mask bit in vm is clear, VWADDU.WX does not modify vd[i] and leaves its previous value unchanged.
RULE 4: VWADDU.WX processes elements only up to the current vector length (VL) as defined by the active configuration of SEW and LMUL.

INSTRUCTION: VWMACC.VV
FORMAT: vwmacc.vv vd, vs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VWMACC.VV must, for each element i selected by the current vector length, compute the full-width product of the signed SEW-bit elements vs1[i] and vs2[i], add the existing 2 × SEW-bit element vd[i], and write the 2 × SEW-bit result back to vd[i].
RULE 2: VWMACC.VV must treat both source operands vs1 and vs2 as signed values, and no unsigned operand combinations are defined for this instruction.
RULE 3: VWMACC.VV must produce a 2 × SEW-bit result for each element, where the multiplication yields a full-width 2 × SEW-bit product and the addition also operates on 2 × SEW-bit operands.
RULE 4: If a mask register vm is supplied to VWMACC.VV, the instruction must update only those elements i whose corresponding mask bit is 1 and must leave elements with mask bit 0 unchanged.
RULE 5: If a mask register is not supplied to VWMACC.VV, the instruction must update all elements selected by the current vector length.

INSTRUCTION: VWMACC.VX
FORMAT: vwmacc.vx vd, rs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VWMACC.VX interprets the scalar operand in integer register rs1 and each element of the vector source operand vs2 as signed SEW-bit integers for the multiplication.
RULE 2: VWMACC.VX computes for each element i a full 2*SEW-bit product by multiplying the signed SEW-bit scalar x[rs1] with the signed SEW-bit vector element vs2[i].
RULE 3: VWMACC.VX adds the 2*SEW-bit product to the existing 2*SEW-bit value held in destination element vd[i].
RULE 4: VWMACC.VX overwrites each destination element vd[i] with the 2*SEW-bit sum produced by the multiply-add operation.
RULE 5: VWMACC.VX updates only those elements whose corresponding mask bit in vm is true; elements whose mask bit is false retain their prior value in vd.
RULE 6: VWMACC.VX produces a 2*SEW-bit result for each element regardless of the original SEW of the source operands.

INSTRUCTION: VWMACCSU.VV
FORMAT: vwmaccsu.vv vd, vs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VWMACCSU.VV treats each element vs1[i] as a signed SEW-bit integer for the multiplication.
RULE 2: VWMACCSU.VV treats each element vs2[i] as an unsigned SEW-bit integer for the multiplication.
RULE 3: VWMACCSU.VV computes the product of signed(vs1[i]) and unsigned(vs2[i]) as a full 2*SEW-bit value.
RULE 4: VWMACCSU.VV adds the full 2*SEW-bit product to the existing 2*SEW-bit element value in vd[i].
RULE 5: VWMACCSU.VV writes the 2*SEW-bit sum back to the corresponding element of vd.
RULE 6: VWMACCSU.VV produces a 2*SEW-bit result for each element regardless of operand signs.
RULE 7: VWMACCSU.VV is a widening integer multiply-add instruction that adds the full 2*SEW-bit product to a 2*SEW-bit accumulator.

INSTRUCTION: VWMACCSU.VX
FORMAT: vwmaccsu.vx vd, rs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VWMACCSU.VX must treat the scalar source register rs1 as a signed integer for the multiplication.
RULE 2: VWMACCSU.VX must treat each element of the vector source register vs2 as an unsigned integer for the multiplication.
RULE 3: VWMACCSU.VX must compute, for each element i of the destination vector register vd, the product of signed(rs1) and unsigned(vs2[i]) as a SEW-bit × SEW-bit operation that yields a full 2 × SEW-bit intermediate product.
RULE 4: VWMACCSU.VX must add the 2 × SEW-bit product to the existing 2 × SEW-bit value currently stored in vd[i].
RULE 5: VWMACCSU.VX must store the resulting 2 × SEW-bit sum back into vd[i] for each element whose mask is true.
RULE 6: VWMACCSU.VX produces a 2 × SEW-bit result for each element of the destination vector.
RULE 7: VWMACCSU.VX is a widening signed-unsigned integer multiply-add operation that overwrites the addend.
RULE 8: VWMACCSU.VX supports all signed-unsigned operand combinations for the multiply.
RULE 9: VWMACCSU.VX must not modify vd[i] when the corresponding mask bit vm for element i is false.
RULE 10: VWMACCSU.VX must perform the multiply-add and write the result into vd[i] when the corresponding mask bit vm for element i is true.

INSTRUCTION: VWMACCU.VV
FORMAT: vwmaccu.vv vd, vs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VWMACCU.VV is a widening unsigned-integer multiply-add instruction that overwrites the addend in the destination vector register.
RULE 2: For each element i that is active according to the optional mask vm, VWMACCU.VV multiplies the unsigned SEW-bit element vs1[i] by the unsigned SEW-bit element vs2[i] and produces a full 2×SEW-bit product.
RULE 3: For each active element i, VWMACCU.VV adds the 2×SEW-bit product to the existing 2×SEW-bit value held in vd[i].
RULE 4: For each active element i, VWMACCU.VV writes the resulting 2×SEW-bit sum back to the destination element vd[i].
RULE 5: VWMACCU.VV adds the full 2×SEW-bit product from a SEW-bit × SEW-bit unsigned multiply to a 2×SEW-bit value and therefore yields a 2×SEW-bit result for each element.
RULE 6: VWMACCU.VV treats the source operands as unsigned integers; signed multiplication is not performed by this instruction.
RULE 7: The optional mask vm of VWMACCU.VV determines which element positions are processed; elements for which the mask is not active are not written by the instruction.

INSTRUCTION: VWMACCU.VX
FORMAT: vwmaccu.vx vd, rs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VWMACCU.VX uses the destination vector register vd, the scalar integer register rs1, the source vector register vs2, and the mask register vm as its operands.
RULE 2: For each element index i for which the mask vm element is true, VWMACCU.VX computes the full 2 × SEW-bit product of the unsigned integer value in scalar register x[rs1] and the unsigned integer element vs2[i].
RULE 3: For each element index i selected by the mask vm, VWMACCU.VX adds the existing 2 × SEW-bit value stored in vd[i] to the product computed from x[rs1] and vs2[i].
RULE 4: For each element index i selected by the mask vm, VWMACCU.VX writes the 2 × SEW-bit result of the addition back into vd[i].
RULE 5: The result stored by VWMACCU.VX in each vd[i] element is 2 × SEW bits wide.
RULE 6: The multiplicand taken from scalar register x[rs1] for VWMACCU.VX is SEW bits wide.
RULE 7: The multiplicand taken from source vector register vs2 for VWMACCU.VX is SEW bits wide for each element.
RULE 8: VWMACCU.VX overwrites the destination vector register vd with the newly computed 2 × SEW-bit values.
RULE 9: VWMACCU.VX implements the unsigned-unsigned signedness combination of the widening integer multiply-add family.
RULE 10: For each element index i for which the mask vm element is false, VWMACCU.VX does not modify vd[i].

INSTRUCTION: VWMACCUS.VX
FORMAT: vwmaccus.vx vd, rs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VWMACCUS.VX uses the overwrite-addend form of widening integer multiply-add, meaning the 2×SEW-bit addend stored in each destination element is replaced by the computed sum.
RULE 2: VWMACCUS.VX treats the scalar integer register rs1 as an unsigned operand for the multiplication.
RULE 3: VWMACCUS.VX treats each element of the source vector register vs2 as a signed operand for the multiplication.
RULE 4: VWMACCUS.VX multiplies the unsigned SEW-bit value from rs1 by the signed SEW-bit element from vs2, producing a full 2×SEW-bit product.
RULE 5: VWMACCUS.VX adds the 2×SEW-bit product to the existing 2×SEW-bit value held in the corresponding element of destination register vd.
RULE 6: VWMACCUS.VX stores the 2×SEW-bit sum into the corresponding element of destination register vd, overwriting the previous addend.
RULE 7: VWMACCUS.VX produces a 2×SEW-bit result for each element of the destination vector.
RULE 8: VWMACCUS.VX respects an optional mask vm such that for each element where the mask bit is zero the destination element is not modified.
RULE 9: VWMACCUS.VX respects an optional mask vm such that for each element where the mask bit is one the multiplication, addition, and writeback described above are performed.

INSTRUCTION: VWMUL.VV
FORMAT: vwmul.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: For each element index, VWMUL.VV must multiply the signed SEW-bit element from vs2 by the signed SEW-bit element from vs1 to obtain the full 2 × SEW-bit product.
RULE 2: VWMUL.VV must write the full 2 × SEW-bit product for each element into the corresponding element of the destination vector register group vd.
RULE 3: VWMUL.VV must produce result elements whose width is exactly twice the standard element width (2 × SEW bits).

INSTRUCTION: VWMUL.VX
FORMAT: vwmul.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VWMUL.VX MUST perform a signed integer multiplication of each SEW-bit element of the source vector register group vs2 with the SEW-bit signed value held in scalar integer register rs1.
RULE 2: VWMUL.VX MUST produce for each element a 2 × SEW-bit product that constitutes the full result of the SEW-bit by SEW-bit multiplication.
RULE 3: VWMUL.VX MUST write each 2 × SEW-bit product into the corresponding element of the destination vector register group vd, which is sized to hold 2 × SEW-bit elements.
RULE 4: VWMUL.VX MAY be executed with an optional vector mask vm; for each element whose mask bit is 1 the resulting product is written to vd, and for each element whose mask bit is 0 the existing value in vd is left unchanged.
RULE 5: VWMUL.VX without a mask operand behaves as if all mask bits are 1, causing every element of vd to be updated with the multiplication result.

INSTRUCTION: VWMULSU.VV
FORMAT: vwmulsu.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VWMULSU.VV produces, for each element, a 2 × SEW-bit result equal to the product of the signed SEW-bit element from source vector register vs2 and the unsigned SEW-bit element from source vector register vs1.
RULE 2: VWMULSU.VV stores the 2 × SEW-bit product into the corresponding element of destination vector register vd for every element whose mask bit in vm is set.
RULE 3: VWMULSU.VV does not modify the corresponding element of destination vector register vd for any element whose mask bit in vm is clear.

INSTRUCTION: VWMULSU.VX
FORMAT: vwmulsu.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VWMULSU.VX must interpret each element of the source vector register vs2 as a signed SEW-bit integer and the scalar register rs1 as an unsigned SEW-bit integer for the multiplication.
RULE 2: VWMULSU.VX must multiply the signed SEW-bit element from vs2 by the unsigned SEW-bit scalar from rs1 to produce a full 2 × SEW-bit product for each element.
RULE 3: VWMULSU.VX must write the complete 2 × SEW-bit product into the corresponding element of the destination vector register vd, whose elements are 2 × SEW bits wide.
RULE 4: VWMULSU.VX must update only those elements of vd for which the corresponding mask bit in vm is true; elements with a false mask bit must remain unchanged.

INSTRUCTION: VWMULU.VV
FORMAT: vwmulu.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VWMULU.VV multiplies each SEW-bit element of the source vector register group vs2 by the corresponding SEW-bit element of the source vector register group vs1 and produces the full 2 × SEW-bit unsigned product for each element.
RULE 2: VWMULU.VV writes the 2 × SEW-bit product of each element into the corresponding element of the destination vector register group vd.
RULE 3: VWMULU.VV can be executed with an optional mask register vm that determines which elements of the destination vector register group are updated.
RULE 4: When the mask bit for an element is disabled during execution of VWMULU.VV, the corresponding element of vd retains its prior value.

INSTRUCTION: VWMULU.VX
FORMAT: vwmulu.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VWMULU.VX performs an unsigned integer multiply of each element of the source vector register group vs2 with the scalar integer register rs1.
RULE 2: VWMULU.VX returns the full 2 × SEW-bit product for each multiplication, where SEW is the standard element width of the input operands.
RULE 3: VWMULU.VX writes the 2 × SEW-bit widened product of each active element into the corresponding element of the destination vector register group vd.
RULE 4: VWMULU.VX uses the mask register vm to control element-wise activation, performing the multiplication only for elements whose mask bit is set.

INSTRUCTION: VWREDSUMU.VS
FORMAT: vwredsumu.vs vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VWREDSUMU.VS zero-extends each SEW-wide element of the source vector register vs2 to 2*SEW bits before any arithmetic is performed.
RULE 2: VWREDSUMU.VS computes the arithmetic sum of all zero-extended 2*SEW-wide elements from vs2.
RULE 3: VWREDSUMU.VS adds the 2*SEW-width scalar element from the source scalar register vs1 to the sum obtained from the zero-extended vector elements.
RULE 4: VWREDSUMU.VS writes the 2*SEW-width result of the addition into the destination scalar register vd, and any overflow beyond 2*SEW bits wraps around modulo 2^(2*SEW).

INSTRUCTION: VWSLL.VI
FORMAT: vwsll.vi vd, vs2, zimm5, vm
RULE 1: VWSLL.VI widens each SEW-sized element of the source vector register vs2 to 2 × SEW bits by zero-extension before performing the shift.
RULE 2: VWSLL.VI shifts the zero-extended element left by a shift amount taken from the 5-bit immediate, using only the low log₂(2 × SEW) bits of that immediate and ignoring all higher bits.
RULE 3: VWSLL.VI writes the shifted result into the corresponding element of the destination vector register vd, where each destination element has width 2 × SEW.
RULE 4: VWSLL.VI processes vector elements with indices i satisfying vstart ≤ i < vl, performing the operation for each such element.
RULE 5: For each active element i, VWSLL.VI sets vd[i] equal to the zero-extended value of vs2[i] shifted left by (uimm & ((2 × SEW) - 1)) bits.
RULE 6: VWSLL.VI completes execution with a normal successful retirement, raising no architectural exception under defined operation.

INSTRUCTION: VWSLL.VV
FORMAT: vwsll.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VWSLL.VV MUST zero-extend each element of source vector register vs2 to 2 × SEW bits before performing the shift.
RULE 2: VWSLL.VV MUST obtain the shift amount for each element from the corresponding element of source vector register vs1.
RULE 3: VWSLL.VV MUST use only the low log₂(2 × SEW) bits of each shift-amount element and ignore all higher bits.
RULE 4: VWSLL.VV MUST shift the zero-extended vs2 element left by the masked shift amount derived from the corresponding vs1 element.
RULE 5: VWSLL.VV MUST write each shifted result into the corresponding element of destination vector register vd using an element width EEW equal to 2 × SEW.
RULE 6: VWSLL.VV MUST perform the above operation for every element index i from vstart to vl − 1.
RULE 7: VWSLL.VV MUST complete execution with a successful retirement after all active elements have been processed.
RULE 8: VWSLL.VV is defined only when the Zvbb extension is implemented; if the Zvbb extension is not present, the encoding is reserved.

INSTRUCTION: VWSLL.VX
FORMAT: vwsll.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VWSLL.VX obtains the shift amount from the integer register rs1.
RULE 2: VWSLL.VX uses only the low log2(2 × SEW) bits of the value read from rs1 as the shift amount, ignoring all higher bits.
RULE 3: VWSLL.VX zero-extends each SEW-wide element of the source vector register vs2 to 2 × SEW bits before shifting.
RULE 4: VWSLL.VX left-shifts each zero-extended element of vs2 by the masked shift amount derived from rs1.
RULE 5: VWSLL.VX writes the 2 × SEW-wide shifted result into the corresponding element of the destination vector register vd.
RULE 6: VWSLL.VX processes vector elements with indices from vstart through vl-1 inclusive.
RULE 7: VWSLL.VX retires successfully after all active elements have been processed.

INSTRUCTION: VWSUB.VX
FORMAT: vwsub.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VWSUB.VX MUST subtract the signed SEW-wide integer value in integer register rs1 from each signed SEW-wide element of source vector register vs2, performing the subtraction after widening both operands to 2*SEW and producing a 2*SEW-wide result element.
RULE 2: VWSUB.VX MUST write each 2*SEW-wide result element into the corresponding element of destination vector register vd.
RULE 3: VWSUB.VX MUST apply the vector mask vm so that a result element is written to vd only when the corresponding mask bit is 1, and leaves the destination element unchanged when the mask bit is 0.
RULE 4: VWSUB.VX MUST treat the scalar operand in integer register rs1 as a signed SEW-wide value before widening it to 2*SEW for the subtraction.
RULE 5: VWSUB.VX MUST operate only on the active elements specified by the current vector length (VL), and must not produce results for elements beyond the active length.

INSTRUCTION: VWSUB.WX
FORMAT: vwsub.wx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VWSUB.WX is a vector-scalar instruction that takes a destination vector register vd, a source vector register vs2, a scalar integer register rs1, and an optional mask register vm as its operands.
RULE 2: VWSUB.WX reads each SEW-sized element from vs2 and the signed integer value from rs1, subtracts the scalar value from the element, and produces a double-width (2 × SEW) signed result for each element.
RULE 3: VWSUB.WX writes the double-width (2 × SEW) result of each element to the corresponding element of the destination vector register vd.
RULE 4: VWSUB.WX applies the vector mask vm such that for any element where the mask bit is clear the destination element is not written and retains its prior value.
RULE 5: VWSUB.WX performs the subtraction using signed arithmetic, treating both the source vector elements and the scalar operand as signed integers.
RULE 6: VWSUB.WX sign-extends the SEW-sized subtraction result to 2 × SEW width before storing it in the destination vector register.

INSTRUCTION: VWSUBU.VX
FORMAT: vwsubu.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VWSUBU.VX is a vector-scalar instruction that computes a widening unsigned integer subtraction producing a double-width result for each element.
RULE 2: For VWSUBU.VX each narrower source operand (the element from vs2 and the scalar in rs1) is zero-extended to double width before the subtraction is performed.
RULE 3: VWSUBU.VX writes the double-width subtraction result into the corresponding element of the destination vector register vd.
RULE 4: VWSUBU.VX uses the vm field as a mask so that only elements whose mask bit is set are written to vd, while elements with a clear mask bit retain their previous value.

INSTRUCTION: VWSUBU.WX
FORMAT: vwsubu.wx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VWSUBU.WX is a widening unsigned integer subtract instruction that subtracts a scalar unsigned integer operand from each element of a source vector of narrower unsigned integers and produces double-width results.
RULE 2: VWSUBU.WX takes a destination vector register group (vd) which holds the double-width results of the operation.
RULE 3: VWSUBU.WX reads a source vector register group (vs2) that contains the narrower unsigned integer elements to be subtracted.
RULE 4: VWSUBU.WX reads a scalar integer register (rs1) that provides the narrower unsigned integer operand used for subtraction from each element of vs2.
RULE 5: VWSUBU.WX may include an optional vector mask register (vm) that predicates the elementwise operation.
RULE 6: For each element for which the mask vm permits execution, VWSUBU.WX computes the result by zero-extending the vs2 element and the rs1 operand to double width, subtracting the extended rs1 from the extended vs2 element, and writing the double-width result to the corresponding element of vd.
RULE 7: Elements for which the mask vm does not permit execution are not written by VWSUBU.WX and retain their prior values in vd.

INSTRUCTION: VXOR.VI
FORMAT: vxor.vi vd, vs2, simm5, vm
RULE 1: VXOR.VI must compute a bitwise XOR between each active element *i* of the source vector register group *vs2* and the 5-bit signed immediate *imm*, and write the result to the corresponding element *i* of the destination vector register group *vd*.
RULE 2: VXOR.VI must leave the destination element unchanged for any element *i* that is not selected as active by the vector mask register *vm*.
RULE 3: When the immediate *imm* equals –1, VXOR.VI must produce the bitwise complement of each active element of *vs2* and write that complement to the corresponding element of *vd*.

INSTRUCTION: VXOR.VV
FORMAT: vxor.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VXOR.VV reads source vector register vs2 and source vector register vs1 as vectors of equal length.
RULE 2: VXOR.VV computes, for each element index i, the bitwise exclusive OR of the i-th element of vs2 with the i-th element of vs1.
RULE 3: VXOR.VV writes the result of the element-wise bitwise exclusive OR into the corresponding element of destination vector register vd for each element whose mask bit in vm is true.
RULE 4: VXOR.VV leaves unchanged any element of destination vector register vd whose mask bit in vm is false.

INSTRUCTION: VZEXT.VF2
FORMAT: vzext.vf2 vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VZEXT.VF2 vd, vs2, vm zero-extends each element of the source vector register vs2 whose element width is SEW/2 to produce destination elements of width SEW placed in vector register vd, applying the optional mask vm to predicate element-wise operation.
RULE 2: VZEXT.VF2 requires that the source element width (EEW) be SEW/2 and that this EEW be a supported width for the implementation.

INSTRUCTION: VZEXT.VF4
FORMAT: vzext.vf4 vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: When the destination EEW of VZEXT.VF4 is greater than the source EEW, the source EMUL must be at least 1 and the highest-numbered register of the destination vector register group must equal the highest-numbered register of the source vector register group.
RULE 2: VZEXT.VF4 may overlap a source vector register group with a destination vector register group only if the destination EEW equals the source EEW.
RULE 3: VZEXT.VF4 may overlap a source vector register group with a destination vector register group only if the destination EEW is smaller than the source EEW and the lowest-numbered register of the destination vector register group equals the lowest-numbered register of the source vector register group.
RULE 4: VZEXT.VF4 may overlap a source vector register group with a destination vector register group only if the destination EEW is greater than the source EEW, the source EMUL is at least 1, and the highest-numbered register of the destination vector register group equals the highest-numbered register of the source vector register group.
RULE 5: Any VZEXT.VF4 instruction encoding that violates the overlap constraints is reserved.
RULE 6: A VZEXT.VF4 encoding that uses a vector register other than the lowest-numbered register to specify the vector register group is reserved.
RULE 7: When the source and destination registers of VZEXT.VF4 overlap and have different EEWs, VZEXT.VF4 is mask-agnostic and tail-agnostic regardless of the vta and vma bits in vtype.
RULE 8: VZEXT.VF4 must not use a vector register group larger than eight registers (EMUL ≤8); if the required group exceeds eight registers, the encoding is reserved.
RULE 9: VZEXT.VF4 zero-extends each element of the source vector register vs2, whose element width is SEW/4, producing SEW-wide elements written to the destination vector register vd.
RULE 10: VZEXT.VF4 may be executed with an optional mask register vm that controls which destination elements are updated.
RULE 11: VZEXT.VF4 requires that the source operand element width (EEW) equals SEW divided by 4.
RULE 12: VZEXT.VF4 requires that the source operand EMUL equals (1/4) multiplied by the LMUL setting of the instruction.
RULE 13: VZEXT.VF4 produces a destination vector whose element width (EEW) equals SEW.
RULE 14: VZEXT.VF4 produces a destination vector whose EMUL equals the LMUL setting of the instruction.
RULE 15: VZEXT.VF4 encoding is reserved when the source EEW is not a supported width or when the computed source EMUL would be below the minimum legal LMUL.
RULE 16: The vector instruction encoding whose vs1 field equals binary 00100 must be interpreted as the VZEXT.VF4 instruction.
RULE 17: Any vector instruction encoding with a vs1 field value other than binary 00100 is reserved and must not be defined as the VZEXT.VF4 instruction.

INSTRUCTION: VZEXT.VF8
FORMAT: vzext.vf8 vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VZEXT.VF8 zero-extends each source element whose element width is SEW/8 to a destination element of width SEW.
RULE 2: VZEXT.VF8 requires that the destination vector register's element width is SEW and its EMUL field equals LMUL.
RULE 3: VZEXT.VF8 requires that the source vector register's element width is SEW/8 and its EMUL field equals (EEW/SEW) × LMUL.
RULE 4: VZEXT.VF8 updates only those destination elements whose corresponding mask bit in vm is set; destination elements with a cleared mask bit remain unchanged.
RULE 5: VZEXT.VF8 encoding is reserved when the source element width is not a supported width equal to SEW/8.
RULE 6: VZEXT.VF8 encoding is reserved when the source EMUL value would be less than the minimum legal LMUL.

INSTRUCTION: WRS.NTO
FORMAT: wrs.nto
RULE 1: WRS.NTO shall cause the executing hart to temporarily stall execution in a low-power state until either a store occurs to any address in the current reservation set or an interrupt is observed.
RULE 2: WRS.NTO causes the hart to temporarily stall execution in a low-power state while the reservation set is valid and no pending interrupts are observed, even if interrupts are disabled.
RULE 3: WRS.NTO is available in all privilege modes.
RULE 4: WRS.NTO may stall execution only when both the reservation set is valid and no pending interrupt is observed.
RULE 5: When a pending interrupt becomes observed, WRS.NTO resumes execution according to the same rules that govern the WFI instruction.
RULE 6: If the TW bit in mstatus is set, WRS.NTO is executed in any privilege mode other than M mode, and the instruction does not complete within an implementation-specific bounded time limit, then WRS.NTO causes an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 7: If execution is in VS or VU mode, the VTW bit in hstatus is set, the TW bit in mstatus is clear, and WRS.NTO does not complete within an implementation-specific bounded time limit, then WRS.NTO causes a virtual-instruction exception.
RULE 8: WRS.NTO does not cause an illegal-instruction exception when executed in U-mode with the governing TW bit equal to 0.

INSTRUCTION: WRS.STO
FORMAT: wrs.sto
RULE 1: WRS.STO must initiate a stall with the same termination conditions as the WRS.NTO instruction.
RULE 2: WRS.STO must bound the stall duration by an implementation-defined short timeout and must terminate the stall on that timeout if no other stall-termination condition has occurred.
RULE 3: After WRS.STO execution, the architectural state shall provide a mechanism that software may use to determine whether the short timeout caused the stall to terminate.
RULE 4: WRS.STO MUST cause the hart to temporarily stall execution in a low-power state while the reservation set remains valid and no pending interrupts are observed.
RULE 5: The stall induced by WRS.STO MUST be bounded by an implementation-defined short timeout.
RULE 6: WRS.STO MUST be available for execution in all privilege modes.
RULE 7: While a WRS.STO stall is in progress, the stall MUST continue only while the reservation set remains valid.
