RULE 8: While a WRS.STO stall is in progress, the stall MUST continue only while the implementation-defined short timeout has not yet elapsed.
RULE 9: While a WRS.STO stall is in progress, the stall MUST continue only while no pending interrupt is observed.
RULE 10: If a pending interrupt becomes observed during a WRS.STO stall, the stall MUST end and execution must resume according to the same rules that apply to the WFI instruction.

INSTRUCTION: XNOR
FORMAT: xnor rd, rs1, rs2
RULE 1: XNOR must compute the bitwise NOT of the XOR of the values in rs1 and rs2 and write the resulting XLEN-wide value to rd.
RULE 2: XNOR is defined for both RV32 and RV64 base ISA extensions, so it operates on 32-bit values in RV32 and on 64-bit values in RV64.
RULE 3: XNOR reads the integer registers specified as rs1 and rs2 and writes the computed result to the integer register specified as rd.
RULE 4: An implementation that supports the Zbkb extension must provide the XNOR instruction.
RULE 5: The XNOR instruction must be present in RV32 implementations that include the Zbkb extension.
RULE 6: The XNOR instruction must be present in RV64 implementations that include the Zbkb extension.
RULE 7: The XNOR instruction performs a bitwise exclusive-NOR operation on its two source operands and writes the resulting value to the destination register.
RULE 8: The vmxnor.mm XNOR instruction computes each mask element i as the logical NOT of the exclusive-OR of the corresponding bits of vs2.mask[i] and vs1.mask[i], i.e., vd.mask[i] = !(vs2.mask[i] ^^ vs1.mask[i]).
RULE 9: The vmxnor.mm XNOR instruction operates only on mask registers, where each element is a single-bit mask value.
RULE 10: The destination vector register of the vmxnor.mm XNOR instruction may be the same physical register as either source vector register.
RULE 11: During execution of the vmxnor.mm XNOR instruction, mask elements whose indices are less than the current vstart value remain unchanged.
RULE 12: After the vmxnor.mm XNOR instruction completes, the architectural vstart register is reset to zero.
RULE 13: All vmxnor.mm XNOR executions are unmasked, meaning no element is considered inactive regardless of the vm field.
RULE 14: Encodings of the vmxnor.mm XNOR instruction with the vm field equal to zero are reserved and have undefined architectural behavior.
RULE 15: The vmxnor.mm XNOR instruction updates mask elements whose indices are greater than or equal to vl according to the tail-agnostic policy defined for vector mask logical instructions.
RULE 16: The vmxnor.mm XNOR instruction always uses a single vector register for its operation irrespective of the vlmul setting in the vtype register.
RULE 17: Execution of the vmxnor.mm XNOR instruction does not modify the architectural vlmul field.
RULE 18: The assembler pseudoinstruction vmset.m, which expands to vmxnor.mm XNOR vd, vd, vd, sets every mask element in vd to logical one.
RULE 19: The logical function performed by vmxnor.mm XNOR corresponds to the truth-table pattern 1 0 0 1, representing the XNOR of the two input mask bits.
RULE 20: When the vmxnor.mm XNOR instruction is used with identical source and destination registers (vd, vd, vd), the result is a mask register whose every element is set to one.
RULE 21: The XNOR instruction computes a bit-wise exclusive-NOR of the architectural values in rs1 and rs2, producing a result where each bit is 1 if the corresponding bits of rs1 and rs2 are equal and 0 otherwise.
RULE 22: The XNOR instruction writes the computed bit-wise exclusive-NOR result into the destination register rd.
RULE 23: The XNOR instruction reads the architectural values of source registers rs1 and rs2 without modifying either register.
RULE 24: The XNOR instruction MUST be implemented in RV32 base ISA implementations.
RULE 25: The XNOR instruction MUST be implemented in RV64 base ISA implementations.
RULE 26: The XNOR instruction is defined as performing a bitwise exclusive-NOR of its two source registers and writing the result to the destination register.
RULE 27: The vmxnor XNOR instruction is encoded with the funct6 field set to the binary value 011111.

INSTRUCTION: XOR
FORMAT: xor rd, rs1, rs2
RULE 1: The XORI instruction is an integer register-immediate operation that must compute a bitwise XOR between the value in source register rs1 and a 12-bit immediate.
RULE 2: The 12-bit immediate used by an XORI instruction must be sign-extended to XLEN bits before the XOR operation is performed.
RULE 3: The result of the XOR between the sign-extended immediate and the value in rs1 must be written to the destination register rd.
RULE 4: If the immediate field of an XORI instruction is –1, the sign-extended immediate consists of all 1 bits, causing the XOR to produce the bitwise logical inversion of rs1 and store the result in rd, which is equivalent to the NOT pseudoinstruction.
RULE 5: XOR MUST read the contents of registers rs1 and rs2 as source operands and MUST write the computed result into register rd.
RULE 6: XOR MUST compute a bitwise logical exclusive-OR of each pair of corresponding bits of rs1 and rs2, producing an XLEN-wide result that is written to rd.
RULE 7: If register rc equals zero, the conditional XOR operation must write to rd the result of the bitwise exclusive-or of rs1 and rs2.
RULE 8: If register rc does not equal zero, the conditional XOR operation must write to rd the result of the bitwise exclusive-or of rs1 and rs2.
RULE 9: An AMOXOR instruction must atomically load a data value from the address in rs1, place the loaded value into rd, compute the bitwise XOR of the loaded value and the original value in rs2, and store the XOR result back to the same address in rs1.
RULE 10: When an AMOXOR instruction operates on a 32-bit word in RV64, the 32-bit XOR result placed in rd must be sign-extended to 64 bits and the upper 32 bits of rs2 must be ignored.
RULE 11: If the address in rs1 for an AMOXOR instruction is not naturally aligned to the operand size, the implementation must raise either an address-misaligned exception or an access-fault exception.
RULE 12: If a misaligned atomicity granule PMA is defined and all bytes accessed by an AMOXOR instruction lie within the same granule, the instruction must not raise an alignment exception and must perform a single atomic memory operation.
RULE 13: When the aq bit is set on an AMOXOR instruction, no later memory operations in the same hart may be observed to occur before the AMOXOR.
RULE 14: When the rl bit is set on an AMOXOR instruction, other harts must not observe the AMOXOR before any memory accesses that precede it in the same hart.
RULE 15: When both aq and rl bits are set on an AMOXOR instruction, the operation is sequentially consistent, meaning it cannot be reordered with earlier or later memory operations from the same hart.
RULE 16: AMOXOR.B/H must sign-extend the original memory value placed into rd after performing the XOR operation, extending it to the processor's XLEN.
RULE 17: AMOXOR.B/H must ignore any bits of rs2 that lie outside the operand width when computing the XOR result.
RULE 18: AMOXOR.B/H must use an address in rs1 that is naturally aligned to the operand size; if the address is not naturally aligned, the instruction triggers the misaligned-address exception defined for atomic instructions.
RULE 19: AMOXOR.B/H must apply the same exception behavior as specified for atomic instructions in the base A extension when an exception condition occurs.
RULE 20: AMOXOR.B/H may set the aq and rl bits to provide optional acquire and release ordering semantics for the XOR operation, following the release-consistency rules for atomic memory operations.
RULE 21: AMOXOR.B/H uses the width field to select the operand size, where width=0 causes the XOR operation to act on a byte and width=1 causes it to act on a halfword.
RULE 22: The c.not instruction MUST compute the value of the architectural register X(rsdc) as X(rsdc) XOR 1 and write the result back to the same register.
RULE 23: The XORI instruction with immediate –1 MUST compute the bitwise exclusive-or of the source register rd'/rs1' with the constant value –1 (all bits set) and write the result to the destination register rd'.
RULE 24: The XORI instruction with immediate –1 MUST provide the 32-bit equivalent behavior of the c.not instruction.
RULE 25: When the immediate field imm of the vxor.vi (vector-immediate) form of the XOR instruction is set to -1, the instruction performs a bitwise NOT operation on the source vector.
RULE 26: The assembler pseudoinstruction vnot.v vd,vs,vm is defined to expand to the XOR instruction form vxor.vi vd,vs,-1,vm.
RULE 27: The vector reduction XOR instruction (vredxor.vs) MUST use the same SEW width for all source operands and for the destination register.
RULE 28: The vector reduction XOR instruction (vredxor.vs) computes vd[0] as the bitwise XOR of vs1[0] with the bitwise XOR of all active elements of vs2.
RULE 29: The vmxor.mm XOR operation sets each destination mask bit to the exclusive-OR of the corresponding source mask bits from the two source vector registers.
RULE 30: The vmxor.mm XOR instruction operates on mask registers where each element is a single bit, and it always uses a single vector register regardless of the vlmul field in vtype.
RULE 31: The destination register of the vmxor.mm XOR instruction may be the same register as either source operand.
RULE 32: For the vmxor.mm XOR instruction, mask elements whose indices are less than the current vstart value remain unchanged, and the vstart field is reset to zero after the instruction completes.
RULE 33: The vmxor.mm XOR instruction is always unmasked, meaning no elements are considered inactive, and any encoding with vm=0 is reserved.
RULE 34: The vmxor.mm XOR instruction updates mask elements whose indices are greater than or equal to the current vector length (vl) according to the tail-agnostic policy, regardless of the tail status.
RULE 35: Executing vmxor.mm XOR with both source operands equal to the destination register (vd, vd, vd) produces a mask register whose every element is zero, effectively clearing the register.
RULE 36: The vmxor.mm XOR instruction implements the logical truth table where 0 XOR 0 yields 0, 0 XOR 1 yields 1, 1 XOR 0 yields 1, and 1 XOR 1 yields 0 for each mask element.
RULE 37: The XOR operation of this instruction must compute a bitwise exclusive-OR between the 128-bit round-key element and each 128-bit element group of the destination register vd, producing a new 128-bit element group for each group.
RULE 38: After execution, each element group of the destination register vd must contain the result of XORing the corresponding original element group with the round key.
RULE 39: The .vv form of this instruction must produce identical architectural results to the vxor.vv vd, vs2, vd instruction.
RULE 40: The XOR performed by the .vv form must therefore exhibit the same architectural behavior as the XOR performed by this instruction.
RULE 41: The implementation of this XOR instruction must guarantee that its execution latency does not depend on the operand data values.
RULE 42: The XOR operation of the vxor.vv instruction must also have execution latency independent of the operand data values.
RULE 43: The AMOLogical support level indicates that swap instructions and all logical AMOs—including amoand, amoor, and amoxor (XOR)—are supported.
RULE 44: A naturally aligned amoxor (XOR) of a given width is architecturally supported only when the accessed memory region permits reads and writes of that width.
RULE 45: A memory region may not support the amoxor (XOR) operation even if the processor implements amoxor, making the instruction unavailable for that region.

INSTRUCTION: XORI
FORMAT: xori rd, rs1, imm12
RULE 1: XORI must compute the bitwise exclusive-OR of the value in integer register rs1 and the sign-extended 12-bit immediate, and must write the resulting XLEN-bit value into integer register rd.
RULE 2: XORI must sign-extend the 12-bit immediate operand to the processor's XLEN width before the exclusive-OR operation is performed.
RULE 3: XORI with an immediate value of –1 must produce a result equal to the bitwise logical inversion of the value in rs1 and must place that inverted value into rd, which is equivalent to the NOT pseudoinstruction.
RULE 4: An XORI instruction whose destination register equals its source register and whose immediate operand is -1 functions as the 32-bit equivalent of the compressed c.not instruction.
RULE 5: When an XORI instruction has its destination register identical to its source register and its immediate operand is -1, the instruction computes the bitwise NOT of the original register value and writes that result back to the same register.

INSTRUCTION: XPERM4
FORMAT: xperm4 rd, rs1, rs2
RULE 1: XPERM4 implements a lookup-table permutation operation on 4-bit (nibble) elements stored in general-purpose registers.
RULE 2: XPERM4 may also be used to permute 8-bit elements within registers as a lookup-table operation.
RULE 3: In an XPERM4 execution the source register rs1 is interpreted as a vector of N-bit words, where N is the element size selected for the permutation.
RULE 4: In an XPERM4 execution the source register rs2 is interpreted as a vector of N-bit indices that select elements from the rs1 vector.
RULE 5: For each N-bit element position, XPERM4 obtains the index from the corresponding N-bit element of rs2; if the index is less than the number of N-bit elements present in rs1, XPERM4 writes to the destination register the N-bit element from rs1 at that index, otherwise XPERM4 writes zero for that element.
RULE 6: XPERM4 writes the resulting vector of N-bit elements, each selected or zero-filled as specified, into the destination register rd.
RULE 7: XPERM4 MUST interpret the source register rs1 as a vector of XLEN/4 consecutive 4-bit elements (nibbles) and the source register rs2 as a vector of XLEN/4 consecutive 4-bit indexes.
RULE 8: XPERM4 MUST, for each multiple-of-4 bit position i from 0 to XLEN-4, extract the 4-bit index from rs2 bits i+3 down to i.
RULE 9: XPERM4 MUST replace the extracted index nibble with the 4-bit element from rs1 whose nibble position equals the index value, provided the index value is less than XLEN/4.
RULE 10: XPERM4 MUST produce a zero nibble in the destination position when the extracted index value is greater than or equal to XLEN/4.
RULE 11: XPERM4 MUST assemble the destination register rd by placing each resulting 4-bit element (or zero) into the corresponding nibble position i+3..i for all i that are multiples of 4 across the XLEN-bit width.
RULE 12: XPERM4 MUST initialize the result vector to all zeros before processing any nibbles, so that any nibble not written by a lookup remains zero.
RULE 13: XPERM4 MUST write the final XLEN-bit result into rd and retire successfully.
RULE 14: XPERM4 MUST place into bits i+3 down to i of the result the low-order 4 bits of rs1 after right-shifting rs1 by (idx × 4) bits, where idx is the 4-bit index extracted from rs2 for that nibble.
RULE 15: XPERM4 MUST write zero into bits i+3 down to i of the result when the extracted idx value multiplied by 4 is greater than or equal to XLEN (i.e., the index is out of bounds).
RULE 16: XPERM4 MUST write the assembled result to rd after all nibbles have been processed.
RULE 17: XPERM4 operates on 4-bit elements (nibbles) of the source registers.
RULE 18: XPERM4 treats the value in rs1 as a vector of XLEN/4 unsigned 4-bit elements.
RULE 19: XPERM4 treats the value in rs2 as a vector of XLEN/4 unsigned 4-bit index elements.
RULE 20: XPERM4 produces a result in rd where each 4-bit element is the 4-bit element of rs1 indexed by the corresponding 4-bit element of rs2, or zero if that index is out of bounds.

INSTRUCTION: XPERM8
FORMAT: xperm8 rd, rs1, rs2
RULE 1: An RV32 or RV64 implementation MUST support the XPERM8 instruction.
RULE 2: The XPERM8 instruction treats each byte of source register rs1 as an independent 8-bit table entry.
RULE 3: The XPERM8 instruction treats each byte of source register rs2 as an 8-bit index into the byte vector of rs1.
RULE 4: For each byte position i, the XPERM8 instruction selects the byte from rs1 whose index equals the i-th byte of rs2 and writes that selected byte to the i-th byte position of rd.
RULE 5: If the i-th byte of rs2 is greater than or equal to the number of bytes in rs1, the XPERM8 instruction writes a zero byte to the i-th byte position of rd.
RULE 6: XPERM8 treats the source register rs1 as a vector of XLEN/8 independent 8-bit elements.
RULE 7: XPERM8 treats the source register rs2 as a vector of XLEN/8 independent 8-bit index elements.
RULE 8: For each byte position i (0, 8, …, XLEN-8) XPERM8 replaces the result byte at that position with the 8-bit element of rs1 whose index equals the 8-bit value of rs2 at the same position, provided the index is less than XLEN/8.
RULE 9: If the 8-bit index taken from rs2 at a given byte position i is greater than or equal to XLEN/8, XPERM8 writes zero into the result byte at position i.
RULE 10: XPERM8 computes each result byte by shifting the full XLEN-bit value of rs1 right by (index × 8) bits and extracting the low 8 bits of the shifted value.
RULE 11: XPERM8 writes the concatenation of all result bytes into the destination register rd, with any bits not explicitly written remaining zero because the result is initially zero-extended.
RULE 12: XPERM8 completes execution without raising an architectural exception and retires successfully.
RULE 13: XPERM8 treats the source register rs1 as a vector of XLEN/8 consecutive 8-bit elements.
RULE 14: XPERM8 treats the source register rs2 as a vector of XLEN/8 consecutive 8-bit index elements.
RULE 15: XPERM8 initializes a temporary XLEN-bit result value to zero before processing any byte positions.
RULE 16: For each byte position i (where i = 0, 8, …, XLEN-8) XPERM8 reads the index byte from rs2 bits i+7 down to i.
RULE 17: XPERM8 selects a byte from rs1 by right-shifting rs1 by (index × 8) bits and extracting the least-significant 8 bits of the shifted value.
RULE 18: If the index byte read from rs2 is greater than or equal to XLEN/8, XPERM8 writes zero into the corresponding byte position of the destination register rd.
RULE 19: If the index byte read from rs2 is less than XLEN/8, XPERM8 writes the selected byte from rs1 into the corresponding byte position of rd.
RULE 20: XPERM8 writes each resulting byte into rd at the same byte position i from which the index was taken, forming the complete XLEN-bit destination value.
RULE 21: XPERM8 completes execution with a successful retirement and does not raise an exception for any defined index values.
RULE 22: XPERM8 performs a byte-wise crossbar permutation of the source operand bytes to produce its result.
RULE 23: XPERM8 is part of the Zbkb, Zbkc, and Zbkx extensions, so the instruction is available when any of those extensions are implemented.
RULE 24: XPERM8 treats the value in register rs1 as a vector of XLEN/8 independent 8-bit elements.
RULE 25: XPERM8 treats the value in register rs2 as a vector of XLEN/8 independent 8-bit index elements.
RULE 26: XPERM8 writes to each byte i of the destination register rd the byte from rs1 whose position equals the unsigned index value contained in byte i of rs2, provided that index value is less than XLEN/8.
RULE 27: XPERM8 writes zero to byte i of the destination register rd when the unsigned index value contained in byte i of rs2 is greater than or equal to XLEN/8.

INSTRUCTION: ZEXT.H
FORMAT: zext.h rd, rs1
RULE 1: An implementation that advertises support for the Zcb extension MUST provide the ZEXT.H instruction.
RULE 2: The ZEXT.H instruction (compressed form c.zext.h) zero-extends the low 16-bit halfword of the source register and writes the resulting value to the destination register rsd'.
RULE 3: ZEXT.H must take a single operand that is used as both the source and the destination register.
RULE 4: ZEXT.H must zero-extend the least-significant halfword (bits 15 down to 0) of the operand to XLEN bits by inserting zeros into all bits more significant than bit 15.
RULE 5: ZEXT.H must read and write a register whose architectural number is in the range x8-x15.
RULE 6: ZEXT.H is only defined when the Zbb extension is implemented; if Zbb is not present, the instruction is reserved/illegal.
RULE 7: ZEXT.H extracts the least-significant 16 bits of the source register rs.
RULE 8: ZEXT.H zero-extends the extracted 16-bit value to the processor's XLEN.
RULE 9: ZEXT.H writes the zero-extended XLEN-wide result to the destination register rd.
RULE 10: ZEXT.H produces an XLEN-wide result, which is 32 bits on RV32 and 64 bits on RV64.
RULE 11: ZEXT.H does not cause any architectural side effects other than updating rd.
RULE 12: ZEXT.H does not perform privilege checks or raise exceptions under normal operation.
RULE 13: ZEXT.H rd, rs must zero-extend the least-significant 16 bits of the source register rs and write the resulting XLEN-bit value into the destination register rd.
RULE 14: The architectural effect of ZEXT.H rd, rs is identical to the effect of executing the instruction sequence slli rd, rs, 16 followed by srli rd, rd, 16.
RULE 15: ZEXT.H is defined and must be available in implementations of both the RV32 and RV64 base ISAs.
RULE 16: When rs2 = x0, ZEXT.H copies the low XLEN/2 bits of rs1 into the low half of rd and zero-extends them to XLEN.
RULE 17: The ZEXT.H instruction packs the low 16 bits of rs1 and the low 16 bits of rs2 (which is zero) into the low 32 bits of rd.
RULE 18: The ZEXT.H instruction places the low 16 bits of rs1 into bits [15:0] of rd.
RULE 19: The ZEXT.H instruction sets bits [31:16] of rd to zero because rs2 is x0.
RULE 20: The ZEXT.H instruction sign-extends the resulting 32-bit value to the full XLEN of the destination register.
RULE 21: Because the 32-bit value produced by ZEXT.H has bit 31 equal to zero, the sign-extension performed by ZEXT.H leaves all bits above bit 31 in rd equal to zero.
RULE 22: ZEXT.H rd, rs MUST set bits 15:0 of rd equal to bits 15:0 of rs and MUST set all bits of rd more significant than bit 15 to zero, producing an XLEN-wide result.
RULE 23: ZEXT.H rd, rs MUST not modify any architectural state other than the destination register rd.

INSTRUCTION: ZIP
FORMAT: zip rd, rs1
RULE 1: ZIP is available only when the Zbk b (Bit-manipulation for Cryptography) extension is implemented.
RULE 2: ZIP must read the value in rs, perform the architectural bit-interleave operation on that value, and write the resulting word to rd.
RULE 3: The ZIP instruction is defined only for implementations where XLEN = 32; on any implementation with a different XLEN the ZIP encoding is reserved.
RULE 4: For each integer i with 0 ≤ i ≤ 15, execution of the ZIP instruction writes the i-th bit of the source register (rs1) into bit position 2·i of the destination register (rd).
RULE 5: For each integer i with 0 ≤ i ≤ 15, execution of the ZIP instruction writes the (i+16)-th bit of the source register (rs1) into bit position 2·i+1 of the destination register (rd).
RULE 6: The ZIP instruction interleaves the lower half (bits 0–15) of the source register into the even bit positions of the destination register and the upper half (bits 16–31) into the odd bit positions of the destination register.
RULE 7: The result produced by the ZIP instruction is the bitwise inverse of the UNZIP instruction, such that applying UNZIP to the destination register yields the original source register value.
RULE 8: ZIP performs a bit-interleave operation, producing a destination value in which the bits of the two source operands are interleaved.
RULE 9: ZIP rd, rs1 reads the 32-bit integer value from source register rs1, applies a bit-interleave (or de-interleave) transformation to that value, and writes the resulting 32-bit integer to destination register rd.
